The patch list

With EDIT off, the lower half lists all 100 patches under their bank headings. Click a name to load it; the current patch is highlighted.

DCO 1

WAVE
Switches the saw / triangle wave on or off.
PULSE
Switches the pulse wave on or off. WAVE and PULSE can both be on at once.
FREQ
Tuning in semitones, -24 to +24.
SHAPE
Morphs the wave from SAW (fully left) to TRI (fully right).
PW
Pulse width, 5 to 95% (50% is a square).

DCO 2

WAVE / PULSE / SHAPE / PW
As on DCO 1.
NOISE
Replaces DCO 2's waves with white noise.
SYNC
Hard-syncs DCO 2 to DCO 1. Tune DCO 2 up with FREQ (or sweep it with the pitch envelope) for the classic tearing sync sound.
FREQ
Tuning in semitones, -24 to +24.
DETUNE
Fine tuning against DCO 1, -50 to +50 cents (+7 by default) - the beating that makes two oscillators sound thick.

MIX

1 / 2
The balance between the two DCOs, from DCO 1 only to DCO 2 only.

VCF

A 24 dB per octave low-pass filter.

CUTOFF
The filter frequency, 20 Hz to 18 kHz.
RES
Resonance: a peak at the cutoff, for a sharper, more vocal or squelchy tone.
ENV 1
How far ENV 1 moves the cutoff, -100 to +100% (up to six octaves either way). Negative values close the filter as the envelope rises.
KEY
Keyboard tracking: how much the cutoff follows the notes you play (at 100% it moves one octave per octave), so high notes stay as bright as low ones.
FM
DCO 1 sweeps the cutoff at audio rate, for a clangorous, metallic edge.
VEL
Velocity opens the filter: harder notes are brighter (up to +5 octaves).

ENV 1 - VCF and ENV 2 - VCA

Two envelopes with the same controls. ENV 1 moves the filter (and, if you like, the pitch); ENV 2 shapes the volume of each note. The attack rises in a straight line; the decay and release fall away naturally.

DLY
A delay before the envelope starts, 0 to 3 s.
ATK
Attack time, 0.5 ms to 8 s.
DEC
Decay time to the sustain level, 5 ms to 10 s.
SUS
The level held while the key is down, 0 to 100%.
REL
Release time after the key is let go, 5 ms to 10 s.
PITCH (ENV 1 only)
ENV 1 bends the pitch, -24 to +24 semitones - for sync sweeps, zaps and drum-like drops.
DCO 2 / BOTH
Click to choose whether PITCH moves DCO 2 only or both DCOs.

LFO 1 - VIBRATO

SPEED
0.05 to 30 Hz.
DEPTH
Vibrato depth, 0 to 100 cents.
DELAY
The vibrato fades in over this time after each note, 0 to 5 s.
Wave box
Click to choose the shape: TRIANGLE, SAW UP, SAW DOWN, SQUARE, RANDOM (stepped) or NOISE (smooth random).

The mod wheel and channel aftertouch add more vibrato through LFO 1 (up to 50 cents), whichever is higher.

LFO 2

SPEED
0.05 to 30 Hz.
DEPTH
How much LFO 2 moves its destination, 0 to 100%.
Wave box
The shape, as on LFO 1.
TO PW / TO CUT / TO PITCH
The destination: the pulse width of both DCOs (PWM), the filter cutoff (up to +/-5 octaves) or the pitch (up to +/-12 semitones).

The Mod Rack

Four effects in parallel on the whole voice, saved with each patch. Each module has a SYNC key (click to step through off, SYNC straight, SYNC D dotted and SYNC T triplet): when synced, RATE or TIME becomes a note value from 4 BARS to 1/32, locked to the project tempo. The L, C and R keys pick which outputs the module feeds: left, a mono centre, and right. With C plus L or R, the outputs are offset from one another for a wider spread.

TREMOLO
RATE (0.1 - 15 Hz), DEPTH, and CHOP, which squares the wave off for a choppier, on-off tremolo.
CHORUS
RATE (0.05 - 8 Hz), DEPTH, MIX, and STRETCH, which lengthens the chorus delay and lowers its clock for a darker, grainier, warbling chorus.
VIBE
RATE (0.1 - 12 Hz), DEPTH, and LAMP, which makes the sweep lopsided and adds the throb of the old lamp-driven units.
DELAY
TIME (20 - 1500 ms), FDBK (0 - 95%), MIX, and RUNAWAY, which adds tape-like wow and flutter to the repeats and pushes the feedback harder.

PLAY strip

POLY / MONO / UNI
POLY: six voices. MONO: one voice, last-note priority and legato - a note played while another is held glides to the new pitch without restarting the envelopes, and letting go returns to the note still held. UNI (unison): all six voices on one note, detuned and spread across the stereo field, for a huge mono sound.
GLIDE
Portamento time, 0 to 1 s: the pitch slides from the previous note.
VELOCITY
How much velocity changes the volume, 0 to 100% (50% by default).
BEND
Pitch-bend range, 0 to 12 semitones.
DRIFT
See Drift below.
LEVEL
The patch level, -12 to +6 dB, saved with each patch to even out loud and quiet patches.
CH
MIDI channel: OMNI or 1 to 16.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper (no latency, the default), or a lookahead limiter (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER), both holding peaks at -0.3 dBFS.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Drift

DRIFT (0 - 100%, 25% by default) makes the module behave like real analogue hardware: the overall tuning wanders, each voice's two DCOs drift apart in fine pitch and pulse width, DCO 2 drifts against DCO 1, the filter breathes, every note lands slightly differently in tune, and the Mod Rack's free-running rates waver a little. At 0 it is perfectly steady.

Patches

Choosing a patch sets every DCO, VCF, envelope, LFO and Mod Rack setting, plus the voice mode (POLY / MONO / UNISON), GLIDE and the patch LEVEL. VOLUME, VELOCITY, BEND, DRIFT and the MIDI channel are left as they are.

Tips

  • Your edits stay until you choose another patch. To keep an edited patch, save it as a REAPER preset (the + button at the top of the plugin window).
  • Use LEVEL rather than VOLUME to balance one patch against another - LEVEL is saved with the patch.
  • For a thick lead, try a MONO patch and switch to UNI: all six voices stack on one note.
  • Raise DETUNE on DCO 2 and add the CHORUS module for wide, classic pads.
  • If a patch's echo clashes with your song, switch the DELAY module's SYNC on so its time follows the project tempo.

File: gfx-g-m1k.jsfx

Instruments

G Pocket 70

A late-90s sound module: 90 synthesised patches, from pianos to pads to a drum kit, with effects built in.

Overview

G Pocket 70 is a sound module in the spirit of the late-90s desktop boxes: pick a patch and play. There are 90 patches in ten groups - synthetic pianos, FM and chorus electric pianos, clav, drawbar organ, bells and glass, guitars and plucks, strings, pads, choirs, brass, leads, basses and a drum kit on the GM drum map. Every sound is synthesised; there are no samples.

Each patch comes with its own effects: the GFX Mod Rack (tremolo, chorus, vibe and delay) and a reverb (room, hall or plate). The EDIT page lets you move a patch from where it stands - brighter, darker, faster or slower envelopes - and choosing a patch puts everything back.

It plays up to 16 notes at once. GFX drift is built in, fixed and subtle: the tuning wanders a little and every note lands slightly differently.

Reach for it for

  • Quick, playable sounds for sketching and demos
  • 90s-style keys, pads and choirs
  • Basses and leads without building a patch
  • A GM-style drum kit for programming beats
  • Layering a second keyboard part with built-in effects

Playing it

  1. Insert G Pocket 70 on a track as an instrument (it has no audio input; it plays from MIDI).
  2. Arm the track for recording with a MIDI input and turn on record monitoring, or draw notes in a MIDI item.
  3. Click a patch name on the patch list to choose it. The MIDI IN light blinks green when notes arrive.
  4. The mod wheel (CC1) adds vibrato, the sustain pedal (CC64) holds notes, pitch bend follows BEND, and CC120 / CC123 stop all notes. A program change 0 - 89 on the MIDI channel selects patches 1 - 90.
  5. If you use more than one MIDI channel, set MIDI CH on the EDIT page to the one you want; OMNI plays on all.

Controls

The top bar

Display
Shows the patch's group, number and name. While you turn a knob on the EDIT page it shows that knob's name and value for a moment instead. Click the display to go to the patch list.
< / >
Step to the previous or next patch (wrapping round at the ends).
EDIT
Opens the EDIT page.
MIDI IN
Blinks green when a note arrives.
VOLUME
Output level, -24 to +6 dB.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large (it opens at large). The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

The patch list

Every patch, by group, in six columns. Click a name to choose it; the selected patch is lit blue.

EDIT page

The TONE, FILTER ENVELOPE and AMP ENVELOPE knobs move the patch from where it stands: the centre (or 100%) is the patch as made, and choosing a patch puts them back. Controls that the current patch has no use for are dimmed.

TONE

TONE
Brighter or darker, by up to 1.5 octaves either way from the centre.
FILTER
Moves the patch's filter, from -6 to +3 octaves. On the FM, plucked-string, metal-bar, piano and organ patches (which have no filter of their own) it works a low-pass on the output, so only turning it down has an effect.
RESONANCE
Adds resonance to the filter, 0 to 100%.

FILTER ENVELOPE

Works on the analogue and GM-style patches. Session Piano, Bright Piano, Chorus EP, FM E-Piano, Clav, Glockenspiel, Drawbar Organ and Drum Kit have no filter envelope: the row is dimmed and reads (not on this patch). On those patches the AMP ENVELOPE's ATTACK and SUSTAIN are dimmed too, DECAY is dimmed on the organ and the kit, and RELEASE on the kit.

AMOUNT
How far the envelope moves the filter, 0 to 200% of the patch's own amount.
ATTACK / DECAY / RELEASE
Scale the patch's filter envelope times, from 10% (faster) to 400% (slower).
SUSTAIN
Moves the patch's sustain level, -100 to +100.

AMP ENVELOPE

ATTACK / DECAY / RELEASE
Scale the patch's volume envelope times, from 10% (faster) to 400% (slower). On struck and plucked sounds DECAY sets how long they ring.
SUSTAIN
Moves the patch's sustain level, -100 to +100.

PLAY

VELOCITY
0 to 100% (70% by default): how much playing harder makes notes louder (and on many patches brighter). 0 ignores velocity.
TUNE
Fine tuning, -50 to +50 cents.
BEND
Pitch-bend range, 0 to 12 semitones (2 by default).
MIDI CH
The MIDI channel it listens to: OMNI (all) or 1 to 16. Click it for the list.

REVERB

ROOM / HALL / PLATE
The reverb type: a small room, a large hall, or a dense, bright plate.
PRE
Pre-delay before the reverb starts, 0 to 150 ms.
DECAY
Reverb time, 0.2 to 10 s.
DAMP
How quickly the high frequencies die away in the tail, 0 to 100%.
MIX
How much reverb is added, 0 to 100%.

MOD RACK

Four effects working side by side on the instrument: TREMOLO, CHORUS, VIBE and DELAY. Each adds its own effect to the dry sound. They are off when their depth or mix is at zero.

SYNC
Click to step through off, SYNC (straight), SYNC D (dotted) and SYNC T (triplet). With sync on, RATE (or TIME on the delay) becomes a note value from 4 BARS to 1/32, locked to the project tempo.
L / C / R
Which outputs the module works on: L the left side, R the right side, C a mono centre feed to both. With C and a side on together, the sides are offset for stereo movement.
TREMOLO: RATE, DEPTH
Volume wobble: 0.1 to 15 Hz, depth 0 to 100%.
CHORUS: RATE, DEPTH, MIX
Chorus: 0.05 to 8 Hz, depth 0 to 100%, mix 0 to 100%.
VIBE: RATE, DEPTH
A four-stage phase-shift swirl: 0.1 to 12 Hz, depth 0 to 100%.
DELAY: TIME, FDBK, MIX
Echo: 20 to 1500 ms (or a synced note value), feedback 0 to 95%, mix 0 to 100%.

OUTPUT

SAT
Band saturation on the way out: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper (no latency, the default), or a lookahead limiter (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER).
MONO BELOW
Makes everything below this frequency mono, from off (20 Hz) up to 500 Hz.
WIDTH
Widens the stereo image above that, 0 to 100% (up to twice the side signal).

Patches

90 patches in ten groups. The number before each name is its place in the list; MIDI program change 0 - 89 picks patches 1 - 90. Every patch carries its own reverb (type, decay and mix) and Mod Rack settings.

The drum kit (patch 88)

Tips

  • Choosing a patch resets the EDIT knobs and loads its own reverb and Mod Rack, so make your edits after you have picked the patch.
  • Native Dance follows the project tempo: its loop locks to the timeline when the project plays.
  • Turn on the delay's SYNC so the echoes sit in time with the track.
  • On bass patches, set MONO BELOW to around 120 Hz to keep the low end centred if you add stereo chorus or width.

Instruments

G Rainbow

A drone organ with a two-deck time-lag tape-loop delay, after the 60s modified-organ and tape-loop pieces.

Overview

G Rainbow is an eight-voice organ built for drones and minimalist loops. Each voice has two oscillator ranks, each tuned to a modal interval of the note you play, with a wave that blends smoothly from sine through triangle and saw to pulse. The ranks go through a saturating four-pole ladder filter and a simple organ-style amp envelope with vibrato.

The heart of the instrument is the TIME-LAG ACCUMULATOR: two virtual tape decks in series. The organ is recorded on deck 1, deck 1's playback feeds deck 2, and deck 2's playback is fed back into deck 1's record head - so what you play piles up, layer on layer, slowly darkening and wobbling like old tape. HOLD freezes the loop so you can play over it.

A drift control adds a slow, audible wander of pitch, detune, pulse width, filter and tape speed, plus a small random tuning on every note.

Reach for it for

  • Evolving drones and pads
  • Minimalist repeating patterns that build up over time
  • Tape-loop textures to play over (HOLD)
  • Detuned organ tones at modal intervals
  • Ambient beds that keep moving

Getting started

  1. Insert G Rainbow on a track (it is an instrument: it has no audio input).
  2. Arm the track with a MIDI input, or draw notes in a MIDI item. It plays on any MIDI channel and responds to velocity and the sustain pedal (CC64).
  3. Pick a preset from the display at the top, then play a few notes and let the loops build.

Controls

Knobs: drag up or down (Shift for fine), double-click or Ctrl-click to reset. A knob's value shows while you hover over it, and the display at the top briefly shows the control you touched.

The header

Preset display
Shows PRESET nn/16 and the name. Click it for the list of presets; the arrows either side step through them.
SIZE (S / M / L)
Panel size: small, medium or large.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.
PANIC
Releases every note.
MIDI light
Flashes when MIDI arrives.
HW
Switches between the detailed hardware look and a flat look.
COBALT
Switches the panel from copper to a complementary blue colour scheme.

RANK A and RANK B

Interval
The arrows choose where the rank sounds relative to the note you play: -2 OCT, -1 OCT, UNISON, MIN 3RD, MAJ 3RD, 4TH, 5TH, OCTAVE, 12TH or 2 OCT.
Wave scope
Two cycles of the rank's current waveform.
WAVE
Blends the waveform from sine through triangle and saw to pulse, 0 to 100%. The label shows the dominant wave.
LEVEL
The rank's level, 0 to 100%.
DETUNE
Fine tuning of the rank, -50 to +50 cents. A few cents between the ranks gives a slow beating.

LADDER FILTER

CUTOFF
40 Hz to 16 kHz. It follows the keyboard by half, so higher notes are a little brighter.
RESO
Resonance, 0 to 100%. Near the top the filter starts to self-oscillate.
DRIVE
How hard the ranks drive the filter, 0 to 100%, for a thicker, saturated tone.
Response display
A sketch of the filter's frequency response.

AMP

ATTACK
1 ms to 3 s.
RELEASE
10 ms to 8 s.
VIBRATO
Organ-style vibrato at about 5.8 Hz, 0 to 100% (up to 30 cents).
Envelope display
A sketch of the attack and release.

TIME-LAG ACCUMULATOR

DECK 1 / DECK 2 (reels)
The two decks. The readout shows each deck's length, and the bar under the reels shows the loop position.
DECK 1
The length of deck 1's loop, 0.5 to 20 seconds. Click the DECK 1 label under the knob to switch to bars (1/2, 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8 or 12 bars of the project tempo, up to 20 seconds).
DECK 2
The length of deck 2's loop, set the same way. Deck 2 plays deck 1's output a further DECK 2 later.
FEEDBACK
How much of deck 2's playback is re-recorded onto deck 1 on every pass, 0 to 100%. High settings let the layers pile up for a long time.
AGE
Tape wear on every pass, 0 to 100%: the repeats get darker and more saturated (the lows below about 35 Hz are kept clean).
WOW
Wow and flutter of the transports, 0 to 100%: a slow pitch wander with a little fast flutter.
MIX
The level of the loops against the organ, 0 to 100%. The left side carries more of deck 1, the right more of deck 2.
HOLD
Freezes the loop: new playing is no longer recorded and what is on the tape keeps circulating (almost without loss). Play over it; switch HOLD off to record again.

MASTER

DRIFT
0 to 100% (30% by default). A slow wander of pitch (up to 8 cents), extra detune on rank B, pulse width, the filter cutoff and the tape speed, plus a random tuning offset on every new note (up to 5 cents).
OUTPUT
Output level, -24 to +6 dB. A built-in limiter keeps the output just under full scale.
OUT meter
Output level.

Presets

Every preset sets the ranks, filter, amp and accumulator, and resets DRIFT to 30% and OUTPUT to -6 dB. HOLD is switched off.

Clean Organ
Unison and octave ranks, the accumulator off: the plain organ.
Rainbow
Unison and octave with 4 s and 6 s decks building up at 60% feedback.
Dervish
Unison and fifth, darker and more resonant, with deeper vibrato, more feedback, age and wow.
Poppy
A bright pulse-leaning unison rank with a 12th above, strong vibrato and long 8 s / 12 s decks.
In C Pulse
Short, plucky organ notes with the decks synced to 1 and 2 bars, for repeating patterns.
Keyboard Study
A brighter, softer organ with short 1.5 s / 2.5 s loops.
Copper Cathedral
An octave below and two octaves above, slow attack and release, long 12 s / 18 s decks at 80% feedback.
Rust Drone
An octave below and a fifth, dark and heavily driven, with lots of age and wow.
Sunrise Drone
Soft waves an octave below and a fifth, a slow 800 ms attack and no vibrato.
Reed Swarm
Buzzy unison and octave ranks, strong vibrato and short 2 s / 3 s loops.
Tape Echoes
Unison and a 12th with short 0.75 s / 1.25 s decks, for echoes rather than layers.
Modal Fifths
Unison and fifth with 5 s / 7.5 s decks.
Glass Harmonics
Near-sine ranks at an octave and two octaves, an open filter and a slow attack.
Minor Third Mist
Unison and a minor third, a slow attack and well-aged 7 s / 11 s loops.
Pulse Machine
Very short, resonant notes with the decks synced to half a bar and one bar.
Frozen Choir
Unison and fifth, a 1.2 s attack and 6 s release, long 15 s / 20 s decks at 90% feedback.

Tips

  • Make the two deck lengths an uneven ratio (4 s and 6 s, 5 s and 7.5 s) so the layers keep shifting against each other rather than lining up.
  • Build a bed with FEEDBACK high, then press HOLD and play a melody over the frozen loop.
  • Sync the decks to bars when you want the loops to stay in time with the project.
  • If the loops get too dark or muddy, lower AGE; if they get too loud, lower FEEDBACK or MIX.

File: gfx-g-rainbow.jsfx

Instruments

G Tonewheel

A tonewheel drawbar organ with percussion, key click, scanner vibrato / chorus and valve drive.

Overview

G Tonewheel is a drawbar organ in the classic tonewheel style. 91 tonewheels spin all the time and stay locked together, as on the real thing; nine drawbars from 16' to 1' mix their harmonics, with the classic foldback at the top and bottom of the range.

It has the parts that make the sound: percussion on the 2nd or 3rd harmonic (single-trigger - it only strikes again once all keys are lifted), the key click of the contacts, the scanner vibrato and chorus (V1 - V3, C1 - C3), a valve preamp DRIVE, TONE and the SWELL pedal.

The organ is mono and dry by design. For the full sound, put GFX Rotary after it: G Tonewheel passes all MIDI on, so the mod wheel or sustain pedal can switch the rotary's speed.

Reach for it for

  • Jazz, soul and gospel organ parts
  • Rock organ with overdrive (into GFX Rotary)
  • Reggae bubble and other percussive comping
  • Soft flute and ballad registrations
  • Swell-pedal dynamics from an expression pedal (CC11)

Playing it

G Tonewheel is an instrument: it has no audio input and makes its sound from MIDI.

  1. Insert G Tonewheel on a track (as the first plug-in, or after a MIDI effect).
  2. Arm the track for recording and set its input to your MIDI keyboard (or draw notes in a MIDI item).
  3. Pull the drawbars, or pick a registration from PRESETS.
  4. Play. The MIDI IN light shows notes arriving, and the MIDI section shows how many keys are down.
  5. For the classic rotary-speaker sound, add GFX Rotary after G Tonewheel on the same track.

G Tonewheel responds to the expression pedal (CC11 moves SWELL) and all-notes-off (CC120 and CC123). Velocity does not change the level, as on a real organ. All MIDI is passed on down the chain.

Controls

The header

PRESETS, arrows and display
PRESETS (or a click on the display) opens the list of drawbar registrations; the ◄ / ► keys step through them. The display shows the preset number and name. Choosing one sets the drawbars, percussion, vibrato / chorus, CLICK and DRIVE; TONE, SWELL, TUNE, DRIFT and OUTPUT are left as they are. The last entry resets everything to the defaults.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

DRAWBARS

Nine drawbars, left to right: 16', 5 1/3', 8', 4', 2 2/3', 2', 1 3/5', 1 1/3' and 1'. The two brown ones are the sub-harmonics, the white ones the octaves of the note and the black ones the fifths and the third. Drag a drawbar down to pull it out (0 to 8), or use the mouse wheel over it. Each step is about 3 dB; 0 is off. A short stub of each drawbar's stalk always shows, even when it is pushed fully in.

PERC

ON
Switches the percussion on: a decaying ping on the 2nd or 3rd harmonic at the start of each phrase. It is single-trigger: it only strikes again once all keys are lifted, so play detached for percussion on every chord. While it is on, the 1' drawbar is silent (the percussion borrows it).
THIRD
Off: percussion on the 2nd harmonic (an octave up). On: on the 3rd harmonic (an octave and a fifth up).
SOFT
Off (NORMAL): loud percussion, and the drawbars drop by about 3 dB to make room for it. On: softer percussion, the drawbars at full level.
SLOW
Off (FAST): a short ping. On: a longer decay.

THIRD, SOFT and SLOW only work (and only light up) while ON is lit.

V/C

V1 / V2 / V3
Scanner vibrato, from shallow (V1) to deep (V3).
C1 / C2 / C3
Scanner chorus: the vibrato mixed with the straight sound, from shallow to deep.
Off
Click the lit key again to switch vibrato and chorus off.

VOICE

CLICK
The key click, 0 - 100% (40% by default): how fast the contacts switch on and off, plus a little contact noise. Turn it down for a smooth, soft attack; up for a percussive spit.
DRIVE
The valve preamp, 0 - 100% (15% by default). Low settings add warmth; high settings break up into growl.
TONE
Brightness, 0 - 100% (70% by default): a low-pass that rolls the top off, from about 2 kHz at 0% to 12 kHz at 100%.
SWELL
The expression pedal, 0 - 100%. It follows a squared curve, like a real swell pedal. MIDI CC11 moves it.
TUNE
Fine tuning, -50 to +50 cents.

Drift

DRIFT
0 - 100% (25% by default). The motor speed wanders slowly, so the whole organ drifts a little in pitch (up to 20 cents at 100%), TONE drifts a little brighter and darker, and the scanner's speed wavers. The tonewheels always stay locked together, so the organ never goes out of tune with itself.

MIDI

CHANNEL
The MIDI channel G Tonewheel listens to: OMNI (all) or CH 1 to CH 16.
MIDI IN
Lights when a note arrives or keys are held. Below it, the number of keys down.
PANIC
Lets go of every held key at once.

MASTER

OUTPUT
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.
OUT meters
Output level as two bars, L above R, with a floating peak. Click a meter to clear the peaks.

The output is softly clipped at -0.3 dBFS, so peaks never go over.

Presets

Soul Jazz
888000000 with third-harmonic percussion (normal, fast) and C3.
Memphis Groove
888800000, no percussion, C3 and a little more drive.
Full Organ
All drawbars out, C3, more drive.
Gospel
886800008 - the 1' on top - with C3 and more drive.
Modern Jazz
838000000 with soft, slow third-harmonic percussion, no vibrato and almost no drive.
Jazz Ballad
808000000, gentle C1 chorus, a soft click.
Rock Overdrive
888888000, C3, a sharper click and heavy drive.
Reggae Bubble
008800000 with soft, fast second-harmonic percussion and no vibrato.
Flute
The 8' alone with V1 vibrato, a very soft click and no drive.
Slow Perc
888000000 with slow third-harmonic percussion and C3.
Reset all to defaults
Every control back to its starting value.

Tips

  • Drawbar numbers read left to right: 888000000 means the first three drawbars fully out.
  • For percussion on every chord, lift all keys between chords; held legato notes will not re-strike it.
  • Put GFX Rotary straight after G Tonewheel and use the mod wheel or sustain pedal to switch it between slow and fast.
  • Map an expression pedal to CC11 for SWELL: it is the most expressive control on an organ.
  • If the organ sounds harsh with lots of upper drawbars, bring TONE down rather than pushing the drawbars back in.

File: gfx-g-tonewheel.jsfx

Instruments

G Voce 90

A half-rack electric piano module in the spirit of the classic 90s EP modules.

Overview

G Voce 90 is a 12-voice electric piano module with five synthesised voices - no samples. TINE is a warm tine piano with a metallic 'ding' on the attack that barks when you play hard. REED is a reed piano with a soft-clipped bark. PLUCK is a short, bright, sticky-reed pluck. CLAV is a plucked string with a pickup and a damper that mutes it when you let go. BUZZ is a buzzy pulse-wave plucked keyboard.

The voices run through a chain of classic keyboard effects: DRIVE, TONE, VIBRATO (a stereo pan tremolo), CHORUS and AUTOWAH, then VOLUME. The autowah can follow your playing or sweep in time with the project tempo.

Twelve factory patches cover tines, reeds, clavs and more. Higher notes decay faster, as on the real instruments, and every note gets a tiny random tuning offset.

Reach for it for

  • Soul, gospel and neo-soul electric piano parts
  • Barking, driven reed piano for rock and blues
  • Funky clav lines with an envelope or tempo-synced wah
  • Stereo tremolo keys that move across the speakers
  • Retro plucked keyboards (PLUCK, BUZZ)

Playing it

G Voce 90 is an instrument: it has no audio input and makes its sound from MIDI.

  1. Insert G Voce 90 on a track.
  2. Arm the track for recording and set its input to your MIDI keyboard (or draw notes in a MIDI item).
  3. Play. The MIDI light at the bottom right flashes on each note.

It responds to velocity, the sustain pedal (CC64), pitch bend (+/- 2 semitones) and all-notes-off (CC120 and CC123). It listens on every MIDI channel, and incoming MIDI is passed on down the chain. Up to 12 notes sound at once; past that, the oldest note is taken over.

Controls

Patch and voice

Display
Shows the patch number and name. A * after the number means you have changed the patch since loading it. The second line shows the WAH mode and the DRIVE setting, or the knob you are turning and its value. Click the display for the full patch list, grouped into Tines, Reeds and Others.
PATCH up / down
Step to the next or previous patch.
TINE / REED / PLUCK / CLAV / BUZZ
Picks the voice. The lit key shows the one in use. Changing it applies to the next notes you play.

DRIVE

DRIVE
A gentle, slightly uneven soft-clip, 0 - 100%. Low settings (10 - 30%) are almost clean and only round off the peaks; higher settings add grit and bark. The level is matched, so it changes the colour more than the volume.
TONE
A low-pass tone control, 0 - 100%. Lower is darker and rounder, higher is brighter.

VIBRATO

A stereo pan tremolo: the sound moves between the left and right speakers, as on the classic suitcase pianos.

RATE
The speed, 0.5 - 10 Hz.
DEPTH
How far it moves, 0 - 100%. At 0 it is off.

CHORUS

DEPTH
How much chorus, 0 - 100%. At 0 it is off. The chorus crosses left and right for a wide image.
RATE
The speed of the chorus wobble, 0.1 - 5 Hz.

AUTOWAH

SENS
How strongly the wah reacts, 0 - 100%. In ENV mode, how far your playing opens it; in the tempo modes, how far each sweep goes.
RANGE
How high the wah can sweep, 0 - 100%.
Q
The sharpness of the wah peak, 0 - 100%. Higher is more vocal and quacky.
MIX
The blend of wah and dry sound, 0 - 100%. At 0 the wah is off.
ENV / 1/4 / 1/8 / 1/16
Click to step through the wah modes. ENV: the wah follows how hard you play. 1/4, 1/8, 1/16: it sweeps open and closed once per quarter, eighth or sixteenth note, in time with the project tempo (locked to the beat while the transport runs).

MASTER

DRIFT
A slow random wander, 0 - 100% (25% by default). It very slightly moves the pitch (up to +/-20 cents at 100%), the TONE setting, the VIBRATO and CHORUS rates and the wah's centre, and sets how much each new note is randomly detuned (up to +/-6 cents).
VOLUME
The output level, -24 to +6 dB. Each patch also has its own level trim so the hottest patches do not hit the output clipper.
MIDI
Lights when a note arrives.

The header

OUTPUT
Folds the STEREO and OUTPUT controls out below the panel (and back in). The light shows when they are open.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

STEREO and OUTPUT (OUTPUT fold-out)

MONO
Makes everything below the set frequency mono, 20 - 500 Hz (fully left = OFF). Use it to keep the low end centred.
WIDTH
Widens the stereo image above the MONO frequency, from +0% up to +100% (twice the side signal).
SAT
Band saturation on the way out, 0 - 100%: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
BIAS
Shapes the saturation: more bias adds more even (2nd) harmonics, for a warmer, rounder colour.
LO BAND / HI BAND
Where the low and high saturation bands start (40 - 400 Hz and 2 - 16 kHz).
CEILING / THRESH
With CLIP: the level the peaks are softly clipped at. With LIMIT: the threshold of a brickwall limiter that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS - pulling it down limits harder and makes the output louder.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper (no latency), or a lookahead limiter (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). With LIMIT the gain reduction is shown beside the keys; with CLIP a red CLIP shows while it is clipping.

Patches

A patch sets the voice and every effect control; DRIFT, VOLUME and the fold-out controls are left as they are.

Soul Tine
The default: tine piano with light drive, some vibrato and chorus, and a gentle envelope wah.
Clean Tine
Tine piano almost clean: a little drive, no vibrato, chorus or wah.
Suitcase
Tine piano with deep stereo vibrato at 5 Hz.
Gospel Wah
Driven, brighter tine piano with a strong envelope wah and a little chorus.
Neo Soul
Tine piano with a slow vibrato, deeper chorus and a soft wah in the mix.
Reed Bark
Reed piano driven hard for bark, with a quick vibrato.
Reed Trem
Reed piano with deep, quick stereo vibrato.
Pluck Pop
Bright Pluck with chorus.
Funk Clav
Clav with drive and a strong, sharp envelope wah.
Sync Clav
Clav with the wah sweeping on every 16th note, in time with the project.
Buzz 70s
The BUZZ voice with deep chorus.
Dirty Keys
Tine piano driven hard, with an 8th-note wah.

Tips

  • For the classic bark, play harder: TINE and REED both get brighter and grittier with velocity, before DRIVE adds any more.
  • With CLAV, let go of the keys sharply - the damper mutes the string in about 60 ms, which is half the funk.
  • Use the tempo wah modes with the transport running so the sweep locks to the beat.
  • If a patch is too wide for a busy mix, open OUTPUT and raise MONO to keep the lows in the centre.

File: gfx-g-voce-90.jsfx

Drum machines

G Anvil

A two-voice analogue percussion module - one big kick and one snare / metal voice - with a 16-step sequencer.

Overview

G Anvil is a dual-voice analogue percussion module in the spirit of the heavy-duty German drum modules that built industrial music and techno. There are no samples: both voices are synthesised as they play.

The KICK is a bridged-T style oscillator with an exponential pitch sweep, a click for the attack and a hard, lopsided drive. The SNARE / METAL voice uses two detuned square oscillators and white noise through a resonant multi-mode filter: with METAL at 0 it is a fat snare body, turned up it becomes an inharmonic clang with ring modulation. Each voice has its own drive, and BUS SAT can push the whole module into saturation.

A two-row, 16-step sequencer plays the two voices in time with REAPER's transport, and both voices also play from MIDI notes, with velocity.

Reach for it for

  • Big, distorted techno and industrial kicks
  • Long, booming dub and rumble kicks
  • Metallic clanks, rimshots and noisy snares from one voice
  • Gabber and hardcore kicks with the drive turned right up
  • Quick two-voice patterns right inside REAPER, or layering under another drum machine

Loading and playing it

  1. Insert G Anvil on a new track. It is an instrument: it makes its own sound and needs no audio input.
  2. Make sure PLAY (in TRANSPORT) is lit, then press play in REAPER. The sequencer follows the project's tempo and position.
  3. Click a voice's orange LED to hear it, choose a preset, and edit the steps in the grid.
  4. To play it from MIDI, arm the track with a MIDI input and record monitoring on, or put a MIDI item on the track (see MIDI notes below). Switch PLAY off if you want MIDI only. The MIDI light in the header flashes when a drum note arrives.

Controls

The header

PRESETS
Opens the full preset list.
◄ / ►
Step to the previous or next preset (wrapping round).
Preset display
Shows the preset number and name. Click it for the preset list too.
MIDI
Flashes when a drum note arrives.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

KICK

Click the LED at the top left to hear the kick.

PITCH
Where the kick's pitch lands after the sweep, 30 - 150 Hz (50 Hz by default).
SWEEP
How far above PITCH the sweep starts, 0 - 48 semitones (24 by default). 0 gives a flat, tonal boom; more gives a punchier drop.
S.TIME
How fast the pitch falls, 2 - 250 ms (28 ms by default). Short for a tight punch, long for a laser-like zap.
DECAY
How long the kick rings, 40 - 2500 ms (420 ms by default), measured to -60 dB.
WAVE
Morphs the oscillator from a pure sine (SINE) to a triangle (TRIANGLE), 0 - 100% (10% by default). More triangle adds harmonics and helps the kick cut through on small speakers.
CLICK
The level of the attack click, a short pulse and a burst of noise, 0 - 100% (35% by default).
C.TONE
The click's colour, 0 - 100% (50% by default): from a dark thud to a bright tick. Brighter clicks are also shorter.
DRIVE
A hard, lopsided analogue-style saturation, 0 - 100% (25% by default). Low settings thicken the kick; high settings flatten it into a square-edged distorted kick.
LEVEL
The kick's volume, 0 - 100% (85% by default).
PAN
Position, -100 (left) to +100 (right); CENTRE by default.

SNARE / METAL

Click the LED at the top left to hear the snare.

TUNE
The pitch of the two square oscillators, 80 - 1200 Hz (210 Hz by default).
METAL
0 - 100% (20% by default). Spreads the two oscillators apart and adds ring modulation: from a fat, nearly unison snare body to the classic inharmonic metal clang. It also opens up the tone's brightness.
T.DECAY
The decay of the tone, 10 - 2000 ms (110 ms by default).
N.DECAY
The decay of the noise, 10 - 2000 ms (190 ms by default).
NOISE
The balance of tone and noise, from ALL TONE (0%) to ALL NOISE (100%); 60% by default.
SNAP
The attack, 0 - 100% (50% by default): a quick level jump and a pitch blip at the start of each hit.
CUTOFF
The filter frequency, 100 Hz - 16 kHz (7.5 kHz by default).
RESO
The filter's resonance, 0 - 100% (20% by default). The level is kept steady as you turn it up.
LP / BP / HP (MODE)
The filter type: low-pass (the default, for a full snare), band-pass (a focused, boxy or ringing sound) or high-pass (thin, bright and clangy).
DRIVE
The same hard saturation as the kick, 0 - 100% (15% by default).
LEVEL
The snare's volume, 0 - 100% (75% by default).
PAN
Position, -100 (left) to +100 (right); CENTRE by default.

MASTER

LEVEL
Output level, -24 to +12 dB (0 dB by default).
BUS SAT
Lopsided saturation on the whole module, 0 - 100% (10% by default). It glues the two voices together and adds weight; high settings crunch hard.
L / R meters
The output level, left and right.

SEQUENCER

Two rows of 16 sixteenth-note steps, KICK on top and SNARE below. A step is a rest, a hit (orange) or an accent (red, with a light bar). The step that is playing is highlighted, and the LED next to each row's name flashes as the voice plays.

Steps
Click a step to set or clear it; drag to paint along the rows. Shift-click sets an accent; right-click switches a step between hit and accent.

The pattern is saved with the project and with each preset.

TRANSPORT

PLAY
The sequencer on or off (on by default). When on, it plays whenever REAPER's transport is playing, locked to the project tempo and position.
Display
The step and the tempo while playing. With PLAY on and the transport stopped it reads WAITING FOR PLAY; with PLAY off, SEQ OFF - MIDI ONLY.
SWING
Delays the off-beat sixteenths, 0 - 75% (0% by default).
ACC (ACCENT)
How much louder accents are than normal hits, 0 - 100% (50% by default). At 0 every step plays at full level; at 100 normal hits are 45% quieter than accents.
CLEAR
Clears both rows.

The bottom strip

DRIFT
0 - 100% (30% by default). Every hit is detuned a little at random, and the tunings, the snare filter and the click tone wander slowly, like real analogue circuits. At 0% every hit is identical.
MIDI (CH)
The MIDI channel: OMNI (all channels, the default) or 1 - 16.
STEREO / MONO
STEREO keeps the two voices where PAN puts them; MONO folds the output to mono.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises. 0% by default.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper at -0.3 dBFS (no latency, the default), or a lookahead limiter that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). While the output clips, the CLIP key flashes red.

Knobs: drag up or down (Shift for fine moves); double-click or Ctrl-click to reset a knob to its default.

MIDI notes

G Anvil plays from these notes, with velocity: it sets the level, and softer hits are a little darker. Other MIDI passes straight through.

KICK
Notes 35 and 36 (C1)
SNARE
Notes 38 (D1), 39 and 40
RIM
Note 37: the snare voice, shorter and more tonal, with the filter opened up

Presets

Presets set both voices, BUS SAT, SWING, ACCENT and the pattern. MASTER LEVEL, DRIFT, PLAY, the MIDI channel, STEREO / MONO and the output stage are left as they are.

Init
The default kit: a clean, punchy kick on every beat and a snare on 2 and 4.
Industrial Thud
A longer, driven kick and a dark band-pass snare with lots of noise and a ringing filter.
Techno Rumble Kick
A long, low 1.4-second kick with an off-beat high-pass metal tick on the snare voice.
Classic Punch
A bright-clicking, tight kick and a clean snare, with a touch of swing.
Distorted Hardcore
A heavily driven kick with a deep sweep and a crunchy band-pass snare; BUS SAT at 40%.
Metal Clank
The snare voice at full METAL with a long tone: a clanging anvil hit, in a broken pattern.
Rimshot Tick
A short, clicky kick and a very short, high-pitched high-pass snare: a rimshot tick.
Deep Dub
A soft, low kick that rings for 1.8 seconds and a gentle, long snare, with swing.
Gabber
A fast-sweeping kick with full drive and lots of triangle, and BUS SAT at 50%.
Minimal Click
A short, clicky kick with almost no drive and a tiny high metal tick, with swing.
Warehouse Snare
A broken kick pattern with a big, noisy, driven band-pass snare.
EBM Stomp
A slow-sweeping driven kick with plenty of triangle and a thick low-pass snare.

Tips

  • For a long techno kick, set DECAY over 1 second, SWEEP low and S.TIME around 30 - 60 ms, then add DRIVE to taste.
  • The kick's DRIVE and BUS SAT add a lot of level: lower the voice LEVEL as you turn them up.
  • Turn METAL up and NOISE down, with the filter in BP or HP, for cowbells, clanks and bell-like hits.
  • Use note 37 from MIDI for rimshots between the snares without changing the snare settings.
  • Set DRIFT to 0 for a machine-tight loop; leave some in for a more alive, hardware feel.

File: gfx-g-anvil.jsfx

Drum machines

G Byte 6

A six-voice lo-fi digital drum machine with the gritty 8, 10 and 12-bit sound of the mid-80s rhythm boxes.

Overview

G Byte 6 is a six-voice drum machine in the spirit of the gritty mid-80s Japanese 12-bit rhythm boxes of the early digital era. There are no samples: every hit is synthesised live and then pushed through a vintage converter, which is where the character comes from.

The six voices are KICK (a sine with a fast pitch drop and an FM thump that dies away), SNARE (an FM tone plus band-passed noise), CL HAT and OP HAT (a cluster of six out-of-tune square waves and noise, high-passed), CLAP (quick noise bursts and a tail) and RIM (an FM ping with a wooden body). The closed hat chokes the open hat, as on a real kit.

Every voice has its own converter. The sound is sampled at the converter RATE with no anti-alias filter, so the folded-back grain is part of the sound, and quantised to 8, 10 or 12 BITS. As on the originals, TUNE moves the voice's converter clock, so a voice tuned down is also grainier. Each converter is followed by a steep output low-pass (FILTER). G Byte 6 has no sequencer: play it from MIDI drum notes.

Reach for it for

  • 80s pop, synth-pop and electro funk drum sounds
  • Crunchy, lo-fi hip-hop kits
  • Bit-crushed, aliased 8-bit percussion
  • Layering grit and click under a cleaner drum track
  • A light, quick drum machine to play from a MIDI controller or MIDI items

Loading and playing it

  1. Insert G Byte 6 on a new track. It is an instrument: it makes its own sound and needs no audio input.
  2. Arm the track for recording and set its input to your MIDI keyboard or pads, or put a MIDI item on the track. It plays General MIDI drum notes (see MIDI notes below).
  3. Click a pad along the bottom to hear a voice. The LED above the pad lights on every hit, and the MIDI light in MASTER flashes when a note arrives.
  4. If you play on a particular MIDI channel, set CHANNEL in MASTER; OMNI listens to all 16.

Controls

The header

PRESETS
Opens the full preset list. The last entry, Reset all to defaults, loads Init and also resets MASTER and IN / OUT.
◄ / ►
Step to the previous or next preset (wrapping round).
Preset display
Shows the preset number and name. Click it for the preset list too.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

LCD

A green two-line dot-matrix display. The top line shows the voice that last played (or the one whose knob you are turning) with its TUNE and DECAY. The bottom line shows the BITS, the converter RATE, the FILTER frequency and the velocity of the last hit (V--- until something has played).

CONVERTER

BITS (8 / 10 / 12)
The converter's depth (12 by default). 12 is clean with a little grit on quiet tails; 10 is grainier; 8 adds clear quantising noise and a crunchy, buzzing decay.
CONVERTER RATE (22K / 26K / 32K / 44K)
The sample clock, about 22, 26, 31 or 44 kHz (32K by default). Lower rates fold more of the high end back as metallic, aliased grain.
FILTER
The 4-pole output low-pass after every converter, 20 - 100% of the converter's Nyquist frequency (80% by default). The knob shows the frequency in kHz. Lower settings give the soft, band-limited top of the period; 100% lets all the grain through.

Voice columns

Six columns with the same four knobs: KICK, SNARE, CL HAT, OP HAT, CLAP and RIM. The MIDI notes for each voice are printed at the top right of its column.

TUNE
Pitch, -12 to +12 semitones (0 by default). It also moves the voice's converter clock, so lower tunings are grainier and duller, and higher ones cleaner.
DECAY
Length, 25 - 400% of the voice's natural decay (100% by default).
LEVEL
The voice's volume, 0 - 100. Defaults: KICK 85, SNARE 75, CL HAT 60, OP HAT 55, CLAP 70, RIM 60.
PAN
Position, L100 to R100. Defaults: KICK and SNARE centre (C), both hats R25, CLAP L15, RIM L30.

Pads

Six pads, one per voice, along the bottom. Click a pad to play its voice at full velocity. The LED above each pad lights on every hit, from the pad or from MIDI.

MASTER

VOLUME
Output level, -24 to +12 dB (0 dB by default).
VELOCITY
How much MIDI velocity changes the level, 0 - 100% (80% by default). At 0 every hit plays at full level.
DRIFT
0 - 100% (15% by default). Being a digital box, G Byte 6 keeps it very subtle: each hit is retuned at random by up to 12 cents at 100% (under 2 cents at the default). Nothing wanders between hits.
CHANNEL
The MIDI channel: OMNI (all channels, the default) or 1 - 16.
MIDI
Flashes when a drum note arrives.
OUT meters
The output level, left and right.

IN / OUT

SAT
Band saturation on the way out: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises. 0% by default.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper at -0.3 dBFS (no latency, the default), or a lookahead limiter that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). While the output clips, the CLIP key flashes red.
MONO
Makes everything below the set frequency mono, 20 - 500 Hz (fully left = OFF, the default). Use it to keep the low end centred.
WIDTH
Widens the stereo image above the MONO frequency, from +0% up to +100% (twice the side signal).

Knobs: drag up or down (Shift for fine moves); double-click to reset a knob to its default.

MIDI notes

G Byte 6 plays from General MIDI drum notes, with velocity. Other MIDI passes straight through.

KICK
Notes 35, 36
SNARE
Notes 38, 40
CL HAT
Notes 42, 44 (closed and pedal hat). Both choke the open hat.
OP HAT
Note 46
CLAP
Note 39
RIM
Note 37

Presets

Presets set BITS, RATE, FILTER and the six voices. VOLUME, VELOCITY, DRIFT, the MIDI channel and IN / OUT are left as they are, except with Reset all to defaults.

Init
12 bits, 32K, filter 80%, every voice at its defaults.
Classic 80s Pop
12 bits at 32K with a slightly softer filter; a longer snare and clap, tighter closed hat.
Dirty 8-Bit
8 bits at 22K with a dark filter, every voice tuned down a little and lengthened: crunchy and aliased.
Crunchy Hip-Hop
10 bits at 26K and a low filter; a long, low kick and everything tuned down for a dusty, gritty kit.
Synth Pop
12 bits at 44K with the filter almost open; everything tuned up a little, a long snare and clap.
Electro Funk
A long, booming kick tuned down, a short, high snare and a high rim, with tight hats.
Lo-Fi Tape Kit
10 bits at 22K with the darkest filter (42%): soft, muffled and grainy.
Bright 12-Bit
12 bits at 44K with the filter fully open, every voice tuned up: crisp and bright.
Deep Boom Kit
12 bits at 26K; a very long kick tuned down 6 semitones and everything else tuned well down.
Tight & Snappy
12 bits at 32K, every voice tuned up 2 or 3 semitones and shortened to around half length.
Reset all to defaults
Init, plus VOLUME, VELOCITY, DRIFT, CHANNEL and the IN / OUT section back to their defaults.

Tips

  • For the most lo-fi sound, combine 8 bits, the 22K rate and a low TUNE: all three add grain.
  • Tuning a voice up both raises its pitch and cleans it up; to get a high but grainy voice, lower the RATE as well.
  • Long DECAY settings at 8 bits bring out the buzzing quantising noise on the tails - a big part of the period sound.
  • Turn FILTER down when the kit sounds harsh; turn it up to 100% to hear all the aliasing.
  • Set VELOCITY to 0 for the flat, machine-like dynamics of the original boxes.
  • Use MONO at around 120 Hz to keep the kick centred when you widen the kit with WIDTH.

File: gfx-g-byte-6.jsfx

Drum machines

G Clockwork

An analogue preset-rhythm box: 15 synthesised voices, 32 built-in rhythms, ten kits and a phaser.

Overview

G Clockwork is a rhythm box in the spirit of the late-70s preset-rhythm machines. All 15 voices are synthesised, with no samples: kick, snare, rimshot, closed and open hat, cymbal, high and low bongo, conga, clave, maracas, guiro, tambourine, cowbell and metal beat. Each voice has its own TUNE, DECAY and LEVEL, and the KITS keys re-voice the whole machine in one go.

You can play it two ways, or both at once. From MIDI, each voice answers to its General MIDI drum note. Or switch on PLAY and it runs one of 32 built-in rhythms - 16 full-kit rhythms and 16 hand-percussion rhythms - locked to REAPER's tempo while the transport runs, with a B variation, automatic fills, accent and swing. A built-in phaser, the GFX instrument drift and per-hit variation keep it sounding like a real, slightly unruly machine.

Reach for it for

  • Vintage drum-machine grooves for demos, songwriting and lo-fi tracks
  • Hand-percussion loops to play along with (the PERC rhythms)
  • Programming analogue-style beats from MIDI
  • Swirling, phased drum loops
  • Adding a shaker, tambourine or cowbell part under a live kit

Getting started

  1. Insert a new track and add G Clockwork to it as an instrument. It makes its own sound and needs no audio input.
  2. To play the built-in rhythms: click RHYTHMS (or the display) and pick a rhythm, switch PLAY on, then start REAPER's transport. The rhythm follows the project tempo and stops when the transport stops.
  3. To play it from MIDI: record-arm the track with a MIDI input, or draw notes in a MIDI item on the track. Each voice answers to its General MIDI drum note (shown in the NOTE column). The MIDI light in the header flashes on each note.
  4. Both work at the same time: leave PLAY on and add extra hits from MIDI on top.

Controls

The header

RHYTHMS
Opens the rhythm list, in two groups: KIT RHYTHMS and PERCUSSION ONLY.
◄ / ►
Step to the previous or next rhythm.
Display
Shows the rhythm's number and name, whether it is a KIT RHYTHM or PERCUSSION, VAR A or VAR B, and its feel (4/4, 3/4 or TRIPLET). On the right: the current BAR (and FILL while a fill plays), WAIT while PLAY is on and the transport is stopped, or MIDI ONLY when PLAY is off. Click it to open the rhythm list too.
MIDI
Lights briefly on each incoming MIDI note.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Next to the S / M / L size keys. Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

The voices

The 15 voices sit in two columns. Click a voice's name to hear it. Double-click a knob to reset it.

VOICE
The voice's name tab. Click it to hear the voice.
NOTE
The MIDI note that plays the voice: KICK 36 (and 35), SNARE 38 (and 40), RIMSHOT 37, CLOSED HAT 42 (and 44), OPEN HAT 46, CYMBAL 49 (and 51, 52, 55, 57, 59), BONGO HIGH 60, BONGO LOW 61, CONGA 62 (and 63, 64), CLAVE 75, MARACAS 70, GUIRO 73 (and 74), TAMBOURINE 54, COWBELL 56, METAL BEAT 80 (and 81). Note velocity sets how hard each hit is.
TUNE
The voice's pitch, -12 to +12 semitones.
DECAY
How long the voice rings, 25 to 400% of its normal length.
LEVEL
The voice's level in the mix, 0 to 100%.
HIT
Lights in the voice's colour each time it plays.

The closed hat cuts off the open hat, as on the original machines. Each voice also has its own fixed place in the stereo picture, which SPREAD widens or narrows.

RHYTHM

PLAY
Plays the selected rhythm while REAPER's transport runs, locked to the project's tempo and bars. Off: the machine only plays from MIDI.
VAR B
Adds the variation parts on top of the basic rhythm (extra percussion, open hats, cymbals).
PERC
Switches between a kit rhythm and its percussion-only partner (rhythm 1 and 17, 2 and 18, and so on).
FILL
Drops a fill into the second half of the last bar: OFF, EVERY 2, EVERY 4 or EVERY 8 bars.
ACCENT
How much the accented hits stand out, 0 to 100% (50% by default). At 100% the unaccented hits play at about half strength.
SWING
Delays the off-beat 16ths, 0 to 100%, for a shuffled feel. Works on the 16-step rhythms only; the triplet and 3/4 rhythms are left as they are.

KITS

CLASSIC ... BRUSHES
Ten keys, each with its kit's name on it, that re-voice the whole kit (tune, decay and the balance between voices) - see Kits below. The lit key shows the last kit chosen. Your rhythm settings are left alone.
RESET ALL
The key beside the kits: every control back to its starting value.

MASTER

SPREAD
How wide the voices are spread across the stereo picture, 0 to 100% (60% by default). 0 puts everything in the centre.
OUTPUT
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.
CHANNEL
The MIDI channel the voices listen to: OMNI (all channels, the default) or channel 1 to 16. MIDI is always passed on down the chain.
Meter
Output level, left and right, with a peak hold.

PHASER

A six-stage phaser on the whole machine, swept by its own LFO. The display shows the phaser's response as it sweeps, with a marker below it for the sweep position. While the phaser is off the display is dimmed and reads OFF. It fades in and out smoothly, so switching it on or off never clicks.

ON
Switches the phaser in or out.
RATE
The sweep speed, 0.05 to 8 Hz (0.3 Hz by default).
DEPTH
How far the sweep travels, 0 to 100% (70% by default).
FEEDBACK
Resonance around the phaser, 0 to 95% (50% by default). Higher settings give a sharper, more whistling sweep.
MIX
How much phaser, 0 to 100% (100% by default).
STEREO
Offsets the right side's sweep from the left, 0 to 180 degrees (90 by default). 0 is a mono phaser; higher settings give wide stereo movement.

DRIFT + FEEL

DRIFT
The GFX instrument drift, 0 to 100% (25% by default): the tuning of the whole kit wanders slowly (up to about 20 cents), the brightness of the noise voices (snare, hats, maracas, tambourine) and the tone of the metal voices (cymbal, cowbell, metal beat) drift, decays vary slightly, and each hit is tuned a little differently. 0 switches it off.
HITS
Makes every hit a little different in tune, decay and level, 0 to 100% (30% by default).
TIMING
Loosens the groove: each hit is delayed by a random amount up to this time, 0 to 20 ms (0 by default). Works on rhythm and MIDI hits alike.

IN / OUT strip

SAT
Band saturation on the way out: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT (OUT)
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper (no latency), or a lookahead limiter that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). CLIP is on by default; its key flashes red while it is clipping.
MONO
Makes the low end mono below this frequency, from OFF up to 500 Hz.
WIDTH
Widens the stereo picture, from +0% (the default) to +100%.

Presets

Kits

Each kit first sets every voice back to its normal level, then changes the tune, decay and balance.

CLASSIC
The machine as designed: every voice at its normal tune and decay.
TIGHT
Tight & Dry: every voice shortened to 55% (the kick to 70%).
DEEP
Deep & Boomy: tuned down 3 semitones with longer decays; the kick 4 semitones down with twice its decay.
BRIGHT
Tuned up 3 semitones (the kick 2), slightly shorter.
LONG
Long Tails: every voice rings much longer (220%, the kick 160%).
DISCO
A little higher and tighter, with a deep, long kick and louder kick, hats and tambourine.
LATIN
Latin Percussion: kick and snare down, bongos, conga, clave, maracas, guiro and cowbell up.
LO-FI
Lo-Fi Box: tuned down and shorter, with quieter hats and cymbal.
ELECTRO
Short and slightly low, with a very deep, long kick, a punchy short snare, and louder kick and snare.
BRUSHES
Jazz Brushes: a long, low, soft snare, a quieter kit, longer decays and a shorter kick.

Kit rhythms

All kit rhythms have a kick-and-snare fill.

01 Rock 1
Kick, snare on 2 and 4, eighth-note hats. VAR B: open hat and a cymbal on the one.
02 Rock 2
Busier kick, sixteenth hats with accents. VAR B: tambourine and open hat.

Four-on-the-floor kick, snare on 2 and 4, open hats on the off-beats. VAR B: sixteenth tambourine and metal beat.

Syncopated kick and snare, accented sixteenth hats. VAR B: open hat and cowbell.

Gentle kick, rimshot on 2 and 4, eighth hats. VAR B: sixteenth maracas.

Kick, snare, accented eighth hats. VAR B: tambourine backbeat and a cymbal.

A triplet shuffle on kick, snare and hats. VAR B: open hat and cymbal.

A 12/8 triplet rock beat. VAR B: cymbal and tambourine.

A triplet ride pattern on the cymbal, hats on 2 and 4, light kick. VAR B: rimshot.

3/4: kick on the one, rimshot on two and three, hats. VAR B: cymbal and maracas.

Kick, cowbell on the beat, guiro, conga. VAR B: clave and high bongo.

Kick on every beat, rimshot, hats. VAR B: conga and tambourine.

Kick, bongos and maracas. VAR B: cymbal and clave.

Tips

  • Pick a kit rhythm, then press PERC to hear its percussion partner - great for adding a shaker or conga part under a live drummer.
  • Turn FILL to EVERY 4 or EVERY 8 so long loops don't sound stuck.
  • A little SWING (20-40%) and a few ms of TIMING make the 16-step rhythms feel much less mechanical.
  • For a classic swirling drum-machine sound, switch the PHASER on with a slow RATE and FEEDBACK around 50-70%.
  • Use the MIDI notes to add your own hits on top of a running rhythm - a crash on the one, an extra conga, a cowbell part.
