Drum machines

G Deep 16

A fully synthesised deep house drum machine with a 16-step sequencer and a drum-bus chain.

Overview

G Deep 16 makes every sound from scratch - no samples. There are six voices: a kick, a snare, a clap, closed and open hi-hats and a rim shot. The kick is a sine wave whose pitch drops as it hits, with a click on the front and a drive that thickens the start. The snare and clap share a tonal body and a band of filtered noise. The hats are six square-wave oscillators tuned to the classic analogue metal ratios, blended with noise.

The voices then pass through a drum-bus chain: 1 COMPRESSOR, 2 3-BAND EQ, 3 DRIVE and 4 REVERB, then the MASTER volume. Each module has its own ON key.

A 16-step sequencer is built into the panel. It plays in time with REAPER's transport, has swing and a set of factory grooves, and can export its pattern as a MIDI item with the companion script. You can also play the voices from MIDI notes, or both at once.

Reach for it for

  • Deep house, garage and Chicago-style grooves straight from the panel
  • Synthesised kicks you can tune to the key of the track
  • Wide house claps (WIDTH) and short, tuned rim shots
  • Sketching a beat, then exporting it to MIDI to edit in REAPER
  • Lo-fi or dubby drums through the drive and reverb modules

Playing it

G Deep 16 is an instrument: it has no audio input. It plays from its own sequencer, from MIDI notes, or both.

  1. Insert G Deep 16 on a track.
  2. To use the sequencer: switch PLAY on in the SEQUENCER section and start REAPER's transport. The steps follow the project's beat position (four beats to the bar), and the LCD shows the step that is playing and the tempo.
  3. To play it from MIDI: arm the track and set its input to your MIDI keyboard, or draw notes in a MIDI item. Note 36 (C1) is the kick, 38 the snare, 40 the clap, 42 and 44 the closed hat, 46 the open hat and 37 the rim shot. Velocity sets the level (and on the kick, how long it rings).
  4. Click a voice's name in the matrix to hear it on its own.

Triggering is sample-accurate. Incoming MIDI is passed on down the chain. CH in the MASTER section picks the MIDI channel it listens to (OMNI = all).

Controls

The header

KITS
Opens the list of factory kits. A kit sets every voice and module control; the sequencer pattern, the MIDI channel and the master volume are kept.
LCD
The top line shows the kit. The bottom line shows the sequencer: OFF, WAITING FOR PLAY (PLAY is on but the transport is stopped) or the step playing, with the project tempo.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

The voice matrix

One row per voice. On the left is the voice's name and MIDI note, in the middle its 16 steps, and on the right the knobs for that voice. Double-click any knob to reset it.

Voice name
Click it to hear the voice. It lights when the voice plays.
Steps
Click a step to go from off to hit to accent (shown with a white bar) and back to off. Hits play a little softer (72% for the kick, snare and clap, less for the hats and rim); accents play at full level. The steps are grouped in fours, and the playing step is highlighted.

KICK

PITCH
The kick's resting pitch, 30 - 120 Hz (48 Hz by default). Tune it to the key of the track.
DROP
How far above PITCH the hit starts, 0 - 600 Hz. More drop gives a punchier, more 'boomy' attack; none gives a plain sine thud.
P.DECAY
How fast the pitch falls back to PITCH, 2 - 200 ms. Short is a tight click-thump; long is a laser-like sweep.
DECAY
How long the kick rings, 50 - 2000 ms. Harder hits ring a little longer.
CLICK
The level of a short burst of bright noise at the very start, 0 - 1. It helps the kick cut through on small speakers.
DRIVE
Sub drive, 0 - 100%: soft saturation of the sine, strongest at the start of the hit. It thickens and fattens the kick.
LEVEL
The kick's level, -24 to +6 dB.

SNARE (body + level, snare and clap)

The snare and the clap are the same voice. The clap re-strikes its noise four times, 10 ms apart, before the tail (the classic clap), and keeps only a quarter of the tonal body.

TONE
The pitch of the tonal body, 100 - 400 Hz. It starts a little higher and drops quickly.
T.DECAY
How long the body rings, 10 - 500 ms.
TONE MIX
The balance between body and noise, 0 - 100% (40% by default). Low settings are a noisy, papery snare; high settings are a ringing, tom-like one.
LEVEL
The level of the snare and the clap, -24 to +6 dB.

CLAP (noise, snare and clap)

CUTOFF
The centre of the band-pass filter on the noise, 500 Hz - 10 kHz. Lower is darker and thicker, higher is brighter and thinner.
RESO
The filter's resonance, Q 0.5 - 8. Higher settings give the noise a pitched, ringing quality.
N.DECAY
How long the noise lasts, 20 - 800 ms.
WIDTH
Delays the noise on the right side by 0 - 30 ms (12 ms by default) - the wide house clap. At 0 the noise is in the centre.

CLOSED HAT and OPEN HAT

Both hats share one metallic source and filter; each has its own decay. A closed hat cuts off a ringing open hat, as on the originals. The hats sit slightly to the right of centre.

TUNE
Retunes the six oscillators, -12 to +12 semitones.
TONE
The filter that shapes the hats, 3 - 16 kHz. Higher is thinner and more sizzling.
CLOSED
The closed hat's decay, 10 - 300 ms.
NOISE
Blends from the metallic oscillators (0%) to white noise (100%).
LEVEL
The level of both hats, -30 to +6 dB.
OPEN (open hat row)
The open hat's decay, 50 - 2000 ms.

RIM SHOT

Two short pitched pings and a click. It sits slightly to the left of centre.

PITCH
The pitch of the main ping, 600 - 3500 Hz; the second ping follows it lower down.
DECAY
How long it rings, 5 - 120 ms.
LEVEL
The rim's level, -30 to +6 dB.

SEQUENCER

PLAY
Switches the sequencer on. It plays only while REAPER's transport is running, locked to the project's beat position.
SWING
Delays every second 16th note, 0 - 75%. At 75% the off-beat 16ths arrive just over a third of a 16th late.
GROOVES
Opens the list of factory patterns. Choosing one replaces all six rows.
EXPORT MIDI
Sends the pattern to the companion export script (see below). The key lights briefly when pressed.
BARS (1 / 2 / 4 / 8)
How many bars the exported MIDI item lasts; the 16-step pattern is repeated to fill it.
Status line
Reminds you which script does the export, and shows 'export waiting' if you pressed EXPORT MIDI and the script is not running.

1 COMPRESSOR

A feed-forward peak compressor on the whole drum mix, the same gain on both sides.

ON
Switches the compressor in or out.
THRESH
The level where compression starts, -40 to 0 dB.
RATIO
1:1 to 20:1.
ATTACK
0.1 - 100 ms. Longer settings let the front of the kick through before it clamps down.
RELEASE
10 - 1000 ms. Shorter settings pump more.
MAKEUP
Gain after the compressor, -12 to +24 dB.
GR
The column of eight lights shows the gain reduction (about 1.5 dB per light).

2 3-BAND EQ

ON
Switches the EQ in or out.
LOW F / LOW
A low shelf: its frequency, 30 - 300 Hz, and gain, -15 to +15 dB.
MID F / MID Q / MID
A bell: its frequency, 150 Hz - 5 kHz, width (Q 0.3 - 6; higher is narrower) and gain, -15 to +15 dB.
HIGH F / HIGH
A high shelf: its frequency, 2 - 16 kHz, and gain, -15 to +15 dB.

3 DRIVE

ON
Switches the drive in or out.
TAPE / TUBE / SOFT
The saturation type. TAPE: smooth and symmetrical. TUBE: uneven, adding even harmonics for a warmer tone. SOFT: a gentle soft-clipper.
DRIVE
How hard the drums are pushed into the saturation, 0 - 24 dB. The level is compensated so it changes the colour more than the volume.
WET
The blend of driven and clean signal, 0 - 100%.

4 REVERB

ON
Switches the reverb in or out.
SIZE
The size of the room, 0 - 1.
DECAY
How long the tail lasts, 0 - 1.
DAMPING
How quickly the highs die away in the tail, 1 - 12 kHz. Lower is darker.
LOW-CUT
Cuts the lows going into the reverb, 50 - 1000 Hz (250 Hz by default), so the kick does not muddy the tail.
WET
The reverb level, -60 to 0 dB.

MASTER

VOLUME
The final output level, -24 to +12 dB.
CH
The MIDI channel G Deep 16 listens to: OMNI (all) or 1 - 16.
L / R meters
Output level, left and right. Click a meter to clear its peak hold.

Kits

Chosen from KITS in the header. A kit sets the voices and the four modules; the pattern, MIDI channel and master volume stay as they are.

Deep Classic
The default kit: a 48 Hz kick, all four modules on, light tape drive and a small room.
Dusty Warehouse
A lower, longer, more driven kick, a darker snare, tube drive and a bigger, darker reverb; duller hats with more noise.
Tight Tech
A short, clicky 52 Hz kick, a tight bright snare, fast 6:1 compression, soft-clip drive and a small dry room; short, bright hats.
Lo-Fi Dub
A deep 42 Hz kick with a long tail and heavy sub drive, a dark snare, hard tape drive and a dark reverb; dark hats with a long open hat.
Big Room Clap
A mostly-noise snare and clap with a long tail and 22 ms of width, a mid lift and a bigger room.
Soulful Warm
A long, round 46 Hz kick, gentle compression, tube drive and a warm reverb.
Minimal Click
A short, high, clicky kick, a bright snare, fast 8:1 compression, soft-clip drive and a tiny room; very short, bright hats.
Init (dry)
The default voices with all four modules switched off.

Grooves

Chosen from GROOVES in the SEQUENCER section. Each replaces the pattern in all six rows.

Deep Four
Four-on-the-floor kick, clap on 2 and 4, 8th-note closed hats and off-beat open hats.
Soulful
Four-on-the-floor with a pickup kick at the end of the bar, a ghost snare, clap on 2 and 4, busy hats and a rim.
Garage Shuffle
A skipping kick, ghost snares, clap on 2 and 4, a dense hat pattern and rim shots.
Broken Deep
A broken kick pattern with the snare on 2 and 4 and no clap; rims fill the gaps.
Classic Chicago
Four-on-the-floor, snare and clap together on 2 and 4 with a snare fill, 8th hats and off-beat open hats.
Dub Minimal
A sparse kick, one snare, soft claps and a scattered rim and hat pattern.
Off-beat Snare
Four-on-the-floor with the snare on every off-beat 8th, claps on 2 and 4, quarter-note closed hats and off-beat open hats.
Clear
Empties every row.

Exporting the pattern as MIDI

JSFX plug-ins cannot write files, so EXPORT MIDI hands the pattern to a ReaScript, "GFX G-Deep - export MIDI.lua", in the Scripts folder. The script makes a MIDI item at the edit cursor.

  1. In REAPER, open Actions > Show action list, click New action > Load ReaScript, and pick "GFX G-Deep - export MIDI.lua".
  2. Run the script. It keeps running in the background (its toolbar button lights, if you give it one) and exports every time you press EXPORT MIDI. Run it again to stop it.
  3. Select the track that holds G Deep 16, put the edit cursor where the pattern should start, and choose BARS (1, 2, 4 or 8).
  4. Press EXPORT MIDI. A MIDI item called "G-Deep pattern" appears at the edit cursor. If you pressed EXPORT MIDI before starting the script, it exports that pattern as soon as it starts.

The item uses MIDI channel 10 and the same notes as the plug-in: kick 36, snare 38, clap 40, closed hat 42, open hat 46, rim 37. Hits have velocity 100 and accents 127, the swing is kept, and the pattern is repeated for the number of bars chosen. You can then switch the sequencer off and play G Deep 16 from the MIDI item, editing the notes as you like.

Tips

  • Tune the kick: set DROP to 0, find the note with PITCH, then bring DROP back for the punch.
  • For a wider clap, raise WIDTH to 15 - 25 ms and keep the snare's TONE MIX low.
  • Raise the reverb's LOW-CUT if the tail sounds boomy; the kick stays dry and tight underneath.
  • Use accents sparingly - an accented hat every beat with plain hits in between gives the groove a natural lift.
  • The sequencer pattern is saved with the project, so you can build a groove, save, and come back to it.

File: gfx-g-deep.jsfx

Drum machines

G PCM 12

A 12-bit PCM drum machine in the spirit of the big mid-80s digital rhythm programmers.

Overview

G PCM 12 is a drum machine with a sound ROM of 24 drums, built when the plugin loads and stored as 12-bit PCM at 40 kHz - no sample files to install. The set covers punchy and gated kicks and snares, rim, clap, four toms, hats, ride, crash, cowbell, shaker, tambourine, conga, timbale, an electronic tom and a finger snap.

There are 12 pad channels. Each plays one ROM sound, one hit at a time (a new hit restarts the pad), with the classic voice edit: pitch, level, pan, a four-part envelope, a pitch bend that sweeps while the sound plays, reverse, and the MIDI note that plays it. A closed or pedal hat chokes a ringing open hat. The sounds are pitched by resampling and pass through a gentle output low-pass, like the DAC of the original machines.

A pattern sequencer is built in: 8 patterns of 16 steps for all 12 pads, played in time with REAPER's transport, with swing and accents. Patterns can be exported as a MIDI item with a companion script.

Reach for it for

  • Gritty 80s digital drum tracks
  • Gated snares and big electronic toms
  • Programming quick patterns without leaving the plugin
  • Reverse and pitch-bent drum effects
  • Playing drums from a MIDI keyboard or drum pads

Getting started

  1. Insert G PCM 12 on a track (it is an instrument: it has no audio input).
  2. To play it from MIDI, arm the track for recording with a MIDI input, or draw notes in a MIDI item. Each pad responds to the MIDI note shown at the right of its row (the TIGHT FUNK kit follows General MIDI drum notes where it can: kick 36, snare 38 and so on).
  3. Or use the built-in sequencer: switch PLAY on in the PATTERN section, pick a pattern (A - G hold factory patterns) and start REAPER's transport.
  4. Click a pad's name to hear it and select it for VOICE EDIT.

Controls

The header

LCD
Line 1: the selected pad, its sound and the kit. Line 2: the pattern and the sequencer state (SEQ OFF, WAITING FOR PLAY or PLAYING); while playing it shows the step and the tempo.
KITS
Choose a kit: TIGHT FUNK or ELECTRO. A kit sets all 12 pads (sounds, voice edits and notes).
MIDI light
Flashes when a note arrives.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

The voice matrix

One row per pad. The mini knobs show their value while you touch them; drag them (Shift for fine steps), use the mouse wheel, or double-click to reset.

Pad name
Click to hear the pad and select it for VOICE EDIT. It lights when the pad plays.
Arrow
Opens the list of the 24 ROM sounds for that pad: BD 1, BD 2, BD GATED, SD 1, SD GATED, SD TIGHT, RIM, CLAP, TOM 1 - 4, HH CLOSED, HH OPEN, HH PEDAL, RIDE, CRASH, COWBELL, SHAKER, TAMBOURINE, CONGA, TIMBALE, ELEC TOM and SNAP.
Steps 1 - 16
The pad's steps in the current pattern. Click to cycle off, hit, accent (accents are brighter, with a white bar). The playhead is shown while the sequencer runs.
PITCH
-24 to +24 semitones. Pitching is done by resampling, so higher is also shorter and lower is longer.
LEVEL
0 to 100 (80 by default).
PAN
-100 (left) to +100 (right).
DECAY
The pad's DECAY 1 time, 10 ms to 4000 ms; fully up leaves the sound as stored.
BEND
-100 to +100: the pitch sweeps down or up while the sound plays (up to 4 octaves per second at full).
REV
Plays the sound backwards.
NOTE
The MIDI note that plays the pad, 24 to 96. If two pads share a note, the one nearer the top of the list plays.

VOICE EDIT (the selected pad)

The envelope shapes the stored sound: an attack ramp, DECAY 1 towards the decay point, then DECAY 2 to silence. Decay times are the time to fall by 40 dB.

ATTACK
A fade-in at the start of the sound, 0 to 200 ms.
DECAY 1
10 to 4000 ms; fully up reads FULL and leaves the sound's own decay untouched.
D.POINT
The level DECAY 1 falls to before DECAY 2 takes over, 0 to 100%. At 100% DECAY 1 has nothing to do and DECAY 2 shapes the whole sound.
DECAY 2
10 to 4000 ms; FULL leaves the rest of the sound as it is. A short DECAY 2 after a high D.POINT gives a gated, chopped tail.
BEND
As on the pad's row: DOWN or UP and the amount, OFF at the centre.
REVERSE
As REV on the pad's row.

PATTERN

PLAY
Switches the sequencer on. It then plays whenever REAPER's transport runs, locked to the project's beat position (it counts four beats to the bar).
A - H
Choose the pattern. While playing, a new pattern starts at the next bar (its key blinks until then); when stopped it changes at once.
SWING
0 to 75%: delays every second 16th.
COPY TO
Copies the current pattern to another slot (A - H).
CLEAR
Empties the current pattern.
BARS (1 / 2 / 4 / 8)
How many bars EXPORT writes (the pattern is repeated).
EXPORT
Hands the current pattern to the export script (see below). The caption under it reads 'run script' if the script did not pick the pattern up.

Accented steps play at full velocity, normal hits at 70%. The patterns and the pad set are saved with the project.

MASTER

VOLUME
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.
VELOCITY
How much MIDI velocity (and accents) changes the level, 0 to 100% (60% by default). At 0 every hit plays at the same level.
DAC
The output low-pass filter of the converter, 4 to 20 kHz (14 kHz by default). Lower it for a duller, older sound.
CH
The MIDI channel the pads listen to: OMNI (all) or 1 to 16.
Meters
Output level, left and right. Click to reset the peak hold.

Kits

TIGHT FUNK
Short, dry and snappy (the default). Pads: BD 1, SD TIGHT, SD 1, RIM, CLAP, TOM 1, TOM 2, TOM 3, HH CLOSED, HH OPEN, HH PEDAL, COWBELL. The factory patterns are written for this kit.
ELECTRO
Electronic toms raised and shortened with a gentle downward bend, short sounds, a bent kick and BD GATED on the last pad.

Patterns

A 80S ROCK
Kick, backbeat snare, eighth-note closed hats and an open-hat accent.
B FUNK
Syncopated kick and tight snare with ghost notes, busy 16th hats.
C ELECTRO
Electro kick with claps, eighth hats and cowbell.
D DISCO
Four-on-the-floor kick, backbeat and off-beat open hats.
E GATED BALLAD
A slow backbeat with eighth hats and a rim.
F TOM FILL
A tom fill down through the three toms.
G CLAPS + BELL
Four-on-the-floor with claps, a cowbell line and off-beat hats.
H USER
Empty, ready for your own pattern.

Exporting patterns as MIDI

JSFX plugins cannot write files, so EXPORT works with a companion ReaScript, 'GFX G-PCM 12 - export MIDI.lua' in the Scripts folder.

  1. In REAPER, open Actions > Show action list, choose New action > Load ReaScript, and pick 'GFX G-PCM 12 - export MIDI.lua'. Select it and press Run.
  2. The script keeps running in the background (its toolbar button lights if you add it to a toolbar) and exports every time you press EXPORT. Run it again to stop it.
  3. Select the track you want the MIDI on and place the edit cursor where the pattern should start.
  4. Choose BARS (1, 2, 4 or 8) and press EXPORT. A MIDI item named 'G-PCM 12 pattern' appears at the edit cursor.
  5. If you pressed EXPORT before starting the script, it exports that pattern as soon as it starts.

Each pad's row is written on that pad's MIDI note, on MIDI channel 10; hits have velocity 100 and accents 127, and the swing is kept.

Tips

  • For the classic 80s snare, use SD GATED, or set a high D.POINT and a short DECAY 2 on SD 1 to chop its tail.
  • Pitching a sound down makes it longer and grainier - the 12-bit character comes through more.
  • A low BEND on the toms or kick gives the electronic 'pew' of the era; ELECTRO starts you there.
  • Use COPY TO to make variations of a pattern, then switch between them while playing - the change waits for the bar line.

File: gfx-g-pcm12.jsfx

Drum machines

G Pulse

A synthesis drum machine: eight voices, four character models and a 32-step sequencer.

Overview

G Pulse builds every drum sound from scratch - there are no samples. Eight voices (KICK, SNARE, CLAP, CL HAT, OP HAT, TOM, PERC and CYMBAL) are played by a 32-step sequencer that runs with REAPER's transport, or from MIDI drum notes.

One CHARACTER switch changes how the whole kit is made. ANALOG is classic virtual analogue in the spirit of the 80s drum machines: pitch-swept sines, filtered noise and six-square metal for the hats and cymbal. FM uses two-operator FM with its own index envelope. PHYSICAL strikes modelled membranes, plates, wooden bars and hands with a mallet, and the snare has wires that buzz with the head. DIGITAL uses phase distortion, sample-and-hold noise and bit and sample-rate reduction.

Every row of the sequencer can have its own length, so patterns can drift against each other and line up again (polyrhythm). A bus compressor glues the kit, and the pattern can be sent out as MIDI notes to record.

Reach for it for

  • Programming drum parts right inside REAPER, without samples
  • Classic analogue-style kits, FM clicks and metal, or lo-fi digital grit
  • Polyrhythmic patterns with rows of different lengths
  • Playing synthesised drums from a MIDI controller or a MIDI item
  • Turning a sequenced pattern into MIDI notes you can edit

Getting started

  1. Insert a new track and add G Pulse to it. It is an instrument: it makes its own sound and needs no audio input.
  2. Make sure PLAY (in TRANSPORT) is lit, then press play in REAPER. The sequencer follows the project's tempo and position, so it plays in time and restarts where the transport does.
  3. Click a voice's name to hear it, pick a pattern slot (1 - 8), and edit the steps in the grid.
  4. To play it from MIDI instead, arm the track with a MIDI input and record monitoring on, or put a MIDI item on the track: G Pulse responds to General MIDI drum notes (see below). Switch PLAY off if you want MIDI only.

Controls

The header

ANALOG / FM / PHYSICAL / DIGITAL
The CHARACTER model for the whole kit. Each model has its own recipe for every voice; the levels are balanced so switching keeps the kit roughly even.
Display
The model and the pattern slot (with * when it has unsaved edits). While playing it shows the bar, step and tempo; with PLAY on and the transport stopped it reads WAITING FOR TRANSPORT; with PLAY off, SEQUENCER OFF - MIDI ONLY.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Sequencer

Eight rows of 32 sixteenth-note steps. A step is a rest, a hit or an accent (louder, marked with a white bar).

Steps
Click a step to set or clear it; drag to paint along the row. Shift-click sets an accent.
Row length
Right-click a step to end the row there. Every row can have its own length, from 1 to 32 steps; steps past the end are dimmed and a line in the voice's colour marks the end.
VOICE name
Click it to hear the voice. The name flashes in the voice's colour each time it plays.
M
Mutes the row.
LEN
The row's length in steps. Drag up or down on it, or use the mouse wheel.

The closed hat chokes the open hat: a closed hat cuts off a ringing open hat, as on a real kit.

Voice knobs

Each row has six small knobs on a band in the voice's colour.

TUNE
Pitch, -24 to +24 semitones.
DECAY
Length, 25 - 400% of the voice's natural decay.
TONE
Brightness, 0 - 100%. What it changes depends on the model: the filter and noise colour (ANALOG), the FM index (FM), how quickly the upper modes are damped (PHYSICAL), or the phase-distortion warp and the crush rate (DIGITAL).
SNAP
The attack, 0 - 100%: the depth of the pitch sweep and the click, and in PHYSICAL how hard the mallet is.
LEVEL
The voice's volume, 0 - 100%.
PAN
Position, -100 (left) to +100 (right).

PATTERN

1 - 8
The eight pattern slots, saved with the project and in REAPER presets. Pick one to load it. While the sequencer is playing the change waits for the next bar (the slot blinks until then).
SAVE
Stores your edits into the current slot; its light shows there are unsaved edits. Right-click SAVE to store them into a different slot.
COPY / PASTE
Copies the pattern (steps and row lengths) and pastes it back, for example into another slot before saving.
CLEAR
Clears every step (the row lengths stay).
RANDOM
A musical random pattern: each voice favours the places it usually plays (kick on the beats, snare and clap on the backbeat, and so on), with some accents.
FACTORY
Loads one of the built-in patterns: House, Techno, Breakbeat, Electro, Hip-hop, Trap (32 steps), Polyrhythm or Empty.
LOAD
Loads a pattern from a text file in REAPER/Data/gfx-drum-patterns (see below).

The eight slots start out holding the eight factory patterns.

TRANSPORT

PLAY
The sequencer on or off. When on, it plays whenever REAPER's transport is playing, locked to the project tempo.
MIDI OUT
Sends the sequence out as MIDI notes, so you can record it to a MIDI item on another track.
IN
Lights when a MIDI drum note arrives.
SWING
Delays the off-beat sixteenths, 0 - 75%.
CH
The MIDI channel. OMNI (0): it listens to every channel and sends on channel 10. 1 - 16: it listens and sends on that channel only.

COMP

A stereo-linked bus compressor on the whole kit, with a 6 dB soft knee.

THRESH
-40 to 0 dB.
RATIO
1:1 to 20:1.
ATTACK
0.1 - 100 ms.
RELEASE
10 - 1000 ms.
MAKEUP
0 to +24 dB of make-up gain.
MIX
Blends the compressed kit with the dry one, 0 - 100%. It starts at 0%, so the compressor is out until you turn MIX up; lower settings give parallel compression.
GR
The gain reduction, as a bar and a value.

MASTER

VOLUME
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.
OUT meters
Output level, left and right, with a floating peak. Click a meter to clear the peak.

Playing it from MIDI

G Pulse plays from General MIDI drum notes, with velocity. Other MIDI passes straight through.

KICK
Notes 35, 36
SNARE
Notes 38, 40
CLAP
Note 39
CL HAT
Notes 42, 44
OP HAT
Note 46
TOM
Notes 41, 43, 45, 47, 48, 50
PERC
Notes 37, 56, 75, 76, 77
CYMBAL
Notes 49, 51, 52, 55, 57, 59

MIDI OUT sends kick 36, snare 38, clap 39, closed hat 42, open hat 46, tom 45, perc 56 and cymbal 49, at velocity 127 for accents and 100 for hits.

Recording the pattern as MIDI

  1. Switch MIDI OUT on.
  2. Right-click the G Pulse track's record-arm button and choose Record: output > Record: output (MIDI).
  3. Arm the track and record while REAPER plays. The sequence is written to a MIDI item on the track.
  4. Edit the notes as you like, then switch PLAY off and set the track back to normal recording: G Pulse now plays the MIDI item.

Pattern files

LOAD reads plain text files from the folder REAPER/Data/gfx-drum-patterns (three examples are included). One line per voice: a voice name, then the steps.

  • X = accent, x (or o) = hit, . (or - or _) = rest. Spaces and | between steps are ignored.
  • Up to 32 steps; the row's length is the number of steps you write, so short rows make polyrhythms.
  • Voice names: KICK (or BD), SNARE, CLAP (or CP), CH (closed hat, or HH), OH (open hat), TOM, PERC (or RIM, CB, COWBELL), CYMBAL (or CRASH, RIDE). A line of steps with no name goes to the next row.
  • Lines starting with # are comments.
  • A loaded file replaces the steps of the current pattern; press SAVE to keep it in a slot. If the file can't be opened, the PATTERN section says so.

Tips

  • Try the same pattern through all four CHARACTER models - each one gives a completely different kit.
  • Set a hat row to 12 steps and a perc row to 7 against a 16-step kick for patterns that keep evolving.
  • Turn the bus COMP's MIX up to 30 - 50% for parallel punch without flattening the accents.
  • Use accents (Shift-click) on the hats for a more human groove, then add a little SWING.

File: gfx-g-pulse.jsfx

Amps & drive

Bass Head

A big all-valve bass amp and an 8x10 cabinet, with a gate, a tuner and a clean DI blend.

Overview

Bass Head is an all-valve bass amp in the spirit of the classic 300-watt heads, driving a sealed 8x10 cabinet. The preamp valve is clean and round when GAIN is low and starts to growl as you turn it up. The tone stack has bass and treble shelves, a midrange control with five frequencies, and two voicing switches: ULTRA LO for a deep low end with scooped mids, and ULTRA HI for extra top.

MASTER sets how hard the push-pull power stage works. Low settings give clean headroom; higher settings bring in the sag of the power supply, compression and the output transformer's saturation - the big-amp feel when you dig in. After the amp, the cabinet can be switched out for a line-out sound, and a clean DI signal can be blended in underneath, as engineers often do with a real bass rig.

The amp works in mono, as a real amp does: the two inputs are added together, and both outputs carry the amp. It runs at twice the sample rate inside to keep the distortion clean. The valves' bias wanders very slightly over time, so the sound is never quite static.

Reach for it for

  • DI'd bass guitar that needs the weight and growl of an amp
  • Clean, round valve tone for fingerstyle and pick playing
  • Driven, growling rock bass (GAIN and MASTER up)
  • Blending amp and DI for definition and low end together
  • Synth bass that needs a bit of amp character

Controls

The control panel

BASS, MIDRANGE and TREBLE move in 0.5 dB steps; hold Shift to move them freely. Double-click a knob to reset it.

GAIN
The preamp valve, 0 to 10 (4 by default). Low settings are clean and round; as you turn it up the valve starts to growl and distort.
ULTRA LO
A deep low-end lift (about +4 dB around 40 Hz) together with a scooped midrange (about -10 dB around 500 Hz). The classic big, hollow modern bass sound.
ULTRA HI
A bright top-end lift, about +8 dB from 8 kHz up. Adds click and string noise.
BASS
A low shelf at 80 Hz, -12 to +12 dB.
MIDRANGE
Boost or cut at the FREQUENCY set beside it, -12 to +12 dB. Boost for a bass that cuts through a busy mix; cut for a smoother, deeper tone.
FREQUENCY
Where MIDRANGE works: 220 Hz, 450 Hz, 800 Hz, 1.6 kHz or 3 kHz (450 Hz by default). Click a value to choose it, or click / drag / use the mouse wheel on the knob to step through. In the flat look this knob is called FREQ.
TREBLE
A high shelf at 5 kHz, -12 to +12 dB.
MASTER
How hard the push-pull power stage is driven, 0 to 10 (4 by default). Low settings stay clean with plenty of headroom; higher settings add the sag of the power supply, compression and the output transformer's saturation.

The lower strip

GATE
A noise gate before the amp, from OFF (fully down, -80 dB) up to -20 dB. Raise it until the hum and buzz between notes disappear but the notes still ring out.
TUNER
Switches the tuner on. While it is on the output is muted, so you can tune silently. It reads notes from about 35 to 700 Hz. The display shows the note and octave, with a dot that moves left (flat) or right (sharp); the note turns green when you are within 4 cents. It reads PLAY A NOTE until it hears a clear pitch, and TUNER OFF when switched off.
CAB
The 8x10 cabinet in or out (in by default). Out gives the amp's line-out sound, for use with a cabinet simulator of your own.
MIC
The microphone on the cabinet, 0 to 10 (3 by default): from on axis (brighter, with more of the cone's bite) to off axis (darker and softer). Only heard with CAB in.
DI BLEND
Mixes the clean DI signal in under the amp, 0 to 100%. 0% is amp only; 100% is the clean DI only. The DI keeps a solid, clear low end while the amp provides the growl.
OUTPUT
The final level, -24 to +12 dB.
LEVEL
Ten lights showing the output level, green up to amber and red at the top.

The header

Pilot lamp
Shows the amp is on. It takes the theme's accent colour.
Theme picker
Changes the colour of the control panel, its pinstripe and the pilot lamp. Graphite (the default) keeps the aluminium panel with a blue stripe and lamp.
HW
Switches between the hardware-style head and the flat look.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Next to the S / M / L size keys. Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Tips

  • For a classic clean bass, keep GAIN around 3-4 and MASTER low, and shape the tone with BASS and MIDRANGE at 800 Hz.
  • For growl that still sits under the mix, push GAIN and MASTER, then bring in 20-40% DI BLEND to keep the low end solid.
  • If the bass disappears on small speakers, boost MIDRANGE at 800 Hz or 1.6 kHz rather than adding more BASS.
  • Use TUNER before recording: it mutes the output, so you can leave it on while you tune and switch it off to play.
  • Set GATE only as high as you need - too high and it will cut off the tail of long notes.

File: gfx-bass-head.jsfx

Amps & drive

Desert Ten

A little 10-watt solid-state practice combo with an 8-inch open-back speaker, from clean to raspy fuzz.

Overview

Desert Ten is a small 10-watt solid-state practice amp, in the spirit of the little combo the desert-rock records were made with. It has an op-amp preamp that goes from clean to a raspy rail clip, a SATURATION switch that brings in a diode clipper with a midrange push for the gnarly, fuzzy tone, three tone controls, and a 10-watt power amp that stays clean at first and then clips hard.

After the amp comes the 8-inch open-back speaker, with a thin low end and a midrange honk, and a mic you can move from on axis to off axis. Switch the speaker off for a line-out sound to feed your own cabinet.

Like a real amp it runs in mono: the left and right inputs are summed, and both outputs carry the amp. The amp runs at twice the sample rate to keep the clipping clean. A noise gate sits before the amp and a tuner is built in. With SATURATION on, the clipping diodes' balance wanders very slightly over time, as on real hardware.

Reach for it for

  • Raspy, fuzzy guitar and bass tones (SATURATION on)
  • Small-amp crunch that sits in a dense mix
  • Lo-fi, boxy guitar and keys through a little 8-inch speaker
  • Driving a DI'd guitar with the gate and tuner on the same panel
  • Line-out amp tones into your own cabinet (CAB off)

Controls

GAIN

PRE (cream cap)
The preamp gain, 0 to 10 (5 by default): from about -6 to +30 dB into the op-amp. Low settings are clean; higher settings push the op-amp into its rails for a raspy clip. It also sets how hard the SATURATION diodes are driven.
SATURATION
Switches in the diode clipper with its midrange push: the signal is lifted around 900 Hz, trimmed below 220 Hz and above 5 kHz, and clipped by a pair of diodes. This is the gnarly, fuzzy tone. Off by default.
POST (blue cap)
The volume into the 10-watt power amp, 0 to 10 (4 by default). Low settings stay clean; turned up, the power amp clips hard, the way small solid-state amps do.

EQUALIZATION

LOW
A low shelf at 120 Hz, 0 to 10; 5 is flat, and each step is about 2.6 dB.
MID
A broad peak at 700 Hz, 0 to 10. 5 leaves a small built-in mid lift (about 1.5 dB); turn it down to scoop the mids, up to push them.
HIGH
A high shelf at 2.8 kHz, 0 to 10; 5 is flat.

The lower strip

GATE
A noise gate before the amp, from -80 to -20 dB. Fully down (-80) reads OFF. Raise it until the hiss and hum between notes disappear. It opens fast, holds for 50 ms and closes smoothly.
TUNER
Switches the tuner on. While it is on, the output is muted so you can tune in silence. The window shows the note name with its octave and a dot that moves left (flat) or right (sharp); the note turns green when you are within a few cents. It reads PLAY A NOTE when it hears nothing.
CAB
The 8-inch open-back speaker, on by default. Off gives the amp's line out, with no speaker colour.
MIC
Where the mic sits on the speaker, 0 (on axis) to 10 (off axis); 3 by default. On axis is brighter with more cone bite; moving off axis rounds off the top end and darkens the sound.
OUTPUT
The final level, -24 to +12 dB.
LEVEL
Ten LEDs showing the output level, up to 0 dBFS.

The header

Theme picker
Changes the panel's colours: the control panel, the pinstripe and the pilot lamp follow the theme. Graphite keeps the aluminium panel with an orange stripe and lamp.
HW
Switches between the hardware-style faceplate and the flat look.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

There are no factory presets, and no input or output fold-out on this panel. The output passes through the GFX soft clipper, which catches peaks at -0.3 dBFS.

Tips

  • For the classic fuzzy desert tone, switch SATURATION on, set PRE around 6 - 8 and keep POST moderate; then shape it with MID.
  • For a cleaner small-amp crunch, leave SATURATION off and turn POST up: the power amp clips harder than the preamp.
  • If the sound is too boxy, move MIC towards 0 and bring MID down a little.
  • Set the GATE just above the noise floor with SATURATION on - high gain makes hiss between notes much louder.

File: gfx-desert-ten.jsfx

Amps & drive

Grunt

A warm drive stage that keeps the low end tight and the mids clear.

Overview

Grunt is a simple drive stage with a warm, sunrise-orange faceplate. Turn up GAIN and the signal is pushed through a soft pre-clip and a cubic waveshaper that adds weight and harmonics, then through a band saturator on the way out.

Two things keep it from turning to mush. The sub band below 140 Hz is split off and, the higher GAIN goes, the more of it skips the drive altogether (up to 85% at full GAIN) - so the bottom stays tight and un-fuzzed. And as GRUNT and SAG pile up harmonics, a dynamic dip at 330 Hz pulls out up to about 6 dB there, so heavy settings don't go boxy.

Reach for it for

  • Adding weight and grit to bass, synths and drum loops
  • Warming up a bus or a dull source
  • Heavier drive that keeps a tight low end
  • Quick amp-style crunch on guitars and keys

Controls

The six knobs sit in three printed groups on a recessed strip. Hover over a knob and its label shows the value.

PREAMP

GAIN
The drive into the stage, from -24 to +12 dB. It sets the level going in and how hard the waveshaper is pushed. The higher it goes, the more of the sub-140 Hz band bypasses the drive (none at the bottom of the travel, up to 85% at the top), so the low end stays clean under heavy drive.
BOOST (small knob, gold pointer)
Extra drive on top of GAIN, 0 - 100%. It also adds an asymmetric bias, so it brings in even harmonics for a warmer, rounder tone.

CHARACTER

GRUNT
How much cubic waveshaping character is added, 0 to 1. More GRUNT means thicker, more saturated harmonics. Together with SAG it also deepens the dynamic 330 Hz dip.
SAG
The bias of the output saturation, 0 to 1: more SAG gives more even harmonics and a rounder, warmer colour. It also adds to the 330 Hz dip.

OUTPUT

SQUASH
The amount of band saturation in the output stage, 0 to 1. Only the lows (below about 90 Hz) and the highs (above about 7.5 kHz) are saturated; the mids pass clean.
MASTER
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.
Meter
At the right of the strip. Output level, left and right side by side, rising from the bottom, with a floating peak. Click it to clear the peak.

The header

PRESETS, arrows and display
PRESETS (or a click on the display) opens the list of factory presets; the ◄ / ► keys step through them. The display shows the preset number and name.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

The input guard is always on in Grunt: very hot sources are eased down before the drive, so a loud track won't overload it. There is no output-stage switch on the panel; the presets set it to a soft clipper or a lookahead limiter.

Presets

Default
The starting point: GAIN at 0 dB, no BOOST, medium GRUNT and SQUASH, soft clipper on the output.
Warm Console
+6 dB GAIN, full BOOST, light GRUNT with more SAG and SQUASH for a warm, rounded colour; lookahead limiter on the output.
American Crunch
+9 dB GAIN, full BOOST and heavy GRUNT for a bright, biting crunch; little SAG, soft clipper.
British Punch
+4 dB GAIN, no BOOST, medium GRUNT with more SAG and SQUASH for a punchy, thick tone; lookahead limiter.

Tips

  • Because the sub band skips the drive more as GAIN rises, you can push bass and kick hard without the bottom turning fuzzy.
  • Reach for BOOST rather than more GAIN when you want warmth as well as drive - its bias adds even harmonics.
  • If heavy settings sound too thin in the low mids, back GRUNT and SAG off a little: they control how far the 330 Hz dip cuts.
  • Use MASTER to match the level with the plugin bypassed, so you judge the tone and not the loudness.

File: gfx-grunt.jsfx

Amps & drive

Guitar Head

An all-valve guitar amp with spring reverb and a 2x12 or 4x12 cabinet.

Overview

Guitar Head is an all-valve guitar amplifier in the spirit of the classic 50-watt heads. The preamp has a clean channel with one valve, from sparkly to edge-of-breakup, and a DRIVE channel that cascades a second valve for more gain. Then come a tone stack, a spring reverb tank, and a push-pull power stage that sags and crunches as you drive it.

A cabinet stage follows, with a 2x12 open-back or 4x12 closed-back cabinet and a microphone that you can move from on axis to off axis. Turn the cabinet off to use the amp as a line-out head into your own cabinet plugin or impulse response.

The amp runs in mono, as a real amp does, at twice the sample rate for cleaner distortion; both outputs carry the same signal. A built-in noise gate and tuner complete the head.

Reach for it for

  • Clean and edge-of-breakup guitar tones
  • Classic rock crunch and lead tones (DRIVE, MASTER)
  • Re-amping DI guitar recordings
  • Spring reverb on guitars
  • A head into your own cabinet plugin (CAB OFF)

Controls

The amp

GAIN
The preamp gain, 0 to 10 (5 by default). On the clean channel it goes from sparkly to edge-of-breakup; with DRIVE on it goes much further.
BRIGHT
A treble lift in front of the gain. It is strongest at low GAIN settings and fades as you turn GAIN up, like the bright switch on a real amp.
DRIVE
Off: the clean channel (one valve). On: a second cascaded valve for more gain and sustain, with a tighter low end.
BASS / MIDDLE / TREBLE
The tone stack, 0 to 10 each; 5 is flat.
PRESENCE
The top end after the power stage, 0 to 10 (5 by default).
REVERB
The level of the spring reverb, 0 to 10 (2 by default). The spring tank sits between the preamp and the power stage.
MASTER
How hard the power stage is driven, 0 to 10 (4 by default): first headroom, then sag and crunch as you turn it up.

The lower strip

GATE
A noise gate in front of the amp, from OFF (fully down) to -20 dB. Raise it until the hiss and hum between notes disappear.
TUNER
Switches the tuner on. While it is on, the output is muted and the display shows the note and how far off it is; the note turns green when you are in tune. With no note it reads PLAY A NOTE.
CAB
The cabinet: OFF (line out, no cabinet), 2x12 OPEN (open back, the default) or 4x12 (closed back, more low end).
MIC
The microphone position, 0 (on axis, brighter) to 10 (off axis, darker and smoother); 3 by default.
OUTPUT
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.
LEVEL
A row of ten LEDs showing the output peak level, from -30 dB up to just under 0 dBFS.

The header

Pilot lamp
Shows the amp is on; it takes the theme's accent colour.
Theme picker
Changes the colour of the control panel, pinstripe and lamp.
HW
Switches between the hardware faceplate and the flat look.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Tips

  • For edge-of-breakup, keep DRIVE off, set GAIN around 6 - 8 and let MASTER add the crunch as you dig in.
  • BRIGHT is most useful at low GAIN settings, where it adds sparkle to clean tones; at high gain it does little.
  • Set CAB to OFF when you want to use your own cabinet plugin or impulse response after the head.
  • Move MIC towards 10 to smooth out a harsh, fizzy top end before reaching for TREBLE.
  • Use TUNER between takes; remember it mutes the output while it is on.

File: gfx-guitar-head.jsfx

Amps & drive

Head

A one-knob tape saturator with three voices.

Overview

Head is a tape-style saturator in the spirit of the classic one-knob tape plug-ins. Turn up DRIVE and the signal hits the tape curve harder; the level is matched automatically, so DRIVE changes the character, not the loudness.

The tape behaviour is built in: the treble is boosted before the curve and cut again after it, a small fixed bias gives mostly odd harmonics with a little even-order warmth, there is a low-end head bump around 70 Hz that eases off as you drive harder, and a gentle top-end loss. The curve runs at twice the sample rate to keep it clean.

As DRIVE goes up, more of the low end (below about 250 Hz) skips the curve and rejoins clean afterwards. The bass then stops pushing the mids and highs in and out of saturation, so even heavy settings stay smooth and uncluttered. Like a real machine, the bias and the top-end loss wander slowly and independently on each side.

Reach for it for

  • Warming and thickening a vocal, bass or synth
  • Gluing a drum bus with tape colour
  • Taking the digital edge off bright sources
  • Heavier tape grit that keeps the low end tidy

Controls

The tape unit

DRIVE
How hard the signal hits the tape curve, from 0 to 10 (3 by default) - up to +22 dB into the curve. The output level is matched, so you hear more saturation, not more volume. Higher settings also let more of the low end bypass the curve and soften the head bump.
VOICE
Three voicings, picked on the rotary switch (click a name, click the knob to step, or use the mouse wheel). NORMAL: flat, with tape's gentle top-end loss. MEDIUM: a broad lift around 1.4 kHz going into the curve, for body in the mids. BRIGHT: a high shelf going into the curve, so the top end saturates first and stays airier.
TRIM
The clean output level, from -24 to +6 dB, applied after everything else.
BOOST
Adds colour on top of DRIVE, from 0 to 100%. It makes the tape curve more lopsided (more even harmonics, a warmer tone) and brings in band saturation on the way out: only the lows and highs are saturated, the mids stay clean.
LEVEL
Ten lights showing the output peak level, from -30 dBFS up to 0: green, then amber, then red at the top.

Double-click DRIVE or TRIM to return it to its default; hold Shift while dragging for fine moves. The output passes through a soft clipper set at -0.3 dBFS before TRIM, so sudden peaks are caught.

The header

S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Tips

  • Set DRIVE by ear and leave the level to Head: because it is level-matched, you can compare settings fairly without chasing the volume.
  • On bass and kick, high DRIVE stays tight because the deep lows skip the curve - you get the growl in the mids without the low end flapping.
  • Try MEDIUM on vocals and guitars for presence, and BRIGHT on dull drum overheads or pads.
  • Use BOOST sparingly on a mix bus: a little adds warmth at both ends of the spectrum while the mids stay clear.

File: gfx-head.jsfx

Amps & drive

Model 15

A lo-fi channel strip in the spirit of the early-80s portable cassette multitrackers.

Overview

Model 15 is a single channel of the classic early-80s ultra-portable cassette multitracker: a low-voltage, battery-driven preamp, a fixed broad-stroke tone stage, a sluggish tape-head saturation with a top end that closes down as the level rises, and an open, breathing hiss floor with simple single-band noise reduction.

Put it on individual stems to push them towards that compressed, boxy, warm little-recorder sound. The signal runs preamp, then tone, then tape, and MIX blends the result with the dry input.

Reach for it for

  • Bedroom-demo character on drums, guitars and vocals
  • Warm, boxy saturation that glues transients together
  • Lo-fi wobble, hiss and worn-tape dropouts
  • Making a clean stem sit with genuinely lo-fi material
  • Parallel tape colour with MIX

Controls

Channel

Signal light
Glows green when audio is coming out of the plugin.
GAIN
The low-voltage preamp, -6 to +24 dB (+6 dB by default). Above 0 dB it clips early and asymmetrically, smearing transients and thickening the low mids.
TREBLE
A broad high shelf at 10 kHz, -10 to +10 dB. Push it hard and the shelf itself turns a little gritty and grainy rather than staying clean.
BASS
A broad low shelf at 100 Hz, -10 to +10 dB, with the same gentle grit when pushed.
TRIM
Input level before anything else, from -24 to +12 dB.
GUARD
On by default. Gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources.
MIX
The coloured signal against the dry input, 0 - 100%.

Meters

L / R
VU meters showing the left and right output.

Tape

DRIVE
The tape saturation, 0 - 100% (35% by default): a sluggish magnetic memory ahead of a soft saturation curve, plus a top end that rolls off further as the level rises, gluing transients together. A small, fixed head bump around 80 Hz is always there.
WOW
Wow and flutter in the transport, 0 - 100 (25 by default): a slow wow, a slower random wander and a faint 6 Hz flutter.
HISS
The tape's own noise floor, 0 - 100 (50 by default). It ducks a little under the signal and swells back in the gaps. Each side has its own hiss.
AGE
An older, worn tape, 0 - 100 (15 by default): the top end goes further and short dropouts creep in, more often the higher you turn it.
OUTPUT
The output level, -24 to +12 dB.
NR
The simple B-type-style noise reduction, in by default: the quiet highs are lifted before the tape and taken back down after it, which lowers the hiss. Out gives the raw tape, hiss and all.

The header

< and >
Step to the previous or next preset.
PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets. A preset sets GAIN, TREBLE, BASS, the TAPE controls, NR and MIX; OUTPUT, TRIM and GUARD are left as they are. The last entry resets everything to the defaults.
PATCH display
Shows the name of the preset in use.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Output stage (REAPER's parameter list only)

Model 15 has the GFX output stage, but no knobs for it on the panel. Its settings are in REAPER's parameter list, and Reset all to defaults puts them back.

Band sat / Bias
Band saturation on the way out: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean). Bias adds more even (2nd) harmonics. Off by default.
Sat low band / Sat high band
Where the low and high saturation bands start (40 - 400 Hz and 2 - 16 kHz).
Ceiling / limit threshold
With Clip: the level the peaks are softly clipped at. With Limit: the threshold of a brickwall limiter that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS.
Output stage
Off, Clip (a soft clipper, no latency; the default) or Limit (a lookahead limiter, 3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER).

Presets

Bedroom Demo
The standard setting with a touch more TREBLE, BASS and DRIVE, NR in.
Hot and Boxy
The preamp pushed to +15 dB into 70% tape drive, with more hiss and age.
No NR, Open Tape
Noise reduction out and the hiss turned well up.
Worn-out Portable
Heavy wow, lots of age and dropouts, a little extra bass.
Clean-ish Portable
Preamp at 0 dB, light drive, wow, hiss and age.
Reset all to defaults
Every control back to its starting value, including OUTPUT, TRIM, GUARD and the output stage.

Tips

  • GAIN and DRIVE do different things: GAIN gives the preamp's early, edgy clipping, DRIVE the softer tape squash and darkening. Try one high and the other low.
  • On a set of stems, keep WOW low on all but one or two so the mix doesn't drift out of tune with itself.
  • Switching NR out is the quickest way to that hissy, open home-recording sound.
  • Turn MIX down to 40 - 60% to keep the attack of drums while adding the tape's body.

Amps & drive

Model 44

A rack channel strip in the spirit of the early-90s cassette multitrackers: clean preamp, semi-parametric EQ and tape.

Overview

Model 44 is a single channel strip in the spirit of the early-90s rack-mount cassette multitrack gear. The signal goes through a clean, high-headroom preamp, a three-band EQ with a sweepable mid, and then a tape stage: a dense magnetic saturation ceiling with a NORMAL / DOUBLE speed switch and compander noise reduction.

The preamp itself stays clean - the colour and the ceiling come from the tape. Double speed raises that ceiling, extends the top end and tightens the sub-bass, for a punchier, cleaner sound; normal speed is softer, darker and saturates earlier. The noise reduction is tuned to pump less than the earlier 80s machines.

Reach for it for

  • Adding cassette-tape density and warmth to a single track
  • Drums and bass that need to sit tighter and punchier (DOUBLE speed)
  • Quick tone shaping with a sweepable mid
  • A softer, darker lo-fi tone (NORMAL speed)
  • Parallel tape colour with MIX

Controls

CHANNEL

Signal light
The green light glows when audio is coming out.
GAIN
The preamp gain, -6 to +24 dB (+6 dB by default). The preamp itself stays clean; turning it up drives the tape stage harder.
MID FREQ
The centre of the sweepable mid band, 200 Hz to 5 kHz.
MID GAIN
Cut or boost at MID FREQ, -12 to +12 dB, with a fixed, fairly broad width.
TREBLE
A high shelf at 12 kHz, -10 to +10 dB.
BASS
A low shelf at 80 Hz, -10 to +10 dB.
TRIM
Input level before anything else, from -24 to +12 dB.
GUARD
On by default. GUARD gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources. The light shows when it is on.
MIX
Blends the coloured signal with the dry input, 0 to 100%. 100% is fully through the strip.

Meters

L / R
VU meters showing the left and right output.

TAPE

DRIVE
How hard the tape's saturation ceiling works, 0 to 100% (35% by default). More DRIVE rounds the peaks off sooner and harder, for a denser sound.
NORMAL / DOUBLE
The tape SPEED. NORMAL (1 7/8 IPS): the top end rolls off around 10 kHz and the saturation starts earlier. DOUBLE (3 3/4 IPS): the ceiling sits higher, the top end reaches about 16 kHz, the sub-bass below about 45 Hz is tightened and transients stay punchier.
NR
Compander noise reduction, in by default. It compresses the signal 2:1 with a treble lift on the way onto the tape and expands it 1:2 coming back. Because the expander follows what comes off the tape, it breathes a little, though more gently than the 80s units. Switch it off for the raw tape sound.
OUTPUT
The output level, -24 to +12 dB.

Opens the list of factory presets. Choosing one sets the CHANNEL EQ, GAIN, the TAPE section and MIX; OUTPUT, TRIM and GUARD are left as they are. The last entry resets everything to the defaults.
PATCH
Shows the name of the preset last chosen.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.

Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Output stage

These settings have no knobs on the panel; reach them through REAPER's parameter list for the plugin (or automation lanes).

Band sat
Band saturation on the way out: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
Bias
Shapes the saturation: more bias adds more even (2nd) harmonics, for a warmer, rounder colour.
Sat low band / Sat high band
Where the low and high saturation bands start (40 - 400 Hz and 2 - 16 kHz).
Ceiling / limit threshold
With Clip: the level the peaks are softly clipped at. With Limit: the threshold of a brickwall limiter that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS - pulling it down limits harder and makes the output louder.

Off, Clip or Limit: nothing, a soft clipper (no latency, the default), or a lookahead limiter (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER).

Tips

  • Set GAIN for the amount of tape colour you want, then bring OUTPUT down to match the level before you judge it.
  • Switch between NORMAL and DOUBLE on drums: double speed keeps the snap, normal speed softens and thickens it.
  • With NR in and plenty of DRIVE you can hear the compander breathe; switch NR out if you want the saturation without the movement.
  • Use MIX at 30-60% to add tape density under a track without losing its transients.

Amps & drive

PenguinDrive

An envelope filter, phaser, drive and squash compressor in one box, after a 70s art-rock live guitar rig.

Overview

PenguinDrive chains four effects that a 70s art-rock guitarist might have run on stage: a resonant envelope filter, a four-stage phaser, a valve-style drive and a compressor at the end that squashes it all together.

In ENVELOPE mode the filter follows how hard you play: every pick attack opens it upwards from the PEAK setting and it falls back as the note dies, for the vocal, quacking funk sound. In FIXED mode the filter stays put at PEAK, like a wah pedal left half-way down. The filter is a band-pass, so the effect side is focused and nasal; MIX blends it with your dry signal.

A DRIFT dial adds a slow random wander to the filter frequency and the drive, so the sound breathes a little like real analogue gear.

Reach for it for

  • Funky, quacking guitar and clav lines
  • Vocal, wah-like lead tones
  • Driven, phased guitar for 70s rock
  • Synth bass and keys that need movement and bite
  • Cocked-wah tones with FIXED mode

Controls

Mode (header)

ENVELOPE
The filter follows your playing: the harder the note, the further it opens above PEAK. The yellow light above the key shows it is on.
FIXED
The filter sits still at PEAK - a cocked-wah tone. SENS has no effect. The orange light above the key shows it is on.
OFF
Bypasses the filter, phaser, drive and squash. The signal still passes through the input and output stages.

Display

The window on the left shows the current filter frequency (FILTER PEAK) in Hz, with a moving trace that follows it.

Main controls

The signal runs through the controls in this order: filter, phaser, drive, squash.

Z-DRIVE
How hard the filtered signal drives the saturation stage, from 0 to 10. 0 is almost clean; 10 adds up to 25 dB of gain into the soft clipper for a thick, compressed drive.
SENS
Envelope sensitivity, 0 to 100%: how far your playing pushes the filter up from PEAK. Low settings give a subtle movement; high settings sweep it right up for each note. ENVELOPE mode only.
PEAK
The filter's resting frequency, 200 Hz to 5 kHz. In ENVELOPE mode this is where the sweep starts; in FIXED mode it is where the filter stays.
Q / RES
The filter's resonance, 10 to 95%. Higher settings narrow the band and make it ring, for a more vocal, quacking sound.
PHASER
How much of the four-stage phaser is blended in after the filter, 0 to 100%. At 0 the phaser is off. It sweeps slowly between about 500 Hz and 2 kHz.
SQUASH
The compressor at the end of the chain, 0 to 100%. Higher settings lower its threshold for a flatter, more sustained sound; it grabs quickly and lets go slowly. At 0 it is off.

Chaos drift

DRIFT
0 to 100%. 50% is the standard amount; 100% is twice that, 0 turns it off. It makes the filter frequency wander slowly and the drive amount breathe a little. Higher settings also make the wander move faster.

IN / OUT

TRIM
Input level before anything else, from -24 to +12 dB.
GUARD
Click the TRIM knob (without turning it) to switch GUARD on or off; the small light at the knob's upper right shows it is on and glows brighter while it works. On by default. Gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper that holds the peaks just under 0 dBFS (no latency), or a lookahead limiter (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). The CLIP key flashes red while the clipper is working.

Master

MIX
Dry / effect balance, 0 to 100% (50% by default). Lower settings keep more of your clean attack under the filtered, driven sound.
LOW CUT
A high-pass filter on the effected signal only, from off (fully left) up to 500 Hz. The dry low end stays as it is.
OUTPUT
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.
LEVEL
Output meters, left and right.

The header

PRESETS, ◄ ► and the display
PRESETS (or a click on the display) opens the list of factory presets; ◄ and ► step through them. A preset sets the mode and the main controls (including the phaser speed) and MIX; drift, the input and output stages, LOW CUT and OUTPUT are left as they are.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Presets

Art-Rock Lead ('73)
Envelope filter with strong resonance, a fair amount of drive, phaser and squash, mostly wet (85% mix).
Funk Envelope
High sensitivity from a low 800 Hz peak with lots of resonance and heavy squash: the classic quack.
Cocked Wah Drive
FIXED filter at 1.4 kHz, heavy drive and a faster phaser, fully wet.
Mild Auto-Sweep
Gentle envelope sweep from 2 kHz with light drive and no phaser, half wet.

Tips

  • Set PEAK first with SENS at 0 to find the tone the filter rests on, then bring SENS up until the sweep follows your playing the way you want.
  • The envelope responds to level, so a compressor or a louder TRIM before the filter makes the sweep more even; play dynamically for a more expressive one.
  • The phaser's speed has no knob on the panel: the presets set it (0.1 to 0.5 Hz), and it can be changed in REAPER's parameter list as Phaser Speed (0.05 to 5 Hz).
  • For a cleaner funk sound, keep Z-DRIVE low and use SQUASH for sustain instead.

File: gfx-penguindrive.jsfx

Amps & drive

Preamp

A colour preamp with four voicings, a three-band EQ boost and a VU meter.

Overview

Preamp is a stage you put in front of anything that needs warmth, weight or a bit of dirt. Choose one of four voicings, set how hard to drive it, and shape the top end with TONE.

CLEAN adds no drive at all - use it with the EQ BOOST as a clean boost. VIBE recreates the preamp of a classic 60s pedal: extra low-mid weight, a soft, lopsided saturation and a rounded top that gets darker as you drive it. ZEN is a smooth, symmetrical, amp-like breakup. TRANS is a single-transistor drive: lopsided, it bites earlier and turns furry when pushed.

In every voicing except CLEAN a gentle, program-dependent compression (sag) sits in front of the drive, and it grows as you turn DRIVE up, like a real preamp running out of headroom. The EQ BOOST in front adds three gentle peaks a decade apart to push the source into the drive.

Reach for it for

  • Warming up DI guitar, bass and keys
  • Driving synths and drum loops into gentle grit
  • A clean, broad EQ lift on a bus (CLEAN with BOOST)
  • Fuzzy, transistor-style edge on a lead
  • A little analogue movement with DRIFT

Controls

VOICE

CLEAN
No drive and no sag. TONE does nothing here; use it with BOOST as a clean EQ preamp.
VIBE
The 60s pedal preamp: a low-end lift (below about 250 Hz, growing with DRIVE), soft, uneven (even-harmonic) saturation and a top end that rolls off from about 18 kHz down to 7 kHz as DRIVE rises. TONE does nothing in this voicing - the drive sets the top end.
ZEN
Gentle, symmetrical saturation with a smooth, amp-like breakup.
TRANS
Single-transistor drive: lopsided, it starts biting earlier and gets furry when pushed.
BOOST
Switches the EQ boost in or out. It sits in front of the drive, so a boost also drives the voicing harder.
30/300/3K, 50/500/5K, 70/700/7K
The three frequencies the boost lifts at once, each a decade apart: low, mid and high together.
+1 dB, +3 dB, +6 dB
How much each of the three bands is lifted.

VU meter

Shows the input level after TRIM and GUARD, with 0 VU at -18 dBFS. Under it, the panel names the voicing and how hard it is working: WARM, PUSHED or SATURATED, by the DRIVE setting (CLEAN reads NO DRIVE).

DRIVE

DRIVE
How hard the voicing is driven, 0 to 100% (40% by default). It also deepens the sag compression in front of it.
TONE
A top-end roll-off for ZEN and TRANS, sweeping its corner from about 1.2 kHz (dark) to 14 kHz (open), 0 to 100%.
OUT lights
Six lights showing the output peak level: -36, -24, -12, -6, 0 and +3 dB.

Drift

DRIFT
0 to 100%. 50% is the standard amount; 100% is twice that, 0 turns it off. It makes the saturation's lopsidedness (bias) wander slowly and the sag compression breathe in and out. Higher settings also make the wander move faster.

IN / OUT

TRIM
Input level before anything else, from -24 to +12 dB.
GUARD
Click the TRIM knob (without turning it) to switch GUARD on or off; the small light at the knob's upper right shows it is on and glows brighter while it works. On by default. Gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper that holds the peaks just under 0 dBFS (no latency), or a lookahead limiter (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). The CLIP key flashes red while the clipper is working.

MASTER

MIX
Dry / preamp balance, 0 to 100% (100% by default). Lower settings blend the clean signal back in for parallel drive.
OUTPUT
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.

The header

PRESETS, ◄ ► and the display
PRESETS (or a click on the display) opens the list of factory presets; ◄ and ► step through them. A preset sets the voicing, DRIVE, TONE, the EQ boost, the drift amounts and MIX; the drift speed, OUTPUT and the input and output stages are left as they are. The last entry resets everything to the defaults.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Presets

Vibe Warm
VIBE at 35% drive: gentle warmth and weight.
Vibe Driven
VIBE at 85% drive with a little more drift: thick and saturated.
Zen Edge
ZEN at 35% drive: just on the edge of breakup.
Zen Crunch
ZEN at 70% drive with a slightly brighter TONE.
Transistor Grit
TRANS at 50% drive with TONE a little dark.
Transistor Fuzz
TRANS at 95% drive with a darker TONE: full fuzz.
Console Lift
CLEAN with BOOST on at 70/700/7K, +3 dB: a clean, broad lift.
Mid Push into Zen
ZEN at 55% drive with the 50/500/5K boost pushing into it.
Breathing Valve
VIBE at 55% drive with heavy bias and sag drift, so the colour moves.
Reset all to defaults
Every control back to its starting value, including the input and output stages.

Tips

  • Use the VU: aim for the needle around 0 with TRIM before you set DRIVE, so the voicings react as designed.
  • BOOST adds level as well as colour, so it drives the voicing harder too; pull DRIVE back a little when you switch it on.
  • For parallel drive on drums or bass, turn DRIVE up and MIX down to 30 - 50%.
  • TONE only works in ZEN and TRANS; in VIBE the drive itself darkens the top.

File: gfx-preamp.jsfx

Amps & drive

Preamp Q

The four-voicing colour preamp with a four-band console channel EQ on its cream strip.

Overview

Preamp Q is the Preamp with a full channel EQ added. The blue top half is the preamp: four voicings from clean to fuzz, an EQ boost in front of the drive, gentle program-dependent compression (sag) and a VU meter. The cream strip underneath is a four-band console channel EQ with high- and low-pass filters, shelf-or-bell ends, two swept mids with Q, a BROWN or BLACK voicing and a line amp.

The ORDER key decides which comes first: the EQ after the preamp, to shape the driven sound, or in front of it, so the EQ changes what gets driven. The panel shows the unit the signal meets first on top.

Reach for it for

  • Warmth, crunch or fuzz with a proper EQ to shape it
  • Shaping a source before it hits the drive (EQ > PREAMP)
  • A complete channel strip: drive, EQ and output limiting in one
  • Bass, guitars, keys and drums that need colour as well as tone

Controls

VOICE

CLEAN
No drive and no sag. TONE does nothing here; use it with BOOST as a clean EQ preamp.
VIBE
The 60s pedal preamp: a low-mid lift, soft, uneven (even-harmonic) saturation and a top end that rolls off from about 18 kHz down to 7 kHz as DRIVE rises. TONE does nothing in this voicing - the drive sets the top end.
ZEN
Gentle, symmetrical saturation with a smooth, amp-like breakup.
TRANS
Single-transistor drive: lopsided, it starts biting earlier and gets furry when pushed.
BOOST
Switches the EQ boost in or out. It sits in front of the drive, so a boost also drives the voicing harder.
30/300/3K, 50/500/5K, 70/700/7K
The three frequencies the boost lifts at once, each a decade apart: low, mid and high together.
+1 dB, +3 dB, +6 dB
How much each of the three bands is lifted.

VU meter

Shows the level going into the preamp, with 0 VU at -18 dBFS. Under it, the panel names the voicing and how hard it is working: WARM, PUSHED or SATURATED, by the DRIVE setting (CLEAN reads NO DRIVE).

DRIVE

DRIVE
How hard the voicing is driven, 0 to 100% (40% by default). It also deepens the sag compression in front of it.
TONE
A top-end roll-off for ZEN and TRANS, sweeping its corner from about 1.2 kHz (dark) to 14 kHz (open), 0 to 100%.
OUT lights
Six lights showing the output peak level: -36, -24, -12, -6, 0 and +3 dB.

Channel EQ

IN
Switches the four EQ bands in or out (in by default). The HPF and LPF stay in.
BROWN / BLACK
The EQ voicing; the key shows the one in use (BLACK by default). BROWN: broad bells and soft shelves. BLACK: bells that tighten as you boost or cut (proportional Q), and shelves with a small opposite dip just inside the corner.
LINE DRIVE
Pushes the EQ's line amp into gentle saturation, 0 to 100%. It is level matched, so it adds colour rather than volume.
OUT
The EQ's output level, -12 to +12 dB.

FILTERS

HPF
A high-pass filter, 18 dB per octave, from OFF (fully left) up to 400 Hz. Removes rumble and low-end mud.
LPF
A low-pass filter, 12 dB per octave, from 3 kHz up to OFF (fully right). Tames hiss and harsh top.

LF

GAIN
-15 to +15 dB.
FREQ
30 to 450 Hz (100 Hz by default).
BELL
Off (default): a low shelf. On: a bell centred on FREQ.

LMF

GAIN
-15 to +15 dB.
FREQ
200 Hz to 2.5 kHz (600 Hz by default).
Q
The bandwidth, 0.4 to 3 (1 by default). Higher is narrower. In BLACK the bell also narrows as you boost or cut more.

HMF

GAIN
-15 to +15 dB.
FREQ
600 Hz to 7 kHz (2.5 kHz by default).
Q
The bandwidth, 0.4 to 3 (1 by default).

HF

GAIN
-15 to +15 dB.
FREQ
1.5 to 16 kHz (8 kHz by default).
BELL
Off (default): a high shelf. On: a bell centred on FREQ.

Drift

DRIFT
0 to 100%. 50% is the standard amount; 100% is twice that, 0 turns it off. It makes the saturation's lopsidedness (bias) wander slowly and the sag compression breathe in and out. Higher settings also make the wander move faster.

IN / OUT

TRIM
Input level before anything else, from -24 to +12 dB. A single click on the knob (without dragging) switches GUARD on or off; its LED at the knob's upper right is dark when GUARD is off, red when it is on, and glows brighter while it is catching peaks.
GUARD
On by default. Gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out, 0 - 100%: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper (no latency), or a lookahead limiter that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). The CLIP key flashes red while the clipper is working.

MASTER

MIX
Dry / preamp balance, 0 to 100% (100% by default). Lower settings blend the clean signal back in for parallel drive. The EQ is not part of the blend: it is applied to the whole signal, before or after.
OUTPUT
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.

The header

ORDER (PREAMP > EQ / EQ > PREAMP)
Each press swaps the order of the two units; the key shows the current order and the unit the signal meets first is drawn on top. PREAMP > EQ shapes the driven sound; EQ > PREAMP changes what goes into the drive, so EQ boosts also push the voicing harder.
PRESETS, arrows and display
PRESETS (or a click on the display) opens the list of factory presets; the ◄ / ► keys step through them. A preset sets the voicing, DRIVE, TONE, the EQ boost, the drift amounts and MIX; the channel EQ, ORDER, OUTPUT and the input and output stages are left as they are. The last entry resets the preamp, MIX, OUTPUT and the IN / OUT strip to their defaults (the channel EQ, ORDER and the DRIFT dial stay as they are).
S / M / L

Tips

  • Use the VU: aim for the needle around 0 with TRIM before you set DRIVE, so the voicings react as designed.
  • With EQ > PREAMP, use the HPF to stop the low end driving the voicing into mud, then boost the mids you want to break up.
  • With PREAMP > EQ, the LPF and a little HMF cut tame the fizz of TRANS at high drive.
  • For parallel drive on drums or bass, turn DRIVE up and MIX down to 30 - 50%.
  • TONE only works in ZEN and TRANS; in VIBE the drive itself darkens the top.

Amps & drive

Ten Rack

A rack of six compact pedals - chorus / flanger, twin chorus, octave, drive, distortion and mod delay - in any order, in series, parallel or two split chains.

Overview

Ten Rack is a black 2U rack holding six compact pedals in the spirit of the mid-80s Japanese compact pedal series. The sound of each one is designed from scratch. There are six pedal types, and any slot can hold any of them - you can even use the same pedal twice, since every slot has its own memory and settings.

The six pedals are a bucket-brigade style chorus that morphs into a flanger (CF-1), a deep three-dimensional twin chorus (TW-2), an analogue-style octave divider (OC-3), a mid-hump overdrive with a built-in chorus or voice filter (SD-4), a high-gain distortion (DS-5) and a modulated delay (MD-6). Each has its own colour, and its knobs change to match the pedal in the slot.

The ROUTING keys choose how the six slots are wired: one long chain, all six side by side, or two chains of three. A path display above the pedals draws the signal flow, and changing a pedal or the routing crossfades smoothly, so there are no clicks. MASTER sets the dry / rack blend and the output level, and DRIFT lets the delay clocks and LFOs of every pedal wander like real analogue circuits.

Reach for it for

  • Classic 80s clean chorus and echo on guitar and keys
  • Drive, distortion and octave fuzz sounds built pedal by pedal
  • Jet flanging and warped, pitch-bending echoes
  • Lush, wide parallel pads from several modulation pedals at once
  • Two complete rigs side by side, blended with one knob
  • Thickening a bass or synth with the octave and drive

Controls

The header

SERIES / PARALLEL / SPLIT
The ROUTING. See Routing below. SERIES by default.
PRESETS
Opens the full preset list.
◄ / ►
Step to the previous or next preset (wrapping round).
Preset display
Shows the preset number and name. Click it for the preset list too.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Signal path

The strip above the pedals shows how the slots are wired, from IN through the six slots to the MIX node and OUT. Each slot is drawn as a box with its number and pedal name, filled in its pedal's colour when it is on and as an outline when it is bypassed. The corner reads 1 > 6 (SERIES), 1 | 6 (PARALLEL) or A | B (SPLIT).

Routing

SERIES
One chain: 1 > 2 > 3 > 4 > 5 > 6, like a pedalboard. A bypassed slot passes the signal straight on.
PARALLEL
Every slot is fed from the input, and the pedals that are on are averaged at the output. With nothing on, the dry signal passes through. Good for layering several modulation pedals without them feeding each other.
SPLIT
Two chains side by side: slots 1 - 3 make chain A and slots 4 - 6 chain B, each in series. A / B in MASTER blends the two. Each pedal shows an A or B next to its slot number.

A pedal slot

All six slots work the same way. The knobs change with the pedal in the slot (see The six pedals below).

Slot number
1 to 6, in a black circle at the top left. In SPLIT it is followed by A or B.
LED
Lit red while the pedal is on. Click it to switch the pedal on or off, like the footswitch.
◄ / ►
Move the pedal one slot left or right. It swaps places with its neighbour, taking its knob settings and on / off state with it.
Knobs
Drag up or down (Shift for fine moves), or use the mouse wheel; double-click to reset to the pedal's default. While you touch a knob, its label shows the value.
Name plate
The pedal's name (CF-1 to MD-6) and what it is. Click it to choose a different pedal from the list; the new pedal starts at its own default settings. Drag it onto another slot to swap the two slots.
Footswitch
Switches the pedal on (ON) or off (BYPASS). A bypassed pedal dims a little. Switching crossfades over about 12 ms, and a pedal starts with empty delay lines each time it is switched on.

MASTER

A / B
SPLIT only (dimmed otherwise). Blends chain A and chain B, from A 100 (only A) through A = B (an equal mix, the default) to B 100 (only B).
MIX
The balance between the dry input and the rack, 0 - 100% (100% by default).
OUTPUT
Output level, -24 to +12 dB (0 dB by default).
TRIM
Input level before the rack, -24 to +12 dB.
GUARD
Click the TRIM knob (without turning it) to switch GUARD on or off; the small light at the knob's upper right shows it is on and glows brighter while it works. On by default. Gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the pedals. It never boosts quiet sources.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises. 0% by default.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper at -0.3 dBFS (no latency, the default), or a lookahead limiter that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). While the output clips, the CLIP key flashes red.
DRIFT
0 - 100% (25% by default). See Drift below.
IN / L / R meters
The input level and the left and right output levels.

Drift

DRIFT makes the clocks and LFOs of every slot wander slowly and independently, as on real analogue pedals. At 100% the LFO rates move by up to 25% either way and the bucket-brigade clocks (the delay times and the tone of the delay lines) by up to 3%, so two choruses never quite line up. At 0% everything is rock steady.

The six pedals

CF-1 CHORUS / FLANGER (lilac)
