A bucket-brigade style delay swept by a rounded triangle LFO, with the dark, slightly soft top of the old delay chips. MORPH glides between a lush stereo chorus and a metallic flanger.

MORPH
0 - 100% (0% by default). Fully left is a wide stereo chorus (3 - 12 ms delay, the two sides sweeping in opposite directions); fully right a flanger (0.4 - 4 ms delay, a sweep of up to almost two octaves, with full REGEN). In between, it blends smoothly from one to the other.
RATE
The LFO speed, 0.1 - 10 Hz (35% = 0.5 Hz by default).
DEPTH
How far the LFO sweeps, 0 - 100% (55% by default).
MANUAL
Where the delay sits, shown in ms: 3 - 12 ms for the chorus, 0.4 - 4 ms for the flanger (50% by default). For the flanger this sets the pitch of the jet; for the chorus, how thick it sounds.
REGEN
Feedback, 0 - 100% (0% by default). It reaches up to 92% feedback at full MORPH for the ringing, whistling flange, but only about a third of that in chorus mode. The wet level trims down as REGEN rises to keep the level even.

TW-2 TWIN CHORUS (light blue)

Two delay lines per side, swept by two overlapping LFOs. The right side runs in antiphase, with a little cross-cancelling between the sides, for a deep, three-dimensional spread.

SPEED
The first LFO's speed, 0.08 - 4.8 Hz (40% = about 0.4 Hz by default).
DEPTH
How far the two lines sweep, 0 - 100% (60% by default).
RANGE
The second LFO's speed as a multiple of the first, x 1 to x 3.5 (x 2 by default). Higher settings give a busier, more animated shimmer.
WIDTH
The stereo spread, 0 - 100% (100% by default). At 0 both sides move together; at 100 they move in opposite directions.
LEVEL
The chorus level, 0 - 100% (50% by default). The dry signal dips a little as you turn it up.

OC-3 OCTAVE (dark blue)

An analogue-style octave divider. It cleans up the input, follows the pitch you play, and divides it down by one and two octaves. It works on the centre of the stereo image and puts the sub voices in the middle. Play single notes for the cleanest tracking (it follows roughly 35 Hz to 1.4 kHz).

OCT 1
The level of the voice one octave down, 0 - 100% (50% by default).
OCT 2
The level of the voice two octaves down, a square wave that follows your playing level, 0 - 100% (0% by default).
DIRECT
The level of your dry signal, 0 - 100% (100% by default).
GRIT
0 - 100% (20% by default). Low settings give a smooth, round sub; turning it up swaps in a square wave at your playing level plus some rectifier grit, for a buzzier, fuzzier octave.
TONE
A low-pass on the sub voices, 150 Hz - 4 kHz (45% = about 650 Hz by default). Lower for a deep, soft sub; higher for more bite.

A gate stops the divider chattering on silence.

SD-4 SESSION DRIVE (yellow)

A soft-clipping overdrive with the classic mid-hump: only the mids above about 720 Hz are clipped, while the clean signal rides along underneath, so the lows stay tight and the notes stay clear. It has two built-in extras, picked with MODE.

DRIVE
The gain into the clipper, 0 - 100% (45% by default), up to about 42 dB. The top end is rounded off more as the drive goes up, and the output is partly level-compensated.
LEVEL
The output level, -21 to +15 dB (50% = -3 dB by default).
TONE
A low-pass, 600 Hz - 12 kHz (50% = about 2.7 kHz by default).
AMOUNT
How much of the MODE extra is added, 0 - 100% (0% by default, so the extra is off).
CHORUS / VOICE (MODE)
CHORUS (the default) adds a short stereo mini-chorus to the drive. VOICE adds a resonant mid-voice filter at 850 Hz, for a vocal, singing lead.

DS-5 DISTORTION (orange)

A treble-boosted booster into a high-gain stage with warm, lopsided hard clipping, followed by the vintage blend tone stack: a low-pass and a high-pass mixed by TONE, with a mid scoop at noon.

DIST
The amount of distortion, 0 - 100% (50% by default).
LEVEL
The output level, -26 to +10 dB (50% = -8 dB by default).
TONE
0 - 100% (50% by default). Left is dark and thick, right is bright and cutting; in the middle the mids are scooped.

MD-6 MOD DELAY (green)

A delay with a modulated delay time. With a slow RATE and small DEPTH the repeats are gently chorused and pitch-warped; with more they are flanged and seasick. The feedback loop is darkened and saturates softly, and changing TIME glides like a tape machine, bending the pitch of the repeats.

TIME
The delay time, 20 ms - 1 s (73% = about 350 ms by default).
FEEDBACK
How many repeats, 0 - 95% feedback (35% by default). The repeats get darker and softer each time round.
RATE
The modulation speed, 0.05 - 8 Hz (50% = about 0.6 Hz by default).
DEPTH
The modulation depth, 0 - 9 ms (30% = about 0.8 ms by default). The two sides are modulated a quarter-cycle apart for a wide image.
LEVEL
The echo level, 0 - 100% (35% by default). The dry signal always stays at full level.

Presets

Every preset starts from Init and then changes a few pedals. Presets set the routing, A / B, MIX, DRIFT and all six slots; OUTPUT and the IN / OUT section are left as they are.

Init
The classic order in series - OC-3, SD-4, DS-5, CF-1, TW-2, MD-6 - every pedal at its defaults, with only the CF-1 chorus on.
80s Clean Chorus
A slow, deep CF-1 chorus into a short MD-6 slapback of about 200 ms, with a little more drift.
Crunch + Twin Chorus
SD-4 crunch into a wide TW-2 twin chorus and a quiet MD-6 echo.
Octave Fuzz
OC-3 with both octaves and plenty of GRIT into a hot DS-5 distortion.
Session Lead
SD-4 driven harder in VOICE mode for a singing mid-range, into a 375 ms MD-6 echo.
Shimmer Pad (parallel)
PARALLEL routing: a deep CF-1 chorus, a lush TW-2 and a long, modulated MD-6 side by side, with extra drift.
Dual Rig Split
SPLIT routing. Chain A: SD-4 with its mini-chorus, CF-1 chorus and a short echo. Chain B: DS-5, TW-2 and a longer echo.
Warped Echo
CF-1 a little past halfway to a flanger, into an MD-6 with deep modulation and plenty of feedback, with drift at 60%.
Jet Flanger
DS-5 distortion into the CF-1 at full flanger, with a slow sweep and high REGEN.
Sub Bass Synth
OC-3 with both octaves, high GRIT and a dark TONE, into a light SD-4 drive and a gentle chorus: a synth-like bass.

Tips

  • Drive pedals before modulation and delay is the classic order; try a chorus or the octave before a drive for a wilder sound.
  • OC-3 tracks best on clean single notes, so put it first in the chain, before any drive or chorus.
  • For a doubling effect in SPLIT, put the same pedal in both chains with slightly different settings and set A / B to A = B.
  • In PARALLEL the pedals are averaged, so each one sounds a little quieter than on its own; a bypassed pedal drops out of the average.
  • Turn MD-6 TIME slowly while it is repeating for tape-style pitch bends.
  • Set DRIFT to 0 for repeatable sweeps; a little more than the default makes two choruses in the rack sound less locked together.

File: gfx-ten-rack.jsfx

Amps & drive

TS-7

A 1U analogue tape simulator and compressor: input transformer, high-frequency tape tamer and three compressor modes.

Overview

TS-7 is a rack unit in the spirit of the classic multi-stage tape and transformer boxes. The signal runs through three stages in turn: IRON, an input transformer that thickens and saturates; WARMTH, which tames the top end the way tape compresses its own high frequencies; and COMP, a compressor with three characters.

As on the hardware, the thresholds are fixed: INPUT decides how hard you hit everything, from a little warmth to a full slam. The compressor's make-up gain is automatic, and OUTPUT sets the final level.

Everything non-linear runs oversampled (2x or 4x) to keep the distortion clean of aliasing. The small latency this adds is reported to REAPER, so your tracks stay in time.

Reach for it for

  • Gluing a drum or mix bus with tape-style warmth
  • Slamming drums for energy and pump (SLAM)
  • Thickening bass with transformer saturation
  • Taming harsh cymbals and vocals without an EQ (WARMTH)
  • Parallel smash compression with MIX

Controls

Main panel

INPUT
The level into the unit, -12 to +24 dB, in 0.5 dB steps. Because the thresholds are fixed, this is the main control: more INPUT means more iron saturation, more warmth and more compression.
COMP
The white key steps through the compressor modes; the lights show the one in use (all dark = off). TX: gentle 2:1 levelling, slow, into a saturating output transformer. BUS: smooth 3:1 programme compression that lets the transients through. SLAM: a feedback compressor with ultra-fast attack and release that slams transients and pumps.
DRIVE meter
The level after INPUT, from -20 to +6 dBFS. Green is comfortable, yellow at 0, red above.
WARMTH meter
How much top end WARMTH is taking, 1 - 10 dB.
COMP meter
The compressor's gain reduction, 1 - 20 dB.
OUTPUT meter
The final output level, -24 to -1 dBFS.
WARMTH
The orange key steps the high-frequency tape tamer through OFF and 1 - 6; the display shows the setting. A side-chain watches the top (above about 3.5 kHz) and, when transients push past the threshold, pulls the top down fast and lets it back slowly. Each step lowers the threshold by 4 dB, so it tames more.
IRON
The input transformer, on by default (the amber light). The low band (below about 120 Hz) saturates first, then the whole signal meets a gentle asymmetric soft clip whose even harmonics grow with level. Off = a clean input stage.
BYPASS
Passes the dry signal through (the red light), delayed to match the oversampling so nothing jumps in time. The switch fades over a few milliseconds.
OUTPUT
The final level, -24 to +12 dB, in 0.5 dB steps.

Drag a knob up or down (Shift for fine), use the mouse wheel, or double-click to reset it.

The bottom strip

PRESET
The ◄ / ► keys step through the eight presets; click the display for the list. A preset sets INPUT, OUTPUT, WARMTH, COMP, IRON and MIX, and switches BYPASS off.
OS 2X / OS 4X
The oversampling: the key toggles between 2x and 4x (the default). 4x is cleaner on hard settings and uses a little more CPU and latency.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper at -0.3 dBFS (no latency), or a lookahead limiter holding the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). CLIP is on by default and flashes red when it clips.
MIX
Parallel compression, 0 - 100%: blends the uncompressed signal (after IRON and WARMTH) under the compressed one. Only works with a COMP mode on.
DRIFT
The iron's power supply is not quite steady: its drive and asymmetry wander slowly and unevenly, 0 - 100% (20% by default). The level stays put; only how hard the transformer saturates moves. Very subtle.

Panel keys

S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.
HW
Switches between the black anodised rack panel and a soft, light-grey flat panel with the same layout.

Presets

Init
Unity INPUT and OUTPUT, IRON on, WARMTH and COMP off.
Tape Glue Bus
+3 dB in, WARMTH 2, BUS compression.
Drum Slam
+6 dB in, WARMTH 3, SLAM, output pulled down 4 dB.
Vocal Tx
+4 dB in, WARMTH 2, TX levelling.
Warm Master
+2 dB in, WARMTH 1, BUS compression at 60% mix.
Bass Iron
+8 dB into the iron with gentle TX compression, no WARMTH.
Parallel Smash
+8 dB in, WARMTH 2, SLAM blended in at 35%.
Lo-Fi Tape
+12 dB in, WARMTH 6 and SLAM: heavily squashed and dark.

Tips

  • Set INPUT by watching the DRIVE meter: around 0 is a healthy hit; pushing into the red brings out the iron and the compressor.
  • Turn OUTPUT down as you turn INPUT up to keep the level matched, then compare with BYPASS.
  • WARMTH is a quick fix for bright cymbals or sibilant vocals: step it up until the WARMTH meter flickers a few dB on the harsh moments.
  • SLAM on a drum room or bus with MIX at 30 - 50% adds energy without losing the attack.

File: gfx-ts7.jsfx

Dynamics

Compressor

One compressor, four voicings: punchy VCA, smooth bus glue, transparent ride and optical.

Overview

Compressor is a single compressor with four characters, chosen with the VOICING buttons. VCA is a punchy compressor that measures loudness (RMS), with a ratio that goes past infinity into negative ratios and a touch of even harmonics under heavy reduction. BUS is a stereo-linked glue compressor that reads the peaks and stays smooth and clean. RIDE is a transparent 'easy ride' leveller with a wide knee and timing that adapts to the music. OPTO behaves like an optical cell: a soft attack and a two-stage release that lets go quickly at first, then slowly.

With AUTO on, attack and release follow the programme, and the release lengthens when the compressor keeps working, so sustained passages don't pump. The SIDE-CHAIN section either stops the lows from driving the compressor (SC HPF) or compresses only the lows (SPLIT), and MIX blends in the dry signal for parallel compression. The transfer curve shows exactly what the compressor is doing to the level as you play.

Reach for it for

  • Vocals that need levelling (RIDE, OPTO)
  • Drums and drum buses (VCA)
  • Gluing a mix or group bus (BUS)
  • Tightening only the low end of a bass or mix (SPLIT)
  • Parallel crush and pumping effects with negative ratios (VCA, MIX)

Controls

The graph section

SIGNAL
Lights green when audio is arriving.
INPUT
The input level (average) in dB.
Transfer curve
The input-to-output curve from -60 to 0 dB, including the knee. The small black triangle under it marks the THRESHOLD. The red square rides on the curve with the live level, and the red arrow at the right shows the output level after MAKEUP.
Gain reduction meter
24 lights lit from the right, showing how many dB the compressor is taking off (0 to -24).

COMPRESSOR

RATIO
How strongly the compressor works once the level is over the threshold. 1:1 does nothing; 2:1 and 4:1 are moderate; INF:1 is limiting. In the VCA voicing the ratio goes on past INF into negative ratios (down to -1:1): the louder the input, the quieter the output, for pumping and backwards effects. The other voicings stop at INF:1.
KNEE
The soft knee, 0 to 18 dB (6 dB by default). 0 is a hard knee; larger values ease the ratio in gradually around the threshold. RIDE always uses at least 10 dB and OPTO at least 12 dB.
MAKEUP
Make-up gain, 0 to +24 dB, to bring the level back up after compression.
THRESHOLD
The level where the compressor starts working, -50 to 0 dB (-18 dB by default). Turn it down for more compression.
MIX
Parallel compression: blends the dry signal back in. 100% is fully compressed; lower settings keep the dry transients under the compressed sound.

Timing and voicing

ATTACK
How fast the compressor reacts, 0.1 to 100 ms (10 ms by default). The display reads AUTO when AUTO is on, and OPTO in the OPTO voicing, where the knob has no effect.
RELEASE
How fast it recovers, 20 to 2000 ms (150 ms by default). Reads AUTO or OPTO in the same way.
AUTO
On by default. Attack and release follow the programme instead of the knobs: the release gets longer the more the compressor keeps working, so sustained passages don't pump. Always lit in the OPTO voicing, which has its own fixed timing.
VCA / BUS / RIDE / OPTO
The voicing. VCA: punchy, measures loudness (RMS), allows negative ratios and adds a touch of even harmonics under heavy reduction. BUS: stereo-linked, reads the peaks, smooth and low colour. RIDE: transparent leveller with a wide knee and adaptive timing. OPTO: optical cell with a soft attack and a two-stage release (quick, then slow).
LINK
On by default: both sides get the same gain, so the stereo image stays still. Off: each side is compressed on its own. Always lit in the BUS voicing, which is always linked.

SIDE-CHAIN

SC HPF
The compressor ignores the lows below the frequency when deciding how much to compress (a 12 dB per octave high-pass on what it listens to). The whole signal is still compressed. Use it so the bass doesn't make everything pump. This is the default.
SPLIT
Only the lows below the frequency are compressed; everything above passes untouched. The split is a 24 dB per octave crossover that sums back flat when nothing is being compressed. Use it to tighten the low end of a bass or a mix on its own.
Frequency
The side-chain frequency, from OFF (fully left, 20 Hz) to 2 kHz (80 Hz by default). The labels beside it remind you what the mode does: DETECTOR HIGH-PASS or LOWS ONLY COMPRESSED.

IN / OUT

TRIM
Input level before anything else, from -24 to +12 dB.
GUARD
Click the TRIM knob (without moving it) to switch the guard; its light sits at the knob's upper right and is lit while it is on. On by default. Gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources. The light glows brighter while it is catching peaks.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper (no latency), or a lookahead limiter that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). CLIP is on by default; its key flashes red while it is clipping.

Drift

A slow random wander: the threshold and the timing breathe slightly, like an analogue unit.

DRIFT
How much drift, 0 to 100%. 50% (the default) is the standard amount, 100% is twice that, 0 switches it off. More drift also makes the wander a little faster.

Output

Meter
Output level, left and right.
LEVEL
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.

The preset row and colour strip

PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets (a click on the display does the same). Choosing one sets the compressor for that job; drift, output level and the IN / OUT section are left as they are. The last entry resets everything to the defaults.
◄ / ►
Step to the previous or next preset.
Colour swatch
Changes the faceplate colour: GREEN, BLUE or one of the GFX themes.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Next to the S / M / L size keys. Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.
HW / FLAT
Switches between the hardware-style panel and the flat look.

Presets

Vocal Ride
RIDE at 3:1 with a wide knee and AUTO timing, for even, natural vocals.
Drum Smash
VCA at about 6:1, fast 3 ms attack and 80 ms release, a tight 2 dB knee: punchy, aggressive drums.
Bass Glue
VCA at 4:1 with AUTO timing; SPLIT at 120 Hz so only the lows are compressed.
Bus Glue
BUS at 2:1 with a 30 ms attack and 300 ms release: classic gentle bus glue.
Negative Pump
VCA past INF at -2:1 for rhythmic pumping.
Gentle Level
RIDE at 2:1 with a very wide 15 dB knee and AUTO timing: barely-there levelling.
Parallel Crush
VCA crushing hard (about 10:1, 1 ms attack, hard knee), blended in at 40%.
Mastering Touch
BUS at a gentle 1.5:1 with AUTO timing, just 1 dB of make-up.
Opto Vocal
OPTO at 4:1: soft, smooth vocal levelling.
Opto Lows Only
OPTO at 5:1 with SPLIT at 160 Hz: smooth control of the low end only.
Reset all to defaults
Every control back to its starting value.

Tips

  • Watch the transfer curve: if the red square sits far up the flat part of the curve, you are compressing more than you probably need.
  • If the low end makes the whole mix duck, use SC HPF at 80-120 Hz.
  • SPLIT is a quick way to steady a boomy bass or kick without dulling the top.
  • Negative ratios sound best on sustained material and with MIX below 100%.
  • Try the same settings in each voicing: VCA for punch, BUS for glue, RIDE and OPTO for smooth, transparent levelling.

File: gfx-compressor.jsfx

Dynamics

Compressor Q

One compressor with four voicings, plus a four-band EQ that can go before or after it.

Overview

Compressor Q is a single compressor that can take on four characters. VCA is a punchy loudness-sensing (RMS) compressor whose ratio goes past infinity into negative ratios. BUS is a smooth, peak-sensing glue compressor that always works on both sides together. RIDE is a transparent leveller with a wide knee. OPTO behaves like an optical cell: a soft attack and a release that lets go quickly at first, then slowly.

The SIDE-CHAIN section either stops the lows from driving the compressor (SC HPF) or compresses only the lows (SPLIT). MIX blends in the dry signal for parallel compression.

Below the compressor sits a four-band EQ with low and high cut filters. EQ FIRST puts it before the compressor, EQ AFTER after it; the unit the signal meets first is shown on top.

Reach for it for

  • Vocals that need levelling and tone in one place (RIDE or OPTO)
  • Drum smashing and parallel compression (VCA with MIX)
  • Gentle glue on a mix or group bus (BUS)
  • Controlling only the low end of a bass or a mix (SPLIT)
  • Pumping and reverse-dynamics effects (VCA with negative ratios)

Controls

Drag a knob up or down to turn it (hold Shift for fine moves), use the mouse wheel, or double-click to reset it. The value shows under the knob.

The preset row

PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets (clicking the display does the same). Choosing one sets the compressor and side-chain controls; DRIFT, LEVEL, the EQ and the IN / OUT controls are left as they are. The last entry, Reset all to defaults, resets the compressor, DRIFT, LEVEL and IN / OUT.
Left / right arrows
Step to the previous or next preset.
Display
Shows the preset number and name.

The graph

SIGNAL
Lights when there is signal at the input.
INPUT
The input level in dB.
Transfer curve
Shows how input level (along the bottom, -60 to 0 dB) becomes output level for the current threshold, ratio and knee. The small triangle under the graph marks the threshold; the red square rides on the curve with the live level, and the arrow on the right shows the output level after MAKEUP.
Gain reduction meter
Lights from the right, 0 to -24 dB.
Colour swatch
Picks the faceplate colour: GREEN, BLUE or one of the GFX themes.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.
HW / FLAT
Switches between the hardware-style faceplate and the flat look.

COMPRESSOR

THRESHOLD
The level where compression starts, -50 to 0 dB.
RATIO
How strongly the signal is compressed once it is over the threshold: from 1:1 up to INF:1 (limiting). In VCA only, the ratio carries on past INF into negative ratios, down to -1:1: the louder the input, the quieter the output - for pumping and swelling effects. The other voicings stop at INF:1.
KNEE
How gradually the compression eases in around the threshold, 0 - 18 dB (6 dB by default). RIDE always uses at least 10 dB and OPTO at least 12 dB.
MAKEUP
Gain after compression, 0 to +24 dB.
MIX
Parallel compression: 100% is fully compressed, lower settings blend the dry signal back in.

Timing and voicing

ATTACK
How fast the compressor reacts, 0.1 - 100 ms. Shows AUTO when AUTO is on and OPTO in the OPTO voicing.
RELEASE
How fast it lets go, 20 - 2000 ms. Shows AUTO or OPTO in the same way.
AUTO
Programme-dependent timing (on by default): the attack is set by the voicing and the release lengthens the longer the compressor keeps working, so sustained passages do not pump. Always on in OPTO.
VCA
The punchy loudness-sensing (RMS) compressor. It adds a touch of even harmonics under heavy reduction, and is the only voicing with negative ratios.
BUS
Peak-sensing, smooth and low in colour, for glue on groups and mixes. Always linked.
RIDE
A transparent 'easy ride' leveller with a wide knee and adaptive timing.
OPTO
An optical cell: a soft attack and a two-stage release that lets go quickly, then slowly. ATTACK and RELEASE are not used.
LINK
On (default): both sides get the same gain, so the stereo image stays still. Off: each side is compressed on its own. Always on in BUS.

SIDE-CHAIN

SC HPF
The compressor ignores the lows below the frequency (a 12 dB/oct high-pass on what it listens to), but the whole signal is compressed. Use it so the bass does not make everything pump. The label reads DETECTOR HIGH-PASS.
SPLIT
Only the lows below the frequency are compressed; everything above passes untouched. The split is a 24 dB/oct crossover that sums back flat when nothing is being compressed. The label reads LOWS ONLY COMPRESSED.
Frequency knob
Where the high-pass or split sits, 20 - 2000 Hz (80 Hz by default). Fully left is OFF.

IN / OUT

TRIM
Input level before anything else, from -24 to +12 dB. Double-click to reset it.
GUARD (click TRIM)
A single click on the TRIM knob switches GUARD on or off; the small light beside the knob shows it (dark = off, red = on, bright = working). GUARD is on by default. It gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out, 0 - 100%: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (no latency), or a lookahead limiter that holds them at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). The CLIP key flashes red while the clipper is working.

The right-hand column

DRIFT
A slow random wander, 0 - 100% (50% is the standard amount, 100% twice that, 0% none). The threshold breathes very slightly, the attack and release times move a little, and the EQ bands' frequencies and gains wander very slightly. The wander also gets a little faster as you turn it up.
Meter
Output level, left and right.
LEVEL
The output level, -24 to +12 dB.

EQ

Four bands in a row, each with a coloured gain knob and its FREQ knob beside it, between a low cut and a high cut. The bell bands get slightly narrower as you boost or cut further.

LO CUT
A 12 dB/oct high-pass filter, 20 - 1000 Hz. Fully left is OFF.
Gain knobs
Every band's gain runs from -16 to +16 dB. Below -12 dB the cut gets much steeper, reaching -40 dB at -16 - a deep notch on a bell band.
LOW / FREQ
Gain -16 to +16 dB, frequency 20 - 500 Hz.
LO MID / FREQ
A bell: gain -16 to +16 dB, frequency 80 Hz - 2 kHz.
HI MID / FREQ
A bell: gain -16 to +16 dB, frequency 400 Hz - 8 kHz.
HIGH / FREQ
Gain -16 to +16 dB, frequency 1.5 - 20 kHz.
HI CUT
A 12 dB/oct low-pass filter, 2 - 20 kHz. Fully right is OFF.
SHELF: LOW / HIGH
Turn the LOW and HIGH bands into shelves (lit, the default) or bells (off).
EQ FIRST / EQ AFTER
Puts the EQ before the compressor (it changes what the compressor hears and reacts to) or after it (it shapes the compressed sound). The panel swaps the two units so the first one is on top. The key's light shows while it is held.

Presets

Vocal Ride
RIDE at 3:1 with auto timing, a 12 dB knee, 3 dB make-up and a 100 Hz SC HPF.
Drum Smash
VCA at about 6:1, a 3 ms attack and 80 ms release, a near-hard knee and 6 dB make-up.
Bass Glue
VCA at 4:1 with auto timing, compressing only the lows below 120 Hz (SPLIT).
Bus Glue
BUS at 2:1, 30 ms attack, 300 ms release, 2 dB make-up and a 90 Hz SC HPF.
Negative Pump
VCA past INF at -2:1, for pumping and reverse-dynamics effects.
Gentle Level
RIDE at 2:1 with a very wide 15 dB knee and a slow auto release.
Parallel Crush
VCA at 10:1 with a hard knee and very fast timing, 10 dB make-up, blended in at 40%.
Mastering Touch
BUS at 1.5:1 with auto timing and a 9 dB knee, just 1 dB of make-up.
Opto Vocal
OPTO at 4:1 with a 12 dB knee, 4 dB make-up and an 80 Hz SC HPF.
Opto Lows Only
OPTO at 5:1 compressing only the lows below 160 Hz (SPLIT).
Reset all to defaults
The compressor, side-chain, DRIFT, LEVEL and IN / OUT back to their starting values. The EQ is left as it is.

Tips

  • Watch the red square on the transfer curve: it shows exactly how far over the threshold the music sits.
  • EQ FIRST with a LO CUT or a low cut on LOW makes the compressor react less to the bass; EQ AFTER keeps the compression the same and only changes the tone.
  • For parallel compression, set heavy compression first, then bring MIX down until the transients come back.
  • SPLIT on a mix bus at 100 - 150 Hz steadies the low end without touching the vocal or the cymbals.

File: gfx-compressor-q.jsfx

Dynamics

Console

Gate, compressor, limiter and output transformer from the centre section of an 80s British broadcast console.

Overview

Console recreates the dynamics section of an 80s British broadcast desk, in the spirit of the classic desk VCA compressor and its companion compressor / gate module. It is fast, clean and transparent: it holds the level without dulling the transients, and the console's output transformer adds a little weight.

The signal runs through four parts in order: the gate / expander, the compressor, a fast peak limiter and the output transformer. MIX blends the dry signal back in for parallel compression.

Reach for it for

  • Tight, controlled drums - with the gate cleaning up spill
  • Spoken voice and presenters that need to stay steady and present
  • Bass DI and acoustic instruments that want even level without losing attack
  • Parallel squash on a drum or mix bus
  • A touch of transformer weight on its own (Transformer Only)

Controls

Gate / Expander

THRESH
The level below which the gate or expander starts turning the signal down, -80 to 0 dB. Fully left is OFF.
RANGE
How far it turns the signal down, 0 - 60 dB. Small settings just lower the background; 40 dB or more shuts it right off.
RELEASE
How fast it closes once the signal falls below the threshold, 20 - 2000 ms. It opens very fast (in about half a millisecond), so the attack of a hit is kept.
IN
Switches the gate / expander in. Off by default.
GATE
Shuts by the full RANGE as soon as the signal falls below the threshold. Tight and obvious - for drums and spill.
EXP
A gentle 1:2 expander: it only takes down what falls below the threshold, and the further below, the more it takes down (up to RANGE). Smoother and more natural - for noise, room and breaths. This is the default.
OPEN
Lights while the gate is letting the signal through.
GATE readout
How far the gate is turning the signal down right now, in dB (GATE OUT when the section is off).

Compressor

A fast VCA compressor with a 3 dB soft knee and a quick RMS detector. Its detector hears the gated signal, so a closed gate does not make it work.

THRESH
The level where the compressor starts working, -40 to 0 dB. Turn it down for more compression.
RATIO
How strongly it compresses once over the threshold, 1:1 to INF:1 (at the top of the knob: limiting).
ATTACK
How fast it clamps down, 0.1 - 30 ms. Short settings catch the transients; longer ones let them through for more punch.
RELEASE
How fast it lets go, 30 - 3000 ms.
GAIN
Make-up gain, -10 to +20 dB. Only applies while the compressor is in.
IN
Switches the compressor in or out.
GR meter
The row of 12 lights shows the gain reduction of the compressor and limiter together, from 1 to 30 dB.

Limit / Output

LIMIT
A fast peak limiter after the compressor, -20 to 0 dB: peaks above this level are held at it. Fully right is OFF. The red light beside the knob shows it limiting.
XFMR
The console's output transformer. On (default): the lows thicken and saturate gently as the level rises, and the extreme top softens. Off: a straight wire.
LINK
On (default): both sides share one gain in the gate, compressor and limiter, so the stereo image stays still. Off: the two sides work as two mono channels.
MIX
Parallel compression: blends the dry signal back in. 100% (WET 100) is fully processed; lower settings keep the dry transients under the processed sound.
IN / OUT meters
Input and output level.
GR readout
The total gain reduction of the compressor and limiter, in dB.

The header

PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets; the ◄ / ► keys step through them and the display shows the one in use. Choosing one sets the gate, compressor, limiter, XFMR, LINK and MIX. The last entry resets everything to the defaults.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Input guard

Console has no input or output fold-out: its own transformer and limiter do that job. An input guard runs in front of everything and is on by default: it gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources. It has no key on the panel; it appears as Input guard in REAPER's parameter list.

Presets

Desk Glue
Gentle 2.5:1 compression with a little make-up gain; gate off.
Presenter Voice
A soft expander under a firm 4:1 compressor with fast attack and the limiter at -2 dB - steady, upfront speech.
Tight Drums (gated)
A hard gate with 40 dB range and a fast 5:1 compressor, limiter at -1 dB.
Kick / Snare Gate
A deep, fast gate (60 dB range, 80 ms release), 4:1 compression and LINK off for two independent channels.
Parallel Squash
Heavy 8:1 compression with very fast timing and the limiter at -3 dB, blended in at 45%.
Transmitter Limit
INF:1 with the fastest attack and release - a broadcast-style brickwall at -14 dB, limiter at -1 dB.
Acoustic Clean-up
A gentle expander with a slow release under 3:1 compression with a 10 ms attack.
Bass DI
4:1 compression with an 8 ms attack to keep the pluck; gate off.
Transformer Only
Gate and compressor out: just the output transformer's colour.
Gentle Programme Bus
A light expander and slow, gentle 2:1 compression with the limiter at -1 dB.
Reset all to defaults
Every control back to its starting value.

Tips

  • Set the gate first with the compressor out, then bring the compressor in: the compressor listens after the gate, so the two settings work together.
  • If the gate chatters on decaying notes, try EXP instead of GATE, or lengthen the RELEASE.
  • For more punch, raise the compressor's ATTACK to 10 - 30 ms so the front of each hit gets through.
  • Use LIMIT as a safety net a few dB below your peaks after a heavy compressor setting.
  • On a stereo bus keep LINK on; switch it off only for dual-mono sources such as two separate drum mics.

File: gfx-console.jsfx

Dynamics

Console Q

Broadcast-desk dynamics - gate / expander, compressor, limiter and transformer - with a four-band channel EQ.

Overview

Console Q is the centre-section dynamics of an 80s British broadcast console, in the spirit of the classic desk VCA compressor and its companion compressor / gate module. It is fast, clean and transparent, with the console's output transformer for a little weight. The signal goes gate / expander, then compressor, then limiter, then transformer.

Below the dynamics sits a four-band console channel EQ: high- and low-pass filters, LF and HF bands (shelf or bell), and two fully parametric mid bands, with two voicings, BROWN and BLACK.

ORDER puts the EQ after the dynamics or in front of them. The unit the signal meets first is shown on top.

Reach for it for

  • Vocals and voice-over that need to sit steady and clear
  • Tight, gated drums (kick, snare, toms)
  • Cleaning up noise and spill between phrases (EXP)
  • A fast, transparent bus compressor with a touch of transformer weight
  • Channel EQ and dynamics in one place, in either order

Controls

Gate / Expander

THRESH
The level below which the gate or expander works, -80 to 0 dB. Fully left = OFF.
RANGE
How far it closes, 0 - 60 dB. Small settings just lower the noise between phrases; 60 dB is fully shut.
RELEASE
How quickly it closes once the signal falls below the threshold, 20 - 2000 ms. It opens in about half a millisecond.
IN
Switches the gate / expander in or out (out by default).
GATE / EXP
GATE: shuts by the full RANGE as soon as the level drops below the threshold. EXP (default): a gentle 1:2 expander that only takes down what falls below - the further below, the quieter, up to RANGE.
OPEN
Lights while the gate is letting the signal through.
GATE readout
How many dB the gate is taking off, or GATE OUT when it is switched out.

Compressor

THRESH
Where the compressor starts working, -40 to 0 dB. It has a 3 dB soft knee and a quick RMS detector, so it holds the level without dulling the transients.
RATIO
1:1 to INF:1 (fully right).
ATTACK
0.1 - 30 ms.
RELEASE
30 - 3000 ms.
GAIN
Make-up gain, -10 to +20 dB.
IN
Switches the compressor in or out (in by default). GAIN only applies while it is in.
GR meter
The row of 12 lights shows the gain reduction of the compressor and limiter together, from 1 to 30 dB.

Limit / Output

LIMIT
A fast peak limiter after the compressor, -20 to 0 dB: the level the peaks are held at. Fully right = OFF. The red light beside it shows it limiting.
XFMR
The output transformer, on by default: the lows thicken and saturate gently as the level rises, and the extreme top softens. Off = straight wire.
LINK
On (default): both sides share one gain for the gate, compressor and limiter, so the stereo image stays put. Off: two independent mono channels.
MIX
Parallel compression, 0 - 100% wet: blends the dry signal back in. The dry signal is taken after the EQ when the EQ comes first.
IN / OUT meters
LED ladders for the input and output level; click one to clear the peak hold.
GR readout
The total gain reduction in dB.

EQ

The four-band channel EQ strip.

IN
Switches the four EQ bands in or out (in by default). The HPF and LPF keep working either way.
BROWN / BLACK
The voicing. BROWN: broad, gentle curves whose width stays the same at any gain. BLACK (default): the bandwidth narrows as you boost or cut further, for a more focused sound, and the shelves get a small opposite dip just inside their corner.
HPF
High-pass filter, 18 dB/oct, 20 - 400 Hz. Fully left = OFF.
LPF
Low-pass filter, 12 dB/oct, 3 - 22 kHz. Fully right = OFF.
LF / FREQ
Low band: -15 to +15 dB at 30 - 450 Hz.
BELL (LF)
Off: a low shelf. On: a bell curve at FREQ.
LMF / FREQ / Q
Low-mid band: -15 to +15 dB, 200 Hz - 2.5 kHz, Q 0.4 - 3 (higher is narrower).
HMF / FREQ / Q
High-mid band: -15 to +15 dB, 600 Hz - 7 kHz, Q 0.4 - 3.
HF / FREQ
High band: -15 to +15 dB at 1.5 - 16 kHz.
BELL (HF)
Off: a high shelf. On: a bell curve at FREQ.

The header

ORDER
DYNAMICS > EQ (default) or EQ > DYNAMICS: click to swap. The panel redraws with the unit the signal meets first on top.
PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets; the ◄ / ► keys step through them and the display shows the one in use (click it for the list too). Choosing one sets the gate, compressor, LIMIT, XFMR, LINK and MIX; the EQ is left as it is. The last entry resets the dynamics to their defaults.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Input guard

There is no input or output fold-out on Console Q: its own character does that job. An input guard runs in front of everything and is on by default: it gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources. It has no key on the panel; it appears as Input guard in REAPER's parameter list.

In REAPER's parameter list only

EQ: Line amp drive
Drives the EQ's line amplifier into gentle, slightly asymmetric saturation, 0 - 100% (0 by default).
EQ: Output
The EQ's output level, -12 to +12 dB.

Presets

Desk Glue
Gentle 2.5:1 compression with linked sides for a bus; gate out.
Presenter Voice
Expander for the room noise, 4:1 compression with 6 dB of make-up and a limit at -2 dB.
Tight Drums (gated)
Gate closing by 40 dB, fast 5:1 compression, a limit at -1 dB.
Kick / Snare Gate
A hard, fast gate closing fully, fast 4:1 compression, sides unlinked.
Parallel Squash
Heavy 8:1 compression with a fast attack, blended in at 45%.
Transmitter Limit
INF:1 with the fastest attack at -14 dB, then a limit at -1 dB.
Acoustic Clean-up
A gentle expander under 3:1 compression with slower timing.
Bass DI
Steady 4:1 compression with an 8 ms attack.
Transformer Only
Compressor and gate out: just the output transformer.
Gentle Programme Bus
A light expander and slow 2:1 compression, with a limit at -1 dB.
Reset all to defaults
The dynamics and the input guard back to their starting values; the EQ is left as it is.

Tips

  • EXP is far more forgiving than GATE on vocals and acoustic instruments: set RANGE to 10 - 15 dB to lower the noise without chopping the tails.
  • EQ > DYNAMICS lets an HPF stop the compressor reacting to rumble; DYNAMICS > EQ keeps your EQ boosts from changing how hard it compresses.
  • Switch LINK off on drum overheads or a wide stereo source if you want each side to breathe on its own.
  • Try BLACK for surgical mid cuts and BROWN for broad, musical tone shaping.

File: gfx-console-q.jsfx

Dynamics

De-Ess

A split-band or wideband de-esser that turns sibilance down only when it gets too loud.

Overview

De-Ess listens only to the esses - a band around FREQ, or everything above it - and when that band goes over the threshold, it turns the sibilance down, by no more than RANGE. It reacts very fast (about 0.3 ms), so even short, sharp esses are caught.

In SPLIT mode only the esses band is turned down and the rest of the voice is untouched, which is the most transparent way to work. In WIDE mode the whole signal ducks on each ess, like the classic hardware boxes. One detector serves both channels, so the stereo image never shifts.

Reach for it for

  • Sibilant lead and backing vocals
  • Spoken word, voice-over and podcasts
  • Fret squeak and harsh pick noise on acoustic guitar
  • Splashy hi-hats and cymbals in a drum overhead (HIGH shape)
  • Taming a bright vocal after heavy compression or EQ

Controls

Mode and shape

SPLIT
Only the esses band is turned down; the rest of the voice is untouched. Transparent - the default.
WIDE
The whole signal ducks on each ess, like the classic hardware boxes. Can sound more natural on some voices, but is easier to hear working.
BAND
The detector listens to a band around FREQ, and in SPLIT mode a dip around FREQ is what turns down. Best for a voice whose esses sit in one place.
HIGH
The detector listens to everything above FREQ, and in SPLIT mode a high shelf dips everything above FREQ. Best for broad, airy sibilance and cymbals.
LISTEN
Hear what the detector hears - just the esses band. Use it to tune FREQ onto the esses, then switch it off.

Knobs

Each knob's value shows while you hover over it or drag it; while dragging, the display in the top strip also shows the control's name and value.

FREQ
Where the esses are, 2 - 12 kHz (starts at 6.5 kHz). Lower for male voices, higher for female voices and cymbals.
THRESHOLD
The level of the esses band where De-Ess starts working, -50 to 0 dB. Turn it down for more de-essing. Above the threshold it works at about 5:1, with a soft 6 dB knee.
RANGE
The most it will ever turn the esses down, 0 - 24 dB. Keeps it from lisping on loud esses.
RELEASE
How quickly the esses band comes back after each ess, 20 - 300 ms.
OUTPUT
Output level, -12 to +12 dB.

Lamp and meters

ESS
The teal lamp lights while De-Ess is turning the esses down (by more than half a dB).
ESS LV
The level of the esses band (bottom -60 dB, top 0 dB). The red line marks THRESHOLD: when the lights pass it, De-Ess is working.
GR
How much the esses are being turned down, lit from the top, up to 24 dB.

The top strip

Preset display
Shows the preset number and name. Click it to open the preset list.
< and >
Step to the previous or next preset.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper at -0.3 dBFS (no latency), or a lookahead limiter that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). CLIP is on by default; its key flashes red while it is clipping.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. A new De-Ess opens at L. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

De-Ess has no input stage: there is no TRIM, GUARD or SAT.

Presets

A preset sets the mode, shape, FREQ, THRESHOLD, RANGE and RELEASE, and switches LISTEN off. OUTPUT and the OUT stage are left as they are.

Init
The starting point: SPLIT, BAND at 6.5 kHz, -26 dB threshold, 10 dB range, 60 ms release.
Female Vocal
SPLIT, BAND at 7.5 kHz with a quicker 50 ms release.
Male Vocal
SPLIT, BAND at 5.5 kHz, slightly gentler, 70 ms release.
Gentle Air
SPLIT, HIGH above 9 kHz with only 6 dB of range - softens the top without dulling it.
Hard Esses
WIDE, BAND at 6.5 kHz with a low threshold, 16 dB of range and a fast release, for very sharp sibilance.
Acoustic Guitar
SPLIT, BAND at 4.5 kHz, 6 dB range and a slow release, for string and pick noise.
Hats and Cymbals
SPLIT, HIGH above 10 kHz, 8 dB range.
Podcast Voice
SPLIT, BAND at 6 kHz with a low threshold and 12 dB of range for steady spoken word.

Tips

  • Switch LISTEN on and sweep FREQ until the esses sound loudest and harshest, then switch LISTEN off and set THRESHOLD.
  • Watch the ESS LV meter: set THRESHOLD so only the esses cross the red line, not the rest of the words.
  • If the voice starts to lisp, lower RANGE rather than raising THRESHOLD.
  • Put De-Ess after compression and bright EQ, which both tend to bring the esses up.
  • Use SPLIT for transparency; try WIDE when SPLIT makes the esses sound dull or hollow.

File: gfx-de-ess.jsfx

Dynamics

Duplex

A two-channel compressor, de-esser and peak limiter for stereo, dual-mono or mid-side work.

Overview

Duplex is a dual-channel dynamics processor in the spirit of the classic 80s British two-channel units. Each channel is a clean VCA compressor with a gentle 6 dB soft knee, a frequency-conscious de-esser and a separate fast peak limiter.

The two channels can work three ways. In STEREO, channel A runs both sides together. In DUAL, A handles the left side and B the right. In M-S, A handles the mid (the centre of the image) and B the side (the edges), so you can compress the middle and the width separately.

The detector measures the average level over a few milliseconds rather than single peaks. AUTO timing follows the music: the attack speeds up the further the signal goes over, and the release lets go quickly after short peaks and slowly after long passages of compression.

Reach for it for

  • Vocals: compression and de-essing in one place
  • Gentle glue on a stereo bus
  • Taming sibilance with SPLIT de-essing
  • Compressing the centre and the sides of a mix separately (M-S)
  • Dual-mono work on two unrelated sources
  • Parallel drum compression

Controls

CHANNEL A and CHANNEL B

The two channels have the same controls. The label beside each name shows its job in the current MODE: BOTH SIDES, LEFT or RIGHT, MID or SIDE. In STEREO mode channel B rests and its panel says so.

IN
Switches the channel in or out. With it out, the audio passes straight through that channel.
THRESH
The level where compression starts, -40 to 0 dB (-20 dB by default).
RATIO
How strongly the channel compresses, 1:1 to INF:1 (3:1 by default). Fully right is INF:1 - limiting.
ATTACK
0.1 to 50 ms (5 ms by default). Reads AUTO when AUTO is on.
RELEASE
20 ms to 2 s (200 ms by default). Reads AUTO when AUTO is on.
GAIN
Make-up gain, -10 to +20 dB.
DE-ESS (knob)
The de-ess frequency, 2 to 12 kHz (6 kHz by default).
LIMIT
A separate fast peak limiter after the compressor, -24 to 0 dB: instant attack, 50 ms release. Fully right (0 dB) is OFF. The red light beside it shows it working.
AUTO
Program-dependent timing, replacing ATTACK and RELEASE: the further over the threshold, the faster the attack; the release is quick after short peaks and slows down after long passages of compression.
DE-ESS (button)
The compressor listens only above the DE-ESS frequency, so it reacts to sibilance rather than to the whole voice.
SPLIT
On by default; used with DE-ESS. The gain reduction is applied only above the de-ess band (the split sits at three-quarters of the DE-ESS frequency, so the whole ess band is caught): the esses come down and the body of the voice is untouched. Off: the whole signal is turned down, as on the original units.
GR
The row of 12 lights under each channel shows its gain reduction (compressor and limiter together), from 1 to 30 dB.

MASTER

MODE
STEREO: channel A runs both sides, with one shared detector. DUAL: A = left, B = right. M-S: A = mid, B = side.
LINK
Always lit in STEREO. In DUAL and M-S it is a switch (on by default): the two detectors share the louder level, so the image stays put, and the two channels' controls are ganged - turning one moves the other by the same amount, and any offset you set while unlinked is kept.
MIX
Parallel compression, 0 to 100% (shown as WET): blends the dry signal back in. 100% is fully processed.
IN / OUT meters
Input and output level. Click them to reset the peak hold.

The header

PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets (or click the preset display). The ◄ / ► keys step through them. Choosing one sets both channels and the MASTER section. The last entry resets everything to the defaults.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Duplex has no input or output fold-out. An input guard is on by default, gently easing very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catching fast peaks above -6 dBFS; it can be switched off from REAPER's parameter list (Input guard).

Presets

Gentle Stereo Bus
STEREO, 2:1 at -24 dB with AUTO timing and 3 dB of gain.
Vocal Compressor
STEREO, 3:1 at -28 dB, 5 ms attack, 180 ms release, the limiter at -3 dB.
De-esser (split)
STEREO, a fast 6:1 de-esser at 6.5 kHz with SPLIT, so only the esses come down.
Broadcast Voice
STEREO, 4:1 with wideband de-essing at 5 kHz and the limiter at -2 dB.
Dual Mono Pair
DUAL, unlinked, the same 3:1 AUTO settings on each side.
M-S Glue
M-S, unlinked: gentle 2.5:1 on the mid, firmer 4:1 on the sides to hold them in.
M-S Widen + Tame Sides
M-S, unlinked: gentle AUTO compression on the mid, a 7 kHz split de-esser on the sides with 3 dB of gain.
Brickwall Safety
STEREO, INF:1 at -12 dB with a 0.3 ms attack and the limiter at -1 dB.
Parallel Drums
STEREO, 10:1 at -36 dB with 12 dB of gain, blended in at 40% mix.
Bass Leveller
STEREO, 4:1 with AUTO timing, 4 dB of gain and the limiter at -2 dB.
Reset all to defaults
Every control back to its starting value.

Tips

  • For de-essing, switch DE-ESS on, sweep the DE-ESS knob until the GR lights react mostly to the esses, then set THRESH so they come down by a few dB.
  • Leave SPLIT on for vocals: only the top end is turned down, so the voice doesn't dip on every 's'.
  • In M-S, a little extra GAIN on channel B (side) widens the mix; compressing B harder than A holds the edges in.
  • With LINK on in DUAL or M-S, set one channel and the other follows; switch LINK off to give the two channels different settings, then switch it back on to move them together.

File: gfx-duplex.jsfx

Dynamics

Duplex Q

A two-channel compressor, de-esser and peak limiter with a tilt EQ and filters.

Overview

Duplex Q is a dual-channel dynamics processor in the spirit of the classic 80s British two-channel units, with a tilt EQ and high- and low-pass filters added underneath. Each channel is a clean VCA compressor with a gentle 6 dB knee, a frequency-conscious de-esser and a separate fast peak limiter.

The two channels can run a stereo signal together, work as two independent mono channels (left and right), or work in mid-side, so you can compress the centre of a mix and its edges separately. LINK keeps the stereo image steady and gangs the two channels' controls.

ORDER puts the EQ after the compressors or in front of them. The two units swap places on screen, so the top one is always the one the signal meets first.

Reach for it for

  • Vocal compression and de-essing in one place
  • Stereo bus glue, or separate control of mid and sides
  • Two different mono sources on one plug-in (DUAL)
  • A peak safety limiter after the compression
  • Quick tonal balancing with TILT and the filters

Controls

CHANNEL A and CHANNEL B

The two channels have the same controls. The label beside each name shows what the channel is working on: BOTH SIDES, LEFT / RIGHT or MID / SIDE. In STEREO mode Channel A runs both sides and Channel B rests (its panel says so).

IN
Switches the channel in or out.
THRESH
The level where compression starts, from -40 to 0 dB (-20 dB by default). The detector measures the average (RMS) level over about 3 ms.
RATIO
From 1:1 up to INF:1 (fully right), 3:1 by default.
ATTACK
From 0.1 to 50 ms (5 ms by default). Shows AUTO when AUTO is on.
RELEASE
From 20 to 2000 ms (200 ms by default). Shows AUTO when AUTO is on.
GAIN
Make-up gain, from -10 to +20 dB.
DE-ESS (knob, green pointer)
The de-ess frequency, from 2 to 12 kHz (6 kHz by default).
LIMIT (red pointer)
A separate fast peak limiter after the compressor, from OFF (fully right, 0 dB) down to -24 dB: the level the peaks are held at. It reacts instantly and recovers in about 50 ms. The red light beside it shows it working.
AUTO
Program-dependent timing: the attack gets faster the further the signal goes over the threshold, and the release lets go quickly after short peaks and slowly (up to about a second) after long passages of compression.
DE-ESS (key)
The compressor listens only to what lies above the DE-ESS frequency, so it reacts to sibilance.
SPLIT
With DE-ESS on: the gain reduction is applied only above the de-ess frequency (the split sits at three-quarters of it, so the whole ess band is caught), and the body of the voice is untouched. Off: the whole signal is turned down, as on the original units. On by default.
GR
Twelve lights under each channel showing its gain reduction, from 1 to 30 dB, including the limiter.

MASTER

MODE
STEREO: Channel A runs both sides (Channel B rests). DUAL: A = left, B = right. M-S: A = mid, B = side. Click a name, click the knob to step, or use the mouse wheel.
LINK
In DUAL and M-S, the two channels' detectors share the louder level so the image stays put, and their controls are ganged: turning one moves the other by the same amount, and any offset you set while unlinked is kept. Switches copy across. In STEREO it is always lit, as one detector already runs both sides.
MIX
Parallel compression, shown as WET 0 - 100: blends the dry signal back in. 100 is fully compressed.
IN / OUT meters
Input and output level, with a floating peak hold. Click a meter to clear the hold.

TILT EQ

IN
Switches the filters and the tilt in or out.
HIGH-PASS FREQ
From OFF (fully left, 10 Hz) up to 1 kHz.
HIGH-PASS SLOPE
6, 12, 18, 24, 36 or 48 dB per octave (12 dB by default).
TILT
Tilts the whole spectrum around the pivot, from -6 to +6 dB at each end: positive brightens (more top, less bottom), negative darkens.
PIVOT
The frequency the tilt turns around, from 100 Hz to 5 kHz (800 Hz by default).
GENTLE / STEEP
The shape of the tilt. GENTLE: a broad first-order slope across the whole range. STEEP: a firmer shelf that changes more quickly around the pivot.
LOW-PASS FREQ
From 1 kHz up to OFF (fully right, 22 kHz).
LOW-PASS SLOPE
6, 12, 18, 24, 36 or 48 dB per octave (12 dB by default).
TILT ON: ST / MID / SIDE
Where the tilt works: the whole stereo signal, the mid (centre) only, or the side only. The filters always work on both sides.
OUTPUT
The EQ unit's output level, from -12 to +12 dB.

The header

ORDER
One key: COMPRESSOR > EQ (the default) or EQ > COMPRESSOR. The units swap places on screen. Its amber light shows while you hold it.
PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets (a click on the display does the same); the ◄ / ► keys step through them. Choosing one sets the two channels and the MASTER section; the EQ is left as it is. The last entry resets both channels and the MASTER section to their defaults.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and this manual's address. Click anywhere to close it.

Knob values appear while you touch a knob. A built-in input guard eases very hot sources down before the compressors, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin; it never boosts quiet ones.

Presets

Gentle Stereo Bus
2:1 at -24 dB with AUTO timing and +3 dB of gain, linked.
Vocal Compressor
3:1 at -28 dB, 5 ms attack, 180 ms release, the limiter at -3 dB.
De-esser (split)
6:1 de-essing above 6.5 kHz with a fast attack, SPLIT on.
Broadcast Voice
4:1 at -26 dB with wideband de-essing at 5 kHz and the limiter at -2 dB.
Dual Mono Pair
DUAL mode, unlinked: two independent 3:1 channels with AUTO timing.
M-S Glue
M-S mode, unlinked: the mid gently at 2.5:1, the sides held harder at 4:1.
M-S Widen + Tame Sides
M-S: light AUTO compression on the mid, the sides lifted 3 dB with de-essing at 7 kHz.
Brickwall Safety
INF:1 at -12 dB with a 0.3 ms attack and the limiter at -1 dB.
Parallel Drums
10:1 at -36 dB with +12 dB of gain, blended in at 40%.
Bass Leveller
4:1 at -24 dB with AUTO timing and the limiter at -2 dB.
Reset all to defaults
Both channels and the MASTER section back to their starting values; the EQ is left as it is.

Tips

  • For a vocal, compress on Channel A, then switch on DE-ESS with SPLIT and set the frequency where the esses are loudest - start around 5 - 7 kHz.
  • In M-S, compress the side channel a little harder than the mid to steady a wide mix, or lift its GAIN to widen it.
  • Use the LIMIT knob as a safety net: set it a few dB under your loudest peaks and watch for the red light.
  • Put the EQ first (EQ > COMPRESSOR) when you use the high-pass to stop the low end driving the compressor; put it last for final tonal shaping.

File: gfx-duplex-q.jsfx

Dynamics

Gate

An analogue-modelled gate, expander and ducker with a filtered key input, lookahead and MIDI triggering.

Overview

Gate is three tools in one rack unit. As a GATE it shuts the signal down by a set amount whenever it falls below the threshold - for tightening drums and cleaning up noise between phrases. As an EXPANDER it turns quiet passages down gradually, at a ratio, which is gentler and more natural. Flip either one to DUCK and the curve turns upside down: the signal is pulled down whenever the key is loud, for ducking music under a voice or a bass under a kick.

The gain moves like an analogue VCA: opening is fast and then settles, and the release is a natural exponential fade. HOLD keeps the gate open for a moment after the level drops, and HYSTERESIS makes it close a few dB lower than it opens, so decaying notes don't make it chatter.

What the gate listens to (the key) can be the input itself or a separate signal on channels 3/4, and it can be filtered with a high-pass, a low-pass and a focus bell, so the gate only responds to the part of the sound you want. Held MIDI notes can also open the gate, for rhythmic chopping in time with a MIDI part.

Reach for it for

  • Tightening kick, snare and toms, and cutting spill between hits
  • Removing hiss, hum and amp noise between phrases
  • Gentle downward expansion on vocals, dialogue and room mics
  • Ducking music or a bass under a voice or kick (DUCK with EXT key)
  • 80s gated reverb and rhythmic MIDI-triggered chopping

Controls

Presets and size (top left)

Display
Shows the preset number and name. While you move a control it shows that control's name and value for a moment. Click it for the preset list.
PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets.
◄ / ►
Step to the previous or next preset (wrapping round).
SIZE S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Displays

Level history
A scrolling picture of the last few seconds, from 0 to -80 dB. The dark fill is the input level, the green OUT line the output, the amber GAIN line the gain the gate is applying. The red line is the threshold; the dashed red line under it is where the gate closes (THRESHOLD minus HYSTERESIS). A blue KEY line appears when the key differs from the input (EXT key, key filters or a non-linked stereo mode). The green bar along the bottom marks when the gate was OPEN (or DUCKED).
Transfer curve
The input-to-output curve. The bright branch is the opening curve, the dim one the closing curve (the gap between them is the hysteresis). The amber dot rides on it with the live level. With the EXT key, it shows the gain against the key level instead.
METERS
IN (left and right), GR (gain reduction of each side, or mid and side in M/S) and OUT (left and right).
GATE lights
Two green lights show when each detector is open (L+R in LINK mode, L and R in DUAL, M and S in M/S). MIDI lights while a MIDI note holds the gate, LSTN while LISTEN is on.

Mode buttons

MODEL: GATE / EXP
GATE: when the key is above the threshold the gate is fully open; below it, the signal drops by RANGE. EXP: a downward expander - below the threshold the gain falls at RATIO (at 1:2, 1 dB under the threshold gives 2 dB less output), limited to RANGE.
FUNCTION: NORM / DUCK
NORM: classic gating or expansion. DUCK: the same curve flipped - when the key goes above the threshold, the signal is pulled DOWN by RANGE.
KNEE: HARD / SOFT
HARD: a clean switch at the threshold. SOFT: on the GATE, the gate part-opens over the 12 dB below the threshold; on the EXP, the ratio eases in over 10 dB. Softer and less obvious.
STEREO: LINK / DUAL / M/S
LINK: one detector (the louder channel) drives both sides, so the image stays put. DUAL: independent left and right gates. M/S: independent mid and side gates (the key is split into mid and side too).
Latency readout
Shows ZERO LATENCY, or the lookahead time in ms when LOOKAHEAD is in use.

DYNAMICS

THRESHOLD
The level where the gate opens, -80 to 0 dB (-40 dB by default).
RANGE
How far the signal is turned down when the gate is shut (or ducked), 0 to 80 dB (60 dB by default). Small amounts (10 - 20 dB) keep some of the sound and are more natural.
RATIO
The expansion ratio, 1:1 to 1:10 (1:2 by default). Used only in EXP; in GATE mode the knob is greyed and reads GATE.
ATTACK
How fast the gate opens (or the duck pulls down), 0.01 to 100 ms; the time is roughly to 95% of the way.
HOLD
How long the gate stays open after the key drops below the close threshold, 0 to 2000 ms (50 ms by default).
RELEASE
How fast the gate closes (or the duck recovers), 5 to 4000 ms (150 ms by default), as a natural exponential fade.
HYSTERESIS
The gate opens at THRESHOLD and closes at THRESHOLD minus this amount, 0 to 20 dB (4 dB by default). Stops chatter on decaying notes.
LOOKAHEAD
0 to 10 ms. The audio is delayed so the gate can open just before a hit arrives, keeping the transient intact. 0 reads ZERO and adds no latency; any other value is reported to REAPER, which compensates for it.

KEY

INT / EXT
INT: the gate listens to its own input. EXT: it listens to the key input on channels 3/4 (see below).
MIDI
When on, held MIDI notes force the gate open (or the duck down). Channel 1 works on both sides, channel 2 on the left (or mid), channel 3 on the right (or side). MIDI is passed through to the next plugin.
LISTEN
Plays the filtered key instead of the output, so you can hear what the gate is listening to while you set the filters.
HPF (yellow cap)
A high-pass filter on the key only, 20 Hz (OFF) to 5 kHz. Raise it so low spill (e.g. kick in a snare mic) doesn't open the gate.
LPF
A low-pass filter on the key only, 100 Hz to 20 kHz (fully right = OFF). Lower it so cymbals and hats don't open a kick or tom gate.
FOCUS
The centre of a bell filter on the key, 100 Hz to 10 kHz. Greyed while F.GAIN is at 0.
F.GAIN
Boost or cut of the focus bell, -12 to +12 dB. Boost to make the gate more sensitive to the instrument's main frequency.
LINK
In DUAL and M/S: how far each detector is pulled towards the louder side, 0 to 100%. 100% behaves like linked; 0% is fully independent. In LINK mode it is greyed and reads FULL.

MASTER

MIX
Blends the dry signal back in, 0 to 100%. 100% is the full effect; lower settings make the gate's cut shallower.
OUTPUT
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.

Knobs: drag up or down (Shift for fine moves); double-click or Ctrl-click to reset a knob to its default. Values show while you hover or drag.

Presets

A preset sets the dynamics, the mode buttons, the key section, MIX and OUTPUT, and switches LISTEN off.

Init
Every control back to its default.
Kick Tight
Fast gate with 2 ms lookahead; the key is filtered to 30 - 400 Hz with a boost at 80 Hz.
Snare Crack
Very fast gate, 40 dB range and 1.5 ms lookahead; key focused on 2 kHz.
Tom Cleanup
Soft-knee gate with a longer hold and release for ringing toms; key focused around 120 Hz.
Hi-Hat Clean
Short, fast gate with the key high-passed at 4 kHz.
Vocal Breath
Soft 1:2 expander that takes 12 dB off breaths and room noise.
Guitar Noise
Soft-knee gate at -55 dB to remove amp hiss between phrases.
Bass DI
1:3 expander for a DI bass, with a 200 ms release.
Gentle Expander
A light 1:1.5 soft expander with up to 15 dB of reduction.
Room Mic Tame
Soft 1:2.5 expander to pull room mics down between hits.
Dialogue Clean
Soft expander for speech, key focused on the voice around 2.5 kHz.
Kick Duck (Ext Key)
Ducks the signal by 9 dB from a kick on the key input (low-passed at 200 Hz).
Voice-Over Duck (Ext Key)
A slow 1:4 expander-ducker that lowers music by up to 12 dB under a voice on the key input.
Gated Reverb 80s
Hard gate with a 250 ms hold and a fast 40 ms release: the classic chopped reverb tail.
MIDI Chop
Threshold at 0 dB and MIDI on: only held MIDI notes open the gate.
Lookahead Drum
Very fast gate with 5 ms lookahead so the transients stay whole.

Using the key input (EXT)

  1. On the track with Gate, open ROUTE and set the track to 4 channels.
  2. On the track you want to key from (e.g. the kick or the voice), add a send to Gate's track and set its destination to channels 3/4.
  3. Click EXT. Use LISTEN to check you can hear the key, set the key filters, then switch LISTEN off.
  4. Set THRESHOLD so the key crosses it; with DUCK, adjust RANGE for how far the signal dips.

Using MIDI triggering

  1. Put Gate on the audio track you want to chop, and make sure it receives MIDI: either a MIDI item on the same track before Gate, or a MIDI send from another track.
  2. Click MIDI in the KEY section. Notes on channel 1 open both sides; channels 2 and 3 open left and right (mid and side in M/S) separately.
  3. Turn THRESHOLD to 0 dB if you want only the MIDI notes to open the gate, not the audio itself.

Tips

  • Set HYSTERESIS to a few dB before reaching for a longer HOLD: it stops chatter without keeping the gate open too long.
  • For natural results on vocals and rooms, use EXP with a soft knee and 10 - 20 dB of RANGE rather than a full gate.
  • Use the key filters with LISTEN: when you can hear only the drum you want in the key, the gate will only open for that drum.
  • A few ms of LOOKAHEAD keeps the attack of fast drums intact even with a slow ATTACK setting.

File: gfx-gate.jsfx

Dynamics

Headsmoother

A tape saturator and an optical compressor as two stacked units, in either order.

Overview

Headsmoother puts two units in one plug-in. HEAD is a tape saturator, the same as the Head plug-in. SMOOTH is an optical-style compressor with a valve-style colour stage. Each can be switched out on its own, and one key swaps their order.

The units swap places on screen when you change the order, so the top unit is always the one the signal meets first. Compress then saturate (SMOOTH > HEAD) for a controlled signal that gets tape colour on the way out; saturate then compress (HEAD > SMOOTH) to let the compressor level out what the tape adds.

SMOOTH behaves like an optical compressor: it listens to the average level (with the deep bass filtered out of what it hears), lets go quickly after short peaks and more slowly after long passages of compression. Like a real machine, the tape bias and top-end loss and the colour stage's drive wander slowly and independently on each side.

Reach for it for

  • A one-stop vocal or bass channel: smooth levelling plus tape warmth
  • Gentle, musical bus compression with colour
  • Parallel optical compression with BLEND
  • Hard levelling and peak control with LIMIT

Controls

The header

ORDER
One key that swaps the units. Its legend shows the order: SMOOTH > HEAD (the default: compress, then saturate) or HEAD > SMOOTH. Its amber light shows while you hold it down.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

HEAD - tape saturator (green unit)

ON
Switches the unit in or out. The change fades over about 30 ms, so there is no click. The unit dims when it is out.
DRIVE
How hard the signal hits the tape curve, from 0 to 10 (3 by default) - up to +22 dB into the curve. The level is matched, so DRIVE changes the character, not the loudness. As it goes up, more of the low end (below about 250 Hz) skips the curve and rejoins clean, and the head bump around 70 Hz softens, so heavy settings stay tidy.
VOICE
NORMAL: flat, with tape's gentle top-end loss. MEDIUM: a broad lift around 1.4 kHz going into the curve, for body. BRIGHT: a high shelf going into the curve, so the top end saturates first. Click a name, click the knob to step, or use the mouse wheel.
TRIM
The tape unit's clean output level, from -24 to +6 dB.
BOOST
Extra colour, 0 to 100%. It makes the tape curve more lopsided (more even harmonics, a warmer tone) and brings in band saturation on the way out, where only the lows and highs are saturated and the mids stay clean. It only works while HEAD is on.
LEVEL
Ten lights showing the tape unit's output peak, from -30 dBFS up to 0.

SMOOTH - optical compressor (blue unit)

ON
Switches the unit in or out, with the same 30 ms fade. The unit dims when it is out.
COLOUR
Switches the valve-style colour stage after the gain cell: a soft, slightly lopsided saturation. Off gives clean compression. On by default.
COMPRESS
How much compression, from 0 to 10 (3 by default). It lowers the threshold from 0 down to -30 dB, so turning it up compresses more. The ratio is 3:1 with a wide, soft 12 dB knee.
BLEND
Parallel compression: 100% (the default) is fully compressed; lower settings blend the dry signal back underneath.
GAIN REDUCTION meter
A needle showing the gain reduction, from 0 to 20 dB.
ATTACK
SLOW (25 ms), MEDIUM (8 ms, the default) or FAST (1.5 ms).
LIMIT
Turns the compressor into a limiter: the ratio goes to 20:1, the knee narrows to 2 dB and the attack is twice as fast. Its red light shows when it is on.
RELEASE
SLOW (900 ms), MEDIUM (350 ms, the default) or FAST (90 ms). The first part of the recovery is quicker; after a long stretch of heavy compression the tail lets go more slowly, as on an optical cell.
OUTPUT
Make-up gain for the compressor, from -12 to +24 dB.
LEVEL
Ten lights showing the compressor unit's output peak, from -30 dBFS up to 0.

Double-click a large knob to return it to its default; hold Shift while dragging for fine moves. After both units the signal passes through a soft clipper set at -0.3 dBFS, so sudden peaks are caught.

Tips

  • On vocals, start with SMOOTH > HEAD: COMPRESS around 3 - 5, MEDIUM attack and release, then add DRIVE to taste. Bring the level back with OUTPUT.
  • For bass, try HEAD > SMOOTH: the tape adds harmonics that help it speak on small speakers, and the compressor evens out what the tape brings up.
  • SLOW attack lets the front of each note through for punch; FAST catches it for a flatter, more controlled sound.
  • For parallel compression, push COMPRESS hard and bring BLEND down to 30 - 50%.

File: gfx-headsmoother.jsfx

Dynamics

MK3

A broadcast channel strip: transformer input, stepped three-band EQ and a fast VCA compressor.

Overview

MK3 is a channel strip in the spirit of the 1970s British local-radio broadcast desks, with a VCA compressor in the spirit of the classic broadcast gain-riding units. The signal runs: input GAIN, phase, input transformer (IRON), high-pass filter, then the EQ and compressor in either order, the output transformer and OUTPUT.

The transformers colour the low end first: the band below about 180 Hz runs into core saturation, so pushing GAIN thickens the bottom before anything else. The EQ has stepped frequencies, like the desk modules, plus FIZZ, a broadcast-style exciter that holds a steady layer of air under the programme.

The compressor is peak-sensing with a fast attack, a small fixed knee and ratios up to infinity. Its release follows the music: spiky material releases faster, dense material slower. Two extras borrowed from broadcast practice - peak reversion correction and the VOICE 2k bell - keep heavy compression from sounding thin or dull.

Reach for it for

  • Speech and presenter voice: levelled, clear and forward
  • Phone-in and telephone-style tidying
  • Drum bus and snare grab with a fast VCA
  • Steady bass and broadcast-style piano or acoustic guitar
  • Transformer warmth on anything (IRON and GAIN)

Controls

PPM

PPM
A stereo peak programme meter on the output, black face with marks 1 to 7: red needle left, green needle right. 4 is -18 dBFS (alignment) and 6 is -10 dBFS; the marks are 4 dB apart (6 dB between 1 and 2). The needles rise quickly and fall back 24 dB in 2.8 seconds.

INPUT

GAIN
Input gain, -20 to +30 dB. It drives the transformers too, so with IRON in, more GAIN means a thicker, more saturated bottom end.
HPF
A stepped 12 dB per octave high-pass filter: OFF, 40, 63, 100 or 160 Hz. Clears rumble and proximity boom before the EQ and compressor.
Ø
Inverts the polarity of the signal.
IRON
Switches the input and output transformers in or out (in by default). The input transformer saturates the lows first; the output one is lighter.
PEAK
Lights when the signal after GAIN and the input transformer peaks above -3 dBFS.

EQ

Three bands with stepped frequencies. Each band has a small grey FREQ knob and a larger gain knob.

BASS FREQ / BASS
A low shelf at 50, 100 or 200 Hz, -15 to +15 dB.
PRESENCE FREQ / PRES
A bell at 700 Hz, 1, 1.4, 2, 2.8, 4 or 5.6 kHz, -12 to +12 dB.
TREBLE FREQ / TREBLE
A high shelf at 5, 8 or 12 kHz, -15 to +15 dB.
FIZZ
The broadcast 'cut-through' top. An exciter makes new harmonics from everything above 3 kHz, shapes them round 8 kHz and holds them at a steady level about 26 dB under the programme, so the air stays constant and a voice cuts through. It runs in parallel (only the new harmonics are added) and works only when the EQ is IN. The LED shows when it is on.
IN
Switches the EQ in or out (in by default).

COMP

THRESH
The level where compression starts, -40 to 0 dB (-12 dB by default).
RATIO
1:1 to 20:1; fully right reads INF:1, which is limiting.
ATTACK
0.1 to 50 ms (2 ms by default).
RELEASE
20 ms to 3 s (250 ms by default). The release is programme dependent: the compressor watches how spiky the material is and releases faster on spiky material and slower on dense material (from 0.4 to 2.5 times the set value).
GAIN
The compressor's make-up gain, 0 to 24 dB.
VOICE 2k: LIFT / FLAT / DIP
A wide bell at 2 kHz (about two octaves) in front of the compressor's detector, whose depth follows the gain reduction: 0.25 dB for every dB of reduction, up to 4 dB. LIFT raises the voice band as it compresses, so a hard-working compressor doesn't dull the voice; DIP sinks it; FLAT (default) leaves it alone. The lamp brightens as the bell comes in.
RELEASE MODE: LOG / LIN
LOG (default): an exponential recovery, fast at first and then easing in. LIN: a steady recovery of 10 dB per release time.
PRC (PEAK REVERSION)
Peak reversion correction, on by default. Under heavy gain reduction it adds back the low end that compression tends to lose, with a gentle shelf around 150 Hz of up to +4 dB that follows the gain reduction.
IN
Switches the compressor in or out (out by default).

Gain reduction, signal flow and output

GR dB
A column of 20 lights showing the compressor's gain reduction, 1 to 20 dB (amber, red from 16 dB).
Signal flow
INPUT, IRON, HPF, EQ, COMP and OUT listed in the order the signal takes; a stage's lamp is lit while it is in use. EQ and COMP swap places with ORDER.
OUTPUT
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.
OUT: OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper at -0.3 dBFS (no latency, the default), or a lookahead brickwall limiter at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). CLIP flashes red while it is clipping.

The header

Preset display
Shows PRESET nn/12 and the preset's name. Click it for the full list. While you move a control, it shows that control's name and value for a moment.
< / >
Step to the previous or next preset.
ORDER
Swaps the EQ and the compressor: EQ > COMP (default) or COMP > EQ. The two units change places on the panel to match.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.
HW
Switches between the hardware-style desk panel and the soft flat panel.
Colour key
The key with the round dot switches the panel between charcoal and cream.

Presets

Init
Every channel control back to its starting value, compressor out.
Presenter Voice
+6 dB gain, HPF 63 Hz, a 2.8 kHz presence lift and a little top; 4:1 compression with a 1 ms attack.
Warm Voice-Over
A touch of 50 Hz weight, 1.4 kHz dipped and 12 kHz air; gentle 3:1 compression.
Interview Guest
HPF 100 Hz, less low end, a 2 kHz lift; firmer 6:1 compression with a fast attack.
Phone-In Tidy
HPF 160 Hz, bass and top cut, 1.4 kHz pushed; heavy 8:1 compression with linear release, PRC off.
Drum Bus Grab
Low and high shelves up, 4:1 with a 5 ms attack and fast linear release.
Snare Smack
200 Hz body and 4 kHz crack; 6:1 with an 8 ms attack to let the hit through, fast linear release.
Bass Even
HPF 40 Hz, 1 kHz dipped; 5:1 compression placed before the EQ (COMP > EQ).
Acoustic Guitar
Transformers out, low end trimmed and 12 kHz sparkle; gentle 2.5:1 compression.
Piano Broadcast
HPF 40 Hz, a little 12 kHz air; light 2:1 compression with a slow release.
Brick Limit
Transformers and EQ out; the compressor at INF:1 with a 0.1 ms attack as a limiter, PRC off.
Iron Warmth
+10 dB into the transformers for low-end thickness, compressor out, OUTPUT at -8 dB to compensate.

Tips

  • The presets set the whole channel, including OUTPUT, but leave the OUT stage (OFF / CLIP / LIMIT) as it is.
  • For broadcast-style speech, try VOICE 2k on LIFT with PRC on: the voice stays bright and full even when the compressor works hard.
  • Put the compressor first (COMP > EQ) when you want the EQ's boosts to stay out of the compressor's way, as in Bass Even.
  • Turn FIZZ on for a voice that has to cut through background music or noise; it adds a steady air rather than more level.
  • Watch the PPM: keeping the needles around 4 to 6 puts the output around -18 to -10 dBFS.

File: gfx-mk3.jsfx

Dynamics

Quad11

A four-band bus compressor with an EQ and a saturator in every band.

Overview

Quad11 splits the signal into four bands - LOW, LO-MID, HI-MID and HIGH - and gives each its own bell EQ, compressor and saturator.
