The crossovers are steep (24 dB per octave) and the bands sum back flat, so with nothing switched on Quad11 is transparent. A master EQ sits on the whole bus ahead of the split.

Each band's compressor can also work the other way: turn THRESH above zero and it expands, lifting the peaks for extra punch. The level is made up automatically, so a band keeps its loudness while its peaks are held down or lifted. MIX blends the processed bus with the dry one for parallel compression, and the dry signal is kept exactly in time with the bands, so the blend is clean.

Reach for it for

  • Mix bus and master glue, band by band
  • Drum bus punch and parallel smash
  • Taming harshness and esses on vocals without dulling the rest
  • Tightening a bass without squashing its top
  • Lifting transients (expansion) or adding tape-style warmth per band

Controls

Display

Response curve
The combined frequency response of the master EQ and the four band EQs, from 20 Hz to 20 kHz, -15 to +15 dB.
Crossover lines
The three splits between the bands. Drag a line to move it. The lowest can go down to 30 Hz, the highest up to 16 kHz, and the splits stay at least a quarter of their frequency apart.
Band dots
One dot per band in its colour: drag it to set the bell's frequency and gain; use the mouse wheel on it to change its width. Hovering shows the frequency, gain and Q.
Gain reduction
Each band's area is tinted, and its gain reduction (or expansion) hangs from the top.

MASTER EQ

Filters and shelves on the whole bus, ahead of the split.

HPF
A high-pass filter from OFF (fully left) up to 500 Hz.
LO FREQ / LO SHELF
A low shelf: its corner from 30 to 500 Hz and its gain, -12 to +12 dB.
LPF
A low-pass filter from 2 kHz up to OFF (fully right).
HI FREQ / HI SHELF
A high shelf: its corner from 2 to 16 kHz and its gain, -12 to +12 dB.

The four bands: LOW, LO-MID, HI-MID, HIGH

Each strip has the same controls, and shows the band's frequency range at the top right.

SOLO
Listen to this band on its own. Several bands can be soloed at once; while any band is soloed, MIX is ignored so you hear only the soloed bands, and SOLO ON shows in MASTER.
BYP
Lets the band through unprocessed (no EQ, compression or saturation).
Gain-reduction bar
Shows how much the band is being turned down (GR, up to 24 dB across the bar) or lifted (EXP, up to 6 dB).

EQ (per band)

FREQ
The bell's centre, 20 Hz to 20 kHz.
WIDTH
The bell's width (Q), 0.3 to 6. Higher is narrower.
GAIN
-12 to +12 dB.

COMPRESSOR (per band)

THRESH
From -48 to +48 dB; 0 is OFF. Turned left (COMP), it is the threshold in dBFS above which the band is compressed. Turned right (EXP), it lifts the peaks above minus that level (for example +18 lifts what goes over -18 dBFS), by up to 6 dB, for punch. Stereo-linked, with a 6 dB soft knee.
RATIO
How hard the compression or expansion works, 1:1 to 20:1.
ATTACK FAST / SLOW
How quickly the band reacts. The times suit each band: FAST is 10, 6, 3 and 1 ms from LOW to HIGH; SLOW is 40, 30, 20 and 10 ms.
RELEASE FAST / SLOW
How quickly the band recovers. FAST is 150, 120, 90 and 60 ms from LOW to HIGH; SLOW is 600, 450, 350 and 250 ms.

The make-up gain is automatic: it follows three quarters of the band's average gain change, slowly (over about 1.5 seconds), so the band keeps its loudness while the peaks are still held down or lifted.

SATURATION (per band)

TAPE / TUBE / CLIP
Click the key to step through the three types; its light is on while DRIVE is above zero. TAPE: a lopsided soft curve with even and odd harmonics. TUBE: one side gives way first - warmer, mostly even harmonics. CLIP: a hard knee, odd harmonics.
DRIVE
How hard the band is driven, 0 - 100% (up to +30 dB into the curve). It is level matched, so it changes the colour rather than the volume. While any band is driven the saturation runs at twice the sample rate to keep it clean.
PRE / POST
Where the saturation sits in the band (the light is on for PRE). PRE: saturation, then EQ, then the compressor - the compressor tames what the saturation adds. POST (default): EQ, compressor, then saturation - the compressor steadies the level going into the saturation.

INPUT

TRIM
Input level before anything else, from -24 to +12 dB. A single click on the knob (without dragging) switches GUARD on or off; its LED at the knob's upper right is dark when GUARD is off, red when it is on, and glows brighter while it is catching peaks.
GUARD
Off by default in this plugin. Gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources.
IN meter
The input level after TRIM and GUARD, with a floating peak. Click a meter to clear the peak.

MASTER

MIX
Parallel compression: blends the processed bus with the dry one, 0 - 100%. 100% is fully processed.
OUTPUT
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out, 0 - 100%: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper (no latency), or a lookahead limiter that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). The CLIP key flashes red while the clipper is working.
OUT meter
Output level, with a floating peak.

The header

PRESETS, arrows and display
PRESETS (or a click on the display) opens the list of factory presets; the ◄ / ► keys step through them. A preset sets the crossovers, the master EQ, all four bands and MIX; TRIM, GUARD, OUTPUT, SAT and the OUT stage are left as they are. The last entry resets everything to the defaults.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Presets

Gentle Glue
All four bands at -14 dB, 1.5:1, slow attack and release: light, even bus glue.
Mix Bus Punch
HPF at 25 Hz; 2:1 to 3:1 on every band with slow attack and fast release, and a little tape drive on the low band.
Drum Bus Smash (parallel)
Heavy, fast compression (-30 dB, 6:1) on every band, tape and clip saturation, a touch of low and high shelf, blended in at 45%.
Transient Lift (expand)
All bands expanding (THRESH above zero) with fast attack and release, to lift the hits.
Vocal Control
Bands split at 200 Hz, 2 kHz and 7 kHz, HPF at 80 Hz; firmer, faster compression on the upper bands to tame harshness and esses.
Bass Tighten
Bands split at 80 Hz, 250 Hz and 2 kHz; the two low bands compressed hard with tube drive before the EQ, a little low-mid cut, the top band left alone.
De-Mud & Air
A 3 dB cut at 350 Hz in the low-mid band, a lift at 12 kHz and an air shelf, with gentle compression throughout.
Tape Warmth
No compression: tape saturation on every band, more on the lows than the highs.
Master Gentle
Very light, slow compression (1.3:1 to 1.5:1) with a little tape drive on the low band.
Parallel Crush
Every band crushed (-36 dB, 10:1, fast) and clipped, blended in at 35%.
Reset all to defaults
Every control back to its starting value.

Tips

  • Solo a band while you set its crossover lines, so you hear exactly what it holds.
  • Start with THRESH just below where the band's GR bar starts moving, then raise RATIO until it sits.
  • For a parallel bus, crush the bands hard and bring MIX down to 30 - 50% - the dry signal stays perfectly in time with them.
  • Use PRE saturation when you want the compressor to smooth out the drive, POST when you want the drive to sit on a steady level.
  • Expansion (THRESH above zero) on the low and hi-mid bands brings back the snap of over-compressed drums.

File: gfx-quad11.jsfx

Dynamics

RND

A clean VCA compressor and a levelling amplifier in one half-rack box.

Overview

RND is a half-rack compressor with two characters, in the spirit of the classic half-rack compressor pair. The MODE keys switch between them, and the panel changes colour to match: off-white for COMP, mustard for LEVEL.

COMP is a clean, transparent VCA compressor with true RMS detection. Its GENTLE switch puts three compressors in series in the side-chain - fast, medium and slow - each doing a third of the ratio, for smoother results on dense material without pumping.

LEVEL is a levelling amplifier: a softer onset, a two-stage optical-style release (a quick first part, then a slow tail that lengthens the longer it has been compressing), and gentle saturation and a little top-end softening that grow with the gain reduction. It runs at twice the sample rate to keep the saturation clean.

In both modes THRESHOLD is in dBu, where 0 dBu = -18 dBFS, and both channels are linked to the louder side so the stereo image stays put.

Reach for it for

  • Transparent vocal and acoustic compression (COMP)
  • Smooth, invisible levelling on dense mixes and buses (GENTLE)
  • Warm, gently coloured levelling on vocals and bass (LEVEL)
  • Parallel compression with MIX

Controls

Main knobs

THRESHOLD
The level where compression starts, from -40 to +20 dBu (0 dBu = -18 dBFS). Turn it down for more compression. The knee is soft.
RATIO
How strongly the level is reduced above the threshold, from 1:1 to 25:1 (3:1 by default).
ATTACK
How fast the compressor reacts, from 0.2 to 200 ms (10 ms by default). In LEVEL the onset is softened further.
RELEASE
How fast it lets go, from 0.05 to 5 s (0.3 s by default). In LEVEL this sets the two-stage release, or the linear release rate when LOG RELEASE is off.
GAIN
Make-up gain for the compressed signal, from 0 to +15 dB.
MIX
Parallel compression: blends the dry signal back in. 100% is fully compressed. In LEVEL the dry signal goes through the same oversampling filters as the saturated one, so the two stay in phase and there is no comb filtering at partial settings.
GR
Ten lights across the top showing the gain reduction, from 1 to 20 dB.

MODE

COMP
The clean VCA compressor.
LEVEL
The levelling amplifier, with its softer onset, optical-style release and saturation.
GENTLE (COMP only)
Three compressors in series in the side-chain, at staggered thresholds and speeds, each doing a third of the ratio; their reductions are added together. Smoother on dense material, with less pumping.
LOG RELEASE (LEVEL only)
On (the default): the release is exponential and two-stage - the first 40% of the reduction lets go quickly, then a slow tail that lengthens with how long it has been compressing. Off: a linear release, a steady number of dB per second.

Drift

One dial, 0 to 100% (50% by default), for a slow random wander like a real analogue box. 50% is the standard amount, 100% twice that, 0 none; higher settings also wander a little faster. What it moves depends on the mode.

DRIFT in COMP
The attack and release times wander slowly.
DRIFT in LEVEL
The saturation (its drive and asymmetry, separately on each side) and the "glue" - the knee width and how long the release remembers past compression - wander slowly.

IN / OUT

TRIM
Input level before anything else, from -24 to +12 dB.
GUARD
Click the TRIM knob once to switch it; the small red light at the knob's upper right shows it is on, and glows brighter while it is working. On by default. It gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources. (A double-click still resets TRIM.)
SAT
Band saturation on the way out: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper at -0.3 dBFS (no latency, the default), or a lookahead limiter holding the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). The CLIP key flashes red while the clipper is working.

The header

S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Tips

  • For transparent vocal control, use COMP with a ratio around 2:1 - 4:1 and a medium attack, and lower THRESHOLD until the GR lights show 3 - 6 dB on the loud phrases.
  • Switch on GENTLE for buses and full mixes: it lets you compress more before the pumping becomes audible.
  • LEVEL's saturation grows with the gain reduction, so the harder you compress, the warmer and softer it gets - drive it for colour, back it off for clean levelling.
  • THRESHOLD is in dBu: 0 dBu sits at -18 dBFS, the usual average level of a well-recorded track, so 0 is a sensible starting point.

File: gfx-rnd.jsfx

Dynamics

Smooth

A smooth, clean optical-style compressor with one main knob.

Overview

Smooth is a clean compressor in the spirit of the classic one-knob software compressors. Turn COMPRESS up for more compression and set OUTPUT to taste: that is most of the job.

Its timing is optical-style: the release lets go quickly at first, then more slowly, and the longer the compression has been held, the slower it recovers. The detector ignores the deep lows, so the bass doesn't make it pump, and the gain is applied evenly across the spectrum, so the top end - the air - stays intact. A gentle valve-style colour stage follows the compressor.

Reach for it for

  • Smoothing vocals without dulling them
  • Gentle levelling on bass, acoustic guitar and keys
  • Easy glue on a mix or group bus
  • Hard, fast limiting of peaks (LIMIT)
  • Parallel compression with BLEND

Controls

Main panel

COMPRESS
The main knob, 0 - 10 (3 by default). It lowers the threshold from 0 to -30 dB: turn it up for more compression.
BLEND
Dry / compressed mix, 0 - 100%, for parallel compression. 100% is fully compressed.
Gain reduction meter
The needle shows how much the compressor is turning the level down, 0 - 20 dB.
ATTACK
SLOW (25 ms), MEDIUM (8 ms, default) or FAST (1.5 ms). Click a label to pick it, or click the knob to step through. With LIMIT on, the attack is twice as fast.
LIMIT
Off: a soft, wide-knee 3:1 curve - transparent. On: a hard 20:1 curve with a faster attack, for catching peaks.
RELEASE
SLOW, MEDIUM (default) or FAST: the overall recovery speed. The first part of the reduction always lets go quickly; the rest takes longer, and longer still after heavy, sustained compression.
OUTPUT
Make-up gain, -12 to +24 dB.
LEVEL
The output level on ten lights, from -30 dBFS (green) to 0 dBFS (red).

Drift, input and output

DRIFT
The colour stage's drive and asymmetry wander slowly, independently on each side, 0 - 100% (50% is the standard amount, 100% twice that, 0% none). The wander also speeds up a little as you turn it up. Subtle: it moves the warmth, not the level.
IN TRIM
Input level before anything else, from -24 to +12 dB.
GUARD
Click the IN TRIM knob to switch it; its LED sits at the knob's upper right. On by default. Gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources. The LED glows brighter while it is working.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper at -0.3 dBFS (no latency), or a lookahead limiter (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). CLIP is on by default and flashes red when it clips.

Panel keys

S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

In REAPER's parameter list only

Drift amount / Drift speed
The standard drift that DRIFT scales: how far the colour stage wanders (25% by default) and how fast (0.02 - 2 Hz, 0.25 Hz by default).
Bias / Sat low band / Sat high band / Ceiling
The rest of the output stage: the saturation's even-harmonic bias, where its low and high bands start (40 - 400 Hz and 2 - 16 kHz) and the clip ceiling or limiter threshold.

Tips

  • Turn COMPRESS up until the meter shows 3 - 6 dB on the loud parts, then bring OUTPUT up to match the bypassed level.
  • SLOW attack lets the front of each note through for a punchier, more natural sound; FAST holds everything steadier.
  • For parallel compression, set COMPRESS high with LIMIT on and pull BLEND down to 30 - 50%.
  • Because the detector ignores the deep lows, Smooth works well on full mixes without the bass making everything duck.

File: gfx-smooth.jsfx

Dynamics

Squish

A stompbox compressor with two characters - a squishy, gritty sustainer and a clean studio compressor - and a big BLEND knob for parallel compression.

Overview

Squish is a pedal compressor with two modes, designed from scratch in the spirit of the classic orange and red guitar sustainer pedals and the modern boutique studio-style pedal compressors. Both modes share the same four controls - SENS, SUSTAIN, ATTACK and RELEASE - and the mode sets the character behind them. The pedal turns orange-red for VINTAGE and steel blue for STUDIO.

VINTAGE is the sustainer: strong ratios, an aggressive squish and a release that stretches out on long, heavy compression, so it holds notes without pumping. Hit it hard and it adds a little lopsided grit, while quiet playing stays clean. STUDIO is a clean, transparent compressor with gentler ratios and a steadier release.

BLEND, the big knob, mixes the dry signal back under the compressed one, so you can keep your pick attack while the notes sustain. Make-up gain is automatic, and there is no latency (with OFF or CLIP on the output), so it is safe for live guitar monitoring.

Reach for it for

  • Country and funk guitar with that squashed, clucky attack
  • Long, singing lead sustain
  • Even, punchy bass
  • Parallel compression that keeps the transients
  • Smoothing strummed acoustic guitar
  • Gentle glue on a vocal

Controls

The header

PRESETS, ◄ ► and the display
PRESETS (or a click on the display) opens the list of factory presets; ◄ and ► step through them, and the display shows the one in use. A new instance opens on Init.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window. A new instance opens in Large.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Mode

VINTAGE / STUDIO
The character (VINTAGE by default). The name and description printed on the pedal follow it. VINTAGE: ratios from about 2:1 to 20:1, a firm knee, a release that stretches on heavy compression, and grit when hit hard. STUDIO: ratios from about 1.5:1 to 8:1, a softer knee, a release that lengthens only a touch, and no grit. Switching is smooth.

The compressor

The same four controls in both modes.

SENS
How early it grabs, 0 to 100% (50% by default). It sets the threshold from about -10 dBFS (0%) down to -40 dBFS (100%); the knob shows both. In VINTAGE it also sets how hard the input drives the grit.
SUSTAIN
How much it squashes, 0 to 100% (50% by default). It sets the ratio - about 2:1 to 20:1 in VINTAGE, 1.5:1 to 8:1 in STUDIO - and the knee tightens as it goes up. The knob shows the ratio.
ATTACK
How fast it clamps down, 0.5 - 50 ms (3 ms by default). Slower settings let more of the pick attack through before it squashes.
RELEASE
How fast it lets go, 30 - 1500 ms (140 ms by default). In VINTAGE it stretches up to three times longer on long, heavy compression; in STUDIO only a little.

Gain reduction

Meter
A 12-segment ladder of the gain reduction, 1 to 24 dB: amber up to 8 dB, red beyond.
dB GR readout
The gain reduction in dB.
OTA GRIT / CLEAN VCA
In VINTAGE (OTA GRIT) the lamp glows as the input drives the compressor into grit. In STUDIO it reads CLEAN VCA and stays dark.

TONE and LEVEL

TONE
A tilt at 3 kHz, -100 to +100% (FLAT by default): left darkens (DARK), right adds sparkle (SPARK), up to 9 dB either way. It comes after BLEND, so it shapes the whole sound.
LEVEL
Output level, -24 to +12 dB (0 dB by default). Make-up gain is already automatic (about 70% of the reduction at a typical level), so use this to match the bypassed loudness.

IN / OUT

TRIM
Input level before anything else, -24 to +12 dB. Raising it works like raising SENS, but also drives the grit harder.
GUARD
Click the TRIM knob (without turning it) to switch GUARD on or off; the small light at the knob's upper right shows it is on and glows brighter while it works. On by default. Gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS. It never boosts quiet sources.
DRIFT
0 to 100% (15% by default). See Drift below.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper at -0.3 dBFS (no latency), or a lookahead limiter (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). CLIP is on by default and flashes red while it clips. For live monitoring use OFF or CLIP.

BLEND

BLEND
The big knob: dry / compressed balance, 0 to 100% wet (100% by default). Lower settings bring the uncompressed signal back underneath - parallel compression - so the transients return while the sustain stays. The two stay perfectly in phase, even with the grit.

In REAPER's parameter list only

On / bypass
Bypass / On (On by default). Bypass crossfades over 10 ms to the raw input, before TRIM; the output stage stays in. There is no key for it on the panel; use REAPER's own bypass, or automate this parameter.

Drift

DRIFT gives the pedal an uneven supply: the threshold wanders very slightly (up to about 1.5 dB either way at 100%), and so does the bias that sets how lopsided the grit is. At 0% it stays exactly where you set it.

Knobs: drag up or down (Shift for fine moves); double-click to reset a knob to its default. The mouse wheel works too.

Presets

Presets set the mode, the four compressor controls, BLEND, TONE, LEVEL and DRIFT, and switch the plug-in on (out of bypass). TRIM, GUARD and the output stage are left as they are.

Init
VINTAGE with every control at its default: a medium squish at 100% wet.
Country Chicken Pickin'
VINTAGE, quite heavy, a little dry blended in and a bright tone, for clucky, snappy picking.
Funk Squish
VINTAGE, very heavy and fully wet with a touch of sparkle, LEVEL down 1 dB.
Clean Sustain
STUDIO, moderate, with a slower attack and a long 420 ms release; 80% wet.
Parallel Punch
STUDIO, hard and fast, blended 50 / 50 with the dry signal: punchy parallel compression.
Bass Even-Out
STUDIO, moderate, with a slow 15 ms attack, a 300 ms release and a slightly darker tone, for bass.
Acoustic Strum
STUDIO, light, with a 20 ms attack to keep the strum and a brighter tone; 70% wet.
Lead Sustain
VINTAGE, very sensitive and very heavy, for long, singing notes.
Vocal Glue
STUDIO, gentle, with a 350 ms release and a touch of air: light levelling for a vocal.
Slide Sustain
VINTAGE, heavy with a slightly darker tone and a little more drift, 75% wet.

Tips

  • Set SENS first, until the meter shows a few dB of reduction on your normal playing, then shape it with SUSTAIN.
  • For more pick attack, lengthen ATTACK or lower BLEND. BLEND keeps the attack natural; ATTACK makes it snappier.
  • Watch the OTA GRIT lamp in VINTAGE: if you want it clean, lower SENS or TRIM until the lamp barely glows.
  • Compression brings up noise as well as sustain. A little DARK on TONE helps with a noisy guitar.
  • Compare with REAPER's bypass and set LEVEL so the loudness matches before you judge it.

File: gfx-squish.jsfx

Dynamics

Tandem

Two RMS soft-knee compressors in series on one panel.

Overview

Tandem puts two classic RMS / VCA compressors one after the other. Each stage does a little of the work, which is the old studio trick for heavy compression that still sounds open: the first stage levels the performance gently, the second catches what gets through.

Both stages measure loudness (RMS) rather than peaks, so they react the way your ears do. There are no attack and release knobs - the timing follows the music, as on the original hardware: the further the signal goes over the threshold, the faster the stage reacts, and it recovers at a steady pace.

Reach for it for

  • Vocals that need to sit still in a mix
  • Drum and mix buses
  • Bass guitar
  • Ducking a music bed under a voice (EXT KEY)
  • Creative pumping and reverse-dynamics effects (negative ratios)

Controls

Stage 1 and Stage 2

The two stages have the same main controls. The signal goes through Stage 1, then Stage 2.

IN
Switches the stage in or out. With a stage out, the audio passes straight through it.
THRESHOLD (red cap)
The level where the stage starts working, from -60 to 0 dB. Turn it down (left) for more compression. Because the detector measures loudness, the threshold relates to the average level rather than the peaks.
RATIO (yellow cap)
How strongly the stage compresses once the level is over the threshold. 1:1 does nothing; 2:1 and 4:1 are moderate; INF:1 (marked in red) is limiting - nothing gets louder. Past INF the ratio becomes negative (-2:1, -1:1): the louder the input, the quieter the output. That reverses the dynamics, for pumping and swelling effects.
OUTPUT (blue cap)
The stage's make-up gain, from -20 to +20 dB. Use it to bring the level back up after compression.
BELOW / KNEE / ABOVE
Three lights showing where the level sits: green below the threshold, amber in the soft knee, red above it.
GR meter
The row of 12 lights under the knobs shows the gain reduction of that stage, from 1 to 30 dB.
HARD
Off (default): a soft knee - the ratio eases in gradually over 10 dB around the threshold, which is smoother and more transparent. On: the full ratio starts right at the threshold, for a firmer, more obvious effect.

Stage 1 only

DECOUPLE
Off: both sides of the stereo signal get exactly the same gain, so the stereo image stays still. On: each side mostly follows its own level (80% its own, 20% shared), like unlinking a bus compressor. The sound becomes wider and livelier, but the image can move a little.
EXT KEY
Stage 1 listens to the key input (plug-in pins 3 and 4) instead of the audio it is compressing. Use it for ducking: route a voice or kick to pins 3/4 and the music dips whenever it plays. Stage 2 always listens to Stage 1's output.
KEY IN
Lights when a signal arrives on the key input - a quick check that your routing works.

Stage 2 only

GRIT
Drives Stage 2's VCA into gentle 2nd and 3rd harmonic distortion. The harder Stage 2 works, the grittier it gets, so it adds attitude on the loud parts and stays clean on the quiet ones.

Master

Transfer curve
Shows the input-to-output curve of both stages together. The white dot rides on it with the live level, so you can see exactly where the music sits.
SC HPF
A high-pass filter on what both stages listen to (fully left = off, up to 400 Hz). Raise it so the bass doesn't make the whole mix pump; the bass itself isn't filtered.
MIX
Parallel compression: blends the dry signal back in. 100% is fully compressed; lower settings keep the transients of the dry signal under the compressed sound.
IN / OUT meters, TOTAL GR
Input and output level, and the combined gain reduction of both stages.

The header

PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets. Choosing one sets the plugin's controls for that job; the input and output stages are left as they are. The last entry resets everything to the defaults.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.
OUTPUT
Folds the input and output stages out below the panel (and back in). The light shows when they are open.

Input and output stages (OUTPUT fold-out)

TRIM
Input level before anything else, from -24 to +12 dB.
GUARD
On by default. Gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources. The tag shows how much it is working.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
BIAS
Shapes the saturation: more bias adds more even (2nd) harmonics, for a warmer, rounder colour.
LO BAND / HI BAND
Where the low and high saturation bands start (40 - 400 Hz and 2 - 16 kHz).
CEILING / THRESH
With CLIP: the level the peaks are softly clipped at. With LIMIT: the threshold of a brickwall limiter that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS - pulling it down limits harder and makes the output louder.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper (no latency), or a lookahead limiter (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER).

Presets

Gentle Glue
Light two-stage smoothing for a mix or group bus.
Vocal Leveller
Stage 1 evens the performance, Stage 2 catches the peaks; a little SC HPF.
Vocal Upfront
Heavier vocal control with a hard knee and GRIT on Stage 2.
Drum Bus Stack
Stage 1 decoupled for a lively image, GRIT on Stage 2, 70% mix.
Parallel Crush
Stage 2 limiting hard with GRIT, blended in at 35%.
Bass Even
Steady bass level with a little GRIT.
Acoustic Guitar
Gentle, with Stage 1 decoupled for width.
Peak Catcher
Light levelling, then a hard-knee limit at -6 dB.
Negative Pump
Stage 2 past INF for rhythmic pumping, 60% mix.
Reverse Swell
Stage 2 at -1:1: loud hits dip and the tails swell up; 50% mix.
Ducker (ext key)
Stage 1 ducks from the key input - route the voice or kick to pins 3/4.
Reset all to defaults
Every control back to its starting value.

Using the key input (EXT KEY)

  1. On the track with Tandem, open ROUTE and set the track to 4 channels.
  2. On the track you want to duck from (e.g. the voice), add a send to Tandem's track and set its destination to channels 3/4.
  3. Switch EXT KEY on. KEY IN lights when the key signal arrives; lower Stage 1's THRESHOLD until the music dips by the amount you want.

Tips

  • Start with Stage 2's ratio at INF and its threshold just under your peaks: it becomes a safety limiter after Stage 1's gentle levelling.
  • If the low end makes the whole mix duck, raise SC HPF to 80-120 Hz.
  • Negative ratios sound best on sustained material (pads, rooms, reverb returns) and with MIX below 100%.
  • Use the transfer curve: if the white dot sits far up the flat part of the curve, you're compressing more than you probably need.

File: gfx-tandem.jsfx

Dynamics

Tandem Q

Tandem's two RMS soft-knee compressors in series, with a passive-style program EQ on the same panel.

Overview

Tandem Q is Tandem with a program EQ added below it. The top unit is two classic RMS / VCA compressors one after the other: each stage does a little of the work, which is the old studio trick for heavy compression that still sounds open. The bottom strip is a passive-style program EQ with LOW, LO MID, HI MID and HIGH sections, running through transformer stages.

Both compressor stages measure loudness (RMS) rather than peaks, so they react the way your ears do. There are no attack and release knobs - the timing follows the music: the further the signal goes over the threshold, the faster the stage reacts (about 15 ms for 10 dB over, 5 ms for 30 dB), and it recovers at a steady 120 dB per second.

ORDER chooses whether the EQ comes before or after the compressors. The unit the signal meets first is shown on top.

Reach for it for

  • Vocals that need levelling and tone-shaping in one place
  • Drum and mix buses that want glue plus a broad EQ lift
  • Bass guitar: even level, then low-end weight from the LOW section
  • Ducking a music bed under a voice (EXT KEY)
  • Creative pumping and reverse-dynamics effects (negative ratios)

Controls

Stage 1 and Stage 2

The two stages have the same main controls. The signal goes through Stage 1, then Stage 2.

IN
Switches the stage in or out. With a stage out, the audio passes straight through it.
THRESHOLD (red cap)
The level where the stage starts working, from -60 to 0 dB. Turn it down (left) for more compression. Because the detector measures loudness, the threshold relates to the average level rather than the peaks.
RATIO (yellow cap)
How strongly the stage compresses once the level is over the threshold. 1:1 does nothing; 2:1 and 4:1 are moderate; INF:1 (marked in red) is limiting - nothing gets louder. Past INF the ratio becomes negative (-2:1, -1:1): the louder the input, the quieter the output. That reverses the dynamics, for pumping and swelling effects.
OUTPUT (blue cap)
The stage's make-up gain, from -20 to +20 dB. Use it to bring the level back up after compression.
BELOW / KNEE / ABOVE
Three lights showing where the level sits: green below the threshold, amber in the soft knee, red above it.
GR meter
The row of 12 lights under the knobs shows the gain reduction of that stage, from 1 to 30 dB.
HARD
Off (default): a soft knee - the ratio eases in gradually over 10 dB around the threshold, which is smoother and more transparent. On: the full ratio starts right at the threshold, for a firmer, more obvious effect.

Stage 1 only

DECOUPLE
Off: both sides of the stereo signal get exactly the same gain, so the stereo image stays still. On: each side mostly follows its own level (80% its own, 20% shared), like unlinking a bus compressor. The sound becomes wider and livelier, but the image can move a little.
EXT KEY
Stage 1 listens to the key input (plug-in pins 3 and 4) instead of the audio it is compressing. Use it for ducking: route a voice or kick to pins 3/4 and the music dips whenever it plays. Stage 2 always listens to Stage 1's output.
KEY IN
Lights when a signal arrives on the key input - a quick check that your routing works.

Stage 2 only

GRIT
Drives Stage 2's VCA into gentle 2nd and 3rd harmonic distortion. The harder Stage 2 works, the grittier it gets, so it adds attitude on the loud parts and stays clean on the quiet ones.

Master

Transfer curve
Shows the input-to-output curve of both stages together. The white dot rides on it with the live level, so you can see exactly where the music sits.
SC HPF
A high-pass filter on what both stages listen to (fully left = off, up to 400 Hz). Raise it so the bass doesn't make the whole mix pump; the bass itself isn't filtered.
DRIFT
A slow random wander in the program EQ, like old components warming up, 0 - 100% (50% is the standard amount, 100% twice that, 0% none). It nudges each side's EQ frequencies and gains independently and very slightly, so the EQ breathes a little. It does not move the compressors.
MIX
Parallel compression: blends the dry signal back in. 100% is fully compressed; lower settings keep the transients of the dry signal under the compressed sound.
IN / OUT meters, GR
Input and output level, and the combined gain reduction of both stages in dB.
CLIP
On by default. Soft-clips the output at -0.3 dBFS, with no latency. The CLIP label turns red while it is clipping. Off: no clipping.

Program EQ

The strip below the compressors. Each frequency knob clicks through fixed steps, as on passive hardware.

IN
Switches the EQ curves in or out. The change glides rather than jumps.
LOW: FREQ
The low shelf frequency: 20, 30, 60, 100 or 150 Hz.
LOW: BOOST
Lifts the lows, 0 - 10. The boost is a resonant shelf with a little bump just above the frequency.
LOW: ATTEN
Cuts the lows, 0 - 10, with a very broad, gentle shelf. Using BOOST and ATTEN together is the old passive-EQ trick: the deepest lows still lift while a dip forms just above them, tightening the low mids.
LO MID: FREQ
150, 220, 330, 470, 680 Hz, 1 kHz or 1.5 kHz.
LO MID: GAIN
Boost or cut, -12 to +12 dB. The band gets narrower as you turn it further from 0.
LO MID: NARROW
Off: a wide, musical bell. On: a narrower bell for more focused changes.
HI MID: FREQ
1, 1.5, 2.2, 3.3, 4.7, 6.8 or 10 kHz.
HI MID: GAIN / NARROW
As LO MID: -12 to +12 dB, and NARROW for a tighter bell.
HIGH: FREQ
The centre of the high boost: 3, 4, 5, 8, 10, 12 or 16 kHz.
HIGH: BOOST
Lifts the highs around that frequency, 0 - 10.
HIGH: WIDTH
The width of the high boost, 0 - 10: low settings are sharp and focused, high settings broad and airy. Starts at 5.
HIGH: ATT FREQ
Where the high cut starts: 5, 10 or 20 kHz.
HIGH: ATTEN
Cuts the highs above ATT FREQ with a gentle shelf, 0 - 10. Combine with BOOST to lift presence while softening the very top.
OUT
The EQ's output level, -12 to +12 dB.

The header

ORDER
COMPRESSOR > EQ or EQ > COMPRESSOR: click to swap. The panel redraws with the unit the signal meets first on top.
PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets; the ◄ / ► keys step through them and the display shows the one in use. Choosing one sets the two compressor stages, SC HPF and MIX; the EQ and DRIFT are left as they are. The last entry resets the compressor, DRIFT and CLIP to their defaults.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Input guard

There is no input or output fold-out on Tandem Q. An input guard runs in front of everything and is on by default: it gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources. It has no key on the panel; it appears as Input guard in REAPER's parameter list.

Presets

Gentle Glue
Light two-stage smoothing for a mix or group bus.
Vocal Leveller
Stage 1 evens the performance, Stage 2 catches the peaks; a little SC HPF.
Vocal Upfront
Heavier vocal control with a hard knee and GRIT on Stage 2.
Drum Bus Stack
Stage 1 decoupled for a lively image, GRIT on Stage 2, 70% mix.
Parallel Crush
Stage 2 limiting hard with GRIT, blended in at 35%.
Bass Even
Steady bass level with a little GRIT.
Acoustic Guitar
Gentle, with Stage 1 decoupled for width.
Peak Catcher
Light levelling, then a hard-knee limit at -6 dB.
Negative Pump
Stage 2 past INF for rhythmic pumping, 60% mix.
Reverse Swell
Stage 1 out, Stage 2 at -1:1: loud hits dip and the tails swell up; 50% mix.
Ducker (ext key)
Stage 1 ducks from the key input - route the voice or kick to pins 3/4.
Reset all to defaults
The compressor controls, DRIFT and CLIP back to their starting values (the EQ is left as it is).

Using the key input (EXT KEY)

  1. On the track with Tandem Q, open ROUTE and set the track to 4 channels.
  2. On the track you want to duck from (e.g. the voice), add a send to Tandem Q's track and set its destination to channels 3/4.
  3. Switch EXT KEY on. KEY IN lights when the key signal arrives; lower Stage 1's THRESHOLD until the music dips by the amount you want.

Tips

  • EQ > COMPRESSOR lets the compressor react to your EQ: boosting the lows makes it work harder on the bass. COMPRESSOR > EQ keeps the EQ's tone moves out of the compressor's way.
  • Start with Stage 2's ratio at INF and its threshold just under your peaks: it becomes a safety limiter after Stage 1's gentle levelling.
  • If the low end makes the whole mix duck, raise SC HPF to 80-120 Hz.
  • On a vocal, try a little HIGH BOOST at 10 or 12 kHz with WIDTH up for air, and a touch of LO MID cut at 220 or 330 Hz to clear mud.

File: gfx-tandem-q.jsfx

Dynamics

Thrust

A stereo VCA bus compressor with stepped controls, a THRUST side-chain filter and new or old detection.

Overview

Thrust is a stereo VCA bus compressor in the spirit of the famous American bus compressor with the stepped controls and the THRUST switch. It is made for gluing mixes, drum buses and groups.

THRUST shapes what the compressor listens to: at LOUD the lows count much less and the highs more, so the bass no longer drives the compression and mixes stay big and punchy. TYPE chooses between NEW (feed-forward: precise and firm) and OLD (feedback: the detector hears its own output, so it is softer and more forgiving and the ratio eases off on big peaks - the vintage feel).

LINK sets how much the two sides share one gain, from independent to fully linked, and LINK FILTER decides what part of the spectrum is shared. MIX blends in the dry signal for parallel compression.

Reach for it for

  • Mix-bus glue
  • Punchy drum and percussion buses
  • Parallel 'smash' compression (MIX)
  • Keeping a bass-heavy mix from pumping (THRUST)
  • Vintage, forgiving bus compression (OLD)

Controls

The header

PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets (a click on the preset display does the same). A preset sets the COMPRESSOR and STEREO controls; the last entry resets everything to the defaults.
◄ / ►
Step to the previous or next preset. The display shows the number and name of the preset in use.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

COMPRESSOR

THRESHOLD (yellow cap)
Where it starts working, -40 to 0 dB (-12 dB by default). Turn it down for more compression.
ATTACK
Stepped: .03, .1, .3, 1, 3, 10 or 30 ms (1 ms by default). Short times catch the transients; longer ones let the punch through.
RATIO
Stepped: 1.5, 2, 3, 4, 6, 10 or INF : 1 (4:1 by default). INF is limiting.
RELEASE
Stepped: .05, .1, .2, .5, 1 or 2 s, or VAR (set by the VAR knob). 0.5 s by default.
VAR
The release time used when RELEASE is on VAR, 0.05 - 3 s. Its cap turns yellow when it is in use.
KNEE
HARD, MED (6 dB) or SOFT (12 dB). Softer knees ease the compression in around the threshold.
THRUST (yellow cap)
What the detector hears. NORM: flat. MED: the lows count less (about 6 dB down) and the highs a little more. LOUD: the lows count much less (about 12 dB down) and the highs more. The audio itself is not filtered.
NEW / OLD
The detector type. NEW: feed-forward - it hears the input; precise and firm. OLD: feedback - it hears its own output; softer and more forgiving, and the ratio eases off on big peaks. The words under the keys show FEED-FORWARD or FEEDBACK.
MAKEUP (blue cap)
Make-up gain, -10 to +20 dB.
IN
Switches the compressor in or out (in by default; the light shows when it is in). Out, the audio passes straight through.

STEREO

VU meters
Two needle meters, left and right, showing gain reduction (GR L / GR R) or output level (OUT L / OUT R).
LINK (blue cap)
How much the two sides share one gain, from IND (each side on its own) to 100 (both sides get the same gain, so the image stays still). 100 by default.
LINK FILTER
What part of the spectrum is shared by the link. OFF: everything. HP: the lows are left out of the link. BP: only the mids are shared.
METER
Switches the VU meters between GR and OUT.
MIX
Parallel compression: the WET amount, 0 - 100. 100 is fully compressed; lower settings blend the dry signal back in.

Thrust has no output-stage fold-out. Its input guard is on in the background: very hot sources are gently eased down towards about -18 dBFS average and fast peaks above -6 dBFS are caught before the compressor; quiet sources are never boosted.

Presets

Mix Bus Glue
2:1, 3 ms attack, 1 s release, soft knee, MED thrust, HP link filter, 2 dB makeup.
Big Mix (loud thrust)
4:1 with LOUD thrust and HP link filter, for a big mix that keeps its low end.
Drum Bus Punch
6:1, 1 ms attack, 0.2 s release, hard knee, MED thrust, 80% link, 6 dB makeup.
Drum Smash (parallel)
10:1 with the fastest attack and LOUD thrust, heavily compressed and blended in at 40%.
Vintage Feedback
3:1 OLD detection, 3 ms attack, VAR release, soft knee.
Vocal Bus
4:1, 0.3 ms attack, medium knee, 4 dB makeup.
Wide Stereo (independent)
2:1 OLD detection with the sides unlinked, for a wider, livelier image.
Bus Limiter
INF:1, 0.1 ms attack, fast release, hard knee: a bus limiter.
Bass Control
4:1, 10 ms attack, medium knee, 5 dB makeup, to even out the low end.
Room Pump
6:1 OLD detection with LOUD thrust, BP link filter at 50% link, 60% mix: a pumping room or parallel sound.
Reset all to defaults
Every control back to its starting value.

Tips

  • For mix-bus glue, start with RATIO 2, ATTACK 10 or 30 ms, RELEASE on .2 or VAR, and aim for 1 - 3 dB on the GR meters.
  • If the kick or bass makes the whole mix pump, move THRUST to MED or LOUD.
  • OLD is more forgiving on dense mixes; NEW is tighter and better for controlling peaks.
  • Lower LINK, or use LINK FILTER HP, if the stereo image feels squashed into the middle.
  • For parallel compression, set a heavy setting first (RATIO 10, fast attack), then bring MIX down until the dry punch comes back.

File: gfx-thrust.jsfx

Dynamics

Transient

A level-independent transient shaper: more or less attack, more or less sustain.

Overview

Transient shapes the front and the tail of every hit. Turn ATTACK up to sharpen the start of each note, down to soften it; turn SUSTAIN up to bring out the ring and room after each hit, down to dry it up and tighten the tail.

There is no threshold. Transient compares fast and slow envelopes of the signal, so it works the same on quiet and loud hits. One detector serves both channels, so the stereo image never shifts.

Reach for it for

  • Snappier snares and punchier kicks
  • Removing room and ring from drums, or bringing more of it up
  • Softening harsh picking on guitar or bass
  • Tightening bass notes
  • Adding snap to a drum bus with MIX

Controls

Shaper

ATTACK (green cap)
-100% to +100%. Plus sharpens the front of every hit, minus softens it - pushing drums back or taming picking. Up to about 12 dB either way on the attack.
SUSTAIN (red cap)
-100% to +100%. Plus brings up the ring and room after each hit, minus dries it up and tightens the tail. Up to about 14 dB either way. Very quiet tails (below about -54 dBFS) are left alone, so the noise floor doesn't pump up.
SPEED
How long the attack window is, 5 - 60 ms: short for clicks and sticks, long for a fatter front.
LENGTH
How long the sustain stage holds on after each hit, 50 - 1000 ms.
FOCUS (yellow cap)
A high-pass filter on what the shaper listens to, 20 - 500 Hz (fully left = OFF), so kick or bass rumble does not trigger it. The audio itself isn't filtered.
MIX
Blends in the dry signal, 0 - 100%.
OUTPUT
Output level, -12 to +12 dB.

Drag a knob up or down (Shift for fine), use the mouse wheel, or double-click to reset it. While you turn a knob, the display shows its name and value.

Meters

ATK
What the attack stage is doing, up or down, +/- 12 dB about the centre line.
SUS
What the sustain stage is doing, +/- 12 dB about the centre line.
OUT
The output level, from -30 dBFS (green, amber, then red near the top).

The top strip

Preset display
Shows the preset number and name. Click it for the preset list.
< and >
Step to the previous or next preset. A preset sets ATTACK, SUSTAIN, SPEED, LENGTH, FOCUS and MIX; OUTPUT and the output stage are left as they are.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper at -0.3 dBFS (no latency), or a lookahead limiter holding the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). CLIP is on by default and flashes red when it clips.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large (it opens at L). The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Presets

Init
Everything neutral: no change to the sound.
Snappy Snare
Strong attack with a short window, a slightly drier tail, FOCUS at 120 Hz.
Punchy Kick
More attack with a fatter 25 ms front and a tighter tail.
Room Killer
Sustain pulled well down to dry up room and ring.
Big Room
Attack softened a touch and sustain well up with a long 600 ms hold.
Soft Pick
Attack pulled down with a short window, FOCUS at 150 Hz, to tame picking.
Tight Bass
More attack and a shorter, tighter tail.
Drum Bus Snap
Moderate attack lift and a slightly drier tail, blended in at 60%.

Tips

  • Watch the ATK and SUS bars: they show exactly how much each stage is adding or taking away on every hit.
  • Boosting ATTACK raises the peaks. Use LIMIT or CLIP, or bring OUTPUT down, if the track starts to clip.
  • On a full drum bus, raise FOCUS to 80 - 120 Hz so the kick doesn't dominate what the shaper reacts to.
  • Negative SUSTAIN on a close-miked tom or snare is a quick alternative to a gate, with no chatter.

File: gfx-transient.jsfx

Dynamics

Twin Comp

Two classic compressors in one rack - a VCA and an Expressor - in either order.

Overview

Twin Comp puts two very different compressors side by side. Unit A is a VCA compressor in the spirit of the classic late-70s RMS boxes: it measures loudness rather than peaks, has a soft knee, and its timing follows the music. Its COMPRESSION knob runs from 1:1 through INF:1 and on into negative ratios.

Unit B is an Expressor in the spirit of the classic 80s expander-compressors: peak-sensing with a smooth knee, with DRIVE, RATIO, ATTACK and RELEASE, an AUTO timing mode, HF EXP to keep the top open under heavy compression, SPR to firm up the low end and LF CUT to stop the bass from pumping it.

ORDER sets which unit comes first, and the two units swap places on the panel to match. MIX blends in the dry signal for parallel compression.

Reach for it for

  • Drum buses that want punch and control (A into B)
  • Vocals that need firm, open-sounding compression (B with HF EXP)
  • Bass that needs a tight, steady bottom (SPR)
  • Gentle glue on a mix (both units lightly)
  • Pumping and reverse-dynamics effects (A past INF)

Controls

Drag a knob up or down to turn it (hold Shift for fine moves), or double-click to reset it. The value shows while the mouse is over a knob, and the display shows the control you are changing.

The top strip

Display
Shows the preset number and name, or for a moment the control you have just changed. Click it for the preset list.
< and >
Step to the previous or next preset.
ORDER
Click to swap the order: A > B or B > A. The two units change places on the panel so the first one is on the left. The key's light shows while it is held.
MIX
Parallel compression, 0 - 100%: 100% is fully compressed, lower settings blend the dry signal back in.
DRIFT
An uneven supply under both VCAs, 0 - 100% (20% by default): each unit's gain wobbles very slightly (up to +/-0.15 dB at 100%) and unit A's colour moves with it. It stays level-neutral.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (no latency), or a lookahead limiter that holds them at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). The CLIP key flashes red while the clipper is working.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

A - VCA COMPRESSOR (true RMS)

ON
Switches unit A in or out. The red light beside it is lit while the unit is in.
BELOW / KNEE / ABOVE
Three lights showing where the level sits against the threshold: green below it, amber in the soft knee, red above it.
THRESHOLD
The level where compression starts, -40 to 0 dB. Because the unit measures loudness, it relates to the average level rather than the peaks.
COMPRESSION
The ratio. From fully left (1:1) it rises to INF:1 at 80% of its travel; past that it becomes negative, down to -2:1 at the end: the louder the input, the quieter the output. The value is shown as a ratio.
OUTPUT
Unit A's make-up gain, -20 to +20 dB.
SOFT KNEE
On (default): the ratio eases in over 10 dB around the threshold. Off: a hard knee, firmer and more obvious.
GR
The column of lights shows unit A's gain reduction, 0 to 20 dB.

There are no attack or release controls on unit A: the further the signal goes over the threshold, the faster it reacts (about 15 ms for 10 dB over, 5 ms for 20 dB), and it recovers at a steady 120 dB per second. It adds a whisper of 2nd harmonic that grows with the gain reduction.

B - EXPRESSOR (peak)

ON
Switches unit B in or out. The red light beside it is lit while the unit is in.
DRIVE
How hard the signal is pushed into the compressor, 0 to +40 dB. More drive means more compression (it sets the threshold from 0 down to -40 dB).
RATIO
1:1 to 20:1.
ATTACK
0.1 - 50 ms. Reads AUTO when AUTO is on.
RELEASE
30 ms - 3 s. Reads AUTO when AUTO is on.
OUTPUT
Unit B's make-up gain, -20 to +20 dB.
AUTO
Automatic, smooth optical-style timing: a quick attack and a two-stage release that follows the programme. ATTACK and RELEASE are not used.
SPR
Rotates the phase of the low end against the top (two all-pass stages at 180 Hz) for a tighter, firmer bottom.
LF CUT
A 150 Hz high-pass on what the compressor listens to only, so bass peaks no longer pump it. The bass itself is not filtered.
HF EXP
0 - 100%: lifts the top end (above about 3.5 kHz) in proportion to the gain reduction, up to +6 dB, so heavy compression stays open and bright.
GR
The column of lights shows unit B's gain reduction, 0 to 20 dB.
OUT
The output level of the whole plug-in, -30 dBFS to 0.

A light between the two units glows when both are on.

Presets

A preset sets the order, both units, which units are on, and MIX. DRIFT and the OFF / CLIP / LIMIT keys are left as they are.

Init
Both units on, A > B, at their starting values.
Drum Bus
Unit A only at 4:1 with a soft knee and 4 dB of make-up.
Vocal Expressor
Unit B only, 4:1 with AUTO timing, LF CUT and HF EXP, 5 dB of make-up.
Bass Tight
A > B: A with a hard knee, then B with SPR for a firm bottom.
Mix Glue
B > A: gentle 2:1 Expressor with AUTO, SPR and LF CUT, then light VCA compression.
Parallel Smash
A > B, both working hard (A with a hard knee, B at 8:1 with fast timing), blended in at 40%.
Acoustic Natural
Unit B only, 2.5:1 with AUTO timing, LF CUT and a little HF EXP.
Negative Pump
Unit A only, past INF for pumping and reverse-dynamics effects.

Tips

  • A > B lets the VCA level the performance and the Expressor catch the peaks; B > A lets the Expressor shape the tone first and the VCA glue it.
  • On a vocal, raise HF EXP as you add DRIVE on unit B, so the voice stays clear as it gets more compressed.
  • Watch the BELOW / KNEE / ABOVE lights: for gentle levelling, the amber KNEE light should be on most of the time.
  • Negative ratios sound best on sustained material and with MIX below 100%.

File: gfx-twin-comp.jsfx

Dynamics

VB30

An aggressive diode-bridge programme limiter with capacitor memory, freeze and a gate.

Overview

VB30 is a characterful, aggressive limiter in the spirit of early broadcast modules and high-ratio industrial limiters. It is made for room mics, shouty vocals and parallel drum buses - the places where you want to hear the compression.

The signal runs gate, then limiter, then make-up gain, then MIX. The limiter has a fast, peak-sensing detector and a diode-bridge style gain cell that thickens as it works harder. Its release has a "capacitor memory": short peaks snap straight back, while sustained squashing clamps down and lets go slowly with a long, musical suck-back.

FREEZE stops the noise floor and the room from pumping up in the gaps: when the audio drops into a pause, the gain reduction is held where it is until the music returns. A gate or gentle expander sits in front of the limiter for tidying up spill.

Reach for it for

  • Smashed room and ambience mics
  • Parallel drum crush (with MIX)
  • Shouty, in-your-face vocals
  • Gritty snares and bass with the diode-bridge COLOUR
  • Big pumping and suck-back effects

Controls

LIMITER (upper row) - DYNAMICS

THRESHOLD (orange plate)
The level where limiting starts, from -40 to 0 dB (-18 dB by default). Turn it down for more squash.
RATIO
From 1.5:1 up to LIMIT (fully right), 4:1 by default. The knee is soft.
ATTACK
Very fast, FET-style: from 50 microseconds to 20 ms (0.8 ms by default). Slower settings let more of each hit's front edge through.
RELEASE
From 30 to 1500 ms (200 ms by default). With MEMORY above zero this is the centre of a release that stretches and shrinks with the music.

LIMITER - CHARACTER

MEMORY
The capacitor memory, 0 to 100% (50% by default). The release follows how much gain reduction there has been over the last half second or so: after short peaks it recovers as fast as a quarter of RELEASE, after sustained squashing as slowly as six times RELEASE. 0 gives a plain, fixed release.
COLOUR
The diode-bridge gain cell, 0 to 100% (40% by default): a soft, slightly lopsided saturation that thickens as the gain reduction deepens. Its level is compensated, so it adds character rather than volume. 0 is clean.
GAIN
Make-up gain after the limiter, from -6 to +24 dB.

LIMITER - keys and meter

IN
Switches the limiter in or out. The POWER light in the header shows it.
LINK
On (the default): both sides share one gain, for the limiter and the gate, so the stereo image stays put. Off: each side works on its own.
MEMORY CHARGE
Twelve lights showing how much the memory is stretching the release right now, from SNAP (quick recovery) to SUCK (long, slow let-go).

GATE (lower row) - GATE / FREEZE

FREEZE THR (orange plate)
The level below which FREEZE holds the gain reduction, from -70 to -25 dB (-50 dB by default). A fast detector watches the input; there is 3 dB of hysteresis, so it does not chatter at the threshold.
GATE THR (orange plate)
The gate's threshold, from -80 to -20 dB (-60 dB by default).
RANGE
How far the gate turns the signal down, from 0 to 60 dB (15 dB by default).
RELEASE
How fast the gate closes, from 20 to 2000 ms (250 ms by default). It opens in about half a millisecond.

GATE - SIDECHAIN / MIX

SC HPF
A high-pass filter on what the limiter listens to, from OFF (fully left) up to 300 Hz. Raise it so the bass doesn't make everything pump; the bass itself isn't filtered.
MIX
Parallel blend with the dry signal, shown as WET 0 - 100. 100 is fully limited; lower settings tuck the crushed sound under the dry one.

GATE - keys and lamps

GATE / EXP
GATE: below the threshold the signal drops by the full RANGE. EXP (the default): a gentle 1:2 expander - the further below the threshold, the more it is turned down, up to RANGE.
OPEN
Lights while the gate is open.
FREEZE
Switches the freeze on or off (on by default). When the signal falls below FREEZE THR - a pause, the gap between hits - the limiter's release stops and the gain reduction stays where it is, so the noise floor and the room do not swell up in the gaps.
GATE IN
Switches the gate in or out (out by default). The readout underneath shows how much the gate is turning down, or GATE OUT.
HOLD
Lights while the freeze is holding the gain reduction.

Meters

GAIN RED.
Two glowing bars, L and R, showing the limiter's gain reduction from 0 to 30 dB, with the current figure underneath.
OUTPUT
Two LED columns, L and R, showing the output level from -36 to +3 dBFS.

The header

PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets (a click on the display does the same); the ◄ / ► keys step through them. Choosing one sets the limiter and gate controls for that job. The last entry resets everything to the defaults.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Presets

Room Mic Smash
Limiting at -24 dB with a 0.2 ms attack, strong memory and colour, freeze on, SC HPF at 90 Hz.
Parallel Drum Crush
12:1 at -28 dB with heavy memory and +12 dB of make-up, blended in at 40%.
Shouty Vocal
6:1 at -20 dB with moderate colour; the gate in as an expander to tidy the gaps.
Big Suck-back
8:1 with MEMORY at 100% and a 300 ms release for long, pumping let-go; freeze off.
Brick Wall Peaks
Limiting at -14 dB with the fastest attack and an 80 ms release: peak control with light colour.
Bass Grab
3:1 at -16 dB with a slower 4 ms attack and SC HPF at 60 Hz.

Limiting at -40 dB, full colour, +18 dB of make-up, blended in at 30%.

1.5:1 with a slow attack and release and no memory - mostly the diode-bridge colour, little compression.
Reset all to defaults
Every control back to its starting value.

Tips

  • For a room mic, keep FREEZE on and set FREEZE THR just above the room's noise between hits: the HOLD lamp should light in the gaps, not during the drums.
  • Turn MEMORY up for the classic long suck-back on sustained material, or down to 0 when you want the release to stay predictable.
  • On a parallel bus, crush hard with a low THRESHOLD and plenty of GAIN, then bring MIX down until the drums thicken without losing their attack.
  • If kick or bass makes the whole signal duck, raise SC HPF to 90 - 150 Hz.

File: gfx-vb30.jsfx

Dynamics

Wingspan

A dynamic mid/side width processor that opens the stereo image (or pulls it in) when the music gets loud.

Overview

Wingspan splits the stereo signal into Mid (what is common to both sides - the centre) and Side (the difference - the width). It then turns the Side up or down as the level rises above a threshold, so the image can open out when a chorus lifts, or pull in towards the centre when things get loud and busy.

A detector listens to the Mid, the Side or a blend of the two. When it passes THRESHOLD, the Side moves by AMOUNT: positive amounts widen, negative amounts narrow. The movement is smoothed in decibels and rounded off, so the image glides rather than pumps or clicks. A static BASE WIDTH, a LOW MONO filter that keeps the bass in the centre, and a parallel MIX complete the chain.

The Mid is never touched, so the mono sum stays exactly as it was: Wingspan is mono-safe by design. It has no drift - like the other mastering tools it is deliberately steady - and no latency unless the output stage is set to LIMIT.

Reach for it for

  • Choruses that open out wider than the verses
  • Reining in spiky, over-wide moments on a master or bus
  • A little extra width on a master with the bass kept mono
  • Rooms and ambience that bloom with each drum hit
  • Keeping a loud lead vocal focused by narrowing the sides around it
  • Locking the low end to mono for vinyl, clubs and mono playback

Controls

The header

PRESETS
Opens the preset list.
◄ / ►
Step to the previous or next preset (wrapping round).
Preset display
Shows the preset number and name. Click it for the preset list too.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window. A new instance opens in L.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

DYNAMICS

The sidechain that decides how much the Side moves. Each knob shows its value under its name while you hover over it or turn it.

THRESHOLD
The level the detector must pass before the image starts to move, -60 to 0 dB (-20 dB by default). The change starts gently over a 6 dB soft knee.
AMOUNT
How hard the Side is moved above the threshold, -100 to +100% (+30% by default), with a centre detent at OFF. Towards WIDEN the image opens; towards NARROW it closes. At 100% the Side moves by 0.5 dB for every dB over the threshold, up to +12 dB wider or -18 dB narrower.
ATTACK
How quickly the image moves once over the threshold, 0.5 to 100 ms (15 ms by default).
RELEASE
How quickly it settles back afterwards, 20 ms to 2 s (300 ms by default).
DETECT
What the detector listens to, from MID (the centre - vocals, kick, bass) through a blend (M 70 / S 30 and so on) to SIDE (the width itself). MID by default.
PEAK / RMS (DETECTOR)
PEAK reacts to every instant peak; RMS (the default) averages the level over 8 ms, for smoother, more musical movement.
LEVEL vs THRESHOLD
A 24-segment ladder showing the detector level from -60 to 0 dB, with a line at the THRESHOLD. Segments above the threshold light amber.
MOVING
A lamp that lights while the Side is being moved by more than a quarter of a dB.

STEREO FIELD

Displays only, all showing the output.

VECTORSCOPE
The output plotted with Mid upwards and Side across, auto-scaled to the level. A mono signal is a vertical line; the wider the image, the more it spreads sideways. The amber "wings" show the current width angle: straight up is mono.
SIDE GAIN 4 s
The last four seconds of side gain, +12 to -18 dB, newest on the right. Amber above the 0 line is wider, cream below it narrower.
CORRELATION
-1 to +1. Near +1 is close to mono, around 0 is wide; it turns red below 0, where the sides are working against each other and the mono sum loses level.
SIDE GAIN readout
The side gain right now, in dB.
IMAGE WIDTH readout
The resulting width: BASE WIDTH with the current side gain added, as a percentage (100% = as it came in).
Gain-change bar
The same side gain as a bar from -18 (NARROWER) to +12 (WIDER), with 0 in the middle.

WIDTH & OUTPUT

BASE WIDTH
A static width, 0 to 200% (100% by default, with a detent there). 0% is mono, 100% leaves the image as it is, 200% doubles the Side.
PRE / POST (WIDTH STAGE)
Where BASE WIDTH sits. PRE: before the detector, so a detector listening to the Side hears the widened image. POST (the default): after the dynamic stage, so the detector hears the original image. With DETECT on MID it makes no difference.
LOW MONO OFF / 80 / 120 / 200
Keeps the bass mono below 80, 120 or 200 Hz by filtering it out of the Side with a steep 24 dB per octave filter (OFF by default). Switching it fades in and out without a click.
MIX
Parallel blend with the untouched input, 0 to 100% (100% by default).
OUTPUT
Output level, -12 to +12 dB (0 dB by default).
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT (OUT STAGE)
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (no latency), or a lookahead limiter that holds them at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). OFF by default; the CLIP key flashes red while it is clipping.
IN / OUT meters
Left and right peak meters for the input and the output, -48 to +3 dB; segments above 0 dB light red.

Knobs: drag up or down (Shift for fine moves) or use the mouse wheel; double-click or Ctrl-click to reset a knob to its default.

Presets

Presets set everything in the DYNAMICS and WIDTH sections and MIX. Init also resets OUTPUT and the output stage; the others leave them as they are.

Init
The defaults: -20 dB threshold, +30% widening, listening to the Mid in RMS, no low mono.
Wide Choruses
+60% from the Mid with a slow 600 ms release, slightly wider base, low mono at 120 Hz: the image opens as the song lifts.
Tame Wide Peaks
-50% listening to the Side in PEAK with a fast attack: spiky bursts of width are reined in.
Gentle Master Width
+25%, a 30% Side blend in the detector, slow and smooth, base 110% with low mono at 80 Hz.
Mono-Safe Lows
A light +15% with base 115%, and the bass locked mono below 120 Hz.
Drum Room Bloom
+70% in PEAK with a 2 ms attack and MIX at 70%: each hit blooms the room, in parallel.
Pad Spread
Low threshold, slow attack and 1.5 s release, base 130% in PRE, low mono at 200 Hz: the swell spreads further.
Narrow Loud Bits
-60% above -14 dB: loud passages pull towards the centre.
Vocal Centre Lock
-35%, fast: a loud centre (usually the vocal) narrows the sides around it.
Wide When Quiet
Base 125% with -40% above -24 dB: wide verses that steady up when the track gets big.

Tips

  • Start with Init and watch LEVEL vs THRESHOLD: set THRESHOLD so only the loud sections light amber.
  • Use DETECT on MID to follow the song's energy (vocals, kick, bass); use SIDE to react to the width itself, for example to tame it.
  • Keep an eye on CORRELATION: if it heads below 0, back off AMOUNT or BASE WIDTH.
  • On a master, turn LOW MONO to 80 or 120 Hz whenever you widen, so the bass stays solid in mono and on vinyl.
  • Because the Mid is untouched, a mono fold-down sounds the same whatever Wingspan is doing - only the width changes.

File: gfx-wingspan.jsfx

EQ & filters

Baxendale

A simple two-knob hi-fi tone control for single tracks.

Overview

Baxendale is a bass and treble tone control in the spirit of the classic hi-fi tone circuit. Its curves are broad 6 dB per octave shelves that keep rising gently towards the extremes rather than flattening off, so a little goes a long way and the middle of the spectrum is left alone.

Each control has a TURNOVER switch that sets where it is half-way to its full effect. With the bass at 75 Hz and the treble at 6 kHz it only touches the very ends; at 300 Hz and 1.5 kHz it reshapes much more of the sound.

A slow drift makes the turnover points and gains wander very slightly, left and right independently, like real components warming up.

Reach for it for

  • Quick, musical bass and treble shaping on a single track
  • Adding weight or air without touching the mids
  • Gentle broad tilts on vocals, keys and acoustic instruments
  • Taming boomy or harsh recordings with a broad cut

Controls

Tone

BASS
Boosts or cuts the low end, -12 to +12 dB in 0.5 dB steps (hold Shift for free movement). The curve is a broad shelf, so the lowest notes move most.
TURNOVER (bass)
Where the bass control is half-way to its full effect: 75, 150 or 300 Hz. Click a label to pick it, or click / drag / scroll the switch. 75 Hz for just the bottom; 300 Hz to reshape the whole low end.
TREBLE
Boosts or cuts the top end, -12 to +12 dB in 0.5 dB steps (Shift for free movement).
TURNOVER (treble)
Where the treble control is half-way to its full effect: 1.5k, 3k or 6k. 6k adds or removes air; 1.5k brightens or darkens much more of the sound.
IN
Switches the tone controls in or out (in by default). When out, the tone section is dimmed and the audio passes flat; OUTPUT still works.

Output

OUTPUT
Output level, -12 to +12 dB. Use it to match the level after a boost or cut.
LEVEL
A row of ten LEDs showing the output peak level, from -30 dB up to just under 0 dBFS (green, then amber, then red).

Drift

The bass and treble turnover points and gains wander very slightly and slowly, separately on the left and right sides.

DRIFT
How much drift, 0 to 100% (50% by default). 50% is the standard amount, 100% is twice that and 0 switches it off. The wander also speeds up a little as you turn it up.

IN / OUT

TRIM
Input level before anything else, from -24 to +12 dB. A single click on the knob (without dragging) switches GUARD on or off; its LED sits at the knob's upper right.
GUARD
On by default. Gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources. The LED is red while it stands by and glows brighter while it is working.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper at -0.3 dBFS (no latency), or a lookahead limiter holding the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). The CLIP key flashes red while the clipper is working.

The header

S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Tips

  • Start with small moves: because the shelves keep rising towards the extremes, 2 or 3 dB is often all a track needs.
  • For air without harshness, set the treble TURNOVER to 6k and add a few dB; for a darker, warmer tone, cut with it at 1.5k.
  • Cutting BASS with TURNOVER at 300 Hz is a quick way to thin out a muddy guitar or piano in a busy mix.
  • Switch IN on and off at matched levels (use OUTPUT) to judge whether the change really helps.

File: gfx-baxendale.jsfx

EQ & filters

Bern EQ

A three-band broadcast channel equaliser with transformer-coupled input and output stages.

Overview

Bern EQ is the channel equaliser in the spirit of the classic Swiss broadcast consoles, and the companion of the Bern 16 summing mixer. It is musical, very open and very clean: a LOW section that is either a gentle shelf or a high-pass filter, a sweepable MID presence bell, and a smooth HIGH shelf for air.

The EQ sits between two amplifiers, as on the desk. The input amplifier has about 14 dB of headroom above 0 dBFS, so it is transparent at normal levels; push it with INPUT and a little even-harmonic warmth comes in, then a soft, round limit. The output amplifier feeds a transformer whose core thickens the lows first when it is driven.

The bands are carefully matched to their analogue curves, so the bell and the high shelf keep their true shape right up to the top of the audio range. Every control glides, so sweeping and switching never click.

Reach for it for

  • Clean, broad tone shaping of vocals, guitars, piano and buses
  • Adding glassy air with the high shelf without harshness
  • Clearing rumble with the high-pass before anything else
  • Presence and focus with the MID bell
  • A touch of console warmth by driving the input
  • Matching channels with Bern 16 for a whole-desk sound

Controls

The header

PRESETS
Opens the preset list.
◄ / ►
Step to the previous or next preset (wrapping round).
Preset display
Shows the preset number and name. Click it for the preset list too.
EQ IN
Switches the three bands in or out (in by default). The amplifiers, the transformer and the OUT level stay in either way, as on the desk, so you hear the EQ alone when you compare.
DRIFT
How much the supply voltage wanders, 0 to 100% (20% by default). Its value shows beside it while you turn it. See Drift below.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (no latency), or a lookahead limiter that holds them at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). CLIP is on by default; its key flashes red while it is clipping.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window. A new instance opens in L.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Response window

Shows the EQ curve from 20 Hz to 20 kHz, plus and minus 18 dB. Each band is drawn faintly in its own colour (LOW blue, or yellow for the high-pass, MID green, HIGH red) with the total curve as the bright line, and a dot marks each band's frequency. The window reads EQ OUT and the curve goes flat when EQ IN is off.

INPUT

TRIM
Level into the input amplifier, -18 to +18 dB (0 dB by default). Turn it up to drive the amplifier for warmth.
WARMTH lamp
Glows amber as the input amplifier works harder. Off means it is clean.
Signal flow
IN AMP, EQ, IRON, OUT: the order the signal takes. EQ dims while EQ IN is off.

LOW

The LOW section is either a shelf or a high-pass filter. Pressing a SHELF key picks the shelf; pressing a HIGH-PASS key picks the filter. Switching between them crossfades without a click.

GAIN
Boost or cut for the low shelf, -15 to +15 dB (0 dB by default). It does nothing in high-pass mode, and its cap fades to show that.
SHELF 60 / 100
A gentle low shelf at 60 or 100 Hz (100 Hz by default; the shelf is the default mode).
HIGH-PASS 40 / 80 / 120
A clean 12 dB per octave high-pass at 40, 80 or 120 Hz, in place of the shelf.

MID PRESENCE

GAIN
Boost or cut, -15 to +15 dB (0 dB by default).
FREQ
The centre of the bell, 150 Hz to 7 kHz (1.2 kHz by default).
BROAD / NARROW (Q)
The width of the bell. BROAD (the default) is a musical, moderately wide curve; NARROW is about two and a half times tighter, for more focused work.

HIGH

GAIN
Boost or cut, -15 to +15 dB (0 dB by default).
HINGE 10 / 15 / 20 kHz
Where the high shelf is half-way to its full gain (15 kHz by default). The slope is gentle, with no bump, so a boost reaches well down into the upper mids: 10 kHz is the most obvious, 20 kHz the most airy.

OUT

LEVEL
Output level after the output amplifier and transformer, -18 to +18 dB (0 dB by default).
Meter
A 16-segment output meter, about -30 to 0 dBFS: green, amber near the top and red at the top two segments.

Drift

DRIFT lets the supply voltage wander slowly and smoothly, as on a real console. As it moves, the band gains ease by up to 2.5% and the band frequencies by up to 0.6% (at 100%), and the amplifiers' headroom rises and falls by up to 2 dB, so a hot signal softens a little more when the rail sags. At 0% the EQ stays exactly where you set it.

Knobs: drag up or down (Shift for fine moves) or use the mouse wheel; double-click to reset a knob to its default.

Presets

Presets set INPUT, the three bands and the OUT level, and switch EQ IN on. Init also resets DRIFT and the output stage; the others leave them as they are.

Init
The defaults: everything flat, 100 Hz shelf, 1.2 kHz BROAD bell, 15 kHz hinge.
Vocal Air
High-pass at 80 Hz, +1.5 dB at 3.2 kHz and +5 dB of air at 15 kHz; OUT -1 dB.
Broadcast Voice
INPUT +3 dB for a little warmth, high-pass at 120 Hz, +3 dB at 2.5 kHz, +2 dB at 10 kHz; OUT -4 dB.
Kick Weight
+6 dB at 60 Hz, -3.5 dB of box at 320 Hz, a little top at 10 kHz.
Guitar Presence
High-pass at 80 Hz, +3.5 dB at 2.2 kHz, +1.5 dB at 10 kHz.
Bass Clean-Up
High-pass at 40 Hz, -3 dB at 250 Hz and -2 dB at 10 kHz to tidy the top.
Mix Bus Sheen
+1 dB at 60 Hz, a hair less at 3 kHz and +3 dB of air at 20 kHz.
Telephone
INPUT +6 dB, high-pass at 120 Hz, a big NARROW +10 dB at 1.5 kHz and -15 dB at 10 kHz; OUT -8 dB.
De-Mud
-5 dB at 300 Hz with a slight low trim and +1.5 dB at 15 kHz.
Piano Open
A little less low end, -2 dB at 600 Hz and +3 dB at 15 kHz.
Warm Drive
INPUT +4 dB into the amplifier, +2.5 dB at 60 Hz, +1 dB at 1.8 kHz, +2 dB at 20 kHz; OUT -4 dB to match.

Tips

  • For a vocal, start with HIGH-PASS 80 and add HIGH gain at 15 kHz before reaching for the MID bell.
  • The high shelf is very gentle, so you can boost more than you would expect before it gets harsh.
  • To hear the amplifiers, turn TRIM up until the WARMTH lamp glows, then bring LEVEL down by the same amount.
  • Use EQ IN to compare: the amplifiers stay in circuit, so you hear only what the bands are doing.
  • Set DRIFT to 0 for a static, repeatable EQ; leave a little in for a less clinical, hardware-like feel.

File: gfx-bern-eq.jsfx

EQ & filters

Board EQ

A four-band channel EQ in the spirit of the classic British large-format console strips.

Overview

Board EQ is a console channel EQ: a high-pass and low-pass filter, a low and high band that can be shelves or bells, and two fully parametric mid bands. It ends in the channel's line amp, which can be driven into gentle saturation.

Two voicings recreate the two well-known generations of console EQ card. BROWN is the earlier, gentler card, with broad bells and soft shelves. BLACK is the later card: the bells tighten as you boost or cut further, and the shelves have a little dip or bump just inside the corner, for a more focused, punchy sound.

The display on the right shows the response of the whole EQ, with a coloured dot for each band.

Reach for it for

  • Everyday channel EQ on vocals, drums, bass and guitars
  • Punchy, focused boosts with the BLACK voicing
  • Broad, musical tone shaping with the BROWN voicing
  • Cleaning up the ends with the HPF and LPF
  • A touch of line-amp warmth (DRIVE)

Controls

FILTERS

HPF
A high-pass filter, 18 dB per octave, from OFF (fully left) up to 400 Hz. Removes rumble and low-end mud.
LPF
A low-pass filter, 12 dB per octave, from 3 kHz up to OFF (fully right). Tames hiss and harsh top.

LF

GAIN
-15 to +15 dB.
FREQ
30 to 450 Hz (100 Hz by default).
BELL
Off (default): a low shelf. On: a bell centred on FREQ.

LMF

GAIN
-15 to +15 dB.
FREQ
200 Hz to 2.5 kHz (600 Hz by default).
Q
The bandwidth, 0.4 to 3 (1 by default). Higher is narrower. In BLACK the bell also narrows as you boost or cut more.

HMF

GAIN
-15 to +15 dB.
FREQ
600 Hz to 7 kHz (2.5 kHz by default).
Q
The bandwidth, 0.4 to 3 (1 by default).

HF

GAIN
