-15 to +15 dB.
FREQ
1.5 to 16 kHz (8 kHz by default).
BELL
Off (default): a high shelf. On: a bell centred on FREQ.

LINE AMP

DRIVE
Pushes the channel's line amp into gentle saturation, 0 to 100%. It is level matched, so it adds colour rather than volume.
OUTPUT
Output level, -12 to +12 dB.
EQ IN
Switches the four EQ bands in or out (in by default). The HPF and LPF stay in.
BROWN / BLACK
The voicing (BLACK by default). BROWN: broad bells and soft shelves. BLACK: bells that tighten as you boost or cut (proportional Q), and shelves with a small opposite dip just inside the corner.

Display

Response curve
The frequency response of the filters, the EQ and OUTPUT together, from 20 Hz to 20 kHz and -18 to +18 dB. While the EQ is in, a dot in each band's cap colour shows its frequency and gain.

The header

PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets; the arrows step through them and the display shows the one in use (click it for the list too). A preset sets all the EQ and line-amp controls (and puts the EQ in, OUTPUT at 0 dB). The last entry resets everything to the defaults.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Board EQ has no input or output stage on its panel: there is no TRIM, SAT or OUT stage, as the line amp does that job. An input guard runs in the background (on by default), gently easing very hot sources down so they never overdrive the line amp.

Presets

Vocal Presence
HPF 90 Hz, a little 200 Hz and 350 Hz cut, a 3.5 kHz lift and a 12 kHz shelf; BLACK.
Kick Punch
A 60 Hz bell boost, a deep cut at 380 Hz and a 3 kHz click; BLACK with a little drive.
Snare Crack
HPF 80 Hz, body at 200 Hz, a 5 kHz crack and a 10 kHz shelf; BLACK with drive.
Bass Weight
LPF 9 kHz, a low shelf boost at 80 Hz, 250 Hz cut, 1.2 kHz definition; BROWN with drive.
Acoustic Sparkle
HPF 70 Hz, a little low and low-mid cut, broad 4 kHz and a 12 kHz shelf boost; BROWN.
Mix Bus Air
Gentle low shelf, a slight 3 kHz dip and a 14 kHz air shelf; BROWN with a little drive.
Telephone
HPF 400 Hz and LPF 3.5 kHz with a 1.5 kHz boost and heavy line-amp drive.
De-mud
HPF 60 Hz and a 5 dB cut at 300 Hz; BLACK.
Brown Warmth
LPF 16 kHz, low shelf boost, broad mids and a soft top; BROWN with 40% drive.
De-harsh
HPF 100 Hz, a narrow 4 dB cut at 6.5 kHz and a 15 kHz air shelf; BLACK.
Reset all to defaults
Every control back to its starting value.

Tips

  • Try the same settings in BROWN and BLACK: BROWN for gentle, broad shaping, BLACK when a boost needs to be focused and punchy.
  • In BLACK, big cuts get narrow on their own, so you can cut a resonance hard without hollowing out the sound around it.
  • Use EQ IN to compare with and without the bands; the filters stay in, so the comparison is fair.
  • A little DRIVE (10 - 25%) adds density without obvious distortion.

File: gfx-board-q.jsfx

EQ & filters

Contour

A drawn-envelope filter: four hand-drawn curves move cutoff, resonance, volume and pan in time with the music.

Overview

Contour is a filter driven by curves you draw. Four curves run at once - CUTOFF, RESONANCE, VOLUME and PAN - each looping over a length you set, from a 1/32 note to 32 bars. Draw a pluck, a sweep, a stutter or a rhythmic gate, and Contour plays it back in time with the project, or restarts it on every MIDI note.

The filter itself is a 4x oversampled state-variable design with low-pass, high-pass, band-pass and notch modes. A diode-style soft clipper sits inside its feedback loop, so DRIVE adds analogue-style grit and resonance stays musical when pushed.

Each curve has its own amount knob, so you can switch any of them on or off without losing the drawing, and DEPTH scales all four together.

Reach for it for

  • Rhythmic filter sweeps and plucks on pads, synths and loops
  • Tempo-synced volume gating and ducking shapes (draw the dips)
  • Auto-panning with any shape you like, not just a sine
  • Triggered filter envelopes from MIDI notes (MIDI mode, one-shot)
  • Wide, moving stereo filtering with STEREO offset

Controls

Curve tabs and the editor

The four tabs - CUTOFF, RESONANCE, VOLUME and PAN - pick which curve you are editing. The light on each tab shows that curve is in use (its amount knob is not at 0). The curve being edited is drawn filled in its colour; the other three show faintly behind it. The grid divides one cycle into 16 steps, with every fourth line brighter. The white line is the playhead; a fainter second line shows the right channel's place when STEREO is up. Top right, the current RATE, trigger mode and LOOP / ONE-SHOT are shown.

Click
Adds a point (up to 32 per curve). Keep the mouse down to drag it straight into place.
Drag a point
Moves it. The first and last points stay at the edges of the cycle; you can only move them up and down.
Double-click a point
Removes it (not the first or last).
Drag a line
Bends the line between two points into a curve. Dragging up lifts the middle of the line, whichever way it slopes.
Double-click a line
Straightens it again.
Right-click
A menu: Reset this curve (back to its starting shape), Flat (top), Flat (middle), Flat (bottom), Copy curve and Paste curve. Use copy and paste to move a shape from one tab to another.

The starting shapes: CUTOFF is a pluck (falls quickly from the top to the bottom), RESONANCE and VOLUME are flat, and PAN moves from centre to the right, across to the left and back to centre. The curves are saved with the project.

Filter

MODE
LP 6, LP 12, LP 18, LP 24 (low-pass, 6 to 24 dB per octave), HP 12 (high-pass), BP 12 (band-pass) or NOTCH. Starts on LP 12.
CUTOFF
The filter's base frequency, 20 Hz to 20 kHz. The cutoff curve moves the filter up (or down) from here.
RESO
Resonance, 0 - 100%. Higher settings add a whistling peak at the cutoff.
DRIVE
Pushes the input harder into the filter, 0 to +24 dB, so the soft clipper inside the filter adds grit. The level is compensated, so it changes the character more than the loudness.

Curve amounts

Each amount knob sits under a stripe in its curve's colour. At 0 that curve does nothing.

CUTOFF
How far the cutoff curve moves the filter, -8 to +8 octaves (starts at +4). The bottom of the curve is the CUTOFF knob's setting and the top is CUTOFF plus this amount. Negative amounts turn the curve upside down.
RESO
How far the resonance curve moves the resonance, -100 to +100%, added to the RESO knob.
VOLUME
How far the volume curve can turn the level down, 0 - 100%. The top of the curve is full level; the bottom is the level minus this amount. Draw dips to duck or gate.
PAN
How far the pan curve moves the sound, 0 - 100%. The middle of the curve is centre; the top pans to the right and the bottom to the left, fully at 100%.
DEPTH
Scales all four amounts at once, 0 - 100%, without changing the drawings. Good for automating the whole effect in and out.
STEREO
Moves the right channel's place in the curves later, up to half a cycle (0 - 50%). The cutoff, resonance and volume then move differently on each side, for wide, moving filtering. (PAN follows the left channel's place.)

Timing

RATE
The length of one cycle of the curves: 1/32, 1/16, 1/8, 1/4, 1/2, 1 BAR, 2, 4, 8, 16 or 32 BARS. Starts at 1 BAR.
SYNC
The curves follow the project's timeline, so they line up with the bars wherever you start playback. When the project is stopped, they keep running at the project tempo.
MIDI
Every MIDI note-on restarts the curves from the beginning. Put Contour on a track that receives MIDI (or send MIDI to it) and each note triggers the shape, like a filter envelope. MIDI passes through unchanged.
FREE
The curves run at the project tempo, without following the timeline - even when the project is stopped.
LOOP / ONE-SHOT
LOOP repeats the curves. ONE-SHOT (with MIDI) plays them once per note and then holds the end of the curve until the next note.
TRIGGER
Restarts the curves by hand. In SYNC mode while playing, it moves where the cycle starts to the current position.
MIDI NOTE IN
Lights when a MIDI note arrives.

IN / OUT

TRIM
Input level before anything else, from -24 to +12 dB.
GUARD
Click the TRIM knob (without moving it) to switch the guard; its light sits at the knob's upper right. Off by default on Contour. When on, it gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the filter. It never boosts quiet sources. The light glows brighter while it is catching peaks.
MIX
Dry / filtered balance, 0 - 100%. The VOLUME and PAN curves act on the mixed signal.
OUTPUT
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper (no latency), or a lookahead limiter that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). CLIP is on by default; its key flashes red while it is clipping.
IN / OUT meters
Input and output level.

The header

PRESETS, arrows and display
PRESETS (or a click on the display) opens the list of factory presets; the ◄ / ► keys step through them. The display shows the preset number and name. See Presets below.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Presets

A preset sets the filter, the timing, the curve amounts, DEPTH and MIX, and redraws all four curves: loading one replaces the curves you have drawn. Every preset uses SYNC and LOOP. TRIM, GUARD, OUTPUT and the output stage are left as they are. A new instance opens on Init.

Init
The defaults: LP 12 at 400 Hz, the pluck curve on the cutoff over one bar.
Acid Pluck 1/16
LP 24 low down with high resonance and some drive, the pluck curve on every 1/16.
Trance Gate 1/16
The filter wide open; the volume curve is on for the first half of each 1/16 and off for the second.
Sidechain Pump 1/4
The filter wide open; the volume ducks at the start of each beat and swells back over the first half of it.
4-Bar Sweep
LP 24 with some resonance and drive: the cutoff opens and closes over four bars, a little offset in stereo.
Stereo Wah 1/4
BP 12 with high resonance, sweeping up and down on each beat, the right side offset by half a cycle.
HP Riser 8 Bars
HP 12 rising from 40 Hz over eight bars, for a build.
Ping-Pong Pan 1/8
The filter wide open; the pan curve jumps left then right on each 1/8.

Using MIDI triggering

  1. Put Contour on the audio track you want to shape (for example a pad or a synth).
  2. Give that track MIDI: draw a MIDI item on it, or send MIDI to it from another track (in the send, set the MIDI to go to the track).
  3. Choose MIDI in the TIMING section, and ONE-SHOT if you want the shape to play once per note.
  4. Play: MIDI NOTE IN lights and the curves restart on every note-on. Set RATE to the length you want the shape to take.

Tips

  • For a classic synth pluck, use the default CUTOFF curve, MODE LP 24, a low CUTOFF and RATE 1/8 or 1/16.
  • For a sidechain-style pump without a key input, draw a dip at the start of the VOLUME curve, set VOLUME to 60 - 100% and RATE to 1/4.
  • Bend the lines rather than adding lots of points: a single bent line gives a smooth exponential sweep.
  • Draw the shape once, then use DEPTH to bring it in gradually through a song.
  • Small STEREO settings (5 - 15%) widen the filter movement without making it sound like two separate effects.

File: gfx-contour.jsfx

EQ & filters

Equaliser

A musical four-band parametric with overlapping bands, notches, shelves, low and high cuts, and mid / side.

Overview

Equaliser is a four-band parametric in the spirit of the classic 70s broadcast parametrics. The four bands - LF, LMF, HMF and HF - have overlapping ranges, so two bands can be stacked on the same area. The width is 'proportional': small boosts and cuts are gentle and broad, and the band gets narrower as you push the gain further. The last part of each GAIN knob's cut dives into a deep notch, down to -40 dB, for removing hum or a single ringing frequency.

LF and HF switch between a bell and a shelf. Separate low-cut and high-cut filters, each with a choice of four slopes, sit in front of the bands. In M / S mode each band can work on both, the mid only or the sides only. You can also work straight on the curve: drag a band's dot to set its frequency and gain.

Reach for it for

  • Broad, musical tone shaping on vocals, instruments and buses
  • Notching out hum or a single ringing frequency
  • Cleaning up the lows and highs with the LOW CUT and HIGH CUT filters
  • Mid / side work: tightening the low end of the sides, adding air to the width
  • Lo-fi telephone and radio sounds

Controls

The bands: LF, LMF, HMF, HF

Each band has its own colour (brown, blue, green and red), used for its knob caps, its dot and its shaded curve on the display.

FREQ
The band's frequency. LF: 20 - 500 Hz (100 Hz by default). LMF: 80 Hz - 2 kHz (400 Hz). HMF: 400 Hz - 8 kHz (2.5 kHz). HF: 1.5 - 20 kHz (8 kHz). The ranges overlap, so bands can be stacked.
GAIN
Boost or cut, -12 to +16 dB in the normal range. Past -12 dB the knob dives into a notch: the last quarter of the cut goes down to -40 dB (shown as NOTCH).
WIDTH
The band's width in octaves, 0.05 to 4 (1 by default). Small values are narrow; large values are broad. Bells get narrower anyway as the gain is pushed. On a shelf, WIDTH sets how gentle the slope is.
SHELF (LF and HF only)
Switches the band between a bell and a shelf. Both start as shelves.
ST / MID / SIDE
Shown in M / S mode only. What the band works on: ST both, MID the centre (what is common to both sides), SIDE the sides (the difference).

FILTERS

LOW CUT
A high-pass filter, from OFF (fully left, 20 Hz) up to 1 kHz.
HIGH CUT
A low-pass filter, from 2 kHz up to OFF (fully right, 20 kHz).
12 / 24 / 36 / 48
The slope of each filter in dB per octave, next to its knob. 12 is gentle; 48 is very steep. The filters have no bump at the corner.

The display

The curve covers 20 Hz to 20 kHz and plus or minus 18 dB. Each band that is boosting or cutting is shaded in its own colour, with the total curve drawn on top. In M / S mode, when any band works on the mid or side only, a second purple curve shows the side and the labels MID and SIDE appear.

Band dots
Drag a band's dot to set its frequency (left and right) and gain (up and down). Shelves show a square dot.
Mouse wheel on a dot
Changes the band's WIDTH.
STEREO / M / S
Under the curve. STEREO works on left and right; M / S lets each band work on the mid or the sides.

IN / OUT

TRIM
Input level before anything else, from -24 to +12 dB.
GUARD
Click the TRIM knob (without moving it) to switch the guard; its light sits at the knob's upper right and is lit while it is on. Off by default. Gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources. The light glows brighter while it is catching peaks.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT (OUT)
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper (no latency), or a lookahead limiter that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). CLIP is on by default; its key flashes red while it is clipping.

Drift

A slow random wander, like an unsteady power supply: the band frequencies and gains move together. Bands set flat stay flat.

DRIFT
How much drift, 0 to 100%. 50% (the default) is the standard amount, 100% is twice that, 0 switches it off. More drift also makes the wander a little faster.

Output

Meter
Output level, left and right.
LEVEL
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.

The preset row and colour strip

PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets (a click on the display does the same). Choosing one sets the bands, filters and STEREO / M / S; drift, output level and the IN / OUT section are left as they are. The last entry resets everything to the defaults.
◄ / ►
Step to the previous or next preset.
Colour swatch
Changes the faceplate colour: GREEN, BLUE or one of the GFX themes.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Next to the S / M / L size keys. Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.
HW / FLAT
Switches between the hardware-style panel and the flat look.

Presets

Vocal Presence
Low cut at 80 Hz, a little less low end and mud, +3 dB at 3 kHz and a +3 dB air shelf at 10 kHz.
Kick Weight
+4 dB bell at 60 Hz, -4 dB at 350 Hz, +3 dB click at 3.5 kHz; low cut at 30 Hz.
Bass Clarity
A little low shelf, -3 dB at 250 Hz, +2 dB at 800 Hz for definition, a slightly softer top.
Acoustic Sparkle
Lows and boxiness trimmed, +2 dB at 5 kHz and a +4 dB shelf at 12 kHz; low cut at 70 Hz.
Mix Air
A broad +3 dB shelf at 15 kHz, low cut at 25 Hz.
Mud Cut
-4 dB at 300 Hz, low cut at 50 Hz.
Telephone
Low cut at 400 Hz and high cut at 3.5 kHz (24 dB per octave) with +6 dB at 1.5 kHz.
De-Harsh
A narrow -4 dB cut at 3.2 kHz and -3 dB at 7 kHz.
Hum Notch 50 Hz
Deep, narrow notches at 50 Hz and 100 Hz.
M/S Wide Air
M / S mode, all on the sides: a -12 dB low shelf at 120 Hz, a little less mud and a +4 dB air shelf at 10 kHz.
M/S Centre Focus
M / S mode: a -10 dB low shelf at 150 Hz on the sides and +2 dB presence at 2 kHz on the mid.
Reset all to defaults
Every control back to its starting value.

Tips

  • Because the width is proportional, small moves stay broad and musical - you rarely need to touch WIDTH for gentle tone shaping.
  • To find a ringing frequency, boost a narrow band, sweep it until the ring jumps out, then turn GAIN right down into the notch.
  • Stack two bands on the same area (the ranges overlap) for a bigger or more complex boost or cut.
  • In M / S mode, try a low shelf cut on SIDE to keep the low end centred and solid.

File: gfx-equaliser.jsfx

EQ & filters

Graphic EQ

A 31-band third-octave graphic equaliser with a real-time analyser behind the faders.

Overview

Graphic EQ is a 31-band, third-octave graphic equaliser in the spirit of the classic studio and live-sound rack graphics. There is one fader for every ISO third-octave band from 20 Hz to 20 kHz, so you can see the shape of your EQ at a glance and draw it with the mouse.

The bands are matched: on a real graphic, neighbouring bands overlap and add up, so the curve you hear is not quite the curve of the faders. Here the overlap is corrected, so the response follows the faders. The yellow line in the display shows the real response, over a live analyser of the output in each band.

Two Q modes cover both sides of the job: proportional Q for smooth tone shaping, constant Q for precise notching. A high-pass and low-pass filter round off the ends.

Reach for it for

  • Broad tone shaping you can see at a glance
  • Notching out feedback and ringing frequencies (CONST Q)
  • Shaping a whole mix, a bus or a PA-style curve
  • Quick 'smile', loudness and telephone curves
  • Spotting where the energy sits with the analyser

Controls

The display

Behind the faders, a column of 16 LEDs per band shows the output level in that band (-72 to 0 dB), green, amber and red. The yellow line is the EQ's actual response, including the cut filters and the output level. It goes flat when EQ IN is off.

The faders

Faders
One per third-octave band, 20 Hz to 20 kHz, labelled with its ISO frequency. Drag a fader cap to move it (Shift for fine moves). Double-click a cap to centre it. The cap lights in the accent colour when its band is not at 0. The value shows above the band under the pointer.
Drawing a curve
Click or drag anywhere in the fader area away from a cap to draw a curve across the bands - the faders follow the mouse.
Mouse wheel
Nudges the band under the pointer (Shift for fine steps).
Scale
The figures at both ends of the fader area show the fader travel in dB; they change with RANGE.

Controls (bottom row)

+-6 dB / +-12 dB (RANGE)
The fader travel: +-6 dB (fine) or +-12 dB (the default). Switching it rescales the whole curve.
PROP Q / CONST Q
PROP (proportional, the default): the bands are broad for small moves and tighten as you push them further, which is smooth for tone shaping. CONST (constant): every band keeps its third-octave width at any setting, which is precise for notching.
LOW CUT (yellow cap)
An 18 dB per octave high-pass filter, 20 to 250 Hz. Fully left is OFF.
HIGH CUT
A 12 dB per octave low-pass filter, 3 to 22 kHz. Fully right is OFF.
OUTPUT
The level after the EQ, -12 to +12 dB.
FLAT
Centres every fader at once.
EQ IN
Switches the EQ and its cut filters in or out. OUTPUT and the IN / OUT section stay in.

IN / OUT

TRIM
Input level before anything else, -24 to +12 dB.
GUARD
On by default. A single click on the TRIM knob switches it; its LED sits at the knob's upper right (dark when off, red when on, brighter while it is catching). It gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out, 0 to 100%: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper at -0.3 dBFS (no latency), or a lookahead limiter that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). While the output clips, the CLIP key flashes red.

The header

PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets. You can also click the preset display. The last entry resets everything to the defaults.
◄ / ►
Step to the previous or next preset (wrapping round).
Theme picker
Changes the panel's colour theme.
HW
Switches between the hardware-style panel and the flat look.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Presets

Presets draw a curve on the faders and set RANGE to +-12 dB, Q, the cut filters, OUTPUT to 0 dB and EQ IN on. The IN / OUT section is left as it is, except Reset.

Smile
Lifted lows (around 90 Hz) and highs (around 9 kHz) with a slight dip at 500 Hz.
Vocal Presence
A lift around 3.5 kHz, a little less at 250 Hz and a touch of top above 12 kHz.
De-mud
A cut of about 5 dB around 300 Hz, and a little at 150 Hz.
Air
A gentle high shelf from around 10 kHz.
Telephone
Everything below 300 Hz and above 3.5 kHz pulled right down, a lift at 1.3 kHz, and LOW CUT at 250 Hz.
Bass Boost
A low shelf of about 7 dB below 80 Hz, with a slight dip at 250 Hz.
Hi-Fi Loudness
Strong lifts at both ends (around 60 Hz and 12 kHz), like a hi-fi loudness switch.
Mid Scoop
A broad dip of about 4 dB centred on 800 Hz.
Warm & Smooth
A little low-end warmth, a dip around 2.8 kHz and a slightly softer top.
Live Feedback Notches
CONST Q with narrow cuts around 250 Hz, 2.5 kHz and 4 kHz, typical feedback areas on stage.
Reset all to defaults
Every control back to its starting value, including the IN / OUT section.

Tips

  • Switch to +-6 dB RANGE for fine mastering moves: the full fader travel then covers only 6 dB.
  • To find a ringing frequency, switch to CONST Q, push one fader right up and sweep along the bands until it rings, then pull that fader down.
  • Draw broad curves with a single drag across the fader area, then fine-tune single bands with the mouse wheel.
  • Use EQ IN to compare, and set OUTPUT so the level matches before you judge.

File: gfx-graphic-eq.jsfx

EQ & filters

Skye

A four-band broadcast parametric equaliser with wide, overlapping band ranges, shelving end bands and high-pass and low-pass filters.

Overview

Skye is a four-band fully parametric equaliser in the spirit of the classic broadcast rack EQs. Each band has its own frequency, bandwidth and gain, and the ranges overlap widely, so any band can reach almost anywhere: band 1 covers 20 - 800 Hz, band 2 80 Hz - 3 kHz, band 3 300 Hz - 10 kHz and band 4 1 - 20 kHz. BAND 1 and BAND 4 can also be switched to low and high shelves, and a high-pass and a low-pass filter, each with a choice of three slopes, bracket the four bands.

Bandwidth is set in octaves, from a deep, narrow notch (0.1 octave) to a broad tilt (4 octaves), with up to 16 dB of boost or cut per band. A response window on the left draws each band in its own colour with the total curve over the top.

Behind the EQ sits a model of the unit's op-amp headroom: it is transparent until a very hot signal nears the rail, and then softens it a little. The DRIFT dial lets the supply voltage wander, as on real hardware.

Reach for it for

  • Broad tone shaping of vocals, instruments and buses
  • Notching out hum and single resonances with a narrow bandwidth
  • Gentle mastering curves (wide bandwidths, small gains)
  • Telephone and radio-voice effects
  • Shaping kick and bass with overlapping low bands
  • Clearing rumble and taming hiss with the HPF and LPF

Controls

The header

PRESETS
Opens the full preset list.
< / >
Step to the previous or next preset (wrapping round).
Preset display
Shows the preset number and name. Click it for the preset list too. While you move a control, it shows that control's name and value for a moment.
EQ IN
Switches the whole equaliser, filters included, in or out. The output level and the output stage stay in either way.
DRIFT
How much the virtual supply voltage wanders, 0 to 100% (20% by default). See Drift below.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper at -0.3 dBFS (no latency), or a lookahead limiter that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). While the output clips, the CLIP key flashes red.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Response window

Shows the EQ curve from 20 Hz to 20 kHz, plus and minus 18 dB. Each band that is in is drawn faintly in its own colour (brown, blue, green, red), and the HPF and LPF in yellow and grey, with the total curve as the bright line. The window reads EQ OUT and the curve goes flat when EQ IN is off.

FILTERS

A high-pass filter (HPF, yellow cap) before the four bands and a low-pass filter (LPF, black cap) after them. Both are out by default.

HPF
The high-pass corner, 10 Hz - 1 kHz (30 Hz by default).
LPF
The low-pass corner, 1 - 22 kHz (18 kHz by default).
x1 / x2 / x4
The slope of each filter: x1 = 12 dB per octave, x2 = 24 dB per octave, x4 = 48 dB per octave. Each is a clean Butterworth filter with no bump at the corner.
IN
Switches that filter in or out.

BAND 1 to BAND 4

The four bands have the same controls; only the frequency range differs. The bands work in series, band 1 first.

FREQ
The centre of the band. BAND 1: 20 - 800 Hz (100 Hz by default). BAND 2: 80 Hz - 3 kHz (500 Hz). BAND 3: 300 Hz - 10 kHz (2.5 kHz). BAND 4: 1 - 20 kHz (8 kHz).
GAIN (coloured cap)
Boost or cut, -16 to +16 dB; 0 dB by default.
BW
The bandwidth in octaves, 0.1 to 4 (1 by default). Small values make a narrow, deep notch or a sharp peak; large values a broad, gentle tilt.
SHELF
BAND 1 and BAND 4 only. Turns the band into a low shelf (BAND 1) or a high shelf (BAND 4) at FREQ. In SHELF mode BW sets the slope of the shelf: lower values give a steeper shelf with a slight bump, higher values a gentler one. BW 1 gives a classic shelf.
IN
Switches the band in or out.

OUTPUT

LEVEL
Output level after the EQ, -12 to +12 dB.
OUT meter
A 20-segment output level meter.

Drift

DRIFT lets the supply voltage wander slowly and smoothly, as on a real rack unit. As it moves, the band gains ease by up to 2.5% and the band centres by up to 0.6% (at 100%), and the op-amp headroom rises and falls by up to 2.5 dB, so a hot signal softens a little more when the rail sags. At 0% the EQ stays exactly where you set it.

Knobs: drag up or down (Shift for fine moves); double-click or Ctrl-click to reset a knob to its default.

Presets

Presets set the four bands and the output level, switch every band in as a bell, and switch both filters out. EQ IN, DRIFT and the output stage are left as they are.

Flat
All four bands at 0 dB in their default positions, output 0 dB.
Vocal Presence
A little cut at 120 Hz and 350 Hz, +3 dB at 3.2 kHz and +2 dB of air at 10 kHz.
Kick and Bass
+4 dB at 60 Hz, -4 dB at 300 Hz and a broad +3 dB at 2.8 kHz for the click.
Air Lift
A gentle lift at 5 kHz and +4 dB around 14 kHz, both broad.
Telephone
Deep, broad cuts at 200 Hz and 6 kHz with a boost at 1.2 and 2.4 kHz: a narrow phone-line voice.
Hum Notch 50 Hz
Very narrow cuts at 50 Hz (-16 dB) and 100 Hz (-12 dB) to remove mains hum.
Mastering Smile
Broad, small moves: +2 dB at 50 Hz and 12 kHz, a slight dip in the mids.
Broadcast Voice
Low end trimmed at 90 Hz, a touch of warmth at 250 Hz, presence at 2.5 kHz and a little less at 7 kHz.

Tips

  • To clean up a vocal, put the HPF in at x2 around 80 - 100 Hz before reaching for any of the bands.
  • For hum, use BW 0.1 and sweep FREQ slowly with a big cut until the hum disappears, then back the gain off as far as you can.
  • Because the ranges overlap, you can stack two bands on the same area - for example two narrow cuts on neighbouring resonances.
  • Use EQ IN to compare: the output level stays in circuit, so set LEVEL to match the loudness before judging.
  • Set DRIFT to 0 for a static, repeatable EQ; leave a little in for a less clinical, hardware-like feel.

File: gfx-skye.jsfx

EQ & filters

Surgical EQ

Six precise parametric bands on a large curve with a live spectrum, mid / side per band and dynamic bells.

Overview

Surgical EQ is a clean, precise equaliser for detailed work: finding and taming resonances, carving space, and shaping the mid and side of a stereo signal separately. Six bands sit on a large curve display over a live spectrum of the output, and you can work directly on the curve by dragging the band dots.

Each band can be a bell, a shelf, a notch or a steep 24 dB per octave cut, and can work on the stereo pair, the mid only or the side only. Bells can also be dynamic: they only move when the band gets loud, so a harsh resonance is cut only when it rings. A high-pass and low-pass filter with selectable slopes sit ahead of the bands.

Reach for it for

  • Notching out ringing resonances, hum or a single harsh frequency
  • Taming harshness or boom only when it happens (dynamic bells)
  • Mid / side work: cleaning the low end of the sides, lifting the centre
  • Steep clean-up filtering with the HPF / LPF at up to 48 dB per octave
  • Finding problem frequencies with SOLO and the analyser

Controls

The display

The curve covers 20 Hz to 20 kHz and plus or minus 24 dB. Each active band is drawn faintly in its own colour, with the total curve on top. When a band is dynamic, a second total curve in the accent colour shows where the EQ is right now. Behind it, the filled spectrum shows the output after the EQ.

Band dots
Each active band has a numbered dot in its colour (round for bells and notches, square for shelves and cuts). Drag a dot to move the band: left and right for frequency, up and down for gain. Hold Shift for fine moves. The dot shows M or S under it when the band works on the mid or side.
Mouse wheel on a dot
Changes the band's Q.
Double-click a dot
Switches that band off.
Double-click empty space
Switches on the first band that is off, as a bell at that frequency and gain.

Band

This section shows the selected band. Clicking or dragging a dot also selects its band.

1 - 6
The six coloured keys pick the band to edit.
Type
Click to choose: OFF, BELL, LOW SHELF, HIGH SHELF, NOTCH, LOW CUT or HIGH CUT. The cuts are 24 dB per octave with no bump at the corner. All six bands start as bells at 0 dB, at 80 Hz, 250 Hz, 800 Hz, 2.5 kHz, 6 kHz and 12 kHz.
SOLO
Hear only the region the selected band works on: a band-pass at its frequency and Q, on its target (stereo, mid or side). Use it to hunt for a resonance, then switch it off. While a band is soloed, the FLAT ALL key in the header becomes SOLO OFF.
FREQ
The band's frequency, 20 Hz to 20 kHz.
GAIN
Boost or cut, -24 to +24 dB. Bells and shelves only - notches and cuts have no gain.
Q
The band's width, 0.1 to 30. Low values are broad and gentle; high values are very narrow, for single resonances. On shelves it sets the shape of the corner (0.3 at least). The cuts have a fixed 24 dB per octave slope instead.
ST / M / S (TARGET)
What the band works on: ST the stereo pair, M the mid (what is common to both sides), S the side (the difference).

Dynamics

For bells only (the section shows BELLS ONLY for other types). A dynamic bell listens to its own band: when the level there goes over THRESH, the band moves away from its GAIN setting by up to RANGE. The further over the threshold, the further it moves - half of RANGE at 6 dB over.

DYN
Makes the selected bell dynamic.
THRESH
The level in the band where it starts to move, -60 to 0 dB.
RANGE
How far the band can move, -24 to +24 dB. Negative cuts a resonance only when it rings; positive lifts the band only when it gets busy.
ATTACK
How fast dynamic bands react, 0.5 - 100 ms. Shared by all bands.
RELEASE
How fast they recover, 10 - 1000 ms. Shared by all bands.

Filters

The high-pass and low-pass come before the six bands. Both are Butterworth filters, with no bump at the corner.

HPF
High-pass filter, 20 Hz (OFF - out of circuit) to 1 kHz.
LPF
Low-pass filter, 1 kHz to 20 kHz (OFF at the top).
SLOPE
Click to step through x1, x2, x3 and x4: 12, 24, 36 or 48 dB per octave. Both start at x2 (24 dB). The key lights while its filter is in.

IN / OUT

TRIM
Input level before anything else, from -24 to +12 dB.
GUARD
Click the TRIM knob (without moving it) to switch the guard; its light sits at the knob's upper right and is lit while it is on. On by default. Gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources. The light glows brighter while it is catching peaks.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper (no latency), or a lookahead limiter that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). CLIP is on by default; its key flashes red while it is clipping.

Master

OUTPUT
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.
OUT meters
Output level, left and right.

The header

Theme picker
Changes the panel's colour theme.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Next to the S / M / L size keys. Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.
HW
Switches between the hardware-style panel and the flat look.
ANALYSER
Shows or hides the spectrum behind the curve.
FLAT ALL
Sets the gain of all six bands to 0 dB (types, frequencies and Q are kept). While a band is soloed this key reads SOLO OFF and ends the solo instead.

Tips

  • To find a resonance: boost a narrow bell (Q 8 or more) by 10 dB or so, sweep it until the problem jumps out, then turn the gain down into a cut.
  • For a resonance that only rings on some notes, use a dynamic bell with GAIN at 0 and a negative RANGE, and set THRESH so it only moves on the loud notes.
  • Cleaning up a stereo mix: a LOW CUT on S at 100 - 150 Hz keeps the low end centred without touching the bass in the middle.
  • SOLO with a narrow Q is a quick way to hear exactly what you are about to cut.
  • Double-click a dot to switch a band off and compare the sound with and without it.

File: gfx-surgical-eq.jsfx

EQ & filters

Tilt EQ

A one-knob tilt equaliser with high- and low-pass filters and mid / side tilt.

Overview

Tilt EQ is in the spirit of the classic one-knob hi-fi and mastering tilt controls. One knob seesaws the whole spectrum around a pivot point: turn it right and the top rises as the bottom falls (brighter), turn it left for darker. It is the quickest way to change the overall balance of a track or a mix without thinking about bands.

High- and low-pass filters with selectable slopes clean up the extremes, and the tilt can work on the whole stereo signal, on the middle only or on the sides only. A response window shows the combined curve.

Reach for it for

  • Quick brightness or warmth changes on a mix bus or master
  • Matching the tonal balance of different takes or stems
  • Brightening the centre or darkening the sides (MID / SIDE)
  • Steep high- and low-pass clean-up
  • Telephone and band-limited effects

Controls

Response window

Shows the combined curve of the filters, the tilt and OUTPUT from 20 Hz to 20 kHz, on a +/- 8 dB scale. The small triangle at the bottom marks the PIVOT.

High-pass

FREQ
The high-pass cutoff, 10 - 1000 Hz. Fully left = OFF.
Slope
The knob beside it: 6, 12 (default), 18, 24, 36 or 48 dB/oct. All slopes are Butterworth (maximally flat).

Tilt

TILT
The big knob. Right is BRIGHTER, left is DARKER; the word under it shows which, or FLAT at the centre. The value is the lift or cut at each end, up to 6 dB (12 dB end to end); hover over the knob to read it.
PIVOT
The balance point, 100 Hz - 5 kHz (800 Hz by default). Frequencies here stay where they are; the tilt rises on one side and falls on the other.
GENTLE / STEEP
GENTLE (default): a long, even 6 dB/oct slope across the whole range. STEEP: turns over more quickly around the pivot and leaves the far ends flatter.

Low-pass

FREQ
The low-pass cutoff, 1 - 22 kHz. Fully right = OFF.
Slope
The knob beside it: 6, 12 (default), 18, 24, 36 or 48 dB/oct, Butterworth.

Bottom row

STEREO / MID / SIDE
Where the tilt works: on both sides (STEREO, default), on the middle only or on the sides only - for example darken the sides and brighten the centre. The filters always work on both sides.
TRIM
Input level before anything else, from -24 to +12 dB.
GUARD
Click the TRIM knob to switch it; its LED sits at the knob's upper right. On by default. Gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources. The LED glows brighter while it is working.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper at -0.3 dBFS (no latency), or a lookahead limiter (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). CLIP is on by default and flashes red when it clips.
OUTPUT
Output level, -12 to +12 dB.
EQ IN
Switches the filters and tilt in or out (in by default). OUTPUT and the IN / OUT stages keep working.

The header

HORIZON / CLASSIC
Switches between the two looks: HORIZON (sky and sea, gold) and CLASSIC (cream and red). The sound is the same.
PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets; the ◄ / ► keys step through them and the display shows the one in use (click it for the list too). Choosing one sets the tilt, filters, STEREO / MID / SIDE, OUTPUT and EQ IN; the input and output stages are left as they are. The last entry resets everything to the defaults.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

In REAPER's parameter list only

Bias / Sat low band / Sat high band / Ceiling
The rest of the output stage: the saturation's even-harmonic bias, where its low and high bands start (40 - 400 Hz and 2 - 16 kHz) and the clip ceiling or limiter threshold.

Presets

Mix Bus Lift
1.5 dB brighter around 1 kHz, with a gentle 24 dB/oct high-pass at 25 Hz.
Warm It Up
1.5 dB darker around 800 Hz, with a soft roll-off above 18 kHz.
Vocal Forward
3 dB brighter with the STEEP shape at 1.5 kHz, and an 18 dB/oct high-pass at 80 Hz.
Dark & Thick
3 dB darker at 600 Hz, a high-pass at 30 Hz and a low-pass at 9 kHz.
Bright Sides
2 dB brighter on the sides only, pivoting at 700 Hz.
Tame the Sides
2 dB darker on the sides only, STEEP at 2 kHz.
Telephone Band
No tilt; a band from 300 Hz to 3 kHz with 24 dB/oct filters.
Thin Out (steep HPF)
2.5 dB brighter at 400 Hz with a 48 dB/oct high-pass at 120 Hz.
Vintage Mid Roll-off
1 dB darker on the middle only, with a 24 dB/oct high-pass at 20 Hz and an 18 dB/oct low-pass at 14 kHz.
Clean Up (filters only)
No tilt; 36 dB/oct filters at 30 Hz and 20 kHz.
Reset all to defaults
Every control back to its starting value, including the input and output stages.

Tips

  • Small moves go a long way: 1 - 2 dB of tilt is often enough on a full mix.
  • Move the PIVOT to where the music's important energy sits; that part stays put while the balance shifts around it.
  • Use EQ IN to compare, and adjust OUTPUT so the louder version doesn't fool your ears.
  • SIDE with a darker tilt keeps wide cymbals and reverbs from getting harsh, without dulling the vocal in the centre.

File: gfx-tilt-eq.jsfx

EQ & filters

TransEQ

A hybrid program equaliser: passive-style curves at the ends, versatile bells in the middle, and transformer iron on the way in and out.

Overview

TransEQ combines the broad, musical curves of a classic passive program equaliser at the low and high ends with two more flexible bell bands in the middle. The signal passes through an input transformer, the EQ and an output transformer.

The low band has the famous trick of the passive designs: BOOST and ATTEN are separate and can be used together. The boost is a slightly resonant shelf and the cut a much broader one, so using both lifts the lowest lows while carving a dip above them. The high band has a boost bell with its own BANDWIDTH and a separate cut shelf.

The IRON transformers saturate on magnetic flux, so the low end thickens first with mostly low-order harmonics while the mids and highs stay clean; below saturation they are transparent. A very slight drift is built in: the band frequencies and gains wander a little, like components warming up. The name is a tribute to the trans community, hence the blue and pink accents.

Reach for it for

  • Broad, musical tone shaping on a mix bus or master
  • The classic 'boost and cut' low end on kick, bass and full mixes
  • Air and presence on vocals and acoustic instruments
  • Adding transformer weight and density to the lows
  • Quick clean-up with the HPF and LPF

Controls

Big knobs: drag up and down (Shift for fine control) or use the mouse wheel; double-click to reset. The value shows while you hover. Stepped selectors: click a printed value to pick it, or click, drag or scroll on the knob to step through.

The header

S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.
EQ IN
Switches the EQ curves in and out (in by default; the light shows when it is in). The transformers stay in circuit either way, so you can compare the EQ alone. While it is out the EQ section is dimmed.

LOW

FREQ
The low band's frequency: 20, 30, 60, 100 or 150 Hz.
BOOST
A slightly resonant low shelf, 0 to 10 (up to about +14 dB).
ATTEN
A much broader, gentler low cut, 0 to 10 (up to about -13 dB). Used together with BOOST it lifts the lowest lows with a dip above them - at 60 Hz with both full, about +7 dB at 30 Hz and -6 dB at 100 Hz.

LO MID and HI MID

Frequency selector
LO MID: 150, 220, 330, 470, 680 Hz, 1 kHz or 1.5 kHz. HI MID: 1, 1.5, 2.2, 3.3, 4.7, 6.8 or 10 kHz.
WIDE / NARROW
The base width of the bell. Click to switch.
GAIN
Boost or cut, -12 to +12 dB. The bell narrows as the gain goes up (proportional Q), so small moves are broad and gentle, big ones more focused.

HIGH

BOOST FREQ
The high boost's frequency: 3, 4, 5, 8, 10, 12 or 16 kHz.
ATTEN FREQ
Where the high cut starts: 5, 10 or 20 kHz.
BOOST
A high bell, 0 to 10 (up to about +16 dB).
BANDWIDTH
The width of the high boost, 0 (sharp) to 10 (broad). 5 by default.
ATTEN
A high shelf cut, 0 to 10 (up to about -16 dB).

Bottom row

HPF (yellow cap)
A 12 dB / octave high-pass, 20 - 500 Hz. Fully left is OFF (out of circuit).
LPF (black cap)
A 12 dB / octave low-pass, 2 - 20 kHz. Fully right is OFF.
IRON IN
How hard the input transformer is driven, 0 to 10 (3 by default). Higher settings thicken the lows with mostly low-order harmonics; the mids and highs stay clean.
IRON OUT
The same for the output transformer, after the EQ and OUTPUT. Boosting the lows into it saturates it more.
OUTPUT
Output level, -12 to +12 dB, set before the output transformer - so it also changes how hard IRON OUT works.
LEVEL
LED meter of the output level.

Two stages work in the background with no controls on the panel: an input guard that gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS (it never boosts quiet sources), and a soft clipper that holds the output peaks at -0.3 dBFS.

Tips

  • For the classic low end, set FREQ to 30 or 60 Hz and turn BOOST and ATTEN up together: the lows get bigger and tighter at the same time.
  • For air without harshness, use HIGH BOOST at 10 - 16 kHz with a broad BANDWIDTH, and a touch of ATTEN at 20 kHz if it gets fizzy.
  • Use EQ IN to compare: the transformers stay in, so you only hear what the EQ curves are doing.
  • Turn IRON IN and IRON OUT to 0 for a clean EQ; raise them on bass, kick and mix bus for weight.
  • Because OUTPUT sits before the output transformer, raising it pushes IRON OUT harder - lower IRON OUT if you only want more level.

File: gfx-transeq.jsfx

Modulation & space

ADPMod

A harmonic ring modulator with five carrier waves, tape-style drive and a tone filter.

Overview

ADPMod is a ring modulator: it multiplies your signal by a carrier wave. At audio-rate carrier frequencies this turns notes into clangorous, bell-like and metallic tones that no longer follow the original pitch; at very low carrier frequencies it becomes a pulsing, tremolo-like effect. The carrier can be a sine, triangle, square, saw or noise, and HARMONICS adds the carrier's octave on top for a richer, more complex result.

After the modulation, the effected (wet) signal goes through a tape-style DRIVE stage and a TONE low-pass filter before it is blended with the dry signal. A slow random 'chaos drift' moves the carrier frequency, the character of the drive and the tone filter, so the effect never sits completely still. A low cut on the blend keeps the bass clean if you want it to.

Reach for it for

  • Metallic, bell-like and robotic vocals
  • Clangorous textures from synths, guitars and drums
  • Slow, pulsing tremolo with low carrier frequencies
  • Wide, shimmering stereo modulation (DUAL)
  • Adding grit and movement to sound design

Controls

Modes (header)

AUDIO
One carrier for both sides, so the result stays mono-compatible. The default.
SUB
The same ring modulator. Use it with low FREQ settings, where the carrier is below the audible range and the effect becomes a pulse or tremolo.
DUAL
The right side's carrier runs very slightly detuned (0.5%) from the left, so the two sides drift against each other for a wider stereo image.
OFF
The ring modulator is switched off: the audio passes through the input and output stages only.

The carrier

CARRIER WAVEFORM display
Shows the carrier wave, moving with it.
SIN / TRI / SQR / SAW / NOISE
The carrier wave. SIN gives the purest ring-mod tones; TRI is a little brighter; SQR and SAW are harsh and buzzy, with many more side tones; NOISE turns the signal into a gritty, broken texture.
FREQ
The carrier frequency, 0.5 Hz to 2 kHz (220 Hz by default). Low settings give a pulse or tremolo; audio-rate settings give metallic, bell-like tones.
DRIVE
A tape-style saturation stage on the modulated (wet) signal, 0 to 10 (0 by default). It warms the sound at low settings and adds thickness and grit as you turn it up.
DEPTH
How much the carrier modulates the signal, 0 to 100% (100% by default). 100% is full ring modulation; lower settings keep more of the unmodulated signal in the wet sound.
HARMONICS
Adds the carrier's octave (twice its frequency) to the carrier, 0 to 100% (50% by default), for a denser, more complex set of tones.
TONE
A low-pass filter on the wet signal, 20 Hz to 18 kHz (1 kHz by default). Turn it down to tame harsh side tones; turn it up for a brighter, more metallic effect.

Drift

CHAOS DRIFT: a slow random wander of the carrier frequency, the character of the drive and the TONE filter.

DRIFT
How much drift, 0 to 100%. 50% (the default) is the standard amount, 100% is twice that, 0 switches it off. More drift also makes the wander a little faster.

IN / OUT

TRIM
Input level before anything else, from -24 to +12 dB.
GUARD
Click the TRIM knob (without moving it) to switch the guard; its light sits at the knob's upper right and is lit while it is on. On by default. Gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources. The light glows brighter while it is catching peaks.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT (OUT)
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper (no latency), or a lookahead limiter that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). CLIP is on by default; its key flashes red while it is clipping.

Master

MIX
Dry / wet balance, 0 to 100% (50% by default).
LOW CUT
Keeps the lows dry: below this frequency the effect is not blended in, so the bass stays clean and solid. From off (fully left, 20 Hz) to 500 Hz.
OUTPUT
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.
LEVEL meters
Output level, left and right.

The header

PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets (a click on the display does the same). Choosing one sets the mode, carrier, drive, depth, harmonics, tone and mix; drift, LOW CUT, output and the IN / OUT section are left as they are.
◄ / ►
Step to the previous or next preset.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Next to the S / M / L size keys. Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Presets

Vocal Ring (Classic)
AUDIO mode, 440 Hz sine, light drive, 30% harmonics, tone at 2.5 kHz, 70% mix.
Sub Tremolo
SUB mode, 6.5 Hz triangle at 80% depth with some drive: a throbbing tremolo, 50% mix.
Metallic Chaos
DUAL mode, 180 Hz square, heavy drive and 85% harmonics, bright tone, 85% mix.
Subtle Saturation Mod
AUDIO mode, 220 Hz sine at 50% depth, no harmonics, open tone, 40% mix.

Tips

  • For tuned, musical results, set FREQ to a note that fits the key of the song (for example 220 Hz or 440 Hz for A).
  • Sweep FREQ slowly with automation for dramatic, sci-fi movement.
  • On a bass or a full mix, raise LOW CUT to 150-300 Hz so the low end stays dry and solid.
  • If the effect is too harsh, lower TONE or HARMONICS before turning down MIX.

File: gfx-adpmod.jsfx

Modulation & space

Autopan

An LFO auto-panner and tremolo, free or locked to the tempo.

Overview

Autopan moves your sound with an LFO. In PAN mode it sweeps the sound across the stereo field; in TREM mode both sides move together, so the level pulses up and down - a tremolo.

The LFO can run freely in Hz or lock to the project tempo, in four shapes from a smooth sine to stepped random. A gentle drift can make the rate, depth and centre wander slowly, so the movement never feels mechanical.

Reach for it for

  • Slow, wide movement on pads, keys and guitars
  • Tempo-synced ping-pong panning
  • Classic amp-style tremolo
  • Choppy, rhythmic gating effects (square wave)
  • Random, wandering stereo motion

Controls

Mode (header)

PAN
Moves the sound from side to side across the stereo field.
TREM
Tremolo: both sides move together, so the level rises and falls. At 100% DEPTH the sound dips to silence at the bottom of each cycle.

Display

Pan field / TREM GAIN
In PAN mode, a line from L to R with the range the sweep covers and a dot where the sound is now. In TREM mode, a bar showing the current gain.
LFO scope
The LFO shape with a marker riding on it. With RND, a running trace of the random movement.
INPUT
A VU meter of the input level (0 VU = -18 dBFS average).

Autopan

SINE / TRI / SQR / RND
The LFO shape. Sine and triangle give smooth sweeps; square jumps between two positions; random moves to a new place each cycle.
MONO / STEREO
PAN mode only. MONO sums the input to mono and pans it with an equal-power law (unity in the centre) - a true pan. STEREO works as a balance control and keeps the stereo image: the sweep fades the opposite side down.
FREE / SYNC / FLOW
How the rate is set: FREE in Hz, SYNC locked to the project tempo and timeline, FLOW tempo-synced but running continuously through loops.
RATE
The LFO speed: 0.05 - 20 Hz in FREE, or a note value from 4 bars to 1/32 (with dotted and triplet values) in SYNC and FLOW.
SMOOTH
Rounds off the square wave's corners and turns the random shape from stepped jumps into glides, 0 - 100%.
PHASE
Shifts the LFO within its cycle, 0 - 360 degrees. In SYNC, use it to move the sweep against the beat.
DEPTH
How far the sweep travels (PAN) or how deep the level dips (TREM), 0 - 100%.
CENTRE
PAN mode: where the sweep is centred, from -100% (left) to +100% (right).

Drift

A slow random wander that keeps the movement from sounding mechanical.

DRIFT
One dial, 0 - 100%. It makes the LFO rate and the depth wander slowly, and in some presets the pan centre too (in SYNC the rate stays locked on average and the timing wobbles instead). 50% is the standard amount, 100% twice that, 0 none; the wander also gets a little faster as you turn it up.

IN / OUT

TRIM
Input level before anything else, from -24 to +12 dB. A single click on the knob (without dragging) switches GUARD on or off; its LED at the knob's upper right is dark when GUARD is off, red when it is on, and glows brighter while it is catching peaks.
GUARD
On by default. Gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out, 0 - 100%: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper (no latency), or a lookahead limiter that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). The CLIP key flashes red while the clipper is working.

Master

MIX
Dry / effect balance, 0 - 100%. Lower settings make the movement subtler.
OUTPUT
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.
OUT meters
Output level, left and right, with a floating peak. Click a meter to clear the peak.

The header

PRESETS, arrows and display
PRESETS (or a click on the display) opens the list of factory presets; the ◄ / ► keys step through them. The display shows the preset number and name.
Theme picker
Changes the panel's colour theme.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.
HW
Switches between the hardware-style panel and the flat look.

The presets set the mode, shape, smoothing, rate and the depth, centre and drift amounts; phase, the MONO / STEREO input, mix, output and the IN / OUT strip are left as they are. The last entry in the list resets everything to the defaults.

Presets

Init
The default settings: a free-running sine pan at 0.12 Hz, 30% depth, with a little drift. A new instance opens on it.
Gentle Sway
Sine pan over one bar at 50% depth, with a little drift.
Classic Auto-Pan
Full-width sine pan on quarter notes.
Hard Ping-Pong
Square wave, full depth, on eighth notes: the sound jumps hard left and right.
Wandering
Smooth random pan over two bars with plenty of drift.
Slow Swirl
Free-running sine pan at 0.8 Hz, with the rate drifting.
Amp Trem
Sine tremolo at 5 Hz, 60% depth.
Choppy Trem
Square tremolo on 1/16 notes at full depth - a rhythmic chop.
Warble Trem
Triangle tremolo on eighth-note triplets, 50% depth, with rate and depth drift.
Reset all to defaults
Every control back to its starting value.

Tips

  • Keep a stereo source's image intact with STEREO; use MONO when you want the whole sound to travel from speaker to speaker.
  • Raise SMOOTH on a square-wave pan to soften the clicks of the jumps.
  • In SYNC, PHASE lets you make the sound arrive left or right exactly on the downbeat.
  • For tremolo that sits in a mix, try DEPTH around 30 - 60% rather than full.

File: gfx-autopan.jsfx

Modulation & space

Chopper

A tempo-synced stutter and gate sequencer for vocal chops, beat-repeats and trance gating.

Overview

Chopper records your audio all the time and plays it back through a pattern of up to 16 steps, locked to the project tempo. Each step either lets the live sound through, repeats the slice that has just played, plays that slice backwards, or mutes it. That covers stutter-house vocal chops, beat-repeat rolls and the classic trance gate in one panel.

Every step can also be ratcheted (the slice restarts 2, 3 or 4 times inside the step), pitched up, and filtered. A short FADE window at every edge of every slice means nothing clicks.

HOLD (or a key on your MIDI keyboard) locks whatever is playing now and repeats it at the current division - the 'stutter' button of a DJ effect. Chopper follows the transport; when it is stopped the pattern runs on at the project tempo.

Reach for it for

  • Stutter-house and pop vocal chops
  • Beat-repeat rolls and fills into the next bar
  • Trance-style rhythmic gating of pads and chords
  • Reverse swells on the off-beats
  • Live stutter performance from a MIDI keyboard

Controls

Left panel

Display
Shows the preset number and name. While you touch a control it shows that control and its value for a moment; while HOLD is down it shows HOLD (or MIDI HOLD) and the division. Click it to open the preset list.
PRESETS
Also opens the preset list.
◄ / ►
Step to the previous or next preset.
SIZE: S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also shrinks to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, a short guide to the pattern and the GFX website address. Click anywhere to close it.
LIME / MAGENTA
Switches the faceplate between acid lime (black print, magenta lights) and hot magenta (white print, cyan lights).
MIDI OCTAVE
Click to choose the octave that triggers HOLD from MIDI: C1, C2, C3, C4 or C5 (middle C = C4). The display shows the range of keys, e.g. C3 - G#3.
MIDI light
Lights while a trigger key is held.

PATTERN

DIVISION
The length of each step: 1/2, 1/3, 1/4, 1/6, 1/8, 1/12, 1/16, 1/24 or 1/32 of a bar. 1/3, 1/6, 1/12 and 1/24 are triplets.
STEP numbers
Click a step number to set the pattern length (1 to 16 steps). Steps past the length are dimmed. The playing step is lit and outlined.
MODE
What the step does. Dark: THRU, the live sound. Lit: REPEAT, the slice that has just played is looped. Lit with ◄: REVERSE, the slice played backwards. Black with ×: MUTE.
RATCHET
x1 to x4: the slice is shortened and restarted 2, 3 or 4 times inside the step, for rolls.
PITCH
Off, UP (raised by the SHIFT knob, shown as +5 etc.) or +12 (an octave up). The slice plays faster to raise its pitch.
FILTER
Off, LP (low-pass, set by the LP knob) or HP (high-pass, set by the HP knob). The change is smoothed so it never zips.

Click a cell to step it forward, right-click to step it back. RATCHET, PITCH and FILTER work on repeated and reversed steps; FILTER also works on live (THRU) steps.

HOLD
Momentary: while you hold the mouse down on it, the slice playing now (one division long) is locked and repeated at the current division, with the pitch and filter of the moment. Let go and the pattern carries on.
FREEZE
Keeps the first slice it catches: every REPEAT and REVERSE step then replays that same slice instead of catching a new one. Switching it on catches a fresh slice; a silent slice is never kept.

PITCH & TIME

SHIFT
How far UP steps are raised, 0 to +12 semitones (+5 by default).
DRIFT
See Drift below.
SWING
0 - 50%. Makes the first step of each pair longer and the second shorter, for a shuffled feel.

FILTER & FADE

LP (blue cap)
The cutoff of the low-pass used by LP steps, 100 Hz - 20 kHz (1.2 kHz by default).
HP (yellow cap)
The cutoff of the high-pass used by HP steps, 20 Hz - 8 kHz (600 Hz by default).
FADE
The fade at the start and end of every slice, 1 - 10 ms (3 ms by default). Shorter is snappier; longer is smoother if you hear clicks on bass-heavy material.

Drift

DRIFT
Only the repeated and reversed slices are affected: they wander slowly in pitch (up to a quarter of a semitone at 100%), and each new slice gets its own small offset, like tape. The live sound and the timing are never touched. 0 - 100%, 25% by default.

MASTER

INPUT
The level into Chopper, -24 to +24 dB.
MIX
Dry / chopped balance. 100% (the default) is the chopped signal only; lower settings blend the untouched input back in.
OUTPUT
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.

Knobs: drag up and down (Shift for fine control) or use the mouse wheel; double-click or Ctrl-click to reset. The value shows while you hover.

Presets

A preset sets every control, including the pattern, INPUT, MIX and OUTPUT; only MIDI OCTAVE is left as it is.

Init
All 16 steps live (THRU) at 1/16: a blank pattern to start from.
Lavern Gate
Chopped 1/16 repeats with gaps, and a ratchet roll into the next bar.
Breathless 16ths
Every 1/16 step repeats, the off-beats lifted a fifth (SHIFT +7), ending on a roll up an octave.
Triplet Flutter
12 steps of 1/24 triplets over two beats: repeats with gaps, a few lifted, a roll at the end.
Underwater Build
Low-passed repeats that roll faster and faster and rise in pitch at the end.
Reverse Swell
1/8 steps over one bar: live and backwards in turn, then three reversed steps to finish, the last one doubled.
Half-Time Freeze
FREEZE on: one caught slice replayed quarter by quarter, the last one doubled and lifted.
24th Roll
12 steps of 1/24: live for half the pattern, then repeats that roll faster and rise, high-passed at the end.
Trance Pulse
The classic gate on the live sound, with a little swing and two high-passed steps.
Sidechain Pump
The first 1/16 of each beat muted and the next one high-passed, for a ducking pump.
Offbeat Gate
The straight 1/16 on-off gate: every other step muted.
Dotted Gate
One live step, then two muted, in a 3 + 3 + 3 + 3 + 4 pattern across the bar.
Pitch-Up Chops
Live and repeated steps in pairs, the repeats lifted a fifth (SHIFT +7), ending in faster rolls up an octave.
Triplet Stutter
12 steps of 1/12 over one bar: live, repeated and backwards on each beat, with a rising roll at the end.
Snare Roll Build
1/8 repeats over two bars that roll faster and faster, rising and high-passed in the second bar.
Rewind Bar
1/8 steps over one bar: live for the first half, then backwards and low-passed, the last step doubled and lifted.
Filter Ladder
A gate with a muted step in each beat; the live steps are low-passed for the first beat, high-passed for the second, then wide open.
IDM Glitch
1/32 steps scattered between live, repeats, backwards and mutes, with rolls, pitch jumps, filters and more DRIFT (60%).
Half-Time Chops
1/4 steps over two bars with a little swing: repeats drag the groove to half time, the last step doubled, lifted and high-passed.

Triggering HOLD from MIDI

One octave of keys holds the slice at each division, rising: C 1/2, C# 1/3, D 1/4, D# 1/6, E 1/8, F 1/12, F# 1/16, G 1/24, G# 1/32. Holding a key locks the slice and repeats it at that key's division; let go and the pattern carries on (if another trigger key is still held, it takes over). All MIDI passes through Chopper unchanged.

  1. Put Chopper on the track with the audio you want to chop.
  2. Get MIDI to that track: add a MIDI item on the same track, or arm the track with your MIDI keyboard as its input, or send MIDI to it from another track.
  3. Click MIDI OCTAVE until it shows the octave you want to play in (the display shows the range of keys).
  4. Hold the keys in that octave while the music plays; the MIDI light and the display show MIDI HOLD and the division.

Tips

  • For a beat-repeat fill, leave most of the bar on THRU and set only the last few steps to REPEAT with rising RATCHET.
  • Use MIX below 100% for a subtler stutter that keeps the original phrase underneath.
  • For a trance gate on a pad, use THRU and MUTE steps only at 1/16, with a little SWING.
  • If repeats click on bass or kick-heavy material, raise FADE to 5 - 8 ms.
  • Turn DRIFT to 0% when the repeats must stay exactly in tune with the track.

File: gfx-chopper.jsfx

Modulation & space

Chorus

A bucket-brigade stereo chorus in the spirit of the classic 80s poly-synth choruses, with modes I, II and I+II.

Overview

Chorus mixes your signal with two short delayed copies, one per side, whose delay times are swept by a slow triangle LFO. The right side sweeps in the opposite direction to the left, so the image spreads wide while the mono sum stays solid - the sound of the built-in chorus on the classic 80s poly-synths.

The delay lines behave like bucket-brigade chips: the signal is filtered before and after the delay, gently compressed and saturated, and can carry a little hiss. TONE sets how dark the repeats are.

Three mode keys recall the classic settings: I is slow and gentle, II faster and deeper, and I+II adds a second, faster sweep running the other way for a richer, wider sound without the throb of a single fast sweep. From there every knob can be adjusted.

Reach for it for

  • Width and shimmer on synth pads and strings
  • Clean guitars that need movement
  • Thickening a mono source into stereo
  • Vintage, slightly dark chorus with hiss for character
  • Tempo-synced swirls on loops

Controls

Modes (header)

I
Slow and gentle: 0.513 Hz, 1.85 ms depth around a 3.5 ms centre.
II
Faster and deeper: 0.863 Hz with the same depth and centre.
I+II
Both at once: the I sweep (set by the knobs) plus a second tap swept about 1.68 times faster, a little longer and swinging the other way. Richer and wider.
OFF
The chorus is bypassed; the IN / OUT stage and OUTPUT stay active.

Pressing I, II or I+II sets RATE, DEPTH and CENTRE to the classic values for that mode and the rate mode to FREE. The red LEDs above the keys show which sweeps are running.

Display

Delay window
The two dots show the live delay time of the left (cyan) and right (orange) taps on a 0 - 10 ms scale, with the times in ms above. CHORUS OFF is shown when the chorus is bypassed.
LFO
The triangle sweep with a dot riding on it.

Chorus

FREE / SYNC / FLOW (RATE MODE)
How the sweep speed is set: FREE in Hz, SYNC locked to the project tempo and timeline, FLOW tempo-synced but running continuously through loops. In I+II the second sweep runs freely at its scaled speed when synced.
RATE
The sweep speed, 0.05 to 12 Hz in FREE, or a note value from 4 bars to 1/32 (dotted and triplet included) when synced.
DEPTH
How far the delay time swings either side of the centre, 0 to 3 ms. More depth gives more pitch wobble.
CENTRE
The delay time the sweep moves around, 1 to 8 ms. Short settings are tighter; longer ones sound bigger and more doubled.
WIDTH
0 to 100%. At 100% the right side sweeps in antiphase to the left for the classic wide image; at 0% both sides sweep together (a mono-style chorus).
TONE
The bucket-brigade filtering on the delayed signal, 0 to 100%: low settings roll the repeats off around 3 kHz for a dark, vintage sound; high settings open them up towards 16 kHz.
HISS
Adds the faint noise of the delay chips into the chorus path, 0 to 100%. Off by default.

Drift

A slow random wander of the chorus rate, its depth and its tone, like ageing components.

DRIFT
0 to 100%. 50% gives the plugin's standard drift (the amounts the presets set), 100% doubles it, 0 turns it off. The wander also speeds up as you turn it up.

IN / OUT

TRIM
Input level before anything else, from -24 to +12 dB.
GUARD
On by default. Click the TRIM knob (without dragging) to switch it; the small LED at the knob's upper right shows it is on and glows brighter while it works. It gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out, 0 to 100%: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT (OUT)
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper (no latency) that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS, or a lookahead limiter (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER) that holds them at -0.3 dBFS. The CLIP key flashes red while the clipper is working.

Master

MIX
Dry / chorus balance, 0 to 100%; 50% (the default) is the classic even blend.
LOW CUT
A 12 dB/oct high-pass on the chorused signal only, from off (fully left, 20 Hz) up to 500 Hz. The bass stays dry, clean and centred at full level.
OUTPUT
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.
OUTPUT LEVEL meters
Output level, left and right. Click to reset the peak hold.

The header

PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets; the arrows beside it step through them, and the display shows the one in use (click it for the list too). The last entry resets everything to the defaults.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Presets

The presets set the mode, rate, depth, centre, width, tone, hiss, mix and drift amounts; the DRIFT dial, LOW CUT, OUTPUT and the IN / OUT stage are left alone.

Classic I
Mode I at its classic settings.
Classic II
Mode II at its classic settings.
Classic I+II
Both sweeps together at the classic settings.
Lush Wide
Deeper (2.5 ms) and longer (5 ms centre) sweep at 0.6 Hz, a little more mix and drift.
Subtle Thicken
Slow 0.3 Hz, shallow 0.8 ms sweep, 70% width and 30% mix for gentle thickening.
Vintage Hiss
Mode II with a darker tone and audible chip hiss.
Synced Swirl
Mode II locked to half-note sweeps of the project tempo, a little deeper and longer.
Dark Bucket
Slow sweep with the tone filter well down, a touch of hiss and more tone drift.
Reset all to defaults
Every control back to its starting value.

Tips

  • On bass or kick-heavy material, raise LOW CUT to 150 - 250 Hz so the low end stays solid and mono.
  • I+II is the one to try when II sounds too seasick: it is just as wide, but the throb is smoother.
  • For a mono-compatible effect, pull WIDTH down a little; at 100% the antiphase sweep is at its widest.
  • Lower TONE and add a little HISS for the lo-fi sound of an old synth's chorus.

File: gfx-chorus.jsfx

Modulation & space

Dynatron

A bubble-and-jet modulator: flange, phase or a resonant filter, swept by an LFO, by hand or by your playing.

Overview

Dynatron is three modulation effects sharing one set of controls. FLANGE is a wide-sweep studio flanger with regeneration for the jet sound. PHASE is a six-stage phaser. FILTER is a resonant auto-wah / envelope filter with low-pass, band-pass and high-pass outputs.

What moves the sweep is up to you. LFO gives the usual automatic sweep. HOLD freezes it so you can place it by hand with CENTRE. ENV is the old two-tape-machine trick: your playing drives the sweep, so the harder you play, the further it goes.

A slow drift can make the rate, depth and centre wander, like a hand on the tape or an old clock.

Reach for it for

  • Jet flanging and bubbling phase on guitars, synths and drum loops
  • Touch-sensitive envelope filter and auto-wah on bass, funk guitar and keys
  • Static comb or filter colour placed by hand (HOLD)
  • Tempo-synced phasing
  • Hollow, nasal tones with negative feedback

Controls

Mode, sweep source and filter type

FLANGE
A wide-sweep flanger. The delay sits between about 0.15 and 14 ms (set by CENTRE) and sweeps up to two octaves of delay either side. The loop is softened above about 9 kHz.
PHASE
A six-stage all-pass phaser, centred between 100 Hz and 2.4 kHz and sweeping up to three octaves either side.
FILTER
A resonant state-variable filter, centred between 150 Hz and 4 kHz and sweeping up to about three octaves either side. With RESONANCE up it squelches and nears self-oscillation.
LFO / HOLD / ENV
The sweep source. LFO: the usual automatic sweep. HOLD: the sweep stops and CENTRE places it by hand. ENV: your playing drives the sweep - louder notes push it further (fast attack, about 180 ms to settle back).
LP / BP / HP
FILTER mode only: low-pass, band-pass or high-pass. The keys are dimmed in the other modes.
DELAY / CUTOFF display
The live delay time (FLANGE, in ms) or the cutoff of the sweep (PHASE and FILTER, in Hz).

Sweep

These knobs work in all three modes.

CENTRE
Where the sweep sits at rest, 0 - 100%. In FLANGE, low settings give short delays and high, metallic combs; high settings lower, more chorus-like tones. In PHASE and FILTER it sets the centre frequency.
DEPTH
How far the sweep travels either side of CENTRE, 0 - 100%.
FEEDBACK / RESONANCE
FLANGE and PHASE: feedback, from -95% to +95%. Positive is thick, ringing and jet-like; negative is hollow and nasal. The level is compensated so strong settings don't jump out. In FILTER the knob becomes RESONANCE (the Q, shown as 0 - 95% either way round): turn it up for a sharper, squelchier peak.
SPREAD
Offsets the right side's LFO from the left, 0 - 180 degrees, for stereo width. It only applies with the LFO as the source.
SENS
ENV only: how hard you have to play to push the sweep. Higher settings react to quieter playing. It is greyed out with the other sources.

LFO

SIN / TRI / SQR / RND
The LFO shape: sine, triangle, square or random.
FREE / SYNC / FLOW
How the LFO rate is set: FREE in Hz, SYNC locked to the project tempo and timeline, FLOW tempo-synced but running continuously through loops.
RATE
The LFO speed: 0.02 - 10 Hz in FREE, or a note value from 4 bars to 1/32 (with dotted and triplet values) in SYNC. In FREE it reads 'stopped' while the sweep source is HOLD.

Master

MIX
Dry / effect balance, 0 - 100% (50% by default). Around 50% gives the deepest flange and phase notches; for the filter, higher settings put more of the filtered sound up front.
DRIFT
A slow random wander of the LFO rate, the depth and the centre, 0 - 100%. 50% is the standard amount, 100% twice that, 0 none; the wander also gets a little faster as you turn it up.
HPF
A high-pass filter on the effect only, from off (fully left) up to 500 Hz. Raise it to keep the low end clean and solid while the sweep happens higher up.
OUTPUT
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.
OUT meters
Output level, left and right, with a floating peak. Click a meter to clear the peak.

IN / OUT

TRIM
Input level before anything else, from -24 to +12 dB. A single click on the knob (without dragging) switches GUARD on or off; its LED at the knob's upper right is dark when GUARD is off, red when it is on, and glows brighter while it is catching peaks.
GUARD
On by default. Gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out, 0 - 100%: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper (no latency), or a lookahead limiter that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). The CLIP key flashes red while the clipper is working.

The header

PRESETS, arrows and display
PRESETS (or a click on the display) opens the list of factory presets; the ◄ / ► keys step through them. The display shows the preset number and name. A preset sets the mode, LFO, sweep and MIX controls; DRIFT, HPF, OUTPUT and the IN / OUT strip are left as they are. The last entry in the list resets everything to the defaults.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Presets

Classic Bubble Flange
The default: FLANGE with a slow triangle sweep and moderate feedback.
Dyna Flange
FLANGE with a very slow, deep triangle sweep, strong feedback and 90 degrees of SPREAD.
Smooth Phase Sweep
PHASE with a gentle sine sweep and light feedback.
Square Throb Phase
PHASE with a slow triangle sweep, strong feedback and 120 degrees of SPREAD - a throbbing, wide phase.
Vocal Auto-Wah
FILTER (band-pass) with a slow sine sweep, low centre and 70% mix.
Random Bubble Filter
FILTER (band-pass) with a random LFO, full depth and high resonance - bubbling, unpredictable steps.
Stepped Jump Flange
FLANGE with a random LFO at 1.2 Hz and light feedback.
Synced Wide Phase
PHASE locked to quarter notes with the sides 180 degrees apart.
Envelope Squelch
FILTER (band-pass) driven by your playing (ENV), low centre, deep sweep and high resonance.
Hollow Negative Flange
FLANGE with a very slow sine sweep and negative feedback for a hollow, nasal tone.
Reset all to defaults
Every control back to its starting value.

Tips

  • For the classic touch-sensitive swoosh, pick ENV and set SENS so only your harder notes push the sweep all the way.
  • In HOLD, automate CENTRE for sweeps you control exactly, in time with the music.
  • If the low end gets thin or wobbly, raise HPF so only the upper part of the sound is swept.
  • Negative FEEDBACK in FLANGE gives a hollow tone that sits well on busy mixes; positive gives the jet.
