Modulation & space

E-Delay

Four European studio and broadcast delays of the 1980s: pristine repeats, ducking, multi-tap and pitch loops.

Overview

E-Delay brings together four digital delays in the spirit of the European studio and broadcast units of the 1980s: clean, precise repeats, careful spatial design and transformer-coupled inputs. The MODEL keys pick the algorithm.

CRYSTAL is an ultra-clean dual delay with feedback filters and a cross-feed for ping-pong; when you change the time it crossfades to the new one, so the pitch never bends. DYNAMIC is the same line with a ducker: the repeats dip while you play and bloom back when you stop. MULTI-TAP spreads up to eight taps across the time, alternating sides, each gently chorused. TIME STRETCH runs the feedback through a pitch shifter and a soft limiter, for endless rising or falling repeats that never run away.

Left and right times are set separately in milliseconds or synced to the project tempo. The DUCK section works on every model, and VINTAGE runs the line through the converters of the era.

Reach for it for

  • Clean slapback and tempo-synced echoes on vocals and guitars
  • Ping-pong delays with the cross-feed
  • Vocal echoes that stay out of the way while the singer sings (DUCK)
  • Rhythmic multi-tap patterns and chorus-like taps
  • Shimmering, rising or falling pitch-shifted repeats (TIME STRETCH)

Controls

Working the displays

Every parameter has its own display. Drag up or down on a display (Shift for fine moves), use the mouse wheel over it, or press the - / + keys underneath (hold for repeat). Double-click a display to reset it to its default. The last display you touched is outlined. Displays that do nothing on the current model are dimmed.

DELAY PROCESSOR

INPUT / ECHO meters
VU meters showing the input level and the level of the echoes (0 VU = -18 dBFS).
CRYSTAL
The ultra-clean dual delay: feedback filters, cross-feed for ping-pong, and a crossfade when the time changes, so the pitch never bends.
DYNAMIC
The same line with the ducker working. Choosing it sets DUCK DEPTH to 60% if it was at 0.
MULTI-TAP
Up to 8 taps (TAPS) spread evenly across the longer of TIME L and TIME R, alternating left and right, each quieter than the last and gently chorused by MOD DEPTH and MOD RATE. The feedback comes from the end of the line.
TIME STRETCH
The repeats pass through a pitch shifter (PITCH, -12 to +12 semitones per repeat) and a soft limiter inside the loop, so they can rise or fall forever without running away.
SYNC
Locks TIME L and TIME R to the project tempo; the displays then show note values (1/1 down to 1/32, with dotted D and triplet T values) and the header shows the tempo.
VINTAGE
Runs the delay line through 16-bit converters at the era's clock rate: 48 kHz on CRYSTAL, 32 kHz on DYNAMIC and MULTI-TAP, 40 kHz on TIME STRETCH.
PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets.
◄ / ►
Step to the previous or next preset (wrapping round).
Preset display
Shows the last preset recalled (P1 - P10, or P--) and whether the times are FREE or SYNC.

TIME

TIME L / TIME R
The left and right delay times, 1 to 2700 ms (375 and 500 ms by default), or note values when SYNC is on (1/8D and 1/4 by default).

FEEDBACK

FEEDBACK
How much of the echo goes back into the line, 0 to 99% (35% by default). More gives more repeats.
CROSS-FEED
How much of each side's feedback crosses to the other side, 0 to 100%. Turn it up for ping-pong echoes.
LO CUT
A high-pass filter on the repeats, 20 to 1000 Hz (80 Hz by default). Each repeat gets thinner.
HI CUT
A low-pass filter on the repeats, 1 to 20 kHz (9 kHz by default). Each repeat gets darker.

DUCK

DEPTH
How far the echoes are pulled down while you play, 0 to 100% (up to about 18 dB). Works on every model.
RECOVERY
How long the echoes take to bloom back when you stop, 20 to 2000 ms (400 ms by default).

MODULATION

MOD DEPTH
How far each tap's time is swept, 0 to 10 ms. MULTI-TAP only.
MOD RATE
The speed of that sweep, 0.05 to 10 Hz. MULTI-TAP only.
PITCH
The pitch shift applied to each repeat, -12 to +12 semitones. TIME STRETCH only.
TAPS
The number of taps, 1 to 8 (6 by default). MULTI-TAP only.

CHARACTER

DRIVE
The input transformer, 0 to 100% (15% by default). The lows saturate first, for warmth and weight before the delay.
WIDTH
The stereo width of the echoes, 0 to 150%. 0 is mono; above 100 is wider than normal.
DRIFT
How much the delay clock wanders, 0 to 100% (50% by default). See Drift below.
MIX
The dry / echo balance, 0 to 100% (30% by default).

Drift

The delay clock is not quite steady: the left and right times each wander slowly and independently by a fraction of a millisecond, which keeps the repeats from sounding sterile. At 50% you get the standard amount; 100% doubles it and makes it wander a little faster; 0% switches it off.

IN / OUT

TRIM
Input level before anything else, -24 to +12 dB.
LEVEL
The output level, -24 to +12 dB.
GUARD
Off by default. When on, it gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources. GUARD CATCHING shows while it is working.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out, 0 to 100%: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper at -0.3 dBFS (no latency), or a lookahead limiter that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). The OUT caption turns into a red CLIP while the clipper is working.

The header

S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.

CRYSTAL synced to 1/8 and 1/4 with 70% cross-feed for ping-pong.

Reset all to defaults

Tips

  • For vocals, try DUCK DEPTH at 50 - 70%: the echoes stay out of the way of the words and fill the gaps.
  • Lower HI CUT and raise LO CUT for repeats that sit behind the mix rather than on top of it.
  • In TIME STRETCH, +12 or +7 semitones with high feedback makes a shimmer; keep MIX low and HI CUT down to tame it.
  • On a send, set MIX to 100% and use LEVEL to set the return level.

Modulation & space

E-Verb

Four European broadcast reverbs of the 1980s in one, each with its own faceplate.

Overview

E-Verb recreates four styles of European broadcast reverb from the 80s, built for acoustic precision rather than lushness: there is no chorusing in the tail, and the input runs through a transformer. The MODEL keys change both the algorithm and the faceplate.

ROOM SIM works out the early reflections from the shape and size of a real room, then feeds an unmodulated network that holds up in mono. BROADCAST is a compact, warm plate and hall. DIALOGUE is a dense, quick-recovering reverb tuned for speech. NON-LIN is a gated / reverse processor.

Every parameter sits on its own seven-segment display, with the reverb time set in seconds and adjusted separately at the low and high end, and the room size given in cubic metres.

Reach for it for

  • Natural rooms for voices, acoustic instruments and drums
  • Speech and voice-over that needs space without blur
  • Warm plates and halls with a vintage converter sound
  • Gated and reverse effects on drums
  • Mono-compatible reverb

Controls

Using the displays

Every parameter has its own display. Click a display to hand it to the data wheel (its frame lights up); drag the display up or down (hold Shift for fine steps), use the mouse wheel over it, or press its - and + keys (hold them to repeat). Double-click a display to return it to its default.

Meters

IN / REV
Two LED columns in 3 dB steps: the input level and the reverb's output level.

MODEL

ROOM SIM
A room simulator: the reflections are worked out from the room's shape (first and second order, with a slight difference between the ears), then an 8-line network with no modulation at all - true room modes that hold up in mono.
BROADCAST
The compact broadcast plate / hall: damped parallel combs into allpasses, a warm loop and a heavier input transformer.
DIALOGUE
A dense allpass tank tuned for speech: high diffusion, fast recovery, no flutter.
NON-LIN
A long tapped line with a shaped envelope instead of a natural decay. TIME sets the length of the shape (up to about 1.25 s, stretched further by SIZE).
SHAPE (RM / GT / RV)
NON-LIN only. RM: a falling, room-like envelope. GT: gate - flat, then cut off. RV: reverse - the sound rises, then stops.
VINTAGE
Runs the reverb through the converters of the era, with the clock rate and bit depth of each model (14-bit on BROADCAST, 16-bit on the others; 31.25 to 40 kHz).

1st REFLECTION

DELAY
When the early reflections start, 0 to 120 ms.
LEVEL
The level of the early reflections, -30 to +6 dB; fully down reads OFF.

REVERB

DELAY
The pre-delay before the reverb tail, 0 to 250 ms.
LEVEL
The level of the tail, -30 to +6 dB; fully down reads OFF.

REVERB TIME

HIGH END
A multiplier for the decay time of the highs, 0.1 to 1.5 x. Below 1 the top dies away faster, like real air absorption.
TIME
The decay time at mid frequencies, 0.1 to 20 seconds.
LOW END
A multiplier for the decay time of the lows (below about 250 Hz), 0.5 to 2.5 x.

ROOM

SIZE
The room volume, 10 to 12000 cubic metres. It sets the spacing of the reflections and the size of the reverb network (and the length of the NON-LIN shape).

REFLECTIONS

DENSITY
How many early reflections are used, 0 to 100%. Low settings give a few distinct reflections; high settings a dense cluster.
DIFFUSION
How much the input is smeared before it enters the reverb, 0 to 100%. More diffusion gives a smoother, less grainy onset.

FILTER

LO CUT
A high-pass on the reverb, 20 to 1000 Hz.
HI CUT
A low-pass on the reverb, 1 to 20 kHz (shown in kHz).

CHARACTER

DRIVE
The input transformer, 0 to 100%: the lows saturate first, for weight and warmth. BROADCAST's transformer is heavier.
WIDTH
The stereo width of the reverb, 0 to 150%. 0 is mono; above 100 is wider than natural.
DRIFT
0 to 100%. A slow wander of the air absorption (the high-end damping) - never the delay lengths, so there is no pitch movement. On NON-LIN the tap levels also shift slightly. The wander speeds up as DRIFT goes up.

OUTPUT

MIX
Dry / reverb balance, 0 to 100% (30% by default).

PARAMETERS

Data wheel
Changes whichever display was clicked last (named under SELECTED). Drag it (Shift for fine steps) or use the mouse wheel.
Program display
Shows the preset in use (P1 to P10), or P-- when none is loaded.
LIST
Opens the list of all presets by name. The last entry resets everything to the defaults.
◄ / ►
Step to the previous or next preset.
1 - 9, 0
Memory keys: recall presets 1 to 9, and 0 for preset 10.

IN / OUT

TRIM
Input level before anything else, -24 to +12 dB.
GUARD
Off by default. Gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS. It never boosts quiet sources.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out, 0 to 100%: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
LEVEL
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper (no latency) that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS, or a lookahead limiter (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER) that holds them at -0.3 dBFS. CLIPPING shows under the keys while the clipper is working.

Tips

  • For a natural room, start in ROOM SIM, set SIZE to the space you imagine, then adjust TIME; it holds up well when the mix is checked in mono.
  • Pull HIGH END below 1 to make a long reverb sound darker and further away without touching HI CUT.

  • For the 80s gated drum sound, use NON-LIN with GT and set TIME to choose how long the gate stays open.

Modulation & space

Echo 34

An analogue bucket-brigade delay: warm, darkening repeats with drive in the loop.

Overview

Echo 34 is a bucket-brigade (BBD) delay in the spirit of the budget analogue echo units of the late 70s and 80s. The input goes through a driven op-amp preamp, then into the delay line; every repeat passes back through the same drive and through the chip's clock filter, so the echoes get darker and grittier as they fade.

Two chips are on board: LONG gives delays from 50 to 550 ms, SHORT from 50 to 110 ms for slapback and doubling. As on a real bucket-brigade unit, turning TIME glides the delay rather than jumping, so the repeats bend in pitch while you turn it. Push REPEATS past 100% and the unit runs away into self-oscillation.

Reach for it for

  • Warm, dark analogue echoes on guitars, vocals and synths
  • Slapback on vocals and guitars (SHORT)
  • Stereo doubling (DUAL CHIP)
  • Dub-style runaway feedback and pitch bends
  • Lo-fi, noisy repeats

Controls

PREAMP

TRIM
Input level, -24 to +12 dB.
DRIVE
The preamp's drive, 0 to 100% (30% by default). It saturates the input unevenly (the two halves of the wave clip differently, adding even harmonics) and also sits in the feedback loop, so each repeat is driven again.

DELAY

LONG / SHORT
The chip. LONG: 50 to 550 ms. SHORT: 50 to 110 ms, for slapback and doubling; TIME stays within that range.
TIME
The delay time. Changes glide smoothly, so the repeats bend in pitch as you turn it.
REPEATS
Feedback, 0 to 110% (40% by default). Above 100% the repeats grow instead of fading - self-oscillation.
DUAL CHIP
Runs the right side about 3% longer than the left, so the two sides' repeats fall slightly apart: a wider, doubled stereo echo.
OSC LIMIT
On by default. Holds the feedback in check so self-oscillation stays at a usable level instead of running away without limit.

TONE

CLOCK LPF
The low-pass filter on the repeats, 1 to 8 kHz (3.5 kHz by default). Because it is inside the loop, every repeat is darker than the one before. Lower it for murky, distant echoes; raise it for brighter ones.
NOISE
The chip's clock noise added to the repeats, 0 to 100% (15% by default).

MASTER

MIX
The dry / echo balance, 0 to 100% (35% by default). Up to 50% the dry signal stays at full level and the echo comes up; from 50% the dry fades out, leaving only the echo at 100%.
DRIFT
See Drift below.
OUTPUT
The output level, -24 to +12 dB.
OUT meters
Output level, left and right. Click them to reset the peak hold.

The header

PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets (or click the preset display). Choosing one sets everything except TRIM and OUTPUT.
◄ / ►
Step through the presets.
Preset display
Shows the preset number and name. Click it for the list.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Echo 34 has no input guard and no output stage: the levels are set with TRIM and OUTPUT only.

Drift

DRIFT (0 - 100%, 50% by default) brings the unsteadiness of a real analogue unit: the chip's clock wanders, so the delay time breathes a little, and the clock filter - the tone of the repeats - slowly opens and closes. 50% is the standard amount, 100% twice that, 0 steady; the wander also speeds up as you turn it up.

Presets

Init
The default settings: LONG, 250 ms, 40% repeats.
Slapback
SHORT, 95 ms, a single faint repeat, bright.
Dark Repeats
LONG, 380 ms, 60% repeats through a dark 1.8 kHz filter.
Dual Chip Doubler
SHORT, 60 ms, DUAL CHIP on: a wide doubled sound.
Ambient Wash
LONG, 520 ms, 78% repeats with DUAL CHIP and more drift.
Self-Oscillation
LONG, 300 ms, 105% repeats and more drive: the echoes build up on their own.
Lo-Fi Short
SHORT, 80 ms, heavy drive, a dark filter and lots of noise.
Clean Long
LONG, 450 ms, little drive, a bright 7 kHz filter and very little noise.

Tips

  • For dub throws, set REPEATS around 90-105% and turn TIME by hand: the repeats swoop in pitch as the delay glides.
  • If the echoes get too dark too fast, raise CLOCK LPF rather than REPEATS.
  • On a send, set MIX to 100% so only the echo comes back.
  • Use the SHORT chip with DUAL CHIP for a quick stereo double on a mono vocal or guitar.

File: gfx-echo-34.jsfx

Modulation & space

Echofish

An envelope and step filter with a tape, bucket-brigade or digital echo below it.

Overview

Echofish is two effects on one panel. The top is an automatic filter in the spirit of the classic 70s envelope-filter pedals and the modern sample-and-hold filter machines: a resonant two-pole filter that is swept by your playing (the classic auto-wah quack) or stepped in time with the project.

The bottom strip is an echo with three characters - warm, saturating TAPE, darker BUCKET (bucket-brigade) and clean DIGITAL - with sync, ping-pong, freeze, ducking and a vintage EQ on the repeats.

ORDER sets which comes first: FILTER > ECHO, so the echoes repeat the filtered sound, or ECHO > FILTER, so the filter moves over the echoes too.

Reach for it for

  • Funky envelope-filter guitar, clav and bass
  • Rhythmic sample-and-hold filter steps locked to the song
  • Dub-style echoes that wobble and saturate (TAPE, high FEEDBACK)
  • Filtered synth blips and gurgles with a ping-pong echo
  • Vocal or lead delays that duck out of the way while the part plays (DUCK)

Controls

The header

HOLD
Freezes the filter where it is. The cutoff stops moving until you switch HOLD off.
ON
Switches the filter in or out. With it out, the dry signal passes through the filter section; the echo keeps working.
PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets; the ◄ / ► keys step through them and the display shows the one in use (click it for the list too). Choosing one sets the filter controls; the echo and the input / output strip are left as they are. The last entry resets the filter and the IN / OUT strip to their defaults.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Display

Cutoff trace
The filter's cutoff over the last few moments, on a 25 Hz - 18 kHz scale, with its current value at the top right. In S&H modes a faint line marks each new step.
ENV
Lights when the envelope follower picks up your playing.

Sweep

Up / down arrows
The sweep direction. Click the up arrow (blue block, left) for UP: the filter opens upwards from FREQ. Click the down arrow (red block, right) for DOWN: it sweeps down from FREQ. The block in use is lit.
DEPTH
How far the sweep goes, 0 - 6 octaves.
FREQ
Where the sweep starts, 60 Hz - 3 kHz.
FILTER
The filter type: LP (low-pass), BP (band-pass, the classic wah), HP (high-pass) or N (notch).
RANGE LO / HI
HI moves the whole sweep up an octave.
PATTERN FREE / LOOP
For the S&H modes. FREE: a new random step every time. LOOP: a one-bar random pattern that repeats, so it grooves with the song.
ENV / S&H / BOTH
What moves the filter. ENV: the envelope follower - the harder you play, the further it sweeps. S&H: random steps in time with the project. BOTH: the steps, scaled by how hard you play.

The knob row

PEAK
The filter's resonance, 0.5 - 15. Higher is more vocal and whistling.
SENS
How strongly the envelope follows your playing. Raise it for quiet sources, lower it if the filter sits fully open.
RESPONSE
How fast the envelope follows: from fast and snappy (about 1 ms attack, 30 ms release) to slow and vocal (about 20 ms attack, 800 ms release).
RATE
The step length for S&H: 1/2, 1/4, 1/8, 1/16 or 1/32 of a note.
MULT
Multiplies the step rate by 1, 2 or 4: x2 gives twice as many steps per RATE.
GLIDE
Smooths the jump between steps, 0 - 100%. Higher settings slide from one step to the next.
DRIVE
Pushes the filter for a fatter, grittier sound, 0 - 100%.
MIX
Dry / filtered balance, 0 - 100%.
OUTPUT
The filter's output level, -12 to +12 dB.

The steps follow the project's position while it plays, and keep running at the project tempo when it is stopped (120 BPM if there is no tempo).

Echo

SYNC
On (default): TIME is a note value at the project tempo. Off: TIME is in milliseconds.
PING-PONG
The input enters on the left and the echoes bounce from side to side.
FREEZE
Holds the echoes exactly as they are and lets no new sound in: an endless loop. Switch it off to let it fade.
VINTAGE
Click to step through off, VINTAGE -3, -6 and -9. On, the echoes you hear get the classic studio send EQ: a high-pass at 140 Hz, a low-pass at 5 kHz and a 3, 6 or 9 dB cut at 500 Hz. The tone inside the feedback loop is still set by LO CUT and HI CUT.
ORDER
FILTER > ECHO or ECHO > FILTER: click to swap.
TAPE / BUCKET / DIGITAL
The echo's character. TAPE: soft saturation and a gently rolled-off top; changing TIME bends the pitch slowly, like a tape machine. BUCKET: darker, with a firmer clip and a slight chorus on the tails. DIGITAL: clean, with only a safety limit on the feedback.
TIME
The echo time: 20 - 2000 ms, or with SYNC from 4 bars down to 1/32, including dotted (D) and triplet (T) values. 1/8 D by default.
FEEDBACK
How much each echo feeds the next, 0 - 110%. Above 100% the echoes build up and run away (TAPE and BUCKET saturate to keep it in check).
DRIVE
How hard the feedback loop saturates in TAPE and BUCKET, 0 - 100%. DIGITAL stays clean.
LO CUT / HI CUT
Filters inside the feedback loop, so each repeat gets thinner and darker: LO CUT 20 - 1000 Hz, HI CUT 1 - 20 kHz.
DUCK
Lowers the echoes while the input is playing, 0 - 100%, so they fill the gaps rather than the part.
DRIFT
The tape's wow and flutter, 0 - 100% (50% is the standard amount, 100% twice that, 0% none). It wobbles the echo time, and the wander speeds up a little as you turn it up.
WIDTH
The stereo width of the echoes, 0 - 100%. Without PING-PONG, the right echo is also very slightly longer at full width.
MIX
Dry / echo balance, 0 - 100%. The dry signal stays at full level up to 50%, then fades; at 100% you hear only the echoes.

IN / OUT

TRIM
Input level before anything else, from -24 to +12 dB.
GUARD
Click the TRIM knob to switch it; its LED sits at the knob's upper right. On by default. Gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources. The LED glows brighter while it is working.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper at -0.3 dBFS (no latency), or a lookahead limiter (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). CLIP is on by default and flashes red when it clips.

In REAPER's parameter list only

EQ: Wow / EQ: Flutter
The standard amounts of wow and flutter that DRIFT scales (20% and 15% by default).
EQ: Drift speed
How fast the wow wanders, 0.05 - 2 Hz.
EQ: Output
The echo section's output level, -24 to +12 dB.
Bias / Sat low band / Sat high band / Ceiling
The rest of the output stage: the saturation's even-harmonic bias, where its low and high bands start (40 - 400 Hz and 2 - 16 kHz) and the clip ceiling or limiter threshold.

Presets

Init
The default filter settings: an upward band-pass envelope wah from 250 Hz. A new instance opens on it.
Funk Up
The classic up-sweeping envelope wah: band-pass from 250 Hz, a little drive.
Bass Quack (down)
Low-pass sweeping down from 900 Hz, for bass.
Ship Steps
A looping one-bar pattern of 1/16 steps through a resonant band-pass.
Random Gurgle
Random, never-repeating steps (1/8 x2) with a lot of glide through a resonant low-pass.
Envelope Steps
A looping 1/16 pattern, scaled by how hard you play.
Notch Sweep
An envelope-driven notch with a slower response.
Slow Talker
A very resonant band-pass with a slow, vocal response.
Clav Wah
Fast, bright band-pass wah in the HI range with extra drive.
Synth Blips
Very resonant 1/32 steps in the HI range, no glide.
Lowpass Bloom
A gentle low-pass opening five octaves up from 120 Hz with a slow response.
Reset all to defaults
The filter and the IN / OUT strip back to their starting values; the echo is left as it is.

Tips

  • If the envelope filter barely moves, raise SENS; if it sits wide open, lower it or turn TRIM down.
  • S&H with PATTERN on LOOP gives a riff that repeats every bar; a little GLIDE makes the steps sound less mechanical.
  • For dub throws, use TAPE with FEEDBACK around 85 - 100% and automate MIX; FREEZE holds the last phrase while you play over it.
  • ECHO > FILTER lets each echo get swept as well, which sounds great with S&H on sustained parts.
  • DUCK at 30 - 50% keeps a busy echo out of the way of a vocal or lead.

File: gfx-echofish.jsfx

Modulation & space

Echomax

A multi-mode delay line: tape, bucket-brigade, clean digital and a driven kit-built echo in one box.

Overview

Echomax is four echoes in one. Each type has its own sound in the feedback loop, so the repeats change character as they circulate: TAPE saturates warmly and darkens, BUCKET is very dark and gritty with a slow clock wobble, DIGITAL stays clean, and ECHORDER drives an op-amp hard into a dark bucket-brigade filter with a little background hiss.

The delay time can be set in milliseconds or locked to the project tempo. When you change it, the repeats glide to the new time rather than jumping - slowly on TAPE (for the classic pitch swoop), quickly on DIGITAL.

Around the echo sit ping-pong, freeze, ducking, a vintage 500 Hz cut on the echoes, drift for wow and flutter, and the IN / OUT strip with trim, saturation and a clipper or limiter.

Reach for it for

  • Slapback and tape echo on vocals and guitars
  • Dub echoes that build into self-oscillation
  • Clean, ducked vocal delays that stay out of the way
  • Ping-pong echoes across the stereo field
  • Freezing a phrase into a loop

Controls

Echo types (header)

TAPE
Warm tape saturation and a high-frequency roll-off (about 5.5 kHz) on every pass. Time changes glide slowly, so turning TIME gives the tape pitch swoop.
BUCKET
A bucket-brigade echo: a steep, dark roll-off (about 2.8 kHz), gritty clipping in the loop and a slow clock wobble on the delay.
DIGITAL
Clean and uncoloured: DRIVE has no effect (the knob reads clean), and only very hot repeats are softly limited.
ECHORDER
A driven kit-built echo: an overdriven op-amp with lopsided saturation, a dark bucket-brigade filter (about 3.2 kHz), a slow clock drift and background hiss that rises with DRIVE. DUAL CHIP works only in this mode.

Readout

Time display
The delay time in ms (also when synced), with the sync division or FREE beside it, and FROZEN, RUNAWAY (feedback above 100%) or PING-PONG when they apply.
Repeat ladder
Sixteen lights showing how the repeats die away at the current feedback. With ping-pong on, the repeats alternate between two rows.

Switches

SYNC
Locks TIME to the project tempo (on by default).
PING
Ping-pong: the input is summed to mono and the repeats bounce between left and right.
FREEZE
Stops recording new input and keeps what is in the delay circulating without loss - an endless loop you can play over.
DUAL CHIP
ECHORDER only: the right side runs off its own slightly mistuned clock, so with WIDTH up it drifts and beats against the left instead of sitting at a fixed offset.
VINTAGE EQ (OFF / -3dB / -6dB / -9dB)
The classic studio trick on the echoes only: a cut at 500 Hz of the chosen depth, with a high-pass at 140 Hz and a low-pass at 5 kHz. OFF bypasses it.

Delay

Hover over a knob to see its value.

TIME
The delay time, 20 to 2000 ms, or with SYNC a note value from 4 bars to 1/32 (dotted and triplet included).
FEEDBACK
How much of each repeat is fed back, 0 to 110%. Past 100% the echo grows on its own (RUNAWAY) - the loop's saturation keeps it from exploding.
DRIVE
How hard the repeats are pushed into the type's saturation, 0 to 100%. Not used in DIGITAL.
LO CUT
A high-pass inside the feedback loop, 20 to 1000 Hz: each repeat gets thinner than the last.
HI CUT
A low-pass inside the feedback loop, 1 to 20 kHz: each repeat gets darker than the last.
DUCK
Turns the echoes down while you play, 0 to 100%, and lets them come back up in the gaps (fast to dip, about a quarter of a second to recover).

Drift

Tape-style wow (a slow random wander of the delay time) and flutter (a fast wobble around 6.5 Hz). The amounts of wow and flutter come with the presets.

DRIFT
0 to 100%. 50% gives the standard amount of wow and flutter, 100% doubles it, 0 turns it off. The wander also speeds up as you turn it up.

IN / OUT

TRIM
Input level before anything else, from -24 to +12 dB.
GUARD
Off by default. Click the TRIM knob (without dragging) to switch it; the small LED at the knob's upper right shows it is on and glows brighter while it works. It gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS. It never boosts quiet sources.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out, 0 to 100%: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT (OUT)
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper (no latency) that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS, or a lookahead limiter (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER) that holds them at -0.3 dBFS. The CLIP key flashes red while the clipper is working.

Master

MIX
Dry / echo balance, 0 to 100% (35% by default). At 50% both are at full level; below that the echoes fade, above it the dry signal fades.
WIDTH
The stereo spread of the echoes, 0 to 100%. At 0% the echoes are mono; turning it up keeps the difference between the sides and makes the right side's delay a touch longer than the left.
OUTPUT
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.
OUT meters
Output level, left and right. Click to reset the peak hold.

The header

PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets; the arrows beside it step through them, and the display shows the one in use (click it for the list too). The last entry resets everything to the defaults.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Presets

The presets set the echo type, time, feedback, drive, filters, duck, ping-pong, wow, flutter, mix and drift, and switch FREEZE off; the Echorder dub presets also set DUAL CHIP. WIDTH, OUTPUT, VINTAGE EQ and the IN / OUT strip are left alone.

Tape: Worn Tape Echo
Dotted-eighth tape echo with heavy wow and plenty of drift.
Tape: Slapback Saturation
A 110 ms tape slap with saturation.
Tape: Dub Siren Oscillation
Quarter-note tape echo at 85% feedback, dark and wobbly, on the edge of oscillation.
Bucket: Memory Lane Ping-Pong
Eighth-note bucket-brigade echoes bouncing left and right, lightly ducked.
Bucket: Dark Analog Crunch
180 ms dark, driven bucket echoes with strong feedback.

280 ms driven Echorder repeats at 70% feedback.

An 85 ms slap pushed to near-full drive.

Clean quarter-note echo, ducked by 50%, no wow or flutter.

Clean eighth-note-triplet ping-pong, open to 18 kHz.
Tape: Ambient Flutter Wash
A 1.2 s tape echo with 75% feedback, ducking and lots of wow - an ambient wash.

300 ms with more wow, DUAL CHIP on for a drifting stereo image.
Echorder: Chaos Dub Grit

450 ms dark dub echo with wow, DUAL CHIP on.
Reset Defaults
Every control back to its starting value.

Tips

  • On TAPE, sweep TIME during a repeat for the classic pitch-bending dub swoop.
  • For a vocal echo that never clutters the words, try DIGITAL with DUCK at 40 - 60%.
  • Push FEEDBACK past 100% and ride HI CUT or FREEZE for dub build-ups; pull it back before it gets too loud.
  • VINTAGE EQ at -6 dB takes the boxiness out of the repeats so they sit behind the dry sound.

File: gfx-echomax.jsfx

Modulation & space

Flanger

Three flangers in one: bucket-brigade pedal, wide-sweep rack and true through-zero.

Overview

Flanger mixes your signal with a copy of itself delayed by a few milliseconds, and sweeps that delay. The two combine into a comb of peaks and notches that moves up and down the spectrum - the swooshing, jet-plane sound.

Three characters are built in. PEDAL is the classic bucket-brigade stompbox: a darker loop with a little grit, made for strong regeneration. RACK is the clean, wide-sweep studio unit, going from metallic flange down to slow, chorus-like movement. THRU-0 recreates the original tape trick, where the swept copy passes right through the dry signal for a deep cancellation at the crossing point.

Besides the usual LFO, the sweep can be frozen so you can place the comb by hand (MATRIX), or driven by how hard you play (ENVELOPE).

Reach for it for

  • Jet-plane sweeps on guitars, synths and drum loops
  • Through-zero tape flanging on a whole mix or a bus
  • Hollow, nasal tones with negative regen
  • Static comb-filter colour with MATRIX
  • Touch-sensitive swooshes with ENVELOPE
  • Wide stereo motion with SPREAD

Controls

Modes (header)

PEDAL
The bucket-brigade pedal. The delay sweeps from about 0.3 to 6 ms, and the loop is darker with the delay chips' faint hiss. High REGEN gives the classic jet sound.
RACK
The wide-sweep studio rack: clean, sweeping from 0.15 to 14 ms. Short settings are metallic; long ones become a slow, chorus-like sweep.
THRU-0
Through-zero flanging, as done with two tape machines. The dry signal is delayed too, and the swept copy passes through it, giving the deep cancellation as it crosses. Best with MIX at 50%.

Flanger

SINE / TRI / SQR / RND
The LFO shape. Triangle is the classic even flanger sweep; sine lingers at the ends; square jumps between two settings; random wanders.
SWEEP
Click to choose what moves the sweep. LFO: the usual automatic sweep. MATRIX: the sweep stops and MANUAL places the comb by hand (the 'filter matrix' trick). ENVELOPE: your playing drives the sweep - louder notes push it further.
FREE / SYNC / FLOW
How the LFO rate is set: FREE in Hz, SYNC locked to the project tempo and timeline, FLOW tempo-synced but running continuously through loops. Not used in MATRIX or ENVELOPE.
RATE / SENS
The LFO speed (0.02 - 10 Hz, or a note value when synced). In ENVELOPE mode this knob becomes SENS: how far your playing pushes the sweep.
MANUAL
Where the sweep sits - the delay time at its centre. Low settings give high, metallic combs; high settings lower, more chorus-like tones.
WIDTH
How far the sweep travels either side of MANUAL. Zero gives a static comb (not used in MATRIX).
REGEN
Feedback, from -95% to +95%. Positive gives the ringing, metallic jet; negative gives a hollow, nasal tone. The level is compensated so strong settings don't jump out.
SPREAD
Offsets the right side's sweep from the left, from 0 to 180 degrees. 0 is a classic mono flange; 90 - 180 gives wide stereo motion.
Display
The live comb filter (frequency response) and the current delay time; MATRIX or ENVELOPE is shown when active.

Drift

A slow random wander, like a hand on the tape reel or an old clock.

RATE
How much the LFO rate wanders.
WIDTH
How much the sweep width wanders.
MANUAL
How much the centre of the sweep wanders.
DRIFT SPEED
How fast the drift moves (0.02 - 2 Hz).

Master

MIX
Dry / flanged balance. 50% gives the deepest notches; lower is subtler; 100% is the delayed signal only (a pitch-wobbling vibrato when sweeping).
OUTPUT
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.
OUTPUT button
Folds the input and output stages out below the panel.
OUT meters
Output level, left and right.

The header

PRESETS
The factory presets. They set the flanger controls; drift speed, output and the input / output stages are left alone. The last entry resets everything.
Theme picker
Changes the panel's colour theme.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large.
HW
Switches between the hardware-style panel and the flat look.

Input and output stages (OUTPUT fold-out)

TRIM
Input level before anything else, from -24 to +12 dB.
GUARD
On by default. Gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources. The tag shows how much it is working.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
BIAS
Shapes the saturation: more bias adds more even (2nd) harmonics, for a warmer, rounder colour.
LO BAND / HI BAND
Where the low and high saturation bands start (40 - 400 Hz and 2 - 16 kHz).
CEILING / THRESH
With CLIP: the level the peaks are softly clipped at. With LIMIT: the threshold of a brickwall limiter that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS - pulling it down limits harder and makes the output louder.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper (no latency), or a lookahead limiter (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER).

The ? key

?
Next to the S / M / L size keys. Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Presets

Classic Pedal
The everyday bucket-brigade flanger: triangle sweep, moderate regen.
Jet Plane
Slow, wide sweep with strong regen - the jet.
Negative Hollow
Negative regen for a hollow, nasal sweep.
Stereo Pedal
The pedal with the right side offset by 90 degrees.
Rack Wide Sweep
The rack's long sweep, slow and wide in stereo.
Rack Chorus-Flange
Long delays, gentle width and faster rate: between chorus and flanger.
Thru-Zero Tape
Slow through-zero sweep without regen, like two tape machines.
Thru-Zero Jet
Through-zero with regen for a more intense crossing.
Filter Matrix
The sweep stopped: set the comb by hand with MANUAL.
Envelope Swoosh
Your playing drives the sweep; strong regen.
Synced 1/2 Bar
Rack flanger locked to half-bar sweeps of the project tempo.
Reset all to defaults
Every control back to its starting value.

Tips

  • For the classic through-zero effect on a mix bus, use THRU-0 with REGEN at 0, a slow sine sweep and MIX at 50%.
  • In MATRIX mode, automate MANUAL for sweeps you control exactly, in time with the music.
  • ENVELOPE works well on drums and funky guitar: each hit swooshes, then the comb settles back.
  • If the flange sounds too thin or harsh, try negative REGEN or the RACK mode at a higher MANUAL setting.

File: gfx-flanger.jsfx

Modulation & space

Haas Doubler

Short-delay stereo widening and doubling, with detune and drift.

Overview

Haas Doubler widens a sound with short delays. A copy delayed by a few milliseconds and placed on the other side is heard as the same sound, but wider - the Haas effect. Add a little drift and detune and it becomes a convincing double-tracked part.

Three layouts are built in. L/R is the classic: the dry signal on one side, the delayed copy on the other. LCR keeps the dry signal in the centre with delayed copies hard left and right. SPREAD uses the delayed copies only, left and right. In LCR and SPREAD the right copy's delay is the left one's times a multiplier (x2 to x6), so the two sides never line up.

DRIFT makes the delay time, the pan position and the level of each copy wander slowly and independently, like a second take. DETUNE pitch-shifts each side by a few cents for the classic detuned-doubler width.

Reach for it for

  • Widening mono vocals, guitars and synths
  • Fake double-tracking on lead vocals
  • A wet-only widener on an FX send (DRY off)
  • Detuned stereo width on pads and leads
  • Wobbly, tape-style double tracking

Controls

Modes (header)

L/R
The classic Haas: dry signal on one side, the delayed copy on the other.
LCR
Dry signal in the centre, delayed copies hard left and right (the right copy at DELAY times the multiplier).
SPREAD
Delayed copies only, left and right (the right at DELAY times the multiplier). No dry signal in the centre.

Display

Stereo stage
Shows where each copy sits between L and R, its delay time and (by the size of the dot) its level, moving as it drifts. DRY marks the dry signal in the centre in LCR, or on its side in L/R.
INPUT
A VU meter of the input level (0 VU = -18 dBFS average).

Doubler

DELAY
The delay time of the copy, 1 - 40 ms, in 1 ms steps (hold Shift for fine adjustment). Short times give width; longer ones start to sound like a separate double.
x2 - x6
LCR and SPREAD only: the right copy's delay is DELAY times this number.
SWAP SIDES
Swaps the left and right copies (in L/R: which side gets the dry signal).
MONO / STEREO
MONO sums the input to mono before doubling; STEREO doubles each side from its own channel.
LEVEL
The level of the delayed copies, -24 to +6 dB.
LOW SPLIT
A high-pass filter (12 dB/oct) on the delayed copies only, 20 - 1000 Hz. Raising it keeps the low end centred and solid while the width happens higher up.
WIDTH
Overall stereo width, 0 - 100%. Lower it to narrow the effect.
DRY
The dry signal on or off. Off gives the delayed copies only, for use on an FX send.
MONO CHK
Collapses the output to mono, so you can check how the doubled sound holds up on a mono system.

Drift

A slow random wander, independent for each copy, like a second take.

DRIFT
One dial, 0 - 100%. It makes each copy's delay time, pan position (wandering inward from its side) and level drift slowly. 50% is the standard amount, 100% twice that, 0 none; the wander also gets a little faster as you turn it up.

IN / OUT

TRIM
Input level before anything else, from -24 to +12 dB. A single click on the knob (without dragging) switches GUARD on or off; its LED at the knob's upper right is dark when GUARD is off, red when it is on, and glows brighter while it is catching peaks.
GUARD
On by default. Gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out, 0 - 100%: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper (no latency), or a lookahead limiter that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). The CLIP key flashes red while the clipper is working.

Detune

LEFT / RIGHT
Pitch-shifts the left and right copies by -50 to +50 cents. Small opposite settings (for example -8 and +8) give the classic detuned-doubler width. Detune follows the side a copy sits on; in L/R the dry side takes its side's detune too.

Master

OUTPUT
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.
OUT meters
Output level, left and right, with a floating peak. Click a meter to clear the peak.

The header

PRESETS, arrows and display
PRESETS (or a click on the display) opens the list of factory presets; the ◄ / ► keys step through them. The display shows the preset number and name.
Theme picker
Changes the panel's colour theme.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.
HW
Switches between the hardware-style panel and the flat look.

The presets set the mode, delay, multiplier, level, low split, width and the drift settings; DRY, SWAP SIDES, the input mode, MONO CHK, detune, output and the IN / OUT strip are left as they are. The last entry in the list resets everything to the defaults.

Presets

Short
LCR with 6 ms copies, 3 dB down, and a light drift - tight width.
Med
LCR with 12 ms copies at full level and a moderate drift.
Long
LCR with 20 ms copies, 4 dB down, low split at 200 Hz and a little more drift - closer to a separate double.
Classic Haas
L/R with an 18 ms copy and no drift - the textbook Haas widener.
Wide Spread
SPREAD with 12 ms copies, low split at 100 Hz, gentle drift.
Super Wobble
SPREAD with x3 copies and heavy, fast drift of time, pan and level - deliberately seasick.
Vintage ADT
L/R with a 22 ms copy, plenty of time drift and a little level drift at 70% width - tape-style automatic double tracking.
Mono Safe
LCR with x3 copies, 6 dB down, low split at 300 Hz and 70% width, so it stays solid in mono.
Reset all to defaults
Every control back to its starting value.

Tips

  • Use MONO CHK while you set DELAY: some delay times cancel badly in mono. Mono Safe is a good starting point when that matters.
  • On an FX send, switch DRY off and blend the doubler in with the send level.
  • Raise LOW SPLIT on bass-heavy sources so the low end stays in the middle.
  • A few cents of opposite DETUNE plus a little DRIFT is often more convincing than a longer delay.

File: gfx-haas-doubler.jsfx

Modulation & space

Ivans Echo

A small solid-state echo box: one bucket-brigade line, narrow and hissy, from slapback to runaway.

Overview

Ivans Echo is in the spirit of the budget mini echo boxes of the early 80s: four knobs on a small metal box, with one bucket-brigade (BBD) chip inside. The input goes through a simple op-amp preamp, then a compander squeezes it into the chip and expands it again on the way out, so quiet passages breathe and the hiss sinks between notes. The echo path is narrow (about 4 kHz at the shortest delays, falling to about 2.6 kHz at the longest, because the chip's clock slows down) and it is mono, as on the original; the dry signal stays in stereo.

Short settings give a hard, metallic, obviously electronic sound; longer ones give a single slapback or dark, deep repeats. Turn REPEAT past about 2 o'clock and the echoes run away. As on a real BBD unit, turning DELAY TIME glides the delay rather than jumping, so the repeats bend in pitch while you turn it.

Reach for it for

  • Rockabilly and rock'n'roll slapback
  • Quick doubling (ADT) on vocals and guitars
  • Metallic, boxy short echoes
  • Lo-fi dub repeats and runaway feedback
  • A cheap, characterful echo on a mic'd vocal

Controls

INPUT

INST / MIC
The two input sockets. INST is flat. MIC adds 12 dB of preamp gain, cuts the lows below about 150 Hz and brings up the hiss.
VOLUME
Input level into the preamp, -24 to +18 dB. Set it so the PEAK light only flickers; push it further and the preamp clips, roughly and a little unevenly.
PEAK
Lights as the preamp starts to clip.
MOMENTARY
Echo on or off (on by default). Off stops new signal reaching the echo, and the repeats already in it die away naturally.

ECHO

BALANCE
Dry to echo, 0 to 100% (35% by default). Up to 50% the dry signal stays at full level and the echo comes up; from 50% the dry fades out, leaving only the echo at 100%.
REPEAT
Feedback, 0 to 110% (35% by default): from a single echo to many. Past about 85% (2 o'clock) the repeats build up instead of fading; the loop clips like the original's op-amp, so it stays bounded.
REPEAT light
Shows the level in the feedback loop: it glows when the repeats are strong.
DELAY TIME
20 to 400 ms (180 ms by default). The longer the delay, the darker and grittier the echo. Changes glide, so the repeats bend in pitch as you turn it. With SYNC on, the knob is overridden and its readout shows the synced time.
SYNC
Click for a list: FREE, or a note value locked to the project tempo (1/32, 1/16T, 1/16, 1/8T, 1/16D, 1/8, 1/4T, 1/8D). The time stays within 20 to 400 ms, so slow tempos may reach the limit.

MASTER

DRIFT
See Drift below.
OUTPUT
The output level, -24 to +12 dB.
OUT meters
Output level, left and right. Click them to reset the peak hold.

The header

PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets (or click the preset display). Choosing one sets everything except MOMENTARY and OUTPUT.
◄ / ►
Step through the presets.
Preset display
Shows the preset number and name. Click it for the list.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Ivans Echo has no input guard and no output stage: the levels are set with VOLUME and OUTPUT only.

Drift

DRIFT (0 - 100%, 50% by default) works on two things. The chip's clock wanders, so the delay time breathes a little. And the supply voltage wanders and sags: the preamp's headroom and gain move slowly, and loud passages pull the supply down so the preamp squashes a little more. 50% is the standard amount, 100% twice that, 0 steady; the wander also speeds up as you turn it up.

Presets

Init
The default settings: INST, 180 ms, 35% repeat.
Slapback
110 ms, a single repeat, the input pushed a little.
ADT Double
28 ms, no repeats, more drift: a quick double.
Metallic Room
24 ms with heavy repeats: the hard, metallic short-echo sound.
Dub Repeats
375 ms, 68% repeat: dark, long repeats.
Runaway
260 ms, 98% repeat, a hot input and lots of drift: the echoes build up on their own.
Mic Booth
MIC input, 140 ms, a few repeats.
Lo-Fi Haze
320 ms, a clipped input and maximum drift.

Tips

  • For dub throws, tap MOMENTARY off and on to catch single words, and ride DELAY TIME by hand for pitch swoops.
  • On a send, set BALANCE to 100% so only the echo comes back.
  • If the repeats get muddy, shorten the delay: the echo opens up as the clock speeds up.
  • Use MIC for a rougher, hissier character, even on line-level sources (turn VOLUME down to suit).

File: gfx-ivans-echo.jsfx

Modulation & space

J-Delay

Four 1980s Japanese digital delays: chorus-delay, pitch-shift, compact and sampling.

Overview

J-Delay brings together four characters of the early Japanese digital delays of the 1980s - the 12- and 14-bit converters, modulated loops, pitch shifting in the feedback and sample loops. The four program keys change the algorithm.

VINTAGE DIGITAL is the flagship rack delay, with a dual-phase LFO (sine on the left, cosine on the right) for a wide chorus-delay. PITCH 12 is a 12-bit-style, 32 kHz unit with a pitch shifter in the loop, so every repeat moves by the PITCH interval. COMPACT is the small unit: a darker loop (held under 8 kHz) and a compander that breathes, with its faint hiss. SAMPLE HOLD locks the last stretch of audio into a loop for stutters and phrase sampling.

In every program, changing the delay time glides the pitch as on the hardware, and the clock is never quite steady. The VINT key switches on the vintage converters - a lower sample clock and fewer bits - for the gritty, aliased sound of the originals.

Reach for it for

  • Wide 80s chorus-delay on guitars, synths and vocals
  • Cascading octave and fifth repeats (PITCH 12)
  • Dark, breathing slapback and vocal echoes (COMPACT)
  • Stutters and live phrase loops (SAMPLE HOLD)
  • Lo-fi, bit-reduced repeats

Controls

Programs and status keys

VINTAGE DIG
The rack chorus-delay. The LFO runs sine on the left and cosine on the right, so MOD DEPTH spreads the repeats wide.
PITCH 12
Every repeat is pitch-shifted by PITCH semitones, so with feedback the echoes climb or fall. This program always uses the vintage converters, at 32 kHz and the BITS setting (12 by default).
COMPACT
The compact unit: the loop is held under 8 kHz (HI CUT can go no higher), and a 2:1 compander squeezes the signal into the loop and expands it coming out, so the repeats breathe, with a faint hiss.
SAMPLE HOLD
The sampling delay. With HOLD on, the last TIME L of audio plays round and round; nothing new is recorded.
SYNC
Locks TIME L and TIME R to the project tempo as note values: 1/1 to 1/32, with dotted (D) and triplet (T) values.
HOLD
Freezes the loop in SAMPLE HOLD. Switching it on from another program selects SAMPLE HOLD. The display shows HOLD in red while it is held.
VINT
The vintage clock: the delay line runs at the old hardware's sample rate with nearest-sample reads, and the converters use BITS bits. Off is the clean MODERN path. Always lit in PITCH 12.

Display, meters and presets

DELAY L / DELAY R
The delay times in milliseconds, in orange. The green line underneath shows FREE RUNNING, or the SYNC note values and tempo, and the converter setting (VINTAGE with its bits, or MODERN 24-BIT).
INPUT / DELAY
Level meters for the input and for the delayed signal.
BITS
Steps the vintage converters through 10, 12, 14 and 16 bits (12 by default). Fewer bits give more grit and noise. Used when VINT is on (and always in PITCH 12).
PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets. The ◄ / ► keys beside it step through the ten presets, and the display beside them shows the number of the one in use. The last entry resets everything to the defaults.

The knobs

Each knob has a green readout underneath that shows its value while you touch it, and a red light that is lit whenever the knob is away from its default. Double-click a knob to reset it; hold Shift while dragging for fine moves.

TIME L / TIME R
The left and right delay times, 1 to 2700 ms (300 and 390 ms by default), or a note value with SYNC on. Changing the time glides the pitch, as on the hardware.
FEEDBACK
How much of the delay is fed back for more repeats, 0 to 99% (35% by default).
CROSS
Cross-feed, 0 to 100%: sends the feedback to the opposite side, so the repeats ping-pong.
LO CUT
Thins the repeats below this frequency, 20 to 1000 Hz (80 Hz by default). Each repeat gets thinner.
HI CUT
Darkens the repeats above this frequency, 1 to 20 kHz (9 kHz by default). Each repeat gets darker.
MOD DEPTH
How far the LFO sweeps the delay time, 0 to 10 ms (1.2 ms by default). Low settings give a gentle shimmer, high settings a deep chorus or vibrato.
MOD RATE
The LFO speed, 0.05 to 10 Hz (0.8 Hz by default).
PITCH
The interval each repeat is shifted by in PITCH 12, from -12 to +12 semitones (+12 by default). Labelled (PITCH) in the other programs, where it does nothing.
DUCK
Pulls the repeats down while you play, up to 18 dB, and lets them bloom back when you stop, 0 to 100%. Keeps busy parts clear.
RECOVERY
How quickly the repeats come back after ducking, 20 to 2000 ms (400 ms by default).
DRIVE
Drives the input stage before the delay, 0 to 100%, for a little grit.
WIDTH
The stereo width of the repeats, 0 to 150% (100% by default). 0 is mono; above 100% widens beyond the original.
MIX
Dry / delayed balance, 0 to 100% (30% by default).
LEVEL
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.

Drift

DRIFT
The clock is not quite steady: the left and right delay times wander slowly and independently, by up to a fraction of a millisecond, for a gentle, living pitch movement. 0 to 100% (50% by default): 50% is the standard amount, 100% twice that, 0 none; higher settings also wander a little faster.

IN / OUT

TRIM
Input level before anything else, from -24 to +12 dB.
GUARD
Off by default. When on, it gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources. An orange light shows while it is working.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper at -0.3 dBFS (no latency, the default), or a lookahead limiter holding the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). The OUT caption turns to a red CLIP while the clipper is working.

The header

S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Presets

80s Chorus Delay
VINTAGE DIGITAL at 300 / 390 ms with 1.8 ms of modulation: the classic wide chorus-delay.
Lead Guitar Echo
Synced 1/8 dotted repeats with 40% feedback, a little cross-feed and a darker top.
Dimension Wide
Very short 14 / 21 ms delays with deep, slow modulation and no feedback, for width.
Octave Cascade
PITCH 12 at 330 ms: each repeat climbs an octave.
Fifth Spiral
PITCH 12 with uneven times and cross-feed: each repeat rises a fifth and bounces side to side.
Octave Drop
PITCH 12 at 360 ms: each repeat falls an octave.
Dark Vocal Echo
COMPACT with synced quarter and dotted-quarter repeats, vintage clock on.
Compact Slap
COMPACT at 120 / 135 ms with light feedback: a short slapback.
Stutter Loop (press HOLD)
SAMPLE HOLD synced to a quarter note at 14 bits, 60% mix: press HOLD to loop it.
10-Bit Grit
VINTAGE DIGITAL on the vintage clock at 10 bits, with feedback and cross-feed.
Reset all to defaults
Every control back to its starting value.

Tips

  • The presets set the program, times, feedback, filters, modulation, pitch, clock and mix; DRIVE, DUCK, WIDTH, DRIFT, LEVEL and the IN / OUT settings are left alone (except Reset).
  • For live phrase sampling, choose SAMPLE HOLD, set TIME L to the length you want to catch (or use SYNC), then press HOLD at the end of the phrase.
  • Automate TIME for tape-style pitch swoops: the time glides, so the repeats bend as it changes.
  • Add DUCK on vocals and lead lines so the echoes stay out of the way while the part is playing and fill the gaps afterwards.

File: gfx-j-delay.jsfx

Modulation & space

J-Verb

Four Japanese studio and broadcast reverbs of the 1980s: dense reflection grids and glassy digital clarity.

Overview

J-Verb brings together four digital reverbs in the spirit of the Japanese studio and broadcast units of the 1980s: dense reflection grids, glassy clarity and clean converters. The PROGRAM keys pick the algorithm, and the whole faceplate changes with it.

HALL is the big modular hall: a dense eight-line network with extra diffusion inside the loop. 3D is a broadcast engine that builds up to 32 reflections from a three-dimensional room model (levels only, so they never cancel in mono) before a high-density tail. FLAGSHIP 40 places up to 40 individual early reflections, then a smooth tail. 12-BIT is the early high-density pioneer: six combs running at a 31.5 kHz clock through 12-bit converters.

Every parameter has its own display on the front panel, as on the hardware remotes: the early reflections and the tail each have their own delay and level, the decay time has separate multipliers for the lows and highs, and the room size sets the scale of everything. A dot-matrix grid shows the reflections as they fall in time.

Reach for it for

  • Big, dense halls for pads, strings and ballads (HALL)
  • Realistic rooms and stages with strong early reflections (3D, FLAGSHIP 40)
  • Grainy, bright 80s plates and drum rooms (12-BIT)
  • Long synthwave washes and ambient tails
  • Separate control of reflections and tail for placing a sound in a space

Controls

Working the displays

Every parameter has its own display. Drag up or down on a display (Shift for fine moves), use the mouse wheel over it, or press its DOWN / UP keys (hold for repeat). Double-click a display to reset it to its default. The last display you touched is outlined.

HEADROOM

HEADROOM LEDs
Four lights showing the input level at -30, -20, -10 and 0 dB. The 0 dB light is red.

PROGRAM

A HALL
The big modular hall: an eight-line network with allpasses inside the loop for massive density. DRIFT moves its read taps by a sample or two so the tail never sets, without any chorusing. Sparse, wide early reflections.
B 3D
The broadcast engine: up to 32 reflections from a three-dimensional room model (DENSITY sets how many) into a high-density tail. Runs at 48 kHz when VINTAGE is on.
C FLAGSHIP 40
Up to 40 individual early reflections (DENSITY sets how many and how wide they spread), then a smooth tail.
D 12-BIT
Six combs at a 31.5 kHz clock through 12-bit converters, with a few early reflections. The vintage clock is always on for this model; BITS can raise the converters to 16 or 24 bits. Its tail is darker at the top.
VINTAGE CLOCK
Runs the reverb at the unit's own clock rate (44.1 kHz for HALL and FLAGSHIP 40, 48 kHz for 3D, 31.5 kHz for 12-BIT) through converters with the bit depth set by BITS. Always lit on 12-BIT.
BITS
Steps the converter bit depth: 12, 16 or 24. Used whenever the vintage clock is on.
READ PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets.
Preset display
Shows the last preset recalled (P1 - P10), or P-- if none.
◄ / ►
The two keys under READ step to the previous or next preset (wrapping round).

The lamp rows

One-touch settings. The lamp lights on the key that matches the current value.

LOW CUT FREQ
Sets LOW CUT to 30, 50, 100 or 200 Hz.
HIGH CUT FREQ
Sets HIGH CUT to 2.5, 5, 10 or 13 kHz.
RT LOW FREQ RANGE
Sets RT LOW to x0.5, x1.0, x1.5 or x2.0.
RT HIGH FREQ RANGE
Sets RT HIGH to x0.25, x0.35, x0.5 or x1.0.

EARLY REFLECTIONS

A dot-matrix picture of the early reflections across 0 - 240 ms: each column of lit dots is a reflection, taller for louder. A faint dotted column marks where the reverb tail starts (PRE DELAY). The grid is empty when E.RFL LEVEL is OFF.

Reverb parameters

REVERB TIME
How long the tail takes to die away at mid frequencies, 0.1 to 20 seconds (2.8 s by default).
PRE DELAY
The delay before the reverb tail starts, 0 to 250 ms (24 ms by default).
REV LEVEL
The level of the reverb tail, -30 to +6 dB; fully down reads OFF.
E.RFL DELAY
The delay before the early reflections start, 0 to 120 ms (8 ms by default).
E.RFL LEVEL
The level of the early reflections, -30 to +6 dB (-6 dB by default); fully down reads OFF.
ROOM SIZE
The size of the space, 10 to 12000 m3 (3000 by default). It scales the reflection pattern and the lengths of the reverb network.
DENSITY
How many early reflections there are (and on FLAGSHIP 40, how widely they spread), 0 to 100%.
DIFFUSION
How quickly the tail smears into a smooth wash, 0 to 100%. Low settings keep more distinct echoes in the onset.
RT HIGH
The decay time above about 5 kHz (3 kHz on 12-BIT) as a multiple of REVERB TIME, x0.1 to x1.5. Below 1 the top end dies away sooner, as in a real room.
RT LOW
The decay time below about 250 Hz as a multiple of REVERB TIME, x0.5 to x2.5. Above 1 the low end rings on longer.
LOW CUT
A high-pass filter on the reverb, 20 to 1000 Hz (80 Hz by default).
HIGH CUT
A low-pass filter on the reverb, 1 to 20 kHz (12 kHz by default).
DRIVE
A clean preamp drive on the reverb input, 0 to 100%: soft saturation before the reverb.
WIDTH
The stereo width of the reverb, 0 to 150%. 0 is mono; above 100 is wider than normal.
DRIFT
How much the reverb wanders over time, 0 to 100% (20% by default). See Drift below.
MIX
The dry / reverb balance, 0 to 100% (30% by default).

Drift

A slow random wander that keeps the tail alive. On HALL it moves the read taps of the network by up to two samples - more density, never a pitch wobble. On the other models it lets the air absorption (the high-frequency damping in the tail) wander. On 12-BIT the old clock also runs very slightly unsteady. The wander speeds up as you turn DRIFT up.

IN / OUT

TRIM
Input level before anything else, -24 to +12 dB. Click the display to select it, then drag, use the mouse wheel or DOWN / UP.
GUARD
Off by default. When on, it gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources.
LEVEL
The output level, -24 to +12 dB.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out, 0 to 100%: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper at -0.3 dBFS (no latency), or a lookahead limiter that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). The caption reads OUT STAGE - CLIPPING while the clipper is working.

Tips

  • Use E.RFL LEVEL and REV LEVEL separately: reflections alone place a sound in a room without washing it out; the tail alone gives space without clutter.
  • A PRE DELAY of 20 - 60 ms keeps a vocal clear of its own reverb.
  • Lower RT HIGH (the x0.35 or x0.5 lamp) for a more natural, darker room; raise RT LOW for warmth.
  • On a send, set MIX to 100% and use LEVEL to set the return level.

File: gfx-j-verb.jsfx

Modulation & space

Mid/Side

A mid/side processor for width, mono bass, air on the sides and focus in the centre.

Overview

Mid/Side splits a stereo signal into its mid (what both sides share - the centre of the image) and its side (the difference - the width). You can then set their levels separately, widen or narrow the image, keep the bass in mono, add air to the sides and presence to the centre, before it is turned back into left and right.

WIDTH keeps the loudness steady as you widen or narrow, so you hear the change in the image rather than a change in level. A vectorscope, a correlation meter and mid / side energy bars show what is happening.

It can also take in or send out mid/side signals directly, as an M/S encoder or decoder.

Reach for it for

  • Widening or narrowing a mix or a stereo bus
  • Keeping the low end in mono for a solid, cut-friendly bass
  • Adding air to the edges without making the centre harsh
  • Bringing a vocal forward in the centre (FOCUS)
  • Checking a mix in mid or side solo
  • Encoding or decoding M/S recordings

Controls

Display

Vectorscope
The output as a cloud of dots: a vertical line is mono, a wide cloud is wide stereo, and a horizontal spread means the sides are out of phase.
CORRELATION
From -1 to +1. Near +1 the sides are mostly the same (mono-safe); around 0 is very wide; below 0 (the bar turns red) the sides are out of phase and may cancel in mono.
MID / SIDE
The energy of the mid and side signals.
INPUT
A VU meter showing the input level (0 VU = -18 dBFS).

Header

STEREO / MID / SIDE
What you hear. STEREO is the normal output; MID plays the mid signal alone and SIDE plays the side signal alone (in both ears). MID and SIDE are for listening only - switch back to STEREO before you bounce.

M/S

IN L/R / IN M/S
The input format. L/R: a normal stereo input. M/S: the input is already mid (left channel) and side (right channel).
OUT L/R / OUT M/S
The output format. L/R: a normal stereo output. M/S: sends out mid on the left and side on the right, for encoding.
MID
The level of the mid signal, -12 to +12 dB.
SIDE
The level of the side signal, -12 to +12 dB.
WIDTH
0 to 200% (100% leaves the image as it is). 0% is mono; above 100% is wider. The overall loudness is kept steady as you turn it.
BASS MONO
Removes the side signal below this frequency (24 dB per octave), up to 300 Hz, so the low end sits in mono. Below 20 Hz it reads OFF.
AIR
A high shelf at 8 kHz on the side signal only, 0 to +9 dB: an open, airy top to the edges without brightening the centre.
FOCUS
A presence lift at 2.5 kHz on the mid only, 0 to +6 dB: brings vocals and other centre sounds forward.

DRIFT

DRIFT
See Drift below.

IN / OUT

TRIM
Input level before anything else, from -24 to +12 dB. A single click on the knob switches GUARD on or off (on by default); its light at the knob's upper right is lit while GUARD is on and glows brighter while it is catching peaks. GUARD gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper (no latency, the default), or a lookahead limiter (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). The CLIP key flashes red while the output is being clipped.

MASTER

BALANCE
Left / right balance, -100 to +100%.
OUTPUT
The output level, -24 to +12 dB.
OUT meters
Output level, left and right. Click them to reset the peak hold.

The header

Theme picker
Changes the panel's colour theme.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
HW
Switches between the hardware-style panel and the flat look.
PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets (or click the preset display). The ◄ / ► keys step through them. The last entry resets everything to the defaults.
