Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Drift

DRIFT (0 - 100%, 50% by default) brings slow, random movement: the width breathes, the AIR shelf wanders and the left / right balance leans a little. 50% is the standard amount, 100% twice that, 0 none; the wander also speeds up as you turn it up. Some presets set their own amounts for the three movements (the Mono Bass, Narrow & Tight and M/S presets switch them off).

Presets

Subtle Widen
120% width with a touch of air.
Wide Master
140% width, a little more side level and air, bass in mono below 120 Hz.
Mono Bass
No width change: just the bass in mono below 150 Hz, no drift.
Vocal Focus
Slightly narrower (90%), the mid up 1 dB and 3 dB of FOCUS, mono bass below 100 Hz.
Super Wide
175% width, more side level, 4 dB of air, mono below 200 Hz, more drift.
Narrow & Tight
70% width and the sides down 2 dB, mono below 150 Hz, no drift.
M/S Decode
Takes a mid/side input and turns it into left and right.
M/S Encode
Takes a left/right input and sends out mid (left) and side (right).
Reset all to defaults
Every control back to its starting value.

Tips

  • Use SIDE listen to hear what WIDTH and AIR are really adding; if it sounds harsh or hollow, back off.
  • Keep an eye on CORRELATION while widening: if it heads below 0, the mix will lose energy in mono.
  • BASS MONO at 100-150 Hz tightens the low end of almost any mix without changing the image above it.
  • FOCUS is subtler than an EQ on the whole mix: it lifts the centre only, so the sides stay as they are.

File: gfx-midside.jsfx

Modulation & space

Mod Rack

A 2U rack of four parallel effects - chorus, tremolo, vibe and delay - each with a circuit-bending MOD pot.

Overview

Mod Rack puts four classic modulation effects side by side in one rack unit: a bucket-brigade CHORUS, an optical TREMOLO, a photocell VIBE and a BBD / tape DELAY. They all work in parallel on the input, and each one adds only its own change to the sound: the dry signal always passes through, so turning a module up never takes anything away from the original.

Every module has a red MOD pot that bends its circuit in an unusual direction: the chorus's clock stretches into a warbly, seasick, lo-fi sound; the tremolo's smooth pulse chops into a square on / off; the vibe's lamp becomes lopsided and thumps; and the delay's clock runs away into self-oscillation with wow and flutter. Three L / C / R keys per module decide where its effect goes in the stereo field. Rates and times can follow the project tempo, and a gentle DRIFT keeps the LFOs from sounding mechanical.

Reach for it for

  • Lush stereo chorus on guitars, keys and synths
  • Surf and vintage tremolo, from smooth to choppy
  • Throbbing, swirling vibe on guitar and electric piano
  • Dub echoes and runaway, self-oscillating delay
  • Stacking several modulation effects on one source in one plugin

Controls

Left: presets and size

LCD
Shows the current preset, or the name and value of the control you are touching. Click it to open the preset list.
PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets. Choosing one sets all four modules, the master DRIFT and OUTPUT and the master MIX, and switches tempo sync off. The first entry, INIT, is the starting point with every module silent.
◄ / ►
Step to the previous or next preset.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large (new instances open in Large). The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Next to the S / M / L size keys. Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

The four modules

From left to right: TREMOLO, CHORUS, VIBE and DELAY. Knobs: drag up and down (Shift for fine moves); double-click or Ctrl-click to reset.

TREMOLO (optical)

RATE
The tremolo speed, 0.1 to 15 Hz (4 Hz by default), or a note value when synced.
DEPTH
How deep the volume pulses, 0 to 100%. At 0 the module is silent.
SQUARE CHOP (MOD)
Morphs the smooth sine pulse into a hard square on / off chop, and makes the optical response faster to match.

CHORUS (BBD)

RATE
The chorus LFO speed, 0.05 to 8 Hz (0.8 Hz by default), or a note value when synced.
DEPTH
How far the chorus sweeps, 0 to 100% (50% by default).
MIX
How much chorus is added, 0 to 100%. At 0 the module is silent.
CLOCK STRETCH (MOD)
Stretches the bucket-brigade line: longer delays, a lower clock (with its grainy aliasing and darker top), plus a random warble and a slow seasick wander. Turn it up for lo-fi, warped tape-like chorus.

VIBE (photocell)

RATE
The vibe speed, 0.1 to 12 Hz (1.5 Hz by default), or a note value when synced.
DEPTH
How deep the four-stage phase sweep goes, 0 to 100%. At 0 the module is silent.
ASYM LAMP (MOD)
Makes the lamp's sweep lopsided, slows its cooling, and adds a low-end thump in time with the pulse - the throbbing character of the classic vibe pedals.

DELAY (BBD / tape)

TIME
The delay time, 20 to 1500 ms (350 ms by default), or a note value when synced. Moving it glides the time, bending the pitch of the echoes like tape.
FEEDBACK
How many echoes, 0 to 95% (30% by default). The repeats get warmer and darker each time round, with a little saturation.
MIX
How much delay is added, 0 to 100%. At 0 the module is silent.
RUNAWAY CLOCK (MOD)
Pushes the feedback past the point of self-oscillation (the saturation keeps it in check) and adds wow and flutter to the time. For dub-style runaway echoes.

SYNC (RATE / TIME)

SYNC key or the RATE / TIME label
Click to step through: free (Hz or ms), SYNC (straight note values), DOTTED and TRIPLET. The label shows the mode and the knob then picks a note value from 4 BARS to 1/32. While REAPER plays, synced LFOs lock to the beat grid (drift does not move them). Synced delay times are limited to 1.5 s.

L / C / R

Three keys under each module choose where its effect goes. Click a key to switch it in or out.

L
Works on the left channel, out on the left.
R
Works on the right channel, out on the right.
C
Works on the mono sum, out in the centre (both sides).
L + R
A stereo effect (the chorus runs its two sides 90 degrees apart).
C with L and / or R
Spread: the voices run at different points in their cycle, or, for the delay, the taps sit at 3/4, 1 and 5/4 of the time.
All off
The module is muted (the LCD shows MUTED).

MASTER

DRIFT
A slow random wander of each module's LFO rate, independently, up to plus or minus 20% at 100% (20% by default). Synced LFOs are not affected while REAPER plays.
MIX
The master amount of all four modules together, 0 to 100% (100% by default). The dry signal is always at full level.
OUTPUT
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.
OUT meter
Output level.
LIMIT
Lights when the safety limiter is working. A limiter at the end holds the output just under 0 dBFS (about -0.3 dBFS).

Presets

Init
Every module silent: the starting point.
Lush Chorus
Slow, deep stereo chorus at 55%.
Seasick Tape
Slow, deep chorus with a lot of clock stretch on L, C and R, plus a 380 ms stereo delay with some runaway clock; more drift.
Surf Trem
Deep 6 Hz stereo tremolo with a little chop, and a short 120 ms slapback delay in the centre.
Vibe Throb
Deep vibe in the centre with a strongly lopsided lamp.
Dub Echo
450 ms delay on L, C and R with long feedback and some runaway clock.
Rotary Wash
Chorus, tremolo and a fast vibe together, for a swirling rotary-like wash.
Runaway
A 300 ms centre delay right on the edge of self-oscillation; output down 3 dB.

Tips

  • Each module only adds its effect, so you can stack them freely: try a little chorus under the tremolo, or vibe into a short delay.
  • Use the L / C / R keys to place effects: a tremolo on L and R only, with the chorus in C, makes a wide, moving image.
  • RUNAWAY CLOCK with high FEEDBACK can build up - ride MIX or FEEDBACK with automation for dub throws.
  • Lock the tremolo or delay to the tempo with SYNC so the pulse and echoes sit in the groove.

File: gfx-modrack.jsfx

Modulation & space

Oontecho

A rhythmic trance-gate multi-effect: a tempo-synced step gate that also moves the pan and a resonant filter, followed by a stereo delay and a reverb wash.

Overview

Oontecho is in the spirit of the classic rhythmic trance-gate multi-effects of the 2000s. At its heart is a step sequencer of up to 32 steps that chops the volume in time with the project. Each step has a level, drawn as a bar, and an accent. The same pattern can also throw the sound from side to side and play the cutoff of a resonant filter, so one pattern can gate, pan and sweep at once.

The signal runs GATE, then FILTER, then DELAY, then SPACE, then the master MIX and the output stage. The delay is a tempo-synced stereo echo with separate left and right times and a ping-pong mode; SPACE is a smooth eight-line reverb wash. Both add their sound on top of the gated signal, so a chopped pad can trail echoes and a tail into the gaps.

The gate follows the project. While REAPER plays it locks to the timeline, so it is in time from the very first beat; while the transport is stopped it keeps running freely at the project tempo, so you can audition sounds with it.

Reach for it for

  • Classic trance-gated pads, strings and vocals
  • Sidechain-style pumping without a sidechain
  • Sequenced resonant filter lines from a sustained chord
  • Stereo chops that bounce from side to side
  • Dub-style gated throws into a dark ping-pong echo
  • Slow, gliding swells into a long reverb wash

Controls

The header

PRESETS, ◄ ► and the display
PRESETS (or a click on the display) opens the list of factory presets; ◄ and ► step through them, and the display shows the one in use. A new instance opens on Init.
Tempo display
The project tempo in BPM. The light and the word SYNC show while REAPER plays and the gate is locked to the timeline; FREE shows while it is stopped and the gate runs on at the project tempo.
DRIFT
0 to 100% (25% by default). See Drift below.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window. A new instance opens in Large.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

The step grid

The pattern itself, across the top of the panel. Each column is one step; the bar is its level and the cell below is its accent. The beats are shaded in alternate tones, and the step that is playing is outlined in cyan.

STEPS 8 / 12 / 16 / 24 / 32
The length of the pattern (16 by default).
DIV
The length of each step: 1/4, 1/8, 1/8 T, 1/16, 1/16 T or 1/32 (1/16 by default). 16 steps of 1/16 is one bar.
< / >
Rotate the pattern one step left or right, within the current length. Use them to move the groove against the beat.
RND
Makes a random pattern on all 32 steps: every fourth step is full, the others are a mix of rests and half, three-quarter and full hits, with a few accents.
FILL
Sets all 32 steps to full and clears the accents: a blank canvas.
Level bars
Click and drag across the bars to draw the levels, 0 to 100%; a quick sweep paints a smooth line across the steps it passes. Right-drag erases (sets steps to 0). Hover over a step to see its level. Changes are heard at once, even on the step that is playing.
Accent row
Click a cell to switch that step's accent on or off; drag along the row to paint the same state across several steps. Accented steps are drawn in amber.

GATE

The PATTERN MODULATION knobs: what the pattern does to the sound.

DEPTH
How far the pattern moves the volume, 0 to 100% (100% by default). At 100% a silent step is silent; at 0% the volume is left alone and the pattern only drives PAN and FILTER.
GLIDE
How smoothly one step moves to the next, 0 to 100% (15% by default). At 0% the edges are fast but click-free, for a hard chop; higher settings soften them, up to a slow swell over half a step at 100% - the classic pumping feel.
ACCENT
How much the accented steps stand out, shown as 0 to +6 dB (+3 dB at the default 50%). Accented steps are louder by that much and also open the filter by up to 1.5 octaves.
SWING
Delays every second step, 0 to 50% (0% by default). At 50% the off-step lands half a step late.
PAN
-100 to +100% (0% by default). The pattern moves the sound across the stereo field: odd steps to one side, even steps to the other, each as far as its level. With + the first step goes left; - flips the sides.
FILTER
-5 to +5 octaves (0 by default). The pattern plays the cutoff of the FILTER section: a full step moves it this far above (+) or below (-) CUTOFF, a silent step leaves it at CUTOFF. Only heard when a filter type is chosen.

FILTER

A smooth, resonant state-variable filter after the gate.

OFF / LP / BP / HP
The filter type: off, low-pass, band-pass or high-pass (OFF by default). Changing type crossfades, so it never clicks. The CUTOFF cap turns yellow for HP.
CUTOFF
The filter's base frequency, 20 Hz - 20 kHz (2 kHz by default). The pattern, the accents, the LFO and DRIFT all move it from here.
RESO
Resonance, 0 to 100% (20% by default). Higher settings give a sharper, more vocal peak; the level is trimmed a little as it rises.
LFO
The depth of a built-in sine LFO on the cutoff, -4 to +4 octaves (0 by default). Negative values start the sweep the other way.
RATE
The LFO's cycle, from 4 bars down to 1/32, including dotted (D) and triplet (T) values (1 bar by default). It locks to the timeline while REAPER plays.

DELAY

A tempo-synced stereo echo, after the filter. The display shows the time and the left and right multipliers.

PING
Ping-pong: the echoes start on the left and bounce from side to side. Off by default.
TIME
The echo time as a note value, from 4 bars down to 1/32, including dotted (D) and triplet (T) values (1/8 D by default). Echoes are limited to 4 seconds.
L / R
Multiply the time on each side: x0.5, x0.75, x1, x1.5 or x2 (x1 by default). The knob shows the resulting time in ms. Different values on each side give a wide, rhythmic spread.
FDBK
Feedback, 0 to 95% (35% by default): how many repeats you hear. The loop is soft-limited, so it stays musical when pushed.
DAMP
A high-cut in the feedback loop, 1 - 20 kHz (6 kHz by default): lower settings make each repeat darker than the last. A fixed low cut in the loop keeps the repeats from building up mud.
MIX
The level of the echoes on top of the gated sound, 0 to 100% (0% by default, so the delay is silent until you raise it).

SPACE

A smooth reverb wash after the delay, so the echoes are reverberated too. The display shows the decay time in seconds.

SIZE
The size of the space, 0 to 100% (50% by default).
DECAY
How long the tail lasts, 0.4 to 10 seconds (0 to 100%; 50%, about 2.8 seconds, by default). Longer settings are also a little darker.
MIX
The level of the reverb on top, 0 to 100% (0% by default).

MASTER

TRIM
Input level before anything else, -24 to +12 dB.
GUARD
Click the TRIM knob (without turning it) to switch GUARD on or off; the small light at the knob's upper right shows it is on and glows brighter while it works. Off by default. Gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS. It never boosts quiet sources.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out, 0 to 100% (0% by default): only the lows and highs are saturated, the mids stay clean.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper at -0.3 dBFS (no latency), or a lookahead limiter (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). CLIP is on by default and flashes red while it clips.
MIX
Dry / wet balance of the whole effect, 0 to 100% (100% by default). Lower it to keep some of the ungated sound underneath.
OUTPUT
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.
OUT meter
Left and right output levels.

Drift

DRIFT is never rhythmic: the timing always stays locked to the grid. Instead the filter cutoff wanders slowly (up to about a sixth of an octave either way at the default 25%, just over half an octave at 100%), and the delay's colour breathes with it - its damping and a little of its feedback. At 0% everything stays exactly where you set it.

Knobs: drag up or down (Shift for fine moves); double-click to reset a knob to its default. The mouse wheel works too, and the value shows while you hover.

Presets

Presets set the pattern, STEPS, DIV and every GATE, FILTER, DELAY and SPACE control, and the master MIX. OUTPUT, DRIFT and the TRIM / GUARD, SAT and output stage are left as they are.

Init
16 full steps of 1/16 with no accents, filter, delay or space: a blank canvas to draw on.
Classic 16ths
The 3-3-2 trance chop in 1/16ths with accents, dotted-eighth echoes and a little space.
Euphoric Gate
Accented 1/16ths through a resonant low-pass at 900 Hz that the pattern opens, with echoes and a wider space.
Sidechain Pump
Ducked on every beat and swelling back up, with plenty of glide: sidechain pumping without a sidechain.
Half-Time Chop
Long 1/8 hits across two bars, a gentle low-pass and a quarter-note echo behind them.
Filter Sequence
The volume stays put; the pattern plays the cutoff of a very resonant low-pass from 160 Hz, up to 4.5 octaves.
Dub Delay Gate
Sparse 1/8 hits through a high-pass, feeding a dark dotted-quarter ping-pong echo with long feedback.
Ambient Wash
A slow, gliding 8-step quarter-note gate with a two-bar LFO on a low-pass, a long echo and a large, dark space.
Triplet Trance
One bar of 1/16 triplets (24 steps), rolling across the stereo field, with triplet echoes.
Ping-Pong Pan
1/16ths that fly hard left and right, with ping-pong echoes bouncing behind them.
32nd Stutter
A bar of 1/32 chops that roll into the next downbeat, with a short 1/16 echo.
LFO Sweep Gate
A swung 1/16 gate through a resonant band-pass swept three octaves by the LFO over two bars.

Tips

  • Put it on a sustained source - a pad, a held chord or a long vocal note. The gate needs something to chop.
  • The FILTER knob in GATE, ACCENT's filter lift and the LFO do nothing while the filter type is OFF: choose LP, BP or HP first.
  • For a pure filter sequence, turn DEPTH to 0 and FILTER up: the volume stays even while the pattern plays the cutoff.
  • Raise GLIDE for a pumping, sidechain-style feel; keep it low for a hard, choppy gate.
  • Delay and space MIX are added on top, so the gaps in the pattern fill with echoes and tail. Raise them slowly - a little goes a long way.
  • Use the < and > keys to try the same pattern starting on a different step; it can change the groove completely.

File: gfx-oontecho.jsfx

Modulation & space

Phaser

A stereo phase shifter with three voicings: the orange stompbox, the slow opto and real tape phasing.

Overview

Phaser runs your signal through a chain of all-pass stages and mixes it back with the dry sound. Where the two cancel you get notches, and as the LFO moves the stages those notches sweep up and down the spectrum - the classic swirling, liquid phase sound.

Three voicings are built in. 90 is the little orange stompbox: a focused, vocal sweep with its peaks gently rounded off. OPTO follows the old lamp-and-photocell units: the sweep rises quickly and falls back slowly, so it sounds rounder and deeper over a wider range. TAPE is a different machine altogether - phasing done with a short delay, as with two tape machines, including a hand-made motion that wanders like a thumb on the reel.

The rate can run free, lock to the project tempo, or follow the tempo while flowing through loops. A DRIFT dial adds slow, random wander to the rate, depth and centre so the effect never sounds mechanical.

Reach for it for

  • Swirling rhythm guitar and electric piano
  • Slow, deep sweeps on pads and synths
  • Tape-style phasing on drums, a bus or a whole mix
  • Tempo-locked throb with the square LFO
  • Wide stereo movement with SPREAD

Controls

VOICING (header)

90
The orange stompbox. The sweep covers about 120 Hz to 2.4 kHz, and peaks above about -3 dBFS are gently rounded off on the way out. With this voicing a SCRIPT / BLOCK key appears beside the rate keys (see below).
OPTO
The lamp-and-photocell phaser. The sweep rises fast and falls back slowly, covers a wider range (about 80 Hz to 2 kHz) and sounds smoother and deeper.
TAPE
Phasing with a swept short delay (about 0.6 to 8 ms at its centre) against a slightly delayed dry signal, as with two tape machines. The swept copy can pass right through the dry one for the deep cancellation of real tape phasing. In this voicing the STAGES knob becomes MOTION.

Display

The window on the left shows the live frequency response - the notches moving as the effect sweeps. The top line shows the number of stages and the current sweep frequency, or in TAPE the current delay time in ms.

LFO SHAPE and RATE MODE

SINE / TRI / SQR / RND
The LFO shape. Triangle is the even, classic sweep; sine lingers at the ends; square jumps between two settings for a throbbing effect; random wanders from point to point.
FREE / SYNC / FLOW
How the rate is set: FREE in Hz, SYNC locked to the project tempo and timeline, FLOW tempo-synced but running continuously through loops.
SCRIPT / BLOCK (90 only)
A quick feedback switch for the 90 voicing. SCRIPT is the original with no feedback; BLOCK adds 40% feedback for a stronger, more pronounced sweep. If FEEDBACK has been set to any other value the key reads CUSTOM; clicking it then returns to SCRIPT.
STAGES
The number of all-pass stages: 2, 4, 6, 8 or 12. More stages give more notches and a thicker, more complex sweep; 4 is the classic pedal. Click a number to pick it. Not shown in TAPE.
MOTION (TAPE only)
What moves the tape: LFO (the rate and shape above), HAND (a slow, random wander like a hand on the reel) or BOTH (the two blended).

Phaser

RATE
The LFO speed, 0.02 to 10 Hz, or a note value from 4 bars to 1/32 (with dotted and triplet values) when SYNC or FLOW is on. The light beside it blinks with the LFO. In TAPE with MOTION set to HAND the knob becomes HAND SPEED: how fast the hand-made wander moves (0.02 to 10 Hz).
CENTRE
Where the sweep sits, 0 to 100%. Low settings put the notches low in the spectrum; high settings move them up. In TAPE it sets the centre delay time.
DEPTH
How far the sweep travels either side of CENTRE, 0 to 100%. Zero gives a static notch filter.
FEEDBACK
Feeds the output back into the stages, from -95% to +95%. Positive sharpens the notches into a ringing, vocal tone; negative gives a hollower colour. The level is compensated so strong settings don't jump out.
SPREAD
Offsets the right side's sweep from the left, from 0 to 180 degrees. 0 is a classic mono phaser; 90 - 180 sweeps the two sides against each other for wide stereo motion. In TAPE with HAND motion it sets how independently the two sides wander.

Drift

A slow random wander that keeps the sweep from sounding mechanical.

DRIFT
0 to 100%. 50% is the standard amount; 100% is twice that, 0 turns it off. It moves the LFO rate a little, and the depth and centre of the sweep a little more. Higher settings also make the wander move faster.

IN / OUT

TRIM
Input level before anything else, from -24 to +12 dB.
GUARD
Click the TRIM knob (without turning it) to switch GUARD on or off; the small light at the knob's upper right shows it is on and glows brighter while it works. On by default. Gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper that holds the peaks just under 0 dBFS (no latency), or a lookahead limiter (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). The CLIP key flashes red while the clipper is working.

Master

MIX
Dry / effect balance, 0 to 100%. 50% gives the deepest notches; lower is subtler; at 100% you hear only the shifted signal, which gives a gentle pitch wobble (vibrato) rather than phasing.
LOW CUT
A high-pass filter on the effected signal only, from off (fully left) up to 500 Hz. The bass stays dry, clean and centred while the mids and highs swirl.
OUTPUT
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.
OUT meters
Output level, left and right.

The header

PRESETS, ◄ ► and the display
PRESETS (or a click on the display) opens the list of factory presets; ◄ and ► step through them, and the display shows the number and name of the preset in use. Choosing one sets the plugin's controls for that job; the input and output stages are left as they are.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Presets

90 Script
The classic orange pedal: 4 stages, triangle sweep at 0.6 Hz, no feedback.
90 Block
The same pedal with 40% feedback for a stronger, more vocal sweep.
90 Slow Swell
The 90 voicing with a slow (0.15 Hz), deep sweep.
Opto Lush
OPTO, 8 stages, slow sine sweep with some feedback and the right side 90 degrees apart.
Opto Square Throb
OPTO, 6 stages, square LFO locked to 1/8 notes for a rhythmic throb.
Opto Stereo Swirl
OPTO, 12 stages, strong feedback and the two sides 180 degrees apart.
Opto Vibrato
OPTO at 4.5 Hz with MIX at 100%: a pitch vibrato instead of phasing.
Hand Tape Phasing
TAPE moved by hand: a slow, uneven, wandering sweep.
Tape Jet
TAPE with 60% feedback and both LFO and hand motion, for the jet-plane sound.
Through-Zero Sweep
TAPE with a very slow, full-depth sweep that passes right through the dry signal; drift off.
Reset all to defaults
Every control back to its starting value, including the input and output stages.

Tips

  • For the classic tape-phasing effect on a mix bus, use TAPE with FEEDBACK at 0, a slow sine sweep and MIX at 50%.
  • On bass or a full mix, raise LOW CUT to 100 - 200 Hz so the low end stays solid while the top swirls.
  • Try the square LFO with SYNC on for a rhythmic, tremolo-like throb that sits in time with the track.
  • If the effect sounds too thin or nasal, lower FEEDBACK or move CENTRE down; for more drama, add stages.

File: gfx-phaser.jsfx

Modulation & space

Polyvibe

A lamp-and-photocell vibe: four unequal phase stages swept by a modelled light bulb, with a preamp for colour.

Overview

Polyvibe is a vibe effect in the spirit of the classic lamp-and-photocell units. Four unequal phase-shift stages are swept by a light bulb shining on light-dependent resistors. The bulb heats up quickly and cools slowly, and the photocells lag behind it, so the sweep is lopsided - the familiar throbbing, liquid swirl rather than an even phaser.

In CHORUS mode the shifted signal is mixed with the dry one for the classic swirl; in VIBRATO mode you hear only the shifted signal, which gives a pitch wobble. A transistor-style preamp runs in every mode, adding asymmetric saturation, a low-mid lift and a rounded top - and PREAMP mode switches the vibe off so you can use the preamp on its own.

Reach for it for

  • The classic swirling vibe on clean or driven guitar
  • Organ and electric piano that needs slow, throbbing movement
  • Pitch-wobble vibrato on vocals, guitars and synths
  • Wide stereo movement with SPREAD
  • Warm preamp colour on its own (PREAMP mode)

Controls

Modes (header)

CHORUS
The dry signal plus the phase-shifted one, half and half: the classic vibe swirl, with moving notches in the response.
VIBRATO
The phase-shifted signal only. The response stays flat and you hear a pitch wobble.
PREAMP
The vibe is off; only the preamp colours the sound. The lamp shows LAMP OFF.

The display

Lamp
The glowing orb shows the bulb's brightness as it pulses, with L and R bars beside it for each channel (they differ when SPREAD is up).
Response
In CHORUS mode, the live frequency response with the current notch frequency shown above it. In VIBRATO mode the response is flat.

Rate mode

FREE
SPEED is set in Hz.
SYNC
SPEED is a note value locked to the project tempo and timeline, so the sweep lines up with the bars and follows loop jumps.
FLOW
Tempo-synced, but the sweep runs continuously through loops and restarts.

Preamp

AMOUNT
The preamp colour, 0 - 100% (starts at 40%). It adds asymmetric saturation, lifts the low mids and rounds off the top. The word beside the knob shows the character: clean, warm, pushed or driven. At 0% the preamp is clean. Works in every mode.

Vibe

SPEED
How fast the lamp pulses: 0.1 - 12 Hz in FREE, or a note value from 4 BARS to 1/32 (with dotted and triplet values) in SYNC and FLOW.
INTENSITY
How far the sweep travels, 0 - 100%. Low settings are subtle; high settings are deep and swirling.
THROB
How much of the bulb's lopsided character you hear, 0 - 100%. At 0% the sweep follows a smooth sine; turned up, the fast-heat, slow-cool lamp and lagging photocells give the uneven, pulsing throb.
SPREAD
Offsets the right channel's sweep from the left, 0 - 180 degrees. 0 is mono movement; higher settings make the vibe move across the stereo field.

Drift

A slow random wander, like an old lamp and tired components. DRIFT (0 - 100%, starting at 50%) sets how much: 50% is the standard amount, 100% twice that, 0% none. In Polyvibe it moves three things: the speed (in FREE the rate wanders; in SYNC and FLOW the sweep wobbles but stays locked on average), the intensity (the sweep gets a little shallower at times) and the lamp brightness (the bulb dims a little). The wander also speeds up a little as DRIFT goes up.

IN / OUT

TRIM
Input level before anything else, from -24 to +12 dB.
GUARD
Click the TRIM knob (without moving it) to switch the guard; its light sits at the knob's upper right. On by default. Gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources. The light glows brighter while it is catching peaks.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT (OUT)
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper (no latency), or a lookahead limiter that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). CLIP is on by default; its key flashes red while it is clipping.

Master

MIX
Blends the untreated input back in, 0 - 100%. At 100% you hear only the effect (which, in CHORUS mode, already contains the dry signal).
OUTPUT
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.
Meters
Output level, left and right.

The header

PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets; the ◄ / ► keys step through them and the display shows the one in use. Choosing one sets the mode, rate, vibe, preamp, MIX and drift settings; the DRIFT dial, OUTPUT and the IN / OUT controls are left as they are. The last entry resets everything to the defaults.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Presets

Classic Chorus
The everyday vibe swirl: CHORUS at 2 Hz, 70% intensity and throb.
Slow Throb
A slow, deep CHORUS (0.6 Hz) with strong throb and a little more lamp drift.
Fast Swirl
A quick CHORUS at 6.5 Hz, a little less deep.
Vibrato
VIBRATO mode at 4.5 Hz for a pitch wobble, with a lighter preamp.
Stereo Vibe
CHORUS at 1.5 Hz with the right side offset by 90 degrees.
Synced 1/8
CHORUS locked to eighth notes of the project tempo.
Preamp Only (Clean)
The vibe off, with a light preamp colour.
Preamp Only (Pushed)
The vibe off, with the preamp at 80%.
Ageing Lamp
A slow, throbbing CHORUS with lots of drift on the speed, depth and lamp - an old, unsteady unit.
Reset all to defaults
Every control back to its starting value.

Tips

  • For the classic guitar vibe, start with Classic Chorus and set SPEED by ear to the tempo of the song; turn THROB up for more of the pulsing character.
  • Put Polyvibe before a drive or amp for a thick, chewy swirl; after it for a cleaner, more open sound.
  • VIBRATO with a low INTENSITY gives a gentle, tape-like wobble on vocals and keys.
  • On a mono source, a SPREAD of 90 degrees or more makes the movement swirl across the speakers.

File: gfx-polyvibe.jsfx

Modulation & space

Rotary

A two-rotor rotary speaker cabinet: valve drive, treble horn and bass drum, two mics.

Overview

Rotary is a rotary speaker cabinet in the spirit of the classic organ cabinets. The signal first goes through a valve amp (DRIVE), for the growl when you dig in. A crossover at 800 Hz then splits it between a treble HORN and a bass DRUM, which spin separately, each with its own speeds and its own weight: the horn gets up to speed in well under a second, while the heavy drum takes seconds. That difference is what makes the speed change sound so alive.

As each rotor spins you hear the pitch move (Doppler), the level swell, and - for the horn - the tone brighten and darken as it points towards and away from the mics, plus its reflection off the cabinet. Two mics pick it up: DISTANCE trades swirl for cabinet sound, and SPREAD goes from mono to wide stereo. The speed can be switched from the panel, from automation, or from MIDI on the track (mod wheel or sustain pedal), just like the half-moon switch and foot switch of a real organ.

The cabinet takes a mono signal: the two inputs are added together before the amp.

Reach for it for

  • Organ, of course - drawbar and combo organs come alive
  • Electric piano and clav for a swirling, vintage sound
  • Guitar through a rotary cabinet
  • Vocals or pads for a woozy, psychedelic effect
  • Speed-change swells (CHORALE to TREMOLO) as a performance effect

Controls

The window

A view from above onto the HORN and DRUM as they spin, with each rotor's current speed in Hz. The two amber dots show where the mics sit (set by SPREAD). The line underneath says what the cabinet is doing: CHORALE (slow), TREMOLO (fast), SPEEDING UP, SLOWING DOWN, or BRAKE when stopped.

ROTOR SPEED

SLOW / FAST (half-moon switch)
Click the left half for SLOW (chorale) or the right half for FAST (tremolo). The rotors speed up or slow down to the new setting at their own pace.
STOP
The brake: both rotors coast to a stop. Click it again to start them at the last speed you used.

The rotors

HORN SLOW
The horn's slow speed, 0.3 to 1.5 Hz (0.83 Hz by default).
HORN FAST
The horn's fast speed, 4 to 8 Hz (6.75 Hz by default).
HORN ACCEL
How long the horn takes to change speed, 0.2 to 3 s (0.6 s by default). Slowing down takes a little longer than speeding up.
DRUM SLOW
The drum's slow speed, 0.2 to 1.5 Hz (0.67 Hz by default).
DRUM FAST
The drum's fast speed, 3.5 to 7.5 Hz (5.8 Hz by default).
DRUM ACCEL
How long the heavy drum takes to change speed, 1 to 10 s (4.5 s by default).

MIDI SPEED

Rotary switches speed from MIDI on its own track, and passes all MIDI on. If the instrument in front of it doesn't pass MIDI through, set that instrument's MIDI output to 'merge with input'. MIDI does nothing while STOP is on.

MOD WHL
On by default. Mod wheel up (past halfway) = FAST, down = SLOW.
PEDAL
On by default. Each press of the sustain pedal toggles between SLOW and FAST.

CABINET

DRIVE
The valve amp in front of the cabinet, 0 to 100% (20% by default). Adds warmth, then growl and grit as you turn it up, with a slightly darker tone. The level stays roughly steady.
BALANCE
Horn against drum, -6 to +6 dB. Positive gives more horn (brighter, more swirl on top); negative gives more drum.
DISTANCE
How far the mics are from the cabinet, 0 to 100% (50% by default). Close mics hear more of the swell and swirl; far mics hear more of the cabinet and the horn's reflection.
SPREAD
The angle between the two mics, 0 to 100% (70% by default). 0 is mono; higher settings give a wider stereo image, up to the mics on opposite sides.

Drift

MOTOR DRIFT: the motors wander slightly in speed, like a well-used cabinet.

DRIFT
How much drift, 0 to 100%. 50% (the default) is the standard amount, 100% is twice that, 0 switches it off. More drift also makes the wander a little faster.

MASTER

MIX
Dry / cabinet balance. 100% (the default) is the cabinet only; lower settings blend the dry signal back in.
OUTPUT
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.
OUT meters
Output level, left and right.

IN / OUT

TRIM
Input level before anything else, from -24 to +12 dB.
GUARD
Click the TRIM knob (without moving it) to switch the guard; its light sits at the knob's upper right and is lit while it is on. On by default. Gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources. The light glows brighter while it is catching peaks.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT (OUT)
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper (no latency), or a lookahead limiter that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). CLIP is on by default; its key flashes red while it is clipping.

The header

PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets (a click on the display does the same). Choosing one sets the rotor speeds and the cabinet; the rotor speed switch, MIDI keys, drift, master and the IN / OUT section are left as they are. The last entry resets everything to the defaults.
◄ / ►
Step to the previous or next preset.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Next to the S / M / L size keys. Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Presets

Classic Cabinet
The standard speeds and acceleration, 20% drive, mics at medium distance and spread.
Soul Jazz Trio
Slightly faster fast speeds and quicker acceleration, 40% drive, a touch more horn, closer mics.
Modern Jazz Combo
Slower speeds, gentle 10% drive, mics further back and wider.
Gospel Scream
Fast speeds and quick acceleration, 75% drive, more horn and close mics.
Memphis Groove
Standard speeds, 30% drive, a little more drum, narrower mics.

Slow rotors that take their time to change speed (1.4 s horn, 7.5 s drum).
Guitar Through A Rotary
A faster slow speed, 35% drive and +4 dB of horn, for guitar.
Close Mics
The classic cabinet with the mics right up close and wide: maximum swirl.
Room Mics
The classic cabinet heard from across the room, with narrower mics.
Reset all to defaults
Every control back to its starting value.

Tips

  • The magic is in the speed change: switch between SLOW and FAST in the music, and let the drum lag behind the horn.
  • Map the mod wheel or sustain pedal (MOD WHL / PEDAL) so you can switch speed while you play, as on a real organ.
  • For grit on organ, push DRIVE to 50% or more and play harder chords.
  • If the swirl is too much in a mix, raise DISTANCE or lower SPREAD rather than turning MIX down.

File: gfx-rotary.jsfx

Modulation & space

Space

An algorithmic stereo reverb with room, chamber, hall, plate and spring.

Overview

Space is a stereo reverb in the spirit of the 90s desktop reverb units. ROOM, CHAMBER and HALL use early reflections followed by a dense eight-line tail, each sized for its space. PLATE has no early reflections: it is dense from the start, and bright. SPRING is a separate spring-tank model with two springs, one for each side, complete with the 'boing' chirp.

Around the reverb you get the usual shaping - pre-delay, decay, size, damping and modulation - plus tone filters on the reverb only, a vintage 500 Hz cut, ducking so the reverb steps back while you play, and FREEZE to hold the tail for ever.

Reach for it for

  • Rooms and chambers for vocals, drums and acoustic instruments
  • Halls and long ambient washes for pads and keys
  • Bright plates on snares and vocals
  • Surf and dub spring sounds on guitars and percussion
  • Ducked reverb that stays out of the way of the dry part
  • Frozen pads and drones (FREEZE)

Controls

Reverb type and display

ROOM / CHAMBER / HALL
Early reflections plus the reverb tail, from the smallest space to the largest.
PLATE
No early reflections; dense from the start and bright, with the modulation halved.
SPRING
A two-spring tank, left and right, with the characteristic chirp. EARLY and DIFFUSION do nothing here.
FREEZE
Holds the tail for ever: no new sound goes in and the reverb stops decaying. Switch it off to let the tail fade.
Window
A picture of what is ringing - the room, the plate or the springs - with the type's name.
Decay display
The pre-delay gap, the early reflections and the tail fading to -60 dB, with the DECAY time and pre-delay printed above (and FROZEN while FREEZE is on).
Meters
Output level, left and right, with a floating peak. Click a meter to clear the peak.

Reverb

PRE-DELAY
The gap before the reverb starts, 0 - 250 ms. Separates the reverb from the dry sound.
DECAY
How long the tail takes to fade by 60 dB, 0.2 - 20 s.
SIZE
The size of the space, 50 - 150%. For SPRING it sets the length of the springs.
DAMPING
How much sooner the highs die away than the lows, 0 - 100%. Higher is darker. For SPRING it darkens the tank.
MOD
Slow chorusing of the tail, 0 - 100%, so it shimmers rather than rings. Not used by SPRING.
EARLY
The level of the early reflections, 0 - 100% (ROOM, CHAMBER and HALL only; it reads n/a for PLATE and SPRING).
DIFFUSION
How smeared the onset is, 0 - 100%. Low settings let you hear separate echoes at the start; high settings blur them into a smooth wash. Not used by SPRING.
DUCK
Dips the reverb while you play and lets it bloom in the gaps, 0 - 100%.
WIDTH
The stereo width of the reverb, 0 - 100%.

Each knob shows its value while you hover over it or turn it; PRE-DELAY and DECAY are also shown all the time on the display.

Tone

These shape the reverb only - the dry sound is untouched.

LO CUT
Removes low end from the reverb, 20 - 1000 Hz. Keeps the tail from muddying the bass.
HI CUT
Removes top end from the reverb, 1 - 20 kHz.
0 / -3 / -6 / -9
The vintage reverb-send EQ: a cut of 3, 6 or 9 dB at 500 Hz, together with a high-pass at 140 Hz and a low-pass at 5 kHz. 0 switches it off.

Drift

DRIFT
Makes the tail's modulation wander slowly and randomly, 0 - 100% (not used by SPRING). 50% is the standard amount, 100% twice that, 0 none; the wander also gets a little faster as you turn it up.

Master

MIX
Dry / reverb balance, 0 - 100% (30% by default). Up to 50% the dry sound stays at full level and the reverb comes in; above 50% the reverb stays at full level and the dry sound fades out. Use 100% on an FX send.
OUTPUT
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.

IN / OUT

TRIM
Input level before anything else, from -24 to +12 dB. A single click on the knob (without dragging) switches GUARD on or off; its LED at the knob's upper right is dark when GUARD is off, red when it is on, and glows brighter while it is catching peaks.
GUARD
Off by default in this plugin. Gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out, 0 - 100%: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper (no latency), or a lookahead limiter that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). The CLIP key flashes red while the clipper is working.

The header

PRESETS, arrows and display
PRESETS (or a click on the display) opens the list of factory presets; the ◄ / ► keys step through them. A preset sets the type and the reverb, tone (LO CUT, HI CUT) and DUCK controls and MIX, and switches FREEZE off; WIDTH, the vintage EQ, DRIFT, OUTPUT and the IN / OUT strip are left as they are. The last entry in the list resets everything to the defaults.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Presets

ROOM, 0.6 s decay, short pre-delay, plenty of early reflections.

A bigger ROOM, 1.1 s, a little darker at the top.
Vocal Chamber
CHAMBER, 1.6 s with 15 ms pre-delay.

Tips

  • On an FX send, set MIX to 100% so only the reverb comes back.
  • Raise LO CUT to 200 - 400 Hz on vocal and guitar reverbs to keep the low end clear.
  • Try the -3 or -6 vintage EQ for the classic studio trick of a reverb that sits behind the mix without clouding it.
  • DUCK lets you use a longer, bigger reverb on a busy part: it stays back while the part plays and blooms in the gaps.
  • Turn FREEZE on at the end of a chord to hold it as a pad, then automate it off to let it fade.

File: gfx-space.jsfx

Modulation & space

Squashball

An analogue delay stompbox with warm, darkening repeats, self-oscillation and a STRETCH key that bends the whole echo in pitch.

Overview

Squashball is a delay pedal in the spirit of the classic bucket-brigade analogue delays. GAIN drives the signal into the delay line through a warm, slightly lopsided overdrive; the dry signal stays clean. The tone filter sits inside the feedback loop, so every repeat comes back a little darker than the last, and long delay times darken a touch more, as a slower clock does on the real thing.

REGEN goes past 100%. There the loop starts to howl into steady self-oscillation, but a soft limiter inside it holds the level, so it never runs away. Turning TIME glides the delay rather than jumping, so the repeats bend in pitch as you move it.

The STRETCH key sweeps the delay time towards a second time, STRETCH TIME, at a speed set by RATE, then glides back when you let go. Everything in the delay line bends in pitch as it goes: a fast setting sounds like a tape being yanked, a slow one like a lazy dive. HOLD makes the key work while you hold it down; LATCH makes it click on and off.

Reach for it for

  • Warm, dark analogue repeats behind a guitar or synth lead
  • Rockabilly slapback and short doubling
  • Dub throws and runaway, howling feedback
  • Pitch-bending tape-yank and dive effects on the echoes
  • Lo-fi, crunchy repeats with GAIN turned up
  • Warbly chorus-like doubling at very short times

Controls

The header

PRESETS, ◄ ► and the display
PRESETS (or a click on the display) opens the list of factory presets; ◄ and ► step through them, and the display shows the one in use. A new instance opens on Init.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window. A new instance opens in Large.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

The main row

GAIN
Drive into the delay line, 0 to 100% (25% by default), up to about 24 dB of drive. A warm, slightly lopsided overdrive that adds even harmonics; the lows are trimmed before it so it never flubs, and the top is softened so it never fizzes. Only the repeats are driven - the dry signal stays clean.
TIME (yellow cap)
The delay time, 30 - 1500 ms (350 ms by default). With SYNC on it becomes a note value from 4 bars down to 1/32, including dotted (D) and triplet (T) values (1/4 by default). Turning it glides the delay, so the repeats bend in pitch rather than clicking. All times are limited to 4 seconds.
REGEN
Feedback, 0 to 130% (35% by default): how many repeats you hear. Past 100% the loop oscillates on its own and keeps going after the input stops; the limiter in the loop holds it at a steady level.
TONE
The high-frequency roll-off inside the loop, about 700 Hz - 9 kHz (0 to 100%; 55%, about 2.9 kHz, by default). The knob shows the frequency. Because it is inside the loop, each repeat passes through it again and gets darker.
LEVEL
The volume of the repeats, -24 to +6 dB (0 dB by default).
MIX
Dry / wet balance, 0 to 100% (35% by default). At 50% both are at full level; below that the repeats fade down, above it the dry signal does.

STRETCH

The settings for the STRETCH key.

TIME
The delay time the stretch sweeps towards, 30 - 2000 ms (900 ms by default). Longer than the main TIME bends the pitch down as it goes; shorter bends it up. The sweep follows an S-shaped curve, so the bend eases in and out.
RATE
How fast the stretch sweeps, 0 to 100% (50% by default): from a slow 4-second dive at 0% to a 30 ms yank at 100%. The same speed is used on the way back.
HOLD / LATCH
How the STRETCH key works. HOLD (default): the stretch lasts while you hold the key down and glides back when you let go. LATCH: click the key once to stretch and again to glide back.

The centre

Time readout
The actual delay time in ms, including any stretch and drift, so you can watch it glide.
SYNC
Switches TIME between milliseconds (off, by default) and a note value at the project tempo (on).
TEMPO
A red light that blinks once for every repeat.
Yellow double dot
Lights while the stretch is on or still gliding back.
HOWL
Lights when REGEN is above 100% and the loop is sounding: a warning that it is self-oscillating.
STRETCH
The big lit key. Hold it (HOLD) or click it on and off (LATCH) to sweep the delay to STRETCH TIME; it lights while the stretch is on. It is also a parameter (Stretch), so you can automate it in REAPER.

IN / OUT

TRIM
Input level before anything else, -24 to +12 dB.
GUARD
Click the TRIM knob (without turning it) to switch GUARD on or off; the small light at the knob's upper right shows it is on and glows brighter while it works. Off by default. Gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS. It never boosts quiet sources.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out, 0 to 100% (0% by default): only the lows and highs are saturated, the mids stay clean.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper at -0.3 dBFS (no latency), or a lookahead limiter (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). CLIP is on by default and flashes red while it clips.
DRIFT
0 to 100% (50% by default). See Drift below.
OUTPUT
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.
OUT meter
Left and right output levels.

In REAPER's parameter list only

Bias / Sat low band / Sat high band / Ceiling
The rest of the output stage: the saturation's even-harmonic bias (30% by default), where its low and high bands start (40 - 400 Hz and 2 - 16 kHz) and the clip ceiling or limiter threshold (-0.3 dB by default).

Drift

DRIFT makes the delay clock breathe: a slow, small wander of the delay time - a subtle wow, a little different on the left and right, which widens the repeats - and a gentle wander in the repeats' tone. 50% is the standard amount, 100% twice that, and the wander speeds up a little as you turn it up. At 0% the delay is steady.

Knobs: drag up or down (Shift for fine moves); double-click to reset a knob to its default. The mouse wheel works too.

Presets

Presets set the main row, SYNC, the STRETCH settings and DRIFT, and switch the stretch off. OUTPUT and the IN / OUT strip are left as they are.

Init
The default settings: 350 ms, a few dark repeats, 35% mix.
Slapback
A single bright 95 ms slap with a touch of drive.
Dark Repeats
420 ms with long feedback and a very dark tone: each repeat sinks into the background.
Rubbery Lead
Synced to 1/8 D with more drive; the stretch bends towards 520 ms at a fairly slow rate for a rubbery pitch wobble.
Ambient Swell
720 ms, long feedback, the level down 1 dB and more drift; the stretch is on LATCH with a very slow dive to 1100 ms.
Self-Oscillation
REGEN at 112%: the loop howls on its own. LEVEL is down 3 dB to keep it in check.
Tape Yank
450 ms with a very fast stretch down to 60 ms: press STRETCH for a sudden tape-pull squeal.
Dub Throw
Synced to 1/4 D with long, dark feedback; the stretch makes a medium dive to 1200 ms.
Warble Double
A 42 ms double with full drift for a warbly, chorus-like thickening; the stretch is on LATCH.
Lo-Fi Crunch
180 ms with GAIN at 85% and a very dark tone: gritty, broken-sounding repeats.
Siren Howl
REGEN at 118% with a brighter tone; the stretch is on LATCH with a slow sweep down to 40 ms, so the howl rises like a siren.

Tips

  • Try the stretch while the repeats are still sounding: it bends what is already in the line, so the trick is in the timing.
  • A STRETCH TIME longer than TIME drops the pitch; shorter raises it. Set RATE low for a slow dive, high for a sudden yank.
  • Use LATCH when you want to automate the stretch or play it hands-free; HOLD is quicker for one-off throws.
  • For self-oscillation, set REGEN just past 100%, then ride TONE and TIME by hand. Keep LEVEL down and the output stage on CLIP or LIMIT.
  • Because TONE is inside the loop, small moves make a big difference to long tails. Lower it for repeats that sit behind the part.
  • Put it on a send at 100% MIX if you want to EQ or compress the repeats separately.

File: gfx-squashball.jsfx

Mastering

Aureus

A four-band EQ, drive and compressor tuned by the golden ratio.

Overview

Aureus is a multiband processor. It splits the signal into four bands - SUB, LOW-MID, HIGH-MID and PRESENCE or AIR - and gives each one an EQ, a drive stage and a compressor, then a band gain with SOLO and MUTE. The bands add back together flat and in phase, and MIX blends in a dry signal that is phase-aligned with them.

The crossovers are decadic: the second split is ten times the first, and the third is a hundred times the first, held between 5 and 10 kHz so the top band is always presence or air. The first split is picked from the Fibonacci numbers 34, 55, 89 and 144 Hz, or the classic 70 Hz.

Each section has just one knob and a type key. Everything the knobs don't set - EQ frequencies and shapes, attack and release times, ratios and knees - is worked out from the golden ratio (1.618), so the four bands relate to each other in a consistent, musical way. The compressor attack gets faster from band to band (30, 18.5, 11.5 and 7.1 ms, with the release about eleven times longer), before each compressor type adjusts the timing.

Reach for it for

  • Mix-bus and mastering glue across four bands
  • Adding tape, transistor or transformer colour to just the lows or the top
  • Lifting air or presence with a single knob
  • Taming harsh or boomy bands only when they get loud (DYNAMIC EQ)
  • Parallel multiband processing with MIX

Controls

Left: display, presets and setup

Display
Shows the current preset, or the control you are touching and its value. Click it for the preset list.
PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets; the ◄ / ► keys beside it step through them.
SIZE: S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also shrinks to fit a smaller plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.
INPUT
Input level before anything else, -24 to +24 dB. It also sets how hard the signal hits the drive and compressor stages.
CROSSOVER F1 x10 x100
Picks the first crossover: 34, 55, 70 (the default), 89 or 144 Hz. The second sits at ten times that, the third at a hundred times, held between 5 and 10 kHz (5, 5.5, 7, 8.9 and 10 kHz). The display shows all three when you choose. The top band is called PRESENCE with the two lowest settings and AIR with the others.
HW
Switches the hardware finish (shading, bevels and coloured knob caps) on or off.
GOLD / TEAL
Switches the faceplate between yellow and petrol teal.

The four bands

Each band header shows its frequency range and an activity light: green when the band is passing signal, red when it gets near clipping. Each row has a type key - click it to step through the three types - and an amount knob.

EQ type: PROGRAM
Broad, gentle curves. SUB is a low shelf below the first crossover and the top band a high shelf above the third; the two middle bands are broad bells at their centres. Boosting a shelf also adds a small dip right at the band edge, in the spirit of the classic passive program equalisers, so the boost stays clean.
EQ type: CONSOLE
Firmer, console-style shapes: resonant shelves, and bells that get narrower as you boost or cut further.
EQ type: DYNAMIC
The CONSOLE shape, but its gain follows the band's level: nothing below about -30 dB, rising to the full knob setting as the band gets louder. Use a cut to tame a band only when it gets loud.
EQ knob
The EQ gain, -12 to +12 dB.
DRIVE type: TRANSISTOR
A firm, mostly odd-harmonic clip with a harder edge.
DRIVE type: TAPE
Soft, slightly lopsided saturation with a touch of hysteresis; the top end rolls off a little more as the drive goes up. The default.
DRIVE type: TRANSFORMER
The lows of the band saturate hardest, like an iron-core transformer, with a cleaner top.
DRIVE knob
How hard the band is driven, 0 to 100%. The output is partly level-compensated. 0 is clean.
COMP type: OPTO
Smooth and slower, measuring the average level; the release slows as the gain reduction deepens. Up to about 2.6:1 with a wide, soft knee. The default.
COMP type: VCA
Fast and firmer, with a feedback detector and a tighter knee; up to about 4.2:1.
COMP type: GENTLE
Three gentle compressors in series at thresholds 6 dB apart, each carrying a third of the ratio (up to about 4.2:1 in total), with a release that adapts to the music. In the spirit of the COMP mode of RND.
COMP knob
How much compression, 0 to 100%. One knob does it all: turning it up lowers the threshold (from -6 down to -36 dB) and raises the ratio together, and adds some make-up gain automatically. 0 is off.
GR
The band's gain reduction, 0 to 20 dB, top to bottom.
GAIN
The band's output level, -12 to +12 dB.
SOLO
Solo in place; several bands can be soloed at once.
MUTE
Silences the band.

OUTPUT

VU meters
Left and right output level, with 0 VU at -18 dBFS.

MASTER

DRIFT
Golden-ratio breathing, 0 to 100% (25% by default): each band's compressor threshold wanders slowly up and down, by up to 3 dB at 100%, each on its own period (3 seconds times 1.618 to the power of the band), so the bands never move together. Only heard where the compressor is working.
MIX
Dry / processed balance, 0 to 100% (100% by default). The dry signal is phase-aligned with the bands, so partial settings blend cleanly.
OUTPUT
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.
LIMIT
A safety limiter holds the output peaks at about -0.3 dBFS. The light shows when it is working.

Drag the knobs up and down (hold Shift for fine moves); double-click or Ctrl-click to reset one.

Presets

Init
Everything flat and clean: no EQ, drive or compression.
Golden Glue
Light tape drive and GENTLE compression at 30% on every band.
Tape Polish
Tape drive on all bands with gentle opto compression and +1.5 dB on the SUB and top shelves.
Opto Smooth
Opto compression at 40% on every band, no EQ or drive.
Air Lift
+4 dB on the top shelf with a little drive and GENTLE compression, and +1 dB on HIGH-MID (CONSOLE).
Iron Low End
Transformer drive on SUB (45%) with +2.5 dB and opto compression; LOW-MID trimmed 1.5 dB (CONSOLE) with transformer drive.
VCA Punch
VCA compression on the lower three bands, with transistor drive on LOW-MID.
Dynamic Tame
DYNAMIC EQ cuts on LOW-MID, HIGH-MID and the top band, with light opto compression.

Tips

  • The presets set the bands, DRIFT, MIX, INPUT and OUTPUT, and clear SOLO and MUTE; the crossover setting is kept.
  • Use INPUT to set how hard the drive and compressors work, then OUTPUT to bring the level back.
  • Try the crossover choices on a full mix: 55 or 70 Hz keeps kick and bass together in LOW-MID, 89 or 144 Hz puts more of the bass weight in SUB.
  • For a harsh top end, set the top band to DYNAMIC and cut a few dB: it only works on the loud, bright moments.

File: gfx-aureus.jsfx

Mastering

Benthick 3B

A solid-state iron and transistor bus processor, laid out in signal order.

Overview

Benthick 3B is a bus processor for tight low-end weight, fast transient control and dense glue, without woolly valve artefacts. Its stages run left to right across the panel in signal order: FRONT END, COMP 1, the TILT knob, COMP 2, the SUM, BUS COMP and OUTPUT.

The front end is either IRON, an asymmetric transformer core that works hardest on the lows, or CLASS-A, a symmetric transistor clip with a dense edge in the upper mids. Two compressors then share the work: COMP 1 is a slow window that lets the early transients through, COMP 2 a fast FET-style grabber. BALANCE blends the two paths, and a console-style VCA BUS COMP pulls them together.

Every stage has its own IN key. There is no drift: Benthick 3B is clean and repeatable.

Reach for it for

  • Drum bus weight, smash and parallel crush
  • Mix bus glue and polish
  • Low-end weight on bass DI and kick (IRON)
  • Bringing vocals forward with CLASS-A edge
  • Widening a master with M/S drive and side saturation

Controls

1 FRONT END

IN
Switches the front end in or out.
IRON / CLASS-A
IRON (default): an asymmetric transformer core that works hardest on the lows below about 160 Hz, for even harmonics and weight, with a little magnetic hysteresis. CLASS-A: a symmetric transistor clip with odd harmonics and a dense edge in the upper mids. Switching between them crossfades, so it never clicks.
DRIVE
How hard the front end is pushed, 0 to 18 dB (4 dB by default). Small signals pass at unity, so DRIVE adds colour rather than level.
M/S
Off (L / R): both sides are driven equally. On (SIDES DRIVEN): the front end works in mid / side, and the sides are driven harder for a natural width.
S DRIVE
How much harder the sides are driven when M/S is on, 0 to 12 dB (4 dB by default).

2 COMP 1 (SLOW WINDOW)

IN
Switches COMP 1 in or out.
THRESH
-40 to 0 dB (-18 dB by default).
RATIO
1:1 to 10:1 (2:1 by default).
ATTACK
10 to 80 ms (40 ms by default). The slow attack lets the early transients through untouched.
RELEASE
50 ms to 1 s (300 ms by default).
HYST
Hysteresis, 0 to 12 dB (3 dB by default). Once the level goes over THRESH, the release holds back until it falls HYST dB below it, so the compressor never chatters round the threshold.
SC HPF
A high-pass filter on what all three compressors listen to, 30 to 300 Hz (90 Hz by default). It keeps the lows from making the compressors pump; the audio itself isn't filtered.
GR
COMP 1's gain reduction, 1 to 20 dB.

TILT

TILT
The big knob between COMP 1 and COMP 2: a seesaw around 700 Hz, -6 to +6 dB. Turning it right lifts the top and lowers the bottom; left does the opposite. It comes after COMP 1, so it shapes both what COMP 2 hears and what you hear. The plate under it shows the value while you turn it.

3 COMP 2 (FET)

IN
Switches COMP 2 in or out. With COMP 2 out, the SUM section is out too.
4 / 8 / 12 / 20
The ratio (4:1 by default). Higher ratios have a harder knee.
ALL
Every ratio key in: 20:1 with no knee, faster attack and release and more grit from the gain cell - hard and aggressive. Click again to go back to 4:1.
THRESH
-40 to 0 dB (-14 dB by default).
ATTACK
20 to 800 microseconds (400 by default) - fast enough to grab the transients.
RELEASE
50 ms to 1.1 s (300 ms by default).
MAKEUP
COMP 2's level, 0 to 24 dB (2 dB by default).
GR
COMP 2's gain reduction, 1 to 20 dB.

SUM

BALANCE
Blends the two paths: towards 1, the COMP 1 path with its untouched transients (after TILT); towards 2, the clamped COMP 2 signal. The value reads as the two shares, 40 / 60 by default. It is the same signal through two paths, so they sum without phase problems.

4 BUS COMP

Console-style VCA glue that pulls COMP 1 and COMP 2 together. Its detector shares the SC HPF.

IN
Switches the bus compressor in or out.
2 / 4 / 10
The ratio (2:1 by default).
ATTACK
Stepped: 0.1, 0.3, 1, 3, 10 or 30 ms (10 ms by default).
RELEASE
Stepped: 0.1, 0.3, 0.6 or 1.2 s, or A (AUTO, the default). AUTO releases quickly after a transient and slowly once it has been working for a while.
THRESH
-30 to 0 dB (-18 dB by default).
MAKEUP
0 to 18 dB (5 dB by default).
GR
The bus compressor's gain reduction, 1 to 20 dB.

5 OUTPUT

WIDTH
Mid / side width, 0 to 200% (100% by default).
S SAT
Drives the sides into a second transistor saturation stage, 0 to 100%. Adds density and width to the edges of the image.
MIX
Wet / dry: 100% is fully processed; lower blends the unprocessed input back in, for parallel processing.
OUTPUT
Output level, -24 to +12 dB.
GR display
Shows the gain reduction of COMP 1, COMP 2 and BUS in dB.
IN / OUT meters
Input level and output level (left and right). Click any meter to clear the peaks.
LIMIT
The final stage. Off (default): a soft clip that protects the output at -0.3 dBFS. On (red LED): a lookahead brickwall limiter that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER).

Benthick 3B has no IN / OUT strip: the input trim, the input guard (on), the band saturation (off) and the output ceiling stay at their defaults.

The header

PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets; click the display for the list too. The display shows PRESET nn/10 and the name, or Custom. Choosing a preset sets every stage (and puts all the IN keys in); OUTPUT and LIMIT are left as they are. The last entry resets everything to the defaults.
◄ / ►
Step to the previous or next preset.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Presets

Bus Glue
The default settings: IRON at 4 dB, gentle COMP 1, COMP 2 at 4:1, 2:1 bus glue with AUTO release.
Iron Low End
IRON driven to 8 dB, a little darker TILT, slower compressors, bus makeup 7 dB.
Drum Smash
CLASS-A, TILT +2 dB, COMP 2 on ALL with a 20 microsecond attack, 4:1 bus comp, a little width and S SAT.
Vocal Forward
Light CLASS-A, SC HPF 150 Hz, TILT +1.5 dB, COMP 2 at 8:1 weighted to 70%.
Wide Master
M/S with 6 dB side drive, gentle compression, 130% width and 25% S SAT.
Parallel Crush
CLASS-A driven to 10 dB, COMP 2 on ALL crushing hard, 10:1 bus comp, blended at 60% MIX.
Mix Bus Polish
Light IRON with M/S, very gentle compression, 115% width and a touch of S SAT.
Bass DI Weight
IRON driven to 10 dB, SC HPF 60 Hz, TILT -2 dB, COMP 2 at 8:1.
Room Mic Grit
CLASS-A at 12 dB with M/S, TILT +3 dB, COMP 2 on ALL, 140% width and 40% S SAT at 70% MIX.
Gentle Master
Barely any drive, 1.3:1 COMP 1, light bus glue, 105% width.
Reset all to defaults
Every control back to its starting value, including OUTPUT and the hidden input and output settings.

Tips

  • Use BALANCE as the main 'how much' control: towards 1 keeps the punch, towards 2 adds density.
  • If the low end makes the compressors pump, raise SC HPF; the bass itself is not filtered.
  • Switch stages out with their IN keys to hear what each one adds - the controls of a stage that is out are greyed.
  • Keep an eye on the GR display: a few dB on each compressor is usually enough for glue.

File: gfx-benthick-3b.jsfx

Mastering

Master EQ

A mastering equaliser with stepped controls and three dynamic bands.

Overview

Master EQ is built for the last stage of a mix. It has low and high cut filters, a low shelf, three bell bands, a high shelf and an AIR band. The controls move in steps, as on classic mastering equalisers, so settings are easy to note down and recall.

The three bells, DYN 1 - 3, are dynamic. Besides a fixed GAIN, each has a COMP knob: turned left it pulls the band down when that part of the music gets loud (taming harshness or boom only when it happens); turned right it lifts the band as it gets busy. In the middle the band is a plain static bell.

Every band can work on the stereo pair, on the mid (the centre of the image) or on the side (the edges), so you can, for example, tighten the lows in the sides only or add air to the edges.

Reach for it for

  • Final tonal balance on a mix or master
  • Taming harshness or sibilance only when it flares up (DYN with COMP)
  • Controlling a boomy low end that comes and goes
  • Mid/side work: centring the lows, widening the top
  • Adding gentle air above the audio band

Controls

Drag a knob up or down to turn it (hold Shift for fine moves), use the mouse wheel, or double-click to reset it. Gains move in 0.5 dB steps; FREQ knobs step through the standard series (100, 120, 150, 180, 220, 270, 330, 390, 470, 560, 680, 820 and so on), and Q steps through 0.4, 0.5, 0.7, 1, 1.4, 2, 2.8 and 4. The value shows while you touch a knob.

The header

Colour swatch
Opens the list of colour themes for the panel.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.
EQ IN
Switches the shelves, the bells, AIR and the dynamic action in or out (the change glides rather than jumps). The cut filters stay in. While it is out, the display reads EQ OUT.
PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets (clicking the display does the same). A preset sets the EQ and dynamics sections and switches EQ IN on; TRIM, OUTPUT and the OUT stage are left as they are. The last entry, Reset (flat), sets every band flat.
Left / right arrows
Step to the previous or next preset.

The display

Shows the summed EQ curve from 20 Hz to 20 kHz over a range of +/-8 dB, moving with the dynamic bands as they work. The faint vertical lines mark the centres of DYN 1 - 3.

IN + FILTERS

TRIM
Input level before anything else, from -24 to +12 dB. Double-click to reset it.
GUARD (click TRIM)
A single click on the TRIM knob switches GUARD on or off; the small light beside the knob shows it (dark = off, red = on, bright = working). GUARD is on by default. It gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources.
HPF
A 12 dB/oct high-pass filter, stepping from 20 to 300 Hz. Fully left (20) is OFF - out of circuit.
LPF
A 12 dB/oct low-pass filter, stepping from 4 to 20 kHz. Fully right (20 kHz) is OFF.
TIMING
FAST, MED or SLOW: the attack and release of all three dynamic bands (2 / 60 ms, 10 / 150 ms, 30 / 400 ms). FAST follows transients and sibilance; SLOW rides the broader balance.

LOW

30 / 60 / 100 / 200
The low shelf frequency.
GAIN
-6 to +6 dB in 0.5 dB steps.
BROAD / TIGHT
BROAD: a gentle, wide slope. TIGHT: a steeper shelf that changes less above the frequency.
ST / M / S
What the band works on: the stereo pair, the mid only or the side only.

DYN 1, DYN 2, DYN 3

Three identical dynamic bells. The rows are named once on the left: FREQ, GAIN, Q and DYN. The light next to each band's name shows when its dynamic part is working.

FREQ
The centre of the band, 20 Hz - 20 kHz (120 Hz, 1 kHz and 4 kHz by default).
GAIN
A fixed boost or cut, -6 to +6 dB.
Q
The width, 0.4 - 4. Higher is narrower.
DYN (COMP / EXP)
The dynamic part, from COMP 10 on the left through STATIC in the middle to EXP 10 on the right. Towards COMP the band is pulled down when it gets loud, up to 12 dB; towards EXP it is lifted as it gets busy, up to 6 dB. Turning further makes it react at lower levels and more strongly, with a soft, gradual knee. Each band listens only to its own range.
ST / M / S
What the band works on: the stereo pair, the mid only or the side only.

HIGH

2k / 4k / 8k / 12k
The high shelf frequency.
GAIN
-6 to +6 dB in 0.5 dB steps.
BROAD / TIGHT
BROAD: a gentle, wide slope. TIGHT: a steeper shelf.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (no latency), or a lookahead limiter that holds them at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). The CLIP key flashes red while the clipper is working.
ST / M / S
What the band works on: the stereo pair, the mid only or the side only.

AIR

10k / 20k / 40k
Where the broad air lift is centred.
GAIN
0 to +6 dB in 0.5 dB steps.
OUTPUT
The output level, -12 to +12 dB.
Level lights
Ten lights showing the output level, from -30 dBFS up to just under 0 (green, then amber, then red).
ST / M / S
What the band works on: the stereo pair, the mid only or the side only.

Presets

Gentle Polish
A touch of low weight and air, with the low mids, upper mids and harshness gently held in check by DYN 1 - 3.
Warm & Full
A 100 Hz lift, dynamic control of the low mids and the 5 kHz area, and a slightly softer top.
Bright & Open
A small low-mid cut, dynamic control at 2.5 and 6.5 kHz, and more high shelf and air.
Low-End Control
HPF at 30 Hz, the lows cut in the sides only, dynamic control at 110 Hz (mid) and 300 Hz, FAST timing.

Presence lifted and mud cut in the mid, sibilance tamed dynamically in the mid, a little air.

Dynamic cuts at 3.2 kHz and 8 kHz only, working when the harshness flares up.

Lows cut and highs and air lifted in the sides only, keeping the lows centred.

HPF at 30 Hz, a tight 60 Hz lift with dynamic control at 90 Hz and 3.5 kHz, a low-mid cut, a tight 12 kHz lift and air; FAST timing.

Round lows, a softened top and the LPF at 16 kHz.

Barely there: a hint of low and high lift, gentle low-mid control, a little 40 kHz air, SLOW timing.
