Every band flat, filters off, dynamic bands static.

Tips

  • Use DYN instead of GAIN when a problem comes and goes: a harsh vocal at 3 kHz only on the loud notes, or a bass note that booms only now and then.
  • Switch a band to S to work on the edges only - cutting the lows in the sides tightens the bass without making it thinner in the centre.
  • Compare with EQ IN on and off; the HPF and LPF stay in either way, so you hear only what the bands are doing.
  • The stepped controls make settings easy to note down: write them down to recall a master later.

File: gfx-master-eq.jsfx

Mastering

MasterSuite

A seven-stage mastering strip, from valve colour to a true-peak limiter.

Overview

MasterSuite puts a complete mastering chain on one panel, in seven stages: input and oversampling, valve saturation with a broad tone stack, mid / side stereo control, a vari-mu style compressor, a high-frequency limiter, a clipper and a true-peak brickwall limiter.

Every stage has its own ON switch, and switching any of them crossfades over 10 ms, so it never clicks. The saturation and clipper stages can run oversampled (2x or 4x) to keep them clean of aliasing. The plugin reports its latency to REAPER, and keeps it the same whatever you bypass, so switching a stage never shifts the timing.

OUTPUT feeds the limiter, so the CEILING always has the final say on the peak level.

Reach for it for

  • Finishing a mix or a stem master in one plugin
  • Gentle glue and warmth on a mix bus
  • Loud, punchy masters with a clipper into the limiter
  • Streaming-safe masters with a -1 dB true-peak ceiling
  • Mono-ing the low end and adjusting stereo width

Controls

IN / OUT

TRIM
Input level, -12 to +12 dB.
SAT
Band saturation from the GFX output stage: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises. It sits after OUTPUT, before the limiter.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT (OUT)
The GFX output stage, just before the true-peak limiter: nothing (default), a soft clipper at -0.3 dBFS, or a lookahead limiter at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms extra latency). The CLIP key flashes red while it is clipping.
OVERSAMPLING: OFF / 2X / 4X
Runs the TUBE and CLIPPER stages at two or four times the sample rate (2X by default) for cleaner saturation with less aliasing. Higher settings add latency.
IN meters
Input level, left and right. Each shows a floating peak; click any meter to clear the peaks.

TUBE

Asymmetric valve saturation. Small signals pass at unity; turning DRIVE up adds saturation rather than level.

ON
Switches the stage in or out (in by default).
DRIVE
How hard the valve is driven, -12 to +12 dB (-3 dB by default).
DRIFT
How much the valve's bias and drive wander, 0 to 100% (50% by default). See Drift below.
BIAS: LOW / MID / HIGH
The valve's asymmetry. LOW is the cleanest and gentlest; HIGH adds more even (2nd) harmonics and bites harder. MID is the default.

TONE

A broad four-band EQ, -6 to +6 dB on each band, with wide, gentle curves.

ON
Switches the tone stage in or out (in by default).
TAPE
Adds a gentle tape-style roll-off at 18 kHz.
BASS
A low shelf at 90 Hz.
LO-MID
A broad bell at 350 Hz - for warmth or mud.
HI-MID
A broad bell at 2.8 kHz - for presence or harshness.
PRESENCE
A high shelf at 7 kHz - for air and brightness.

STEREO

ON
Switches the stereo stage in or out (in by default).
MONO
Makes everything below this frequency mono, from OFF (fully left) up to 200 Hz. It removes the side signal below the frequency with a steep 24 dB per octave filter, for a tight, centred low end.
WIDTH
The level of the side signal, 0 to 200% (100% by default). 0% is mono; above 100% widens.

VARI-MU

A stereo-linked RMS compressor with a soft knee. Like a valve vari-mu, its ratio grows a little as it works harder, and its release slows after long stretches of gain reduction.

ON
Switches the compressor in or out (out by default).
THRESH
-40 to 0 dB (-18 dB by default).
RATIO
1:1 to 10:1 (2:1 by default).
KNEE
0 to 12 dB (6 dB by default). Wider knees ease the compression in more gradually.
MAKEUP
0 to +12 dB.
MIX
Parallel compression: 100% is fully compressed; lower blends the uncompressed signal back in.
RECTIFIER: GERM / SILICON / LED
The timing. GERM (germanium): slow and smooth (30 ms attack, 350 ms release). SILICON: medium (10 ms, 180 ms), the default. LED: fast (2 ms, 70 ms).
GR
The compressor's gain reduction, 0 to 20 dB, with the reading above it.

HF LIMIT

A fast peak limiter that listens only to the top above 6 kHz and turns down only that band, for harshness and sibilance. The split keeps both bands in phase, so at rest the response is flat.

ON
Switches it in or out (out by default).
THRESH
-30 to 0 dB (-12 dB by default). Lower catches more of the top.
GR
How far the top band is being turned down.

CLIPPER

ON
Switches the clipper in with the last mode chosen (out by default).
DRIVE
How hard the signal is pushed into the clipper at 0 dBFS, 0 to +12 dB.
SOFT / HARD
SOFT: a rounded clip that eases into the ceiling. HARD: a straight clamp. Either can be picked while the clipper is on or off.

LIMITER

A true-peak brickwall limiter: it finds the peaks between the samples, looks 1.5 ms ahead and shapes the gain so the output never goes over the ceiling.

ON
Switches the limiter in or out (in by default).
CEILING
The maximum output level, -1.00 to -0.10 dB (-0.30 dB by default). Use -1 dB for streaming.
RELEASE
How fast the limiter recovers, 10 to 500 ms (80 ms by default).
GR
The limiter's gain reduction.

Output

OUTPUT
The level into the limiter, -12 to +12 dB. Turning it up makes the master louder and the limiter work harder; the CEILING still holds the peaks.
OUT meters
Output level, left and right.

Drift

In MasterSuite, drift makes the TUBE stage's bias and drive wander slowly, like a real valve. The DRIFT knob sits in the TUBE section: 50% is the standard amount, 100% is twice that and 0 switches it off. The wander also speeds up a little as you turn it up.

The header

PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets; the arrows step through them and the display shows the one in use (click it for the list too). Choosing one sets the seven stages and OUTPUT; DRIFT and the IN / OUT strip's SAT and OUT are left alone. The last entry resets to the defaults.
Theme picker
Changes the panel's colour theme.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.
HW
Switches between the hardware-style panel and the flat look.
LATENCY
Shows the plugin's current latency in samples and milliseconds.

Presets

Transparent
No valve, gentle 1.5:1 glue, mono below 30 Hz, a -1 dB ceiling.
Warm Glue
Valve on, a little bass and tape roll-off, slow 2:1 compression, HF limiter and a soft clip into a -0.5 dB ceiling.
Loud & Punchy
4x oversampling, 3:1 fast compression blended at 70%, slightly wider, soft clip into a -0.3 dB ceiling.
Vintage Tape
Valve driven harder on low bias, tape roll-off, side narrowed to 90%, slow compression, a -1 dB ceiling.
Bright Pop
High valve bias, mids scooped and presence lifted, 120% width, soft clip and a -0.3 dB ceiling.
Bass Heavy
4x oversampling, bass up, mono below 150 Hz, fast compression, hard clip.
Acoustic / Classical
Almost nothing: no valve, very light 1.3:1 compression with a wide knee, slow 300 ms limiter release.
Podcast / Voice
Mono (width 0, mono below 200 Hz), bass cut and mids forward, 3:1 compression, HF limiter, a -1 dB ceiling.
Parallel Punch
Heavy 6:1 fast compression blended in at 40% under the dry mix.
Streaming Safe (-1 dBTP)
Controlled, gentle compression, no clipper, a -1 dB true-peak ceiling.
Reset to defaults
Every stage back to its starting value, including the DRIFT knob.

Tips

  • Set the CEILING first, then raise OUTPUT until the limiter's GR shows only a few dB on the loudest parts.
  • A little CLIPPER DRIVE in front of the limiter lets you get louder with fewer limiter artefacts; switch OVERSAMPLING to 4X when you do.
  • Use HF LIMIT instead of a cut with the TONE stage when only some moments are harsh or sibilant.
  • MONO at 80 - 150 Hz tightens the low end for vinyl, clubs and small speakers without narrowing the rest of the mix.
  • Switch stages ON and off as you work: each one crossfades cleanly, so you can compare without clicks.

File: gfx-mastersuite.jsfx

Mastering

Optimiser

A broadcast-style loudness chain: AGC, four-band compression, clipper and limiter.

Overview

Optimiser is a loudness processor in the spirit of the classic broadcast processors that made FM stations sound loud, dense and consistent. The signal runs through a chain: a slow AGC that rides the level, a phase rotator, a four-band compressor, an HF limiter, a soft clipper and finally a brickwall limiter.

The four bands are split at 150 Hz, 1.2 kHz and 6 kHz with a phase-coherent crossover that sums back flat. DRIVE pushes all four compressors harder; BASS, MID, PRESENCE and AIR set each band's output level, which is where the station's 'sound' comes from. The bands are linked by COUPLING, so the tonal balance holds while they work.

MIX blends the processed sound with the unprocessed one (passed through the same crossover, so the two stay in phase) for parallel processing.

Reach for it for

  • Making a master or a stem loud and dense
  • Radio-style, 'on air' processing of a mix
  • Evening out speech and podcasts
  • Parallel punch on drums or a mix bus
  • Lo-fi AM-radio effects

Controls

Display

Bar meters
One bar per stage. AGC: the gain the AGC is adding (up from the centre line) or taking away (down), up to 12 dB. BASS, MID, PRES, AIR: each band's gain reduction. HF: the HF limiter. CLIP: how hard the clipper is working. LIMIT: the final limiter. The scale runs from 0 at the top to 20 dB at the bottom.
INPUT
A VU meter showing the input level (0 VU = -18 dBFS).

Header

SLOW / MED / FAST
How quickly the four band compressors recover. SLOW is open and natural; FAST gives a denser, more obviously processed sound.

PROCESS

AGC
Switches the AGC on: a slow wideband gain rider that brings the average level towards a steady -18 dBFS, adding or taking away up to 12 dB. It rises at 2 dB per second and falls at 6 dB per second.
GATE
Only with AGC on (on by default). Freezes the AGC when the signal falls below -50 dBFS, so fades and pauses are not pumped back up.
DRIVE
Pushes all four band compressors harder, 0 to +18 dB. More DRIVE means more compression and a denser sound.
BASS
The level of the low band (below 150 Hz), -6 to +6 dB.
MID
The level of the low-mid band (150 Hz - 1.2 kHz), -6 to +6 dB.
PRESENCE
The level of the upper-mid band (1.2 - 6 kHz), -6 to +6 dB.
AIR
The level of the top band (above 6 kHz), -6 to +6 dB.
CLIP
Drives the soft clipper ahead of the final limiter, by up to 9 dB. It rounds off the peaks for extra loudness and bite.

SHAPE

ROTATOR
On by default. Four all-pass filters at 200 Hz turn lopsided peaks (typical of speech) into a more symmetrical wave, so the clipper and limiter get more loudness for the same distortion. It changes phase, not tone.
FIZZ
The broadcast exciter: adds only new harmonics from everything above 3 kHz, shaped around 8 kHz and held about 26 dB below the programme, blended in parallel. A little sparkle on top.
COUPLING
How tightly the four bands are linked, 0 to 100% (50% by default). At 100% they move as one; at 0 they work fully independently. In between, each band's gain reduction can stray only so far from the average of the four, so the tonal balance holds.
HF LIMIT
A fast limiter on the top band, 0 to 100% (40% by default). It holds the treble at a steady, pinned level - the bright, even broadcast top end - and stops the highs from hitting the clipper first. Fully left is off.

DRIFT

DRIFT
See Drift below.

IN / OUT

TRIM
Input level before anything else, from -24 to +12 dB. A single click on the knob switches GUARD on or off (off by default); its light at the knob's upper right is lit while GUARD is on and glows brighter while it is catching peaks. GUARD gently eases very hot sources down towards about -18 dBFS average and catches fast peaks above -6 dBFS, so loud sources never overdrive the plugin. It never boosts quiet sources.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage, LIMIT by default. OFF: nothing. CLIP: a soft clipper that clips the peaks at the ceiling (-0.3 dBFS by default); THRESH does nothing in this mode. LIMIT: a lookahead brickwall limiter (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER) that holds the peaks at the ceiling. The CLIP key flashes red while the output is being clipped.

MASTER

MIX
Blends the processed signal with the unprocessed one, 0 to 100%. Below 100% you get parallel processing: the dense, loud sound under the natural dynamics.
OUTPUT
The output level, -24 to +12 dB.
THRESH
With the LIMIT stage: pull it down (to -24 dB) to push the whole chain harder into the limiter's ceiling - louder, with the peaks held. The clipper starts at the same point. With CLIP or OFF it reads LIMIT OFF.
GR
The limiter's gain reduction, 12 lights of 1 dB each, lit from the top down.
OUT meters
Output level, left and right. Click them to reset the peak hold.

The header

Theme picker
Changes the panel's colour theme.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
HW
Switches between the hardware-style panel and the flat look.
PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets (or click the preset display). The ◄ / ► keys step through them. Choosing one sets the release speed, AGC and gate, DRIVE, the four band levels, CLIP and MIX; the SHAPE section, drift, OUTPUT and the input / output settings are left as they are. The last entry resets everything to the defaults.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Drift

DRIFT (0 - 100%, 50% by default) makes the processor breathe slightly, like an unsteady power supply: the drive into the bands wanders by a fraction of a dB and the bands' attack and release times waver. 50% is the standard amount, 100% twice that, 0 none; the wander also speeds up as you turn it up.

Presets

Gentle FM
Slow release, AGC on, light drive with a touch of bass, presence and air; a little clipping.
Rock FM
Medium release, more drive, a lift in the lows and highs, moderate clipping.
Pop Loud
Fast release, heavy drive and a bright, bassy balance with plenty of clipping.
Talk Radio
Medium release, bass cut and a mid and presence lift for clear speech.
Classical
Slow release, very light drive and no clipping, to keep the dynamics.
Dance Smash
Fast release, AGC gate off, very heavy drive, big bass and air, hard clipping.
Vintage AM
Heavy drive, lifted bass, mids and presence with the air cut by 6 dB, hard clipping: the old AM-radio sound.
Master Polish
AGC off, slow release, light drive with a touch of bass, presence and air.
Podcast Voice
Medium release, bass cut and a presence lift for spoken word.
Parallel Punch
Fast, heavily driven and clipped, blended in at 40% mix.
Reset all to defaults
Every control back to its starting value.

Tips

  • Set the sound with DRIVE and the four band levels first, then use THRESH to set the final loudness.
  • Watch the band bars: if one band's reduction is far bigger than the others, lower that band's level or raise COUPLING.
  • If the top end gets harsh as you push harder, raise HF LIMIT before you reach for AIR.
  • Keep the AGC for programmes with uneven levels (speech, live recordings); turn it off for a finished master you have already levelled.
  • For drums or a mix bus, try MIX at 30-50% for parallel density without losing the transients.

File: gfx-optimiser.jsfx

Mastering

Quad22

A four-band compander, saturator and EQ channel strip.

Overview

Quad22 is a modern take on the classic studio noise-reduction compander idea, turned into a four-band channel strip. It splits the audio at 80 Hz, 3 kHz and 9 kHz with phase-coherent crossovers, so the bands add back together cleanly.

Each band runs through saturation, a fixed-frequency EQ, a one-knob compander and a gain control. The compander is the heart of it: turned left it gently lifts the quiet detail and tails in that band while leaving the peaks alone (the encode side); turned right it pushes the quiet parts further down (the decode side).

The bands are summed, blended with a phase-aligned dry signal by MIX, then go through the master TRIM and a soft ceiling limiter.

Reach for it for

  • Bringing up air, breath and detail without boosting peaks
  • Tightening a woolly low end or quietening noise in one band
  • Gentle multiband glue and colour on a mix or bus
  • Tape, transformer or transistor colour on just part of the spectrum

Controls

BAND 1 to BAND 4

Four bands with the same controls: BAND 1 below 80 Hz (SUB), BAND 2 from 80 Hz to 3 kHz (BODY), BAND 3 from 3 to 9 kHz (PRESENCE) and BAND 4 above 9 kHz (BREATH).

OFF / IRON / TAPE / XSTR
The saturation type. IRON (transformer): a symmetric soft clip, mostly 3rd harmonic. TAPE: a smooth, symmetrical saturation. XSTR (transistor): a lopsided curve that adds even harmonics. OFF: no saturation.
DRIVE
How hard the band hits the saturation, 0 to 100%. The output is partly level-compensated. To keep the lows clean, IRON and XSTR are driven less hard in BAND 1 (40%) and BAND 2 (75%).
LF SAFE (BAND 1)
Lights while IRON or XSTR is driving BAND 1, to show the low-frequency safeguard is in use.
LO SHELF / BELL / HI SHELF
The band's EQ, -12 to +12 dB, at a fixed frequency: BAND 1 a low shelf at 50 Hz, BAND 2 a bell at 800 Hz, BAND 3 a bell at 5 kHz, BAND 4 a high shelf at 12 kHz. The bells are broad.
COMPAND
One knob, -100 to +100, with a centre detent (FLAT). Left (LIFT): upward compression - material below about -20 dB is lifted, by up to 15 dB, while the peaks are left alone; the fast 3 ms attack lets transients through unlifted, so the tails and low-level detail come up. Right (EXP): downward expansion - the threshold rises from -50 to -26 dB and the ratio goes up to 1:2, cutting quiet parts by up to 40 dB. It never adds gain, so it cannot push the peaks over. BAND 1 recovers more slowly (200 ms rather than 75 ms) so the sub-bass doesn't flutter.
GAIN
The band's output level, -24 to +24 dB.
Meter
The band's output level, -48 to +3 dB.
SOLO
Solo in place; several bands can be soloed at once.
BYPASS
Passes the band through untouched: no saturation, EQ, compander or gain.

MASTER

MIX
Dry / wet balance, 0 to 100% (100% by default). The dry signal is phase-aligned with the bands, so partial settings blend cleanly.
TRIM
Master output level, -24 to +24 dB, before the ceiling.
CEILING
A soft limiter on the output, -12 to 0 dB (-0.3 dB by default): the peaks are rounded off smoothly towards this level.
OUT
The output level, -48 to +3 dB.

The header

Display
Shows the current preset, or the control you are touching and its value. Click it for the preset list.
< / > (PRESET)
Step through the presets.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.
HW
Switches between the hardware rack and the flat, soft panel.
FINISH (hardware only)
Switches the hardware rack between graphite (the default) and cream & walnut. The swatch on the key shows the other finish. The choice is saved with the project.
Colour key (flat panel only)
Switches the flat panel between light grey and dark blue.

Drag the knobs up and down (hold Shift for fine moves); double-click or Ctrl-click to reset one.

Presets

Init
Everything flat: no saturation, EQ, compander or gain.
Air Lift
Lift on BAND 3 (-35) and BAND 4 (-50), with +1 and +2 dB of EQ.
Gentle Glue
Mild lift on all four bands, with light tape on BAND 2.
Tape Master
Low-drive tape on every band, +1 dB on the 50 Hz shelf and a touch less top.
Vocal Presence
+2 dB at 5 kHz with lift on BAND 3, BAND 2 slightly trimmed, +1 dB of air.
Low-End Control
Expansion on BAND 1 with a light transformer, the 800 Hz bell down 1 dB.
Analog Sheen
Transistor drive on BAND 4 with +2.5 dB of high shelf and lift.
Loudness Push
Lift on all bands, light transformer drive on BAND 1 and tape on BAND 2, master TRIM +2 dB.

Tips

  • The presets set the saturation, drive, EQ, compander and gain of each band plus TRIM, CEILING and MIX; SOLO and BYPASS are left as they are.
  • Start with COMPAND turned left on BAND 3 and BAND 4 to bring up breath and air on a vocal or mix without making it harsher - the peaks are left alone.
  • Turn COMPAND right on BAND 1 to tighten a boomy low end, or on BAND 4 to push hiss and noise down in the quiet passages.
  • SOLO a band while you set its compander: you hear exactly what it is lifting or pushing down.

File: gfx-quad22.jsfx

Summing

Bern 16

Sixteen mono channels into a stereo mix bus, with a broadcast console's clean summing amp and output transformer.

Overview

Bern 16 is a sixteen-channel summing mixer in the spirit of the classic Swiss broadcast consoles: precise and quiet, with a tight discrete-transistor summing amp and an output transformer that adds warmth when you lean on it. Each mono channel has its own gain, pan, solo, mute, meter and name strip, and the sixteen channels are mixed down to a stereo pair.

The colour comes from the master section. DRIVE pushes the mix into the summing amp and the transformer without making it louder: the amp rounds the peaks gently with a little 2nd and 3rd harmonic, and the transformer thickens the lows below about 100 Hz. COLOUR adds the console's own response, TONE tilts it darker or brighter, and IRON adds a whisper of input-transformer saturation to every channel.

It is very clean at DRIVE 0: a 0 dBFS peak loses only about 0.2 dB in the amp. Turn up DRIVE when you want the console to be heard.

Reach for it for

  • Summing drums, stems or a whole mix "through a desk" inside REAPER
  • Gentle glue and weight on a drum or music bus
  • A quick balance and pan of up to sixteen mono sources in one place
  • Darkening or brightening a mix by a hair with the console tilt
  • Checking the balance in mono

Loading and setting it up

  1. Put Bern 16 on a bus track and set that track to 16 channels.
  2. Send each mono source to the bus, and in each send's audio routing pick a single channel, 1 to 16, so every source lands on its own channel strip.
  3. Turn off each source's own master / parent send, so you only hear it through Bern 16.
  4. The stereo mix leaves Bern 16 on channels 1 and 2; channels 3 to 16 are silenced on the way out.
  5. Click a channel's scribble strip and type a name so you know what is where.

Controls

The header

PRESETS
Opens the preset list. The last item, Reset all to defaults, puts every channel and the master section back to the start.
◄ / ►
Step to the previous or next preset (wrapping round).
Preset display
Shows the preset number and name. Click it for the preset list too.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window. A new instance opens in L.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Channels 1 to 16

Sixteen identical mono strips, in groups of four. Channel 1 is the input on track channel 1, and so on.

Scribble strip
The white strip under the channel number. Click it and type a name (up to 16 characters); Enter keeps it, Escape cancels, Backspace deletes. Clicking elsewhere also keeps it. The names are saved with the project.
GAIN
The channel level, -24 to +24 dB (0 dB by default).
PAN
Left to right, L100 to R100 (C by default). Constant power: a centred channel is 3 dB down on each side, so it keeps the same loudness as you pan it.
Meter
A 12-segment level meter, 0 to -48 dBFS, read after GAIN and IRON. Green, amber from -9 dBFS and red from -3 dBFS. It goes dark when the channel is muted or not heard because of a solo.
S (SOLO)
Solo in place. Any number of channels can be soloed at once; then only the soloed channels are heard.
M (MUTE)
Mutes the channel. A muted channel stays muted even when it is soloed.

MASTER

DRIVE
Level into the summing amp and the output transformer, 0 to +18 dB (0 by default). It is level-matched, so it changes the colour, not the volume: more DRIVE gives rounder peaks, a little more harmonic richness and thicker lows.
TONE
The console's tilt, DARK 100 to BRIGHT 100 (FLAT by default). At full DARK the lows (shelf at 250 Hz) come up 2 dB and the highs (shelf at 4 kHz) go down 2 dB; BRIGHT does the opposite. It works only while COLOUR is in.
COLOUR
Switches the console response in or out (in by default): a gentle high-pass at the very bottom, a little transformer weight around 45 Hz, a soft top roll-off (about 1 dB down at 20 kHz) and the TONE tilt. Off is flat; the amp and transformer still work.
IRON
A whisper of input-transformer saturation on every channel (off by default). Mostly felt on hot channels: it softens them a little and adds a touch of 2nd harmonic.
MONO
Folds the mix to mono, to check the balance (off by default).
SOLO light
Flashes while any channel is soloed.
VOLUME
Master output level, -24 to +12 dB (0 dB by default).
DRIFT
How much the power supply wanders, 0 to 100% (20% by default). See Drift below.
SAT
Band saturation on the way out, 0 - 100% (0 by default): only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS (no latency), or a lookahead limiter that holds them at -0.3 dBFS (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER). CLIP is on by default; its key flashes red while it is clipping.
PEAK / RMS meters
Left and right output meters, 0 to -54 dBFS. The segments show the peak in 2 dB steps, with a hold that falls back after 1.5 seconds; the thin blue bar beside each shows the RMS level (300 ms). The figures below show the held peak. Click the meters to clear the hold.

Drift

DRIFT gives the console an uneven power supply. Each channel's gain wanders on its own by up to 0.25 dB (at 100%), and the bias of the summing amp wanders separately on the left and right, so the colour shifts very slightly between the sides. The movements are slow and smooth. At 0% everything stays exactly where you set it.

Knobs: drag up or down (Shift for fine moves) or use the mouse wheel; double-click to reset a knob to its default.

Presets

Presets 1 to 8 set the master section (DRIVE, TONE, COLOUR, IRON, VOLUME, MONO and DRIFT) and leave the channels, SAT and the output stage alone. Stereo Pairs and Wide Fan only set the pans; Mono Check only switches MONO on.

Init
The default master: no drive, flat tone, COLOUR in, IRON out, DRIFT 20%.
Clean Bus
A straight sum: COLOUR out and no drift.
Warm Iron
+6 dB of drive, a little darker, IRON in.
Drum Bus Glue
+9 dB of drive, a touch brighter, IRON in, VOLUME -1 dB.
Pushed Console
+14 dB of drive with IRON in, VOLUME -2 dB and a bit more drift: the console leant on hard.
Dark Broadcast
Light drive with the tone well over to DARK.
Bright Lift
A little drive with the tone towards BRIGHT.
Tired Supply
+7 dB of drive, slightly dark, IRON in, DRIFT at 85%: an old desk on a wandering supply.
Stereo Pairs
Pans the channels as stereo pairs: odd channels hard left, even channels hard right (1/2, 3/4 ... 15/16).
Wide Fan
Spreads the channels evenly from channel 1 hard left to channel 16 hard right.
Mono Check
Switches MONO on and changes nothing else.
Reset all to defaults
Every channel back to 0 dB, centre, unmuted and unsoloed, the master to Init, SAT to 0 and the output stage to CLIP. The channel names are kept.

Tips

  • Use Stereo Pairs when you feed stereo sources in as two mono channels (left into an odd channel, right into the next even one).
  • DRIVE is level-matched, so judge it by the shape of the sound, not the loudness. Watch the peak meter: more drive brings the peaks down a little.
  • For the cleanest sum, load Clean Bus; for a real "through the desk" feel, try Warm Iron and adjust DRIVE to taste.
  • Name the channels as you route them; the names stay with the project.
  • Set DRIFT to 0 for a static, repeatable mix; leave a little in for a less clinical feel.

File: gfx-bern-16.jsfx

Summing

Cassette 4

A cassette four-track as a summing mixer: four stereo stems recorded to tape and mixed down.

Overview

Cassette 4 is a summing mixer in the spirit of the 80s portable multitrack recorders. Each of four stereo stems goes to its own track of the tape - recorded, played back through the heads - and is then mixed down through the recorder's little mixer with its simple tone controls, pan and level.

Every track gets its own tape path: noise-reduction encoding, tape saturation, the wobble of the transport, the head's top-end loss and bump, dropouts and hiss, and the decoding on the way back. All four tracks share one transport, so they wow and flutter together, just like on a real machine.

Two models are on board. MODEL 15 is the compact standard-speed machine: soft top end, fat head bump, more wow and hiss. MODEL 44 is the double-speed machine with compander noise reduction: more top end and steadier, with the gentle breathing and pumping that compander noise reduction is known for.

Reach for it for

  • Lo-fi and bedroom-demo mixes
  • Giving a whole track that warm, wobbly cassette character
  • Gluing drums, keys and vocals with tape saturation
  • Pumping, breathing tape compression (MODEL 44)
  • Worn-out, nostalgic textures (AGE, HISS, WOW)

Setting up the routing in REAPER

  1. Create a bus track and put Cassette 4 on it. Open the track's ROUTE window and set Track channels to 8.
  2. On each stem track, add a send to the bus track and pick its destination channel pair in the send's audio routing: TRACK 1 on 1/2, TRACK 2 on 3/4, TRACK 3 on 5/6, TRACK 4 on 7/8.
  3. Turn off the master send on every stem track, so the stems are heard only through Cassette 4.
  4. TRACK 1 also receives whatever plays on the bus track itself (channels 1/2), so keep the bus track empty or use it as track 1.
  5. The mix leaves on channels 1/2, so leave the bus track's master send on. The green light under each track name lights when signal arrives.

Controls

The mixer: TRACK 1 to TRACK 4

Four channel strips, each with its input channel pair printed under the name.

Signal light
Glows green when audio arrives on the track and it is being heard.
TREBLE
A high shelf at 10 kHz, -10 to +10 dB. Brighten a track that the tape has softened, or dull it further.
BASS
A low shelf at 100 Hz, -10 to +10 dB.
PAN
A balance control: in the centre both sides play at full level; turning it fades the opposite side down.
LEVEL
The track's mixdown level, from OFF (-60 dB) to +6 dB; 0 dB by default.
SOLO
Solo in place; several tracks can be soloed at once.
MUTE
Silences the track. A muted track stays muted even when soloed.

TAPE

DRIVE (coloured cap)
The recording level, -6 to +12 dB. Hotter means more tape saturation. It is level-matched, so it changes the colour rather than the volume.
WOW
Wow and flutter, 0 to 100 (30 by default): a slow wander plus a faster flutter. One transport moves all four tracks together. The 44 model is steadier at the same setting.
HISS
The tape noise, 0 to 100. The middle (50) is the model's own amount. Each track has its own hiss, so four busy tracks mean four times the noise, as on the real thing.
AGE
An older, worn tape, 0 to 100 (10 by default): the top end goes duller and short dropouts creep in, more often the higher you turn it.
MASTER
The final mix level, -24 to +12 dB.
MODEL 15 / 44
Picks the machine. 15: standard speed, B-type noise reduction, a soft top end around 9.5 kHz, a fatter head bump, more wow and hiss. 44: double speed, compander noise reduction, more top end (around 15 kHz), a steadier transport. The panel changes colour with the model.
NR
Noise reduction in or out. Out gives you the raw tape, hiss and all. In MODEL 44 the compander squeezes the signal 2:1 onto the tape and expands it 1:2 coming back; because the expander follows what comes off the tape, it breathes and pumps a little - turn DRIVE up to hear more of it.

Meters

L / R
VU meters showing the left and right mix output.

The header

PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets. Choosing one sets the plugin's controls for that job; the input and output stages are left as they are. The last entry resets everything to the defaults.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.
OUTPUT
Folds the input and output stages out below the panel (and back in). The light shows when they are open.

Output stage (OUTPUT fold-out)

SAT
Band saturation on the way out: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
BIAS
Shapes the saturation: more bias adds more even (2nd) harmonics, for a warmer, rounder colour.
LO BAND / HI BAND
Where the low and high saturation bands start (40 - 400 Hz and 2 - 16 kHz).
CEILING / THRESH
With CLIP: the level the peaks are softly clipped at. With LIMIT: the threshold of a brickwall limiter that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS - pulling it down limits harder and makes the output louder.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper (no latency), or a lookahead limiter (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER).

Presets

Bedroom Demo (15)
The standard-speed machine at its default settings, NR in.
Hot and Woolly (15)
Recorded hot, with more wow, hiss and age.
No NR, Old Tape (15)
Noise reduction off, lots of hiss, wobble and wear.
Worn-out Cassette (15)
Driven hard on a badly worn tape with heavy wow.
Clean Double Speed (44)
The double-speed machine at its cleanest.
Pumping Compander (44)
6 dB of drive to bring out the compander's breathing.
Squashed Tape (44)
Driven hard for a dense, squashed tape sound.
44 without NR
The double-speed machine with the noise reduction out.
Reset all to defaults
Every control back to its starting value, including the mixer and the output stage.

Tips

  • The presets only change the model and the TAPE section; your track levels, EQ, pans, solos and mutes are left alone (except Reset).
  • Use the TREBLE knobs on each track to win back a little top end after the tape, rather than turning AGE down to zero.
  • Put drums or the busiest material on one track and pads on another: each track saturates on its own, so the mix stays clearer than squashing everything through one tape.
  • Every track adds its own hiss even when nothing is sent to it, so MUTE the tracks you are not using to keep the noise floor down.

File: gfx-cassette-4.jsfx

Summing

Compact 12

The little 12-channel desk from every 90s project studio, as a summing mixer.

Overview

Compact 12 is a summing mixer in the spirit of the famous compact low-noise desks of the 90s: four mono channels and four stereo pairs, each with EQ, pan or balance, level, solo and mute, summed to a stereo main mix.

It is clean and honest, with the tight, slightly forward sound of its op-amps. Switch HOT on and the summing amps are driven into their rails for grit, edge and a little bite (level-matched, so it does not just get louder). NOISE adds the desk's own hiss floor.

Each channel strip can be named, and the names are saved with the project.

Reach for it for

  • Summing stems or groups through a simple desk
  • Quick level, pan and tone balancing of up to 12 inputs in one place
  • Adding op-amp edge to a mix or drum bus (HOT)
  • Lo-fi, 'real desk' character with the noise floor
  • Solo and mute of stems while mixing

Setting up the routing in REAPER

  1. Create a bus track and put Compact 12 on it. Open the track's ROUTE window and set Track channels to 12.
  2. For the mono channels, add a send from each mono source to the bus track and set its destination to a single channel: channel 1, 2, 3 or 4.
  3. For the stereo channels, send each stereo stem to a channel pair: 5/6, 7/8, 9/10 or 11/12.
  4. Turn off the master send on every source track, so it is heard only through Compact 12 (otherwise you hear it twice).
  5. The mix leaves on channels 1/2, so leave the bus track's master send on. The green light at the top of each strip lights when signal arrives on it.

Controls

The header

PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets (a click on the preset display does the same). The presets set the master section or flatten the channels; the last entry resets everything to the defaults.
◄ / ►
Step to the previous or next preset. The display shows the number and name of the preset in use.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Channel strips

Eight strips: CH 1 to 4 are MONO, CH 5-6, 7-8, 9-10 and 11-12 are STEREO. Knobs: drag up and down (Shift for fine control) or use the mouse wheel; double-click to reset. The value shows while you hover.

Name
Click the name at the top of a strip and type (e.g. Drums). Enter keeps it, Escape cancels; an empty name goes back to the channel number.
Signal light
The green light glows when audio arrives on that channel and the channel is being heard (not muted, turned off or left out by a solo).
HI (green cap)
High shelf at 12 kHz, -15 to +15 dB.
MID (blue cap)
Mono channels only: a bell at 2.5 kHz, -12 to +12 dB.
LOW (yellow cap)
Low shelf at 80 Hz, -15 to +15 dB.
PAN / BAL
Mono channels: PAN, a constant-power pan from L100 to R100. Stereo channels: BAL, which turns the other side down as you move away from the centre.
LEVEL
The channel's level, from OFF (fully left, -60 dB) to +10 dB; 0 dB by default.
SOLO
Solo in place. Any number of channels can be soloed at once; while anything is soloed, only the soloed channels are heard.
MUTE
Silences the channel. A muted channel stays muted even when it is soloed.

MAIN MIX

Meters
12-LED meters for the main mix, left and right. 0 is 0 VU (-18 dBFS); the top light is CLIP.
MAIN MIX
The main mix level into the summing amps, from OFF (-60 dB) to +10 dB.
HOT
Drives the summing amps into their rails: asymmetric op-amp grit, edge and a little bite, level-matched. Off, the amps stay clean up to just above 0 dBFS.
NOISE
Adds the desk's own hiss floor, about -84 dBFS.
SOLO
Flashes while any channel is soloed.

There is no separate output stage on this unit: its own character does that job.

Presets

The first four presets set only MAIN MIX, HOT and NOISE; the channel settings are left alone.

Clean Desk
MAIN MIX at 0 dB, HOT and NOISE off.
Hot Desk
HOT on: the summing amps driven into their rails.
Hot and Noisy
HOT and NOISE both on - the real thing.
Clean with Desk Noise
Clean amps with the hiss floor, MAIN MIX at -3 dB.
Flat all channels
Every channel back to 0 dB level, flat EQ, centred, with solos and mutes off. The master section and channel names are left alone.
Reset all to defaults
Every control back to its starting value.

Tips

  • Name the strips as soon as the routing is done; it makes solo and mute much quicker to use.
  • HOT is level-matched, so you can switch it in and out to judge the colour fairly.
  • Use the MID on mono channels at 2.5 kHz to bring a vocal or guitar forward, or cut it to push them back.
  • Keep the main-mix meters around 0 (0 VU) and below the red: that is where the desk sounds as intended.
  • BAL on a stereo stem keeps both sides of the stem; to narrow it, do that on the stem's own track.

File: gfx-compact-12.jsfx

Summing

Lustre

A transformer-coupled console summing mixer in the spirit of the silky late-80s British desks.

Overview

Lustre sums up to eight stereo stems through the bus of a transformer-coupled console from the late 80s: smooth, wide and expensive-sounding, with weight in the low mids and a top end that never bites.

The transformer works on the low mids (roughly 60 - 400 Hz). DENSITY sets how hard the iron is driven there, COLOUR chooses the character of the harmonics (even and round, or odd and firm), and a light touch of full-band density sits on top. WEIGHT and AIR add the console's low-mid body and a silky lift at the very top.

With the TRANSFORMER switched out, Lustre is a clean summing mixer with level, pan, solo and mute for each stem.

Reach for it for

  • Summing stems or groups through one console-style bus
  • Adding warmth and low-mid weight without dulling the top
  • A smooth, open top end on a whole mix (AIR)
  • Choosing between rounder (even) and firmer (odd) harmonic colour
  • Quick stem balancing with solo and mute in one place

Setting up the routing in REAPER

  1. Create a mix bus track and put Lustre on it. Open the track's ROUTE window and set Track channels to 16.
  2. Channels 1/2 are STEM 1: whatever plays on the bus track itself (or is sent to it on 1/2).
  3. On each other stem track, add a send to the bus track and pick a different destination channel pair for each: stem 2 on 3/4, stem 3 on 5/6, stem 4 on 7/8, stem 5 on 9/10, stem 6 on 11/12, stem 7 on 13/14, stem 8 on 15/16.
  4. Turn off the master send on every stem track, so the stems are heard only through Lustre (otherwise you hear them twice).
  5. The mix leaves on channels 1/2, so leave the bus track's master send on. The light under each stem's number glows when signal arrives.

Controls

Stems

Eight channel strips. The channel pair is printed under each name (1-2 up to 15-16).

Name
Click the name at the top of a strip and type (up to 16 characters, e.g. Drums). Enter keeps it, Escape cancels, an empty name goes back to the number. The names are saved with the project.
Signal light
Glows green when audio arrives on that stem and the stem is being heard (not muted, not left out by a solo, level not OFF).
PAN
Balance between left and right, from L100 through C to R100. Turning towards one side turns the other side down; the centre leaves both at full level.
LEVEL
The stem's level, from OFF (fully left, -60 dB) to +10 dB; 0 dB by default.
SOLO
Solo in place. Any number of stems can be soloed at once; while anything is soloed, only the soloed stems are heard.
MUTE
Silences the stem. A muted stem stays muted even when it is soloed.

Master

VU meters (L / R)
The output level of the mix.
DRIVE
The level into the bus and its transformers, -6 to +12 dB. It is level-matched: it changes how hard the iron works, not the volume.
DENSITY
How hard the transformer iron works, 0 to 100 (30 by default). The saturation is concentrated in the low mids, roughly 60 - 400 Hz, with a gentle touch of density across the whole band.
COLOUR
The character of the harmonics the iron adds. Left (EVEN) favours the 2nd harmonic: rounder and warmer. Right (ODD) favours the 3rd: firmer and more forward. The centre (BLEND) is the balanced mix.
AIR
A broad, silky lift at the very top (a shelf around 12 kHz), 0 to +6 dB.
WEIGHT
The transformer's low-mid weight: a broad lift around 180 Hz, 0 to +4 dB.
MASTER
The final level of the mix, from OFF (fully left, -60 dB) to +10 dB.
TRANSFORMER
In (lit, the default): the console's colour - DRIVE, DENSITY, COLOUR, WEIGHT and AIR all work, and the label reads TRANSFORMER-COUPLED BUS. Out: a straight, clean sum (STRAIGHT SUM); those five controls then have no effect.

The header

PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets; the arrows beside it step through them, and the display shows the one in use (click it for the list too). The presets change only the bus controls; the last entry resets everything to the defaults.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Presets

Console Sheen
The default iron with a touch of air and weight.
Warm and Round (even)
4 dB of drive, more density, colour towards EVEN, extra air and weight.
Forward and Firm (odd)
4 dB of drive, more density, colour towards ODD for a firmer, more forward sound.
Driven Desk
8 dB of drive and heavy density, slightly even, with generous air and weight.
Open Top (air)
Light density with 3 dB of air for an open top end.
Low-Mid Weight
Even colour with 3 dB of weight and no air, for body in the low mids.
Straight Sum (transformer out)
Transformer out: a clean sum.
Reset all to defaults
Every control back to its starting value, including stem levels, pans, solos and mutes.

Tips

  • The presets change only the bus settings; your stem levels, pans, solos and mutes are left alone (except Reset).
  • Because DRIVE is level-matched, you can sweep it freely and judge the colour without being fooled by a jump in level.
  • Try COLOUR towards EVEN on dense rock or pop mixes that feel hard, and towards ODD on mixes that need more definition.
  • Toggle TRANSFORMER to compare the console colour against a clean sum at the same balance.

File: gfx-lustre.jsfx

Summing

M-Sum

An analogue broadcast-console summing bus with transformer iron and a fast VCA bus compressor.

Overview

M-Sum sums your stems the way an 80s British broadcast console's mix bus does. Up to eight stereo inputs - the bus track's own audio plus seven stems - are added together through the desk's bus physics, then pass through its output transformer and its fast VCA bus compressor.

The special part is the bus itself. The more energy you put on it, the further the virtual power rail sags (down to 65% at most), which acts as a soft, natural bus compression that responds to the whole mix rather than to any one stem. The transformer saturates only the lows below about 150 Hz, mostly with 3rd harmonic, and adds a small resonance at 35 Hz; the mids and highs pass through untouched.

Turned down, it is a clean summing mixer with solo and mute per stem. Pushed with DRIVE, IRON and SAG, it thickens, glues and bends like a desk running hot.

Reach for it for

  • Summing stems or groups through one console-style bus
  • Gluing a mix with a fast VCA bus compressor
  • Adding low-end weight and iron without dulling the top
  • Making a mix feel denser and more 'run through a desk' (SAG, DRIVE)
  • Quick stem balancing with solo and mute in one place

Setting up the routing in REAPER

  1. Create a mix bus track and put M-Sum on it. Open the track's ROUTE window and set Track channels to 16.
  2. On each stem track, add a send to the bus track. In the send's audio routing, pick a different destination channel pair for each stem: STEM 1 on 3/4, STEM 2 on 5/6, STEM 3 on 7/8, STEM 4 on 9/10, STEM 5 on 11/12, STEM 6 on 13/14, STEM 7 on 15/16.
  3. Turn off the master send on every stem track, so the stems are heard only through M-Sum (otherwise you hear them twice).
  4. Channels 1/2 are the MAIN input: whatever plays on the bus track itself (or is sent to it on 1/2). Use it for an eighth stem, or leave it empty.
  5. The summed mix leaves on channels 1/2, so leave the bus track's master send on. The green light above each input lights when signal arrives on it.

Controls

STEMS

Eight input strips: MAIN (channels 1-2) and stems 1 to 7 (channels 3-4 up to 15-16). The channel pair is printed under each number.

Signal light
The green light glows when audio arrives on that input and the input is being heard (not muted or left out by a solo).
Level knob
The input's level, from OFF (fully left, -60 dB) to +6 dB; 0 dB by default.
SOLO
Solo in place. Any number of inputs can be soloed at once; while anything is soloed, only the soloed inputs are heard.
MUTE
Silences the input. A muted input stays muted even when it is soloed.

CONSOLE BUS

DRIVE (yellow cap)
The level into the bus and the transformer, 0 to 24 dB. More DRIVE pushes the iron and the rail sag harder; it also raises the level, so bring OUTPUT down to compensate.
IRON (yellow cap)
How hard the output transformer's core works, 0 to 100 (15 by default). Only the lows below about 150 Hz are saturated, mostly with 3rd harmonic, for weight and density in the bottom end while the top stays clean.
SAG
How much the power rail sags under load, 0 to 0.30 (0.05 by default). The more energy on the bus, the lower the rail - a soft, programme-dependent squeeze. It reacts quickly (2 ms) and recovers in about 60 ms, so it feels like bus compression rather than distortion.
CROSSTALK
The left and right buses bleeding into each other, as on a real desk. Fully left is OFF; turn it up (to 0 dB) to narrow and glue the stereo image slightly.
STRESS
The orange light glows when the rail starts to sag.
RAIL
The bar and number show the rail voltage, from 100 (no sag) down to 65 (maximum sag). It turns orange when the rail drops below 90.

VCA BUS COMPRESSOR

A fast feed-forward compressor after the transformer. It reads the peaks of both sides and applies the same gain to both, so the image stays put.

THRESH (yellow cap)
The level where compression starts, -30 to 0 dB (-12 dB by default).
RATIO
1:1 to 20:1 (4:1 by default). 2:1 for gentle glue, 4:1 and up for obvious control.
ATTACK
0.1 to 20 ms (1 ms by default). Short times catch the transients; longer times let the punch through before it clamps down.
RELEASE
10 to 600 ms (150 ms by default). It is programme dependent: the longer the compressor keeps working (more than about 2 dB of reduction), the slower it releases, up to four times the set value, so sustained passages don't pump.
MAKEUP
Manual make-up gain, 0 to 18 dB. When AUTO is on, the knob reads AUTO and has no effect.
IN
Switches the compressor in or out (in by default).
AUTO
Automatic make-up: adds back half the reduction a full-scale peak would get, so the level stays roughly steady as you change THRESH and RATIO.
OUTPUT (yellow cap)
The final level of the bus, -24 to +12 dB. It works whether the compressor is in or out.

METERS

GR
VU meter showing the compressor's gain reduction.
BUS OUT
VU meter showing the output level of the bus.

The header

PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets. Choosing one sets the plugin's controls for that job; the input and output stages are left as they are. The last entry resets everything to the defaults.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.
OUTPUT
Folds the input and output stages out below the panel (and back in). The light shows when they are open.

Output stage (OUTPUT fold-out)

SAT
Band saturation on the way out: only the lows and highs are saturated (the mids stay clean), with a soft curve that loosens as the level rises.
BIAS
Shapes the saturation: more bias adds more even (2nd) harmonics, for a warmer, rounder colour.
LO BAND / HI BAND
Where the low and high saturation bands start (40 - 400 Hz and 2 - 16 kHz).
CEILING / THRESH
With CLIP: the level the peaks are softly clipped at. With LIMIT: the threshold of a brickwall limiter that holds the peaks at -0.3 dBFS - pulling it down limits harder and makes the output louder.
OFF / CLIP / LIMIT
The final stage: nothing, a soft clipper (no latency), or a lookahead limiter (3 ms latency, compensated by REAPER).

Presets

Clean Sum
A light touch of iron and sag, compressor out.
Warm Console
A little drive and iron with gentle 2:1 bus compression.
Driven Desk
8 dB of drive, more iron, sag and crosstalk, 3:1 compression.
Broadcast Glue
4:1 fast-attack compression with AUTO make-up, for steady broadcast-style glue.
Heavy Iron
Full IRON for maximum low-end weight, slow gentle compression.
Punch Bus
6:1 with a very fast attack and release and more sag, for punchy, pumping drums.
Stressed Rail
10 dB of drive, heavy sag and some crosstalk for a squeezed, overdriven desk.
Straight Sum (no colour)
No iron, no sag, compressor out: a clean sum.
Reset all to defaults
Every control back to its starting value, including stem levels, solos, mutes and the output stage.

Tips

  • The presets change only the bus and compressor settings; your stem levels, solos and mutes are left alone (except Reset).
  • Watch the RAIL number: a few points of sag on the loud parts is glue, a rail sitting in the 70s is an effect.
  • Because DRIVE raises the level, use OUTPUT to match levels before judging whether the colour is better.
  • For mix-bus glue, start with RATIO 2:1, a 10 ms attack and the GR meter showing only 1-3 dB on the peaks.

File: gfx-m-sum.jsfx

Summing

Stage 8

A big theatre console's mix bus as an eight-stem summing mixer, with VCA groups.

Overview

Stage 8 sums eight stereo stems through the mix bus of a big British theatre console, in the spirit of the consoles famous for their clarity in the West End and on Broadway. Each stem has a level, a pan, a VCA assign, solo and mute; two VCA group masters ride whole groups of stems with one knob.

Two console characters are on board. VINTAGE is the earlier, transformer-coupled desk: the lows thicken and saturate gently as the level rises, there is a soft lift in the presence region, a silky top that rolls off just above the audio band, a whisper of crosstalk and noise, and a gentle squeeze when driven. MODERN is the later transformerless desk: flat, wide, true stereo, with enormous headroom.

DRIVE sets the level into the summing amplifiers. It is level-matched, so it changes the colour, not the volume.

Reach for it for

  • Summing stems or groups through one console-style bus
  • Riding several stems together with a VCA master
  • Adding gentle transformer weight and a silky top to a mix (VINTAGE)
  • Clean, wide summing with solo and mute in one place (MODERN)
  • Theatre-style mixing: grouping a band, vocals and effects for quick balancing

Setting up the routing in REAPER

  1. Create a bus track and put Stage 8 on it. Open the track's ROUTE window and set Track channels to 16.
  2. On each stem track, add a send to the bus track and pick its destination channel pair in the send's audio routing: stem 1 on 1/2, stem 2 on 3/4, stem 3 on 5/6, and so on up to stem 8 on 15/16. The pair is printed under each strip's name.
  3. Turn off the master send on every stem track, so the stems are heard only through Stage 8.
  4. Stem 1 also receives whatever plays on the bus track itself (channels 1/2), so keep the bus track empty or use it as stem 1.
  5. The mix leaves on channels 1/2, so leave the bus track's master send on. The green light on each strip lights when signal arrives and the stem is being heard.

Controls

Drag a knob up or down to turn it, or double-click to reset it.

The header

PRESETS
Opens the list of factory presets (clicking the display does the same). A preset sets only MODE and DRIVE; the stems and VCAs are left as they are. The last entry resets everything to the defaults.
Left / right arrows
Step to the previous or next preset.
S / M / L
Panel size: small, medium or large. The panel also scales to fit the plugin window.
?
Opens a pop-up with the design credit, the GFX website address and the address of this manual. Click anywhere to close it.

Stems 1 to 8

Name
Click the name at the top of the strip and type (e.g. Drums), up to 16 characters. Enter keeps it, Escape cancels, and an empty name goes back to the number. The names are saved with the project.
Signal light
Glows green when audio arrives on the stem and it is being heard.
PAN
A balance control, L100 to R100: in the centre both sides play at full level; turning it fades the opposite side down.
LEVEL
The stem's level, from OFF (-60 dB) to +10 dB; 0 dB by default.
VCA
Click to step the stem's VCA assign: none (VCA -), VCA A (orange light) or VCA B (blue light). An assigned stem follows that VCA master's level and mute.
SOLO
Solo in place; several stems can be soloed at once. While anything is soloed, only the soloed stems are heard.
MUTE
Silences the stem. A muted stem stays muted even when soloed.

VCA A and VCA B

LEVEL
The group master, OFF (-60 dB) to +10 dB. It rides every stem assigned to it, on top of their own levels.
MUTE
Mutes every stem assigned to that VCA.

MASTER

VINTAGE / MODERN
Picks the console. VINTAGE: transformer-coupled, with low-end thickening, presence lift, a silky top, a whisper of crosstalk and noise, and a gentle squeeze when driven. MODERN: transformerless - flat and clean, and only extreme levels are softly limited. The caption under the controls reads TRANSFORMER-COUPLED or TRANSFORMERLESS.
DRIVE
The level into the summing amps, -6 to +12 dB. It is level-matched: more DRIVE gives more colour in VINTAGE, not more volume.
MASTER
The final mix level, from OFF (-60 dB) to +10 dB.
L / R meters
Two 12-light meters showing the mix output; 0 on the meter is -18 dBFS.

Presets

Vintage
The transformer-coupled console, DRIVE at 0 dB.
Vintage, driven
The vintage console with 6 dB of drive.
Vintage, pushed hard
The vintage console with 10 dB of drive, for a thicker, squeezed bus.
Modern
The transformerless console, DRIVE at 0 dB.
Modern, driven
The modern console with 6 dB of drive.
Reset all to defaults
Every control back to its starting value, including stem levels, pans, VCA assigns, solos, mutes and the VCA masters.

Tips

  • Assign the band to VCA A and the vocals to VCA B: then one knob each balances the whole show, as on a theatre desk.
  • Because DRIVE is level-matched, you can sweep it and judge the colour without being fooled by loudness.
  • Name the strips - the names are saved with the project, so the console makes sense when you come back to it.
  • Try VINTAGE with DRIVE at +6 dB on a full mix, then switch to MODERN to hear how much of the weight and silk comes from the transformer stages.

File: gfx-stage-8.jsfx

MIDI tools

CC-80

A pocket-calculator chord generator that writes voice-led chords into a MIDI item at the edit cursor.

Overview

CC-80 is a chord scratchpad in the style of an 80s pocket calculator. Build a chord from a root (A - G, flat or sharp), a quality (maj, min, dim, aug, sus4), a seventh and an extension (9, 11, 13), choose a duration and press = to write it into a MIDI item at the edit cursor. The cursor then moves on, ready for the next chord.

Each new chord is voiced close to the last one (voice leading), so a progression moves smoothly instead of jumping around. REST skips time, the memory keys store a sequence of chords and write it out in one go, and DRIFT humanises the velocity and timing.

It is a ReaScript, not a plugin: it runs in its own window and needs the free ReaImGui extension.

Reach for it for

  • Sketching chord progressions quickly
  • Writing pad and keys parts with smooth voice leading
  • Trying out extended chords without working out the notes
  • Storing a progression and writing it again elsewhere in the song

Installing

1. Install ReaImGui (once)

  1. If you don't have ReaPack yet, install it from reapack.com and restart REAPER.
  2. Open Extensions > ReaPack > Browse packages.
  3. Search for ReaImGui (from the ReaTeam Extensions repository), right-click it and choose Install, then Apply.
  4. Restart REAPER. CC-80 needs ReaImGui 0.9 or newer; if it is missing, the script tells you so when you run it.

2. Load the script

  1. Open Actions > Show action list.
  2. Choose New action > Load ReaScript and pick GFX_CC-80.lua.
  3. Select it in the list and press Run. The CC-80 window opens.
  4. To open it quickly next time, assign it a shortcut or add it to a toolbar from the action list.

Where the chords go

  • If a MIDI editor is open, the chords go into the take it is editing.
  • Otherwise they go on the first selected track: into the latest MIDI item that starts at or before the edit cursor (as long as it doesn't end more than 8 beats before it), or into a new MIDI item.
  • The item is lengthened automatically if the chord runs past its end.
  • Notes are written at the edit cursor on MIDI channel 1 at velocity 100, and the edit cursor moves to the end of the chord. Each entry can be undone.

Controls

The display

Top line
The chord name as you build it (e.g. Cmaj7), or ERR.
Second line
The chord spelled note by note (e.g. C - E - G - B), or the error message.
Bottom line
Left: the current duration. Middle: Mn when chords are stored in memory, Dnn when DRIFT is on. Right: the last action (READY, INS Am7, REST 1/4 and so on).

Building a chord

Keys that hold a setting have a small red light.

C D E F G A B
Choose the root. Pressing a root always starts a new chord.
b / #
Flat or sharp the root. Press again to cancel.
maj / min / dim / aug / sus4
The quality. With none chosen, the chord is major.
7
Adds a seventh: a flat 7th on most chords, but a major 7th after maj (maj + 7 = maj7) and a diminished 7th after dim (dim7).
M7
Adds a major 7th.
m7
The minor-seventh chord: it implies min (so A + m7 = Am7); with dim it gives the half-diminished m7b5.
9 / 11 / 13
Adds an extension; the last one pressed wins. An extension brings its seventh with it (a major 7th after maj, otherwise a flat 7th) and the 9th. 11 drops the major 3rd to avoid a clash; 13 leaves out the 11th.

Some combinations are refused, with a message on the display: two different qualities or sevenths, m7 with maj, aug or sus4 (m7 CLASH), any extension on dim (DIM EXT), and 11 on sus4 or aug.

Duration and writing

1/1 / 1/2 / 1/4 / 1/8
The length of the next chord or rest: whole note, half note, quarter note or eighth note.
=
Writes the chord at the edit cursor and moves the cursor on.
REST
Moves the edit cursor on by the current duration without writing anything.
CLEAR
Clears the chord you are building. Pressed again with nothing entered, it resets the voice leading (VOICE RESET), so the next chord is voiced fresh around the middle of the range.

Memory and drift

M+
Stores the current chord, with its duration, at the end of the memory.
MR
Writes every stored chord in order at the edit cursor (its light shows the memory holds something).
MC
Clears the memory.
DRIFT
Steps through 0, 25, 50, 75 and 100%. It humanises each note: at 100% velocity varies by up to 25 either side of 100 and timing by up to 12 ms.

The duration, DRIFT setting and memory are remembered the next time you run the script.

Keyboard shortcuts

While the CC-80 window has focus: A - G choose the root, Enter is =, Space is REST, Backspace is CLEAR, and 1, 2, 4 and 8 choose the duration.

Voice leading

CC-80 picks the inversion and octave of each chord that moves the fewest semitones from the previous one, keeping the notes between E2 and C6 and avoiding close, muddy intervals low down. The first chord (or the first after VOICE RESET) is placed around the middle of that range in root position where it can.

Messages

NO ROOT / NO CHORD
Choose a root first.
2 QUALITIES / 2 SEVENTHS
Only one quality and one seventh per chord - press CLEAR or a new root to start again.
MEM EMPTY
MR was pressed with nothing stored.
NO TRACK
No MIDI editor is open and no track is selected.
NOT MIDI / NO ITEM
The target take is not MIDI, or a new item could not be made.

Tips

  • Open the MIDI editor on the item you are working on and the chords go straight into it.
  • Store a progression with M+ as you try it out, then place the edit cursor at the next section and press MR to write it all again.
  • Use REST between chords for rhythmic stabs, or 1/8 chords for pushes before the bar.
  • If a progression has drifted too high or low, press CLEAR twice to reset the voice leading.

File: GFX_CC-80.lua

GFX (Garden FX) - free JSFX plug-ins for REAPER. Designed by JGWizrad. https://www.gardenfx.uk/
